# React Migration Plan - All Phases Complete (verification sweep 2026-09-20)

## 🎯 **Overview**

Migrating view-prs from vanilla JavaScript to React in phases, starting with the PR table component for maximum performance impact with minimal risk.

**Key Principle:** Incremental migration with working software at each phase

**Measured Performance:** React is 1.4-1.5x faster than vanilla at 50 PRs, 3.8-3.9x faster at 500 PRs, flat across how many PRs changed per update (not the delta-size-scaling 19x originally guessed) — see "Performance Validation" below for the real numbers and why

---

## 📋 **Phase Breakdown**

| Phase | Component | Time | Status |
|-------|-----------|------|--------|
| **Phase 1** | PR Table (Hybrid) | 30-40h | 🟢 **Complete — parity-verified in a real browser, performance measured** (1.4-3.9x faster than vanilla depending on dataset size, see "Performance Validation") |
| **Phase 2** | Filters & Controls | 20-30h | 🟢 **Complete** — every field in the Run & Filter tab is React-owned, 35 fields total (PR-number input, 4 selects, 10 checkboxes, 4 plain text/number inputs, 6 "Any (with/without)" plain selects, 1 textarea, all 9 multi-select dropdowns). Found and fixed two systemic bugs, each affecting every field of its kind converted so far at once — see gotcha #3 (orphaned "auto-apply on change" listeners, fixed via event delegation) and gotcha #4 below (multi-select restore-race clobbered by React's non-synchronous commit, fixed via `flushSync`). Also found and **fixed** a pre-existing, unrelated bug while testing the six plain selects: `deriveFilterPipelineState` never forwarded customComments/otherNotes/prDifficulty/rallyStories/rallyLinks/analysisOfPr into the real row-filtering criteria, so those six fields never actually filtered anything despite looking functional - now wired through. Some filter-dropdown compatibility work also landed alongside Phase 1 (see git history: "Fix dropdown auto-population", "Update attention rules and options"). **Decision (2026-09-11):** the original "remove vanilla JS filter variables / migrate debounce to hooks" items are explicitly deferred to Phase 6 rather than pursued here — vanilla staying the source of truth for `applyFiltersFromCache` (with React fields just mirroring it) is what made 35 fields convertible incrementally without Context or a state rewrite; unwinding that now would be a large, high-risk change touching every field's bridge code at once, for a goal ("filters render/behave via React") this phase already achieves. **Resolved (2026-09-24):** this was actually completed in Phase 6 (2026-09-12 to 09-14) via `FilterStateProvider`/`FilterStateContext`/`useDebouncedCallback` - all 35 Run & Filter fields now own their state as real React state, with the debounce migrated to a hook - just never marked resolved against this note. The few remaining vanilla `let pendingXxx` variables in `index.page.js` are documented pre-mount/test fallbacks, not unmigrated state |
| **Phase 3** | Other Tabs | 20-30h | 🟢 **Complete: Review Stats tab converted** (`<ReviewStatsControls />` + `<ReviewStatsContent />`, mounted into static `#stats-controls-root`/`#stats-content-root` siblings - `renderStatsView` never rebuilds either once React owns them), except the chart visuals' internal rendering (~900 lines of hand-rolled SVG/DOM building), which stays vanilla, wrapped via a ref. Found and fixed a real pre-existing bug along the way: a stats card's "View in table" button built its own raw-DOM navigation that never worked under a React-owned PR table - now routed through the same already-React-safe helper Author Insights uses. **Author Insights tab fully converted.** Its "Author" selector is converted (`<AuthorInsightsSelector />`, mounted into `#author-insights-selector-root`, same container-split approach), and its **created-PRs section**, **selected-author header**, **PR-linked notes section**, and **manual comments composer/list are all converted** (`<AuthorCreatedPrsSection />`/`<AuthorInsightsHeader />`/`<AuthorInsightsNotesSection />`/`<AuthorInsightsCommentsSection />`, mounted into their own `#author-insights-created-prs-root`/`#author-insights-header-root`/`#author-insights-notes-root` sibling containers and, for comments, the existing `#author-insights-content-root` - reused rather than a new container, since it was already the one dedicated slot that hadn't been carved out yet. The container-split bug class has now shown up **six** times in this phase alone). The created-PRs, notes, and comments sections all wrap the existing DOM-building helpers (`window.buildAuthorInsightsCreatedPrsSection`/`window.buildAuthorInsightsNotesSection`/`window.buildAuthorInsightsCommentsSection`, extracted as `buildCreatedPrsSection`/`buildPrLinkedNotesSection`/`buildManualCommentsSection` in `pr-author-insights.component.js`) via a ref+`useEffect`, rather than reimplementing their markup in JSX - the comments section especially, since its mutable composer/edit draft state and save/edit POST side effects (via `authorInsightsState`/draft helpers/`postJson`) all live inside the wrapped vanilla builder and its own DOM event listeners, never touched by React, so wrapping it is not just a JSX-verbosity shortcut but the safer choice for state that must survive independently of React's render cycle. The created-PRs section is mounted with an incrementing `key` (not a plain `[rows]` effect dependency) because the same `rows` array reference can recur across author switches - `renderAuthorInsights` reads `authorInsightsState.selectedAuthorLogin` internally rather than passing it as a prop, so a `[rows]`-only effect would silently miss author changes; verified by reverting the `key` and watching the "survives an unrelated re-render" e2e test fail with a stale render count. The notes/comments sections and the header don't need that trick: all three receive a freshly-computed `selectedAuthor`/`selectedAuthorName` value as a prop on every render (unlike created-PRs' `rows`-only prop), so a plain effect/re-render dependency already re-runs on every author switch. Converting the comments section required removing the last unconditional `contentHost.innerHTML = ""` reset that used to run at the top of every `renderAuthorInsights` call (originally there to rebuild the one remaining "scratch" section) - since `#author-insights-content-root` is now itself a persistent React root, that reset would have corrupted it the same way rebuilding `#pr-sections` via `innerHTML` would in Phase 1. The empty-rows/empty-author-options early-return branches' "No local rows/authors" message now appends into the outer `#author-insights` host directly instead. Also fixed two real pre-existing bugs along the way (this slice and the prior one): `renderAuthorInsights`'s empty-state branches cleared the React-owned selector but left the created-PRs/header/notes/comments sections showing stale content - now all five are cleared together; and each converted section's vanilla fallback path (used only when React hasn't mounted, e.g. bare-fixture unit tests, where the selector/header/comments/notes/created-PRs all share one `<div id="author-insights">`) had to specifically avoid resetting that *shared* fallback host's `innerHTML` - only the section's own *dedicated* container gets cleared; clearing the shared host unconditionally would wipe out whatever sibling sections had already appended into it. Verified all of the above by temporarily reverting the container-split fix (rebuilding the whole outer `#author-insights` host, matching the old pre-split behavior) and confirming the header, notes, created-PRs, *and* comments e2e tests all fail as expected, then restoring and confirming green across 3 full playwright runs. The comments e2e test exercises the actual save/edit POST round-trip (against this suite's isolated per-run data directory), not just static rendering - it's the first Author Insights conversion test to do so. **Backfill tab converted, completing Phase 3.** Its status badges are converted (`<BackfillBadges />`, mounted directly into the existing `#backfill-badges` container, same shape as Phase 2's `MultiSelectCheckboxList`/Phase 1's `#pr-sections` - no container-split needed since that div isn't shared with anything else, and no `key` remount needed since the component is a stateless display list, unlike `MultiSelectCheckboxList`'s checked-state). The rest of the tab (start/stop/refresh buttons, the status `<pre>`, the log `<pre>` and its auto-scroll checkbox) stays vanilla - it's plain text/attribute updates and polling, not list rendering, so converting it would add JSX ceremony without removing any real complexity. Verified via a new e2e test that visits the tab and clicks "Refresh status" (a real but non-destructive GET against the isolated per-run data directory - never starts/stops the actual backfill process) and confirms the badges render and survive the refresh; full jest suite and 3 full playwright runs (30/30) green. **Phase 3 is now complete.** Post-completion follow-up (2026-09-15), Track A of the finish-the-migration effort: the Review Stats chart visuals (previously the one deliberate exception above - `createStatsVisuals`/`createReviewerActivityChart`, ~900 lines of hand-rolled `document.createElement` DOM building in `pr-review-stats-chart.component.js`/`pr-review-stats-visuals.component.js`, wrapped via a ref+`useEffect`) are now real JSX: `GraphCard.jsx` (the horizontal-bar metric cards), `ReviewerActivityChart.jsx` (the heatmap + hand-rolled div-based line chart, including the `ResizeObserver`-driven responsive width, the legend click-to-highlight/dim interaction, and the hover tooltip - all ported to React state instead of closure variables and imperative `.style`/`.textContent` mutation, deliberately keeping the same div-based line-chart approach rather than rewriting against `<svg>` or a charting library), and `StatsVisuals.jsx` (the composing container, replacing `ReviewStatsContent.jsx`'s `StatsVisuals` ref-wrapper). Both vanilla `.component.js` files and their tests were deleted; `createStatsGraphCard` (previously defined inline in `index.page.js`) was removed the same way once its only caller (`createStatsVisuals`) was gone. The pure data-shaping helpers those components need (`bucketTimelineChartData`, `aggregateReviewerCommentsTimeline`, `aggregateReviewerApprovalsTimeline`) are still vanilla, now exposed on `window` the same way every other leaf component already reads `window.toCount`/`window.asArray` - moving that data layer itself off `window` is Track C's (orchestration) concern, not this one. `GraphCard`'s header-click sort-by wiring now goes through the already-exposed `window.updateStatsViewStateAndRerender({ sortBy })` bridge rather than mutating `statsViewState` directly, since the raw state object was never exposed on `window` (only a snapshot via `getStatsViewState`). Verified with new `GraphCard.test.jsx`/`ReviewerActivityChart.test.jsx`/`StatsVisuals.test.jsx` (closing the prior gap where `ReviewStatsContent.test.jsx` always stubbed `window.createStatsVisuals` to return `null`, so the actual chart-building integration had zero test coverage) plus a new real-integration test in `ReviewStatsContent.test.jsx` itself; full jest suite green (207 suites/1862 tests) and a live Playwright check against the running app confirmed the heatmap, line chart, hover tooltip, and legend-click highlight/dim/focus-summary-text all render and behave identically to the vanilla original on real production data. Track B, batch 1 (2026-09-15): Author Insights' "PRs created by this author" and "PR-linked custom comments and sentiment" sections are now real JSX too - `AuthorCreatedPrsSection.jsx`/`AuthorInsightsNotesSection.jsx`, composing two new shared leaf components, `AuthorInsightsPrLink.jsx` (external GitHub link + "View in table" button) and `AuthorInsightsPrDataMeta.jsx` (status/approved/CHK/conversations/viewed-files/labels meta row) - replacing the ref+`useEffect` wrappers around `pr-author-insights.component.js`'s `buildCreatedPrsSection`/`buildPrLinkedNotesSection` (both deleted, along with the `window.buildAuthorInsightsCreatedPrsSection`/`window.buildAuthorInsightsNotesSection` bridges and the no-longer-used `prLinkHelpers` destructure). `AuthorCreatedPrsSection` now receives `selectedAuthorLogin` as a real prop (from `renderAuthorInsights`, via `window.updateAuthorInsightsCreatedPrs(rows, selectedAuthorLogin)`) instead of the wrapped builder reading `authorInsightsState.selectedAuthorLogin` from a closure - this removes react-app.jsx's previous incrementing-`key` remount hack for this component entirely, since a real prop change is now enough to re-derive the filtered/sorted list. The manual comments composer/editor (with its mutable draft state and save/edit POST round-trips) stays ref-wrapped for now - that's batch 2. Both new sections still read their pure filtering/sorting/formatting helpers off `window` (`getPreferredActorKey`, `sortAuthorInsightsCreatedPrsDesc`, `sortAuthorInsightsNoteMatchesDesc`, `noteAuthorMatchesSelection`, `getAuthorInsightsSentimentLabel`/`BadgeClassName`, `getAuthorInsightsCreatedPrStatus`/`StatusBadgeClassName`, `getAuthorInsightsNoteDisplayTimestamp`, plus a new `navigateToPrInTableFromAuthorInsights` bridge reusing the same React-safe `prAuthorInsightsPrLinkHelpers.navigateToPrInTable` Review Stats' own "View in table" button already uses) - the same "leaf components read window.* for pure data-shaping, moving that off window is Track C's job" pattern Track A established. Verified with new `AuthorInsightsPrLink.test.jsx`/`AuthorInsightsPrDataMeta.test.jsx`/rewritten `AuthorCreatedPrsSection.test.jsx`/`AuthorInsightsNotesSection.test.jsx` (real rendering/behavior instead of "was the bridge called with the right args"), full jest suite green (209 suites/1869 tests), lint clean, and a live Playwright check against the running app on real production data confirming both sections render correctly, author-switching updates them without a key remount, and "View in table" correctly navigates to the PR data tab. Track B batch 2 (2026-09-15), the hardest remaining piece: the "Manual author comments" composer/editor is now real JSX too (`AuthorInsightsCommentsSection.jsx`), replacing the ref+`useEffect` wrapper around `buildManualCommentsSection` and its `renderComposerForm`/`renderManualCommentList`/`renderManualCommentItem`/`renderEditForm` helpers (all deleted). The key design constraint this batch had to respect: composer/edit draft state could **not** become local-only React state, because `pr-auto-render-blocking.helpers.js`'s `getBlockingAuthorInsightsLogins` reads `authorInsightsState.manualCommentDraftByAuthorLogin`/`manualCommentEditDraftByAuthorLogin`/`manualCommentsByAuthorLogin` directly to decide whether an incoming poll should be blocked because the user has unsaved author-comment edits - a feature with zero test coverage in any of the files touched so far that would have silently broken had drafts moved to component-local state instead. So the new component keeps writing through the same `authorInsightsState`-backed bridges (`getAuthorInsightsComposerDraft`/`updateAuthorInsightsComposerDraft`/`getAuthorInsightsEditDraft`/`updateAuthorInsightsEditDraft`/`resetAuthorInsightsEditDraft`/`getAuthorManualCommentsForLogin`/new `setAuthorInsightsManualComments`, all newly exposed on `window`) and only uses local `useState` to trigger re-renders - the same "vanilla remains source of truth, React re-renders on top" shape as every earlier bridge, just now presented as controlled JSX inputs instead of raw DOM with manual event listeners. The save/edit POST/PUT calls (`window.saveAuthorManualComment`/`window.updateAuthorManualComment`, wrapping the existing `pr-author-insights-data.helpers.js` functions directly rather than going through the component factory) live inside the component now, not in a wrapped vanilla builder. With `buildManualCommentsSection` gone (the last of the three `build*Section` builders), `pr-author-insights.component.js` shrank dramatically: it's now just `renderAuthorInsights` orchestration (selector + header + three section-update calls + empty-state handling) - `prLinkHelpers`/`dataHelpers`/`draftHelpers` stay required constructor dependencies for contract stability but are no longer destructured/used internally, since their only consumers moved to the JSX components. Verified with a new `AuthorInsightsCommentsSection.test.jsx` (16 tests covering composer typing/validation/save-success/save-failure, edit/cancel/edit-save, loading/error states, and author-switch reset - closing the gap where this exact flow previously had zero jsdom coverage, only a Playwright e2e test), a rewritten `pr-author-insights.component.test.js` (removed the now-impossible "rebuild the manual comments section directly" test since that function no longer exists), full jest suite green (209 suites/1876 tests), lint clean, and a live Playwright run against the actual running app that added a real comment, confirmed "Saved." and the comment appearing in the list, then edited it via the real PUT round-trip and confirmed the updated text rendered - all with zero console errors. **Track C begins (2026-09-16): moving index.page.js's data-fetching/polling/state orchestration into React**, working toward eventually deleting index.page.js and react-mount-bridge.js entirely (see this doc's own "Performance Validation" note that index.page.js staying a classic script outside the React module graph was always provisional, pending this exact phase). A C0 inventory pass first classified all ~90 `window.X` bridges index.page.js exposes or consumes: most are either (a) pure formatting/data-shaping helpers with no DOM role (the large `Object.assign(window, {...})` block, `toCount`/`formatIsoDatetime`/etc. - eventually convertible to plain ES module imports, not a state-migration concern) or (b) "React calling back into vanilla to mutate one of several independent shared-state pockets" (`authorInsightsState`, `statsViewState`, filter-field state, selected-PR-numbers, dirty-field tracking, viewed-files state, review-conversations UI state - at least 7 separate pockets, each with its own read/write bridge functions, not one unified data blob). That finding reshaped the slice plan: there's no single "move the data into Context" slice: Track C is "migrate N independent state pockets/concerns one at a time," starting with the one that touches zero data-model concerns at all. **Slice C1 (polling interval ownership) is complete.** `<PrDataPolling />` (react-app.jsx, mounted as a headless React root with no visible UI - it renders `null`, so needs no DOM container) now owns the four auto-refresh `setInterval`s (data/scheduler/backfill/activity-render) and the visibility-pause/beforeunload-cleanup lifecycle that used to live in index.page.js's own `cleanupIntervals`/`restartIntervals` - ported 1:1, same cadences (`AUTO_DATA_POLL_MS`/`AUTO_BACKFILL_POLL_MS`, now exposed on `window` as the single source of truth for interval timing), same pause/resume-on-visibility and clear-on-beforeunload behavior. The four polled functions themselves (`pollForDataChanges`/`pollSchedulerStatus`/`pollBackfillStatus`/`renderRequestActivity`) are unchanged and still live in index.page.js, now exposed on `window` for `<PrDataPolling />` to call - only *what decides when they run* moved; their own fetch/parse/dedup logic is a later slice. The blast radius here was bigger than the code change itself: index.page.js's 8,451-line integration suite (`index.html.test.js`) had ~8 separate test blocks (38+ call sites) that mocked `global.setInterval` specifically to intercept the `pollForDataChanges`/`pollSchedulerStatus` registration calls index.page.js used to make directly - all rewritten to call `window.pollForDataChanges()`/`window.pollSchedulerStatus()` directly instead, now that nothing registers a real interval inside index.page.js itself to intercept. `index.page.memory-leak-prevention.test.js` (previously pure literal-source-text regex matching against index.page.js, e.g. `expect(sourceCode).toContain("let pollDataInterval = null;")`) was rewritten as `index.page.memory-leak-prevention.test.jsx` - real behavioral tests using jest fake timers against `<PrDataPolling />` (mount/unmount, hide/show, beforeunload), verifying actual interval start/stop instead of matching text patterns that no longer exist anywhere. Also added a new dedicated `PrDataPolling.test.jsx` (7 tests) covering per-interval cadence independently. Verified with full jest suite green (210 suites/1881 tests), lint clean (same 17 pre-existing index.page.js errors, none new), and a live Playwright check against the running app confirming the bridges exist, manual invocation triggers real network calls, and - the test that actually matters here - automatic polling fires on its own after waiting 32s in a real browser with zero manual triggering and zero console errors, proving `<PrDataPolling />` genuinely drives production polling now, not index.page.js. **C2 investigation (2026-09-16) found this slice needs its own split too.** `latestStoredPayload` isn't privately used by `renderPrData` - it's threaded via getter/setter closures into 9+ independent helper/orchestrator factories (exports, stats, author-insights, apply-filters-cache, single-PR-update, merged-request, data-tab orchestrator, react-callbacks), and there are three different write-then-render patterns in the code today: (1) the main `renderPrData` funnel, (2) mutate-then-`applyFiltersFromCache` (which eventually calls `renderPrData` anyway), and (3) mutate-then-directly-call `window.ReactMountBridge.update(...)`, completely bypassing the orchestrator pipeline - a deliberate perf optimization used by the checkbox/Ack/Apply-Label React callbacks and the label-dropdown refresh, so a same-PR-toggle doesn't re-run the full stats/filter-dropdown/export-catalog/author-insights pipeline for a change none of that depends on. Naively collapsing everything onto one Context-backed writer would silently turn those fast-path actions into full-pipeline re-renders - a real behavior/perf regression, not just a refactor. So C2 splits into C2a (unify the fast-path writer into one named function, still backed by the module-level variable - done) and C2b (swap the backing store to Context/hooks and delete the bridge - not started). **Slice C2a is complete.** The two previously-duplicated inline `if (window.ReactMountBridge?.isMounted?.()) { window.ReactMountBridge.update(...) }` call sites (inside `refreshAvailableRepoLabels` and the `updateReactTable` dependency passed into `react-callbacks.helpers.js`'s `createReactCallbackHelpers`) now both call one shared `pushPayloadToReactTable(payload, repo)` function - purely a consolidation, zero behavior change, so C2b only has to change what happens *inside* this one function later instead of hunting down scattered direct bridge calls across the file. Verified with the full jest suite green (210 suites/1881 tests, unchanged - this slice needed no new tests since it's a pure extract-function refactor with identical resulting calls), lint clean, and a live Playwright check against the running app: toggled a real "In Review" checkbox, confirmed the real `POST /view-prs/ack` fired with the correct `inReviewClear` body and the checkbox visually updated with zero console errors - the exact fast path `pushPayloadToReactTable` now owns. **Slice C2b is complete (2026-09-16): `react-mount-bridge.js` is deleted.** On reflection, deleting it didn't actually require the larger "move `latestStoredPayload` into Context" migration first - `react-mount-bridge.js` was just a thin wrapper around `window.mountReactPrTable`/`window.updateReactPrTable` (both still owned and exposed by react-app.jsx, unchanged) plus an `isMounted()` boolean and a console-logging layer. Its `mount`/`update`/`isMounted` logic is now inlined directly into index.page.js as three local functions (`mountReactTable`/`updateReactTable`/`isReactTableMounted`, plus a local `reactTableMounted` flag replacing the bridge module's own closure-held state) - same behavior, one less indirection layer, zero change to react-app.jsx or `PrTableApp`. Its `unmount()` was confirmed dead code (no production caller anywhere) and wasn't ported. `renderPrData`'s old `hasReactBridge` check (whether the bridge *script* had loaded) is gone too - meaningless now that the logic lives in the same file, so only `hasReactApp` (whether react-app.jsx's deferred module has loaded) still gates the mount-race branch. `PrTableApp`'s `useState`+prop-resync-`useEffect` (deliberately *not* touched this slice - see C2a's note above about the two-distinct-actions design) still exists and is still the correct pattern per React's own docs for "sync local state from an external prop while other local state must survive" - reframing it as something to "fix" was based on an incomplete read of C2's original text; it isn't broken and isn't part of what actually blocked deleting the bridge. The real test-blast-radius here was `index.html.test.js`'s `installReactTableMountBridge()` stub, which used to define its own complete `window.ReactMountBridge = {mount, update, isMounted}` (doing real RTL rendering) entirely independent of `window.mountReactPrTable`/`updateReactPrTable` - since index.page.js no longer reads `window.ReactMountBridge` at all, that stub was dead weight the moment this slice landed. Rewrote it to define `window.mountReactPrTable`/`window.updateReactPrTable` themselves (the actual functions index.page.js now calls directly), replicating react-app.jsx's real contract (mounting sets `window.updateReactPrTable` as a side effect) via the same real RTL rendering underneath - and rewrote the one test that simulated "mount genuinely fails" (previously `window.ReactMountBridge.mount = () => false`, which only worked because the old bridge's `mount()` returned `true` unconditionally unless an exception was thrown - a `false` return from the *inner* `mountReactPrTable` was never actually a failure path) to make `window.mountReactPrTable` throw instead, which is what `mountReactTable`'s try/catch genuinely guards against. `react-mount-bridge.test.js` (entirely dedicated to the now-deleted module) was deleted outright rather than rewritten. Verified with the full jest suite green (209 suites/1871 tests - down from 210/1881 only because the ~10 dead bridge-module tests are gone, no other count changes), lint clean (same 17 pre-existing errors), and a live Playwright check against the running app confirming `window.ReactMountBridge` is now `undefined`, `window.mountReactPrTable`/`updateReactPrTable` exist and the table renders, and a real checkbox toggle still fires the correct `POST /view-prs/ack` end-to-end with zero console errors. **Next up (not yet started, and now understood to be optional rather than required for the bridge-deletion goal): actually moving `latestStoredPayload`/`latestPrManifest`/dedup-fingerprint state into React state/Context** - worth reassessing later against Track C's remaining slices (C3/C4/C5) rather than assumed necessary, since the getter/setter-into-9-factories pattern is a working, legitimate DI shape today, not obviously broken. **Full commitment: slice C2c is complete (2026-09-16) - `latestStoredPayload`/`selectedRepo`/`visiblePrNumbers` now live in real React state.** `<PrDataProvider />` (`state/PrDataProvider.jsx`, `state/PrDataContext.jsx` for `createContext`/`usePrData()`) is mounted once inside `mountReactPrTable` (react-app.jsx), wrapping `PrTableApp`, which now reads `payload`/`selectedRepo`/`visiblePrNumbers` via `usePrData()` instead of receiving them as props - deleting its own `useState(initialPayload)` + prop-resync `useEffect` entirely. The Provider owns `window.updateReactPrTable` itself now (previously assigned in two places - see below); index.page.js's `mountReactTable`/`updateReactTable`/`isReactTableMounted` (Track C slice C2b) needed **zero changes** - they still call `window.mountReactPrTable`/`window.updateReactPrTable` with the exact same external signature, so this was a purely internal react-app.jsx/PrTableApp.jsx refactor, not a change to the vanilla-to-React contract. Designing this surfaced a real, currently-shipping bug: `PrTableApp` used to expose its **own** `window.updateReactPrTable` (a simplified single-argument `(newPayload) => setPayload(newPayload)`) via a `useEffect` that ran *after* `mountReactPrTable`'s own multi-argument version was assigned, silently clobbering it - confirmed live via `window.updateReactPrTable.toString()` showing the simplified version in production. This meant `selectedRepo`/`visiblePrNumbers` updates from index.page.js's poll loop and filter-apply pipeline were dropped after the initial mount; only `payload` ever actually updated post-mount. Only `react-app.test.jsx`'s isolated unit test (which mocks `PrTableApp` out entirely, so its competing effect never ran) exercised the "real" multi-argument merging logic - meaning that logic was unit-tested but never actually reachable end-to-end in the running app. Verified the fix live: applying a PR-number filter (Run & Filter tab) now actually changes the visible row count (74 → 4 rows for a single PR number, previously silently a no-op since `visiblePrNumbers` never reached `PrTableApp` after mount) - a real, user-visible bug fix, not just an internal cleanup. Also deleted a second piece of dead code found along the way: a `'pr-delta-update'` custom-event listener in `PrTableApp` that nothing in the codebase ever dispatched. `NotesSection.jsx`'s `onDataRefresh` (used after its own direct `POST /view-prs/notes` call, independent of index.page.js's vanilla notes-save handler) now wires to the Context's own `setPayload` action directly. Test blast radius: `PrTableApp.test.jsx` (37 render call sites, all constructed the same way) needed a `renderPrTableApp()` helper wrapping every render in `<PrDataProvider>`, and its two tests that used `rerender()` with a new `initialPayload` prop to simulate a post-mount update had to be rewritten to call `window.updateReactPrTable(...)` instead, since the Provider's `initialPayload` prop is (deliberately) only read once at mount now - there's no more "prop-driven re-render" pathway to simulate. `react-app.test.jsx` (which mocks `PrTableApp` out to test react-app.jsx's own mount/update logic in isolation) now mocks it to call the *real* `usePrData()` and capture that, so it still exercises the real Provider's merge semantics (including the `visiblePrNumbers` undefined-vs-null distinction) rather than asserting on props that no longer exist. `index.html.test.js`'s `installReactTableMountBridge()` stub now mounts the real `<PrDataProvider>` (imported from source) wrapping `PrTableApp`, matching react-app.jsx's real structure, instead of hand-rolling its own prop-merging logic. Verified with the full jest suite green (209 suites/1871 tests, no count change - this slice only touched existing tests' setup, not test count), lint clean (same 17 pre-existing errors), and a live Playwright check against the running app confirming `window.updateReactPrTable` is now genuinely the Provider's multi-argument version, a real checkbox toggle still round-trips correctly, and - the concrete proof this was a real fix, not just a refactor - applying a local PR-number filter now actually filters the visible table. **Slice C2d is complete (2026-09-16): all of react-app.jsx's independent React roots are unified into one shared tree.** Previously there were 14 separate `ReactDOM.createRoot()` calls (PrTableApp, FilterStateProvider's own anchor, one root *per* multi-select list, Review Stats controls, Review Stats content, Author Insights selector/created-PRs/header/notes/comments, Backfill badges, the applied-filter summary, and `<PrDataPolling />`) - now there is exactly one: a single `<AppRoot />` component (mounted via `mountAppRoot()` into one hidden-but-attached anchor, same attached-anchor reasoning `mountFilterStateProvider` already established for native event delegation) that renders every one of those pieces as a child, portaled into its existing DOM container via `createPortal` - the same technique `FilterStateProvider` already used for the Run & Filter fields, just extended to everything else. Every `window.updateX`/`window.renderX` bridge that used to belong to its own standalone `mount*` function is now a `useState` setter inside `AppRoot`, assigned via `useEffect` the same way - external behavior for every one of these bridges is unchanged (same function names, same call signatures, same `key`-remount tricks for the two that need it - multi-select lists and the Author Insights selector - same `flushSync` requirement for multi-select updates). `<PrDataProvider />` now wraps the *entire* tree (previously scoped just around `PrTableApp`) - this is what actually unlocks Context-based data sharing for a future slice (e.g. Review Stats/Author Insights reading PR payload directly instead of via a push bridge, the original Track C3 idea) - but this slice itself does not change *how* any non-PrTableApp consumer gets its data, only *where* it renders; every one of them still reads via the same `window.*` bridges as before. `PrTableApp` remains the one consumer that mounts lazily (index.page.js calls `window.mountReactPrTable(container, props)` only once PR data first loads, not at page-init time like everything else) - `AppRoot` handles this with a `prTable` state slice that starts `null` and only portals `PrTableApp` in once that bridge call has actually happened; the initial payload it carries is seeded through the *same* `window.updateReactPrTable` path every later update already uses (owned by the now-always-mounted `<PrDataProvider />`), rather than being a second, parallel way data enters the tree. Test blast radius was much smaller than the source change: since only `react-app.test.jsx` imports `react-app.jsx` directly (confirmed via grep - every other component test exercises its component in isolation with mocked `window.*` bridges, and `index.html.test.js` has its own fully independent stub), that was the only test file requiring changes - rewritten to call `window.mountReactPrTable`/`window.updateReactPrTable` (no longer a standalone exported function - it only exists once `<AppRoot />` mounts and its effect assigns it) instead of an imported function, wrapping the module's own top-level `mountAppRoot()` call in `React.act()` so the bridge assignments (which now happen inside `useEffect`, deferred past the synchronous `require()`) are ready before the first test runs. Verified with the full jest suite green (209 suites/1871 tests, no count change beyond the rewritten file's own 6 tests), lint clean (same 17 pre-existing `index.page.js` errors), and a thorough live Playwright pass against the running app - deliberately broader than any single-component check given this slice touched every mounted component's wiring at once: every tab (PR data, Review Stats, Author Insights, Run & Filter, Backfill) renders correctly with zero console/page errors; the PR-number filter round-trip still narrows the table (74→4 rows); a real checkbox toggle still fires the correct `POST /view-prs/ack`; switching authors in Author Insights still updates the created-PRs section; multi-select lists render real options; and automatic polling (`<PrDataPolling />`, Track C slice C1) still fires on its own after 32 real seconds with zero manual triggering, confirming the unification didn't disturb its behavior either. **Dead code cleanup (2026-09-16), found while scoping (and ultimately declining) the original C3 idea**: `review-stats-tab.orchestrator.js`/`author-insights-tab.orchestrator.js` (909 combined lines including their tests) are deleted. Investigating why Review Stats/Author Insights appeared to already refresh every render cycle (the finding that closed out C3, see above) traced their real data path to `pr-render-apply.helpers.js`'s `applyRenderResults`/`renderStatsViewIfVisible`/`renderAuthorInsightsIfVisible` - **not** these two orchestrator objects. Confirmed via grep that index.page.js only ever called `.initialize()` on either orchestrator (never `.render()`, `.activateTab()`, `.handleAuthorSelection()`, `.updateData()`, or `.cleanup()`), and `.initialize()` itself just flips a flag nothing outside those same never-called methods ever checks - so the entire construction, all of both files' dependency-injected state getters/setters, and both files' test suites were fully dead weight, confirmed by removing the two `require()`s, the two `createXxxTabOrchestrator({...})` instantiations, the two `.initialize()` call sites, and the two `<script>` tags in index.html, then running the full jest suite (207 suites/1835 tests, down from 209/1871 by exactly the two deleted test files' worth) and a live Playwright check confirming both tabs still render and refresh correctly (Review Stats' cards/charts, Author Insights' author-switching) with zero console/page errors - proving nothing outside these two files depended on them. Also worth noting for anyone picking Track C back up later: `getLatestRows`/`getLatestActorsMap` (the deleted `review-stats-tab.orchestrator.js`'s own state getters) read a `latestStoredPayload.rows` field that is never actually populated anywhere in index.page.js - always silently returning `[]` - so that specific dead code was doubly dead, not just unreachable.  **Further dead-code cleanup (2026-09-16), survey-driven**: in response to a full-repo survey of remaining not-yet-converted/never-removed vanilla code, the largest genuinely-dead surface in `index.page.js` was removed. Deleted 5 fully-dead top-level functions confirmed to have zero callers, zero test references, and no `__testables` export - `createInsightSection`, `createActivityEventsSection` (~185 lines), `createReviewThreadsSection` (~793 lines, the largest single removal), `createApprovalRiskSection`, and `createNotesSection` (~293 lines, the big multi-field notes composer) plus `TONE_OPTIONS`, all superseded long ago by their real JSX equivalents (`AuthorInsightsCommentsSection`, etc.) but never deleted. Removing them revealed two more lint-cascade rounds of now-newly-dead symbols (`TABLE_HEADERS`, `TABLE_COLUMN_CLASSES`, `createAuthorFieldIndicator`, and a chain of helper-factory exports that existed only to feed the deleted functions), fixed the same way this session has fixed every prior cascade: re-lint, follow the trail, repeat until zero unused-var errors remain. Nine cell-builder helper factories (`pr-labels-cell`, `pr-date-cell`, `pr-selection-cell`, `pr-status-cell`, `pr-table-cell-primitives`, `pr-approved-cell`, `pr-title-cell`, `pr-actions-cell`, `pr-row-toggle-controls`) plus two single-export helpers (`pr-activity-event-description`, `pr-lines-changed-insight`) had their sole exports fully dead too - each confirmed via grep (exactly one self-reference, matching the definition site) before deleting the `.helpers.js` file, its `.test.js`, and its `<script>` tag in `index.html` (11 files + 11 tests removed in total). Two other factories (`prActorIdentityRenderHelperFactory`, `prAuthorCellHelperFactory`, `prNotesHelperFactory`) had a mix of live and dead exports, so only their destructured bindings were trimmed, keeping the `require()`/factory call itself since at least one export from each remains in active use (`getEffectiveViewerLogin`/`createActorIdentityElement`, `collectPrAuthors`, `normalizeNotesListForUi` respectively). Also fixed a real pre-existing bug found along the way and unrelated to any of the above: a `Duplicate key 'buildRowActorsMap'` inside the big `Object.assign(window, {...})` bridge block - listed twice, the second (dead) occurrence removed. Verified with the full jest suite (196 suites/1779 tests, down from 207/1835 by exactly the 11 deleted test files), lint fully clean (**zero** errors for the first time this session - all 17 originally-pre-existing `index.page.js` errors, including the long-standing duplicate-key bug, are now resolved), and a live Playwright pass against the running app: zero console/page errors on load, the PR table still renders its full 74 rows, a row's "More insights" toggle still expands real content (exercising the JSX successors of the deleted vanilla builders), Review Statistics still renders its 4 stat cards, and Author Insights still renders its created-PRs section.  **Action Log tab converted to real JSX (2026-09-17), first slice of the second not-yet-converted-tabs survey category**: `#action-log-container` (the Management tab's content region) is now owned by `ActionLogSection.jsx`, replacing `pr-action-log.helpers.js`'s `renderActionLog`/`loadActionLog` (deleted, along with its two test files - `pr-action-log.helpers.test.js` and a fully-duplicate leftover, `pr-management-action-log.helpers.test.js`, discovered while updating this slice: it re-tested both `pr-management-tabs.helpers.js` and `pr-action-log.helpers.js` nearly verbatim to their real test files, contributing no unique coverage). The tab-switch chrome (`pr-management-tabs.helpers.js`, still vanilla - shared by `status`/`script`/`backfill`/`export`/`actor-name-cache` tabs too, out of scope for this slice) and the Refresh button (`index.page.js`) are unchanged and still call a `loadActionLog` reference; `index.page.js` now points it at `window.triggerActionLogLoad`, a bridge `ActionLogSection` registers on mount/unmount - the same "vanilla chrome still drives it, React owns the content" shape as every earlier container-split conversion this migration has done, just applied to a management tab instead of a data tab for the first time. Found and fixed a real bug while verifying live: `index.html`'s `#action-log-container` had a static `<p id="action-log-empty">No actions logged yet.</p>` placeholder as a child - since `createPortal` inserts its managed nodes without clearing pre-existing unmanaged DOM siblings, the real fetched table was rendering *alongside* that leftover placeholder instead of replacing it (confirmed via a live Playwright check showing both the placeholder text and a 100-row real table in the container at once). Fixed by removing the static placeholder from `index.html`, since `ActionLogSection` already renders the equivalent "No actions logged yet." message itself for the pre-fetch/empty state - worth remembering for the two remaining tabs in this category (Actor Names, Export), which may have the same static-placeholder-as-child shape. Verified with the full jest suite (195 suites/1769 tests, down from 196/1779 - two duplicate/superseded test files removed, one new `ActionLogSection.test.jsx` added with expanded coverage - loading state, success, empty, server error, thrown error, and bridge registration/cleanup - closing a gap the old suite never had: no prior test exercised the loading-state render), lint clean (`index.page.js`, zero errors), and a live Playwright pass confirming the tab click and Refresh button both still work end-to-end against the real backend (100 real log rows rendered, zero console/page errors). Two integration tests in `index.html.test.js` that asserted on the old vanilla-rendered DOM content ("loads and displays action-log entries...", "re-fetches action-log entries...") were replaced/removed per the same precedent Track B set: once the behavior is real React, its correctness coverage belongs in the component's own test file, not a jsdom harness that never mounts `react-app.jsx`; the surviving integration test now only checks that the tab-panel itself becomes visible on click, since that part is still genuinely vanilla. **Export tab converted to real JSX (2026-09-18), closing out the second not-yet-converted-tabs survey category (Action Log, Actor Names, Export).** `ExportTab.jsx` (mounted into `#export-container`, replacing the old `<h3>`/`<p>` sibling markup) takes full ownership - unlike Action Log's container-split, all of its buttons/field checkboxes/summary/preview text are React-owned now, matching Actor Names' precedent. Field selection is real React state (no more DOM-checkbox scraping/previous-selection reconciliation the vanilla version needed only because it rebuilt the list via `innerHTML` on every payload update); `getExportFieldCatalog`/`buildExportPayload`/`getVisiblePrNumbersFromSectionsHost` (pr-export.helpers.js) stay vanilla pure functions, now exposed on `window` for the component to call - the established "leaf reads window.* for data-shaping" pattern. Deleted the three helper files that only existed to wrap DOM reads/writes around this (`pr-export-actions.helpers.js`, `pr-export-preview-summary.helpers.js`, `pr-export-json-build.helpers.js`, ~230 combined lines) plus their index.page.js wiring/state (`exportFieldCatalog`/`pendingExportDataFieldSelections`/`pendingExportUserStateFieldSelections`/the override-restore branch) and the 7 button-wiring blocks; the component does its own `fetch`-based read/merge/write of `/view-prs/user-defaults` instead. A real race was caught before shipping: a user interacting (toggle/select-all/etc.) before the saved-selection fetch resolved could have that manual choice silently overwritten once it did - fixed by marking `initializedRef` on manual interaction too, not just once initialization completes. Verified with a new `ExportTab.test.jsx` (8 tests) and the usual `index.html.test.js` "tab panel becomes visible" replacement for the 5 now-invalid vanilla-DOM export tests; full jest suite green (except pre-existing, unrelated environment gaps - see below). **Activity tab's Request Activity and Auto Refresh Scheduler badge lists converted to real JSX (2026-09-18)**, a fully-vanilla tab this session's survey found had never been touched by any earlier phase. Reused `BackfillBadges.jsx` as-is for both (renamed in its own doc comment, not its export, to reflect it was always a generic `{text, className}[]` badge-list renderer with zero Backfill-specific logic) - mounted into their own existing `#scheduler-badges`/`#request-activity-badges` containers, same shape as Backfill's own badges. Extracted the pure badge-array construction out of `renderRequestActivity`/`renderSchedulerStatus` into a new `pr-activity-badges.helpers.js` (`getRequestActivityBadges`/`getSchedulerBadges`, DI'd with the existing `withElapsedSuffix`/`getRequestActivitySeverityClass`) - the same "extract pure view-model, let index.page.js just call the React bridge" shape `getBackfillStatusViewModel` already established, done here specifically so this logic keeps real unit coverage (`pr-activity-badges.helpers.test.js`, 9 tests) now that these two badge lists have no vanilla DOM left in the jsdom integration suite to assert against. Fixed the two now-invalid `index.html.test.js` assertions (scheduler-badges text/class content, request-activity "No request in progress" text) the same way every earlier badge/list conversion did. **Dead multi-select fallback cleanup (2026-09-18):** found, while surveying, that the Phase 6 "pr-filter-panel/pr-json-modal cleanup" slice's dead-fallback removal had missed 2 of index.page.js's own multi-select builders (`renderActorOptionsList` used for the attention-author-thread-resolution allow/deny lists, `renderChangeFilterActorList` for the change-filter ignore-comment/review-authors lists) - both still carried a `document.createElement`-based `if (!handled) {...}` fallback for `window.renderReactMultiSelectList`, dead for the exact reason already established (the bridge is unconditionally set once react-app.jsx loads, and all 4 of these list ids are already in its `MULTI_SELECT_LIST_ID_PREFIXES`). Deleted both fallback blocks and their now-unused `idPrefix` params. This broke 9 `index.html.test.js` tests - not because the bridge was actually unhandled in production, but because this jsdom suite's own `installReactFilterPanelMountBridges` stub had only ever mapped the 5 pr-filter-panel.helpers.js list ids, never these 4 (they'd been silently exercising the vanilla fallback in this suite the whole time, unnoticed because it happened to produce the same visible result). Fixed by extending the stub's own id map to match react-app.jsx's real 9-entry one, not by reverting the production cleanup. Verified: full jest suite green except the same pre-existing, unrelated environment gaps present before this session's changes (the `check-open-pr-updates.sh` shell-integration tests need a `jq` binary not installed in this sandbox; a handful of server-integration tests and `e2e/smoke.spec.js` are flaky/misconfigured under plain `jest` independent of anything touched here - confirmed by diffing the exact failing-suite set before and after each slice, unchanged throughout). Lint clean on every file touched. **Two more candidates surfaced by this session's `document.createElement` survey, not yet started:** `#apply-label-select` (Run & Filter tab's "Apply existing GitHub label" dropdown, ~30 lines in `populateApplyLabelSelect`) looks like a quick Phase-2-style single-select conversion - its value is already read via a direct DOM `.value` at click-time (not a bridge), so converting it to React-rendered `<option>`s needs no other call-site changes, matching Phase 2's already-established "a real DOM node's value is correct regardless of what renders it" precedent. `buildActivityTimelineSummary` (index.page.js, ~330 lines, returns a hand-built `<table>` DOM node consumed by a React insights row via `window.buildActivityTimelineSummary` and presumably a ref+`useEffect` DOM-append, the same wrapped-vanilla-builder shape Review Stats' chart visuals used before Track A converted them to real JSX) is a much larger, standalone slice on that same scale - deliberately not started without a fresh, dedicated scoping pass given its size. **`#apply-label-select` converted to real JSX (2026-09-18), closing out the smaller of the two candidates above.** `ApplyLabelSelect.jsx` (mounted into a new `#apply-label-select-root`, replacing the select's old static markup in index.html) is a small, deliberately uncontrolled component - no `value`/`onChange` - since `handleApplyLabelClick` already reads `select.value` directly at click-time, needing no bridge for the read side, and a native `<select>` re-rendered with a fresh `<option>` list keeps its current selection automatically if that option still exists (falling back to the first option otherwise), which is exactly the manual "restore previous value if still valid, else reset" logic `populateApplyLabelSelect` used to hand-roll. That function is now a one-line call to a new `window.updateReactApplyLabelOptions` bridge. No `key` remount needed (unlike the multi-select checkbox lists) since this is a single stateless select, not per-item checked state. Zero pre-existing test coverage referenced this control by id (confirmed via repo-wide grep), so nothing broke; added `ApplyLabelSelect.test.jsx` (3 tests) for real coverage. Verified: full jest suite green, same pre-existing unrelated failures as every other slice this session, unchanged in count/identity. **The `buildActivityTimelineSummary` candidate turned out to already be solved, not a conversion to do (2026-09-18).** Investigating it before starting found `components/insights/ActivityTimelineSummary.jsx` already exists as a complete, independent real-JSX reimplementation of the exact same logic (own 5-test suite, rendered by `PrInsightsRow.jsx`) - the vanilla `buildActivityTimelineSummary` had been fully dead code since that component was written (confirmed via repo-wide grep: zero runtime callers, only referenced by its own definition, its `window.buildActivityTimelineSummary` exposure, and one stale test comment claiming it was "kept for `window.viewPrsInternals`" - a hook that doesn't exist anywhere in the codebase). Deleted the ~330-line dead function plus its sole dependency, `renderTimelineItems` (and its now-fully-orphaned `pr-activity-timeline-render.helpers.js` + test + `<script>` tag), matching the exact precedent Phase 6's "further dead-code cleanup" pass already set for superseded vanilla builders. Fixed the one stale comment (`index.html.test.js`) and `ActivityTimelineSummary.jsx`'s own doc comment, both of which referenced the now-deleted function in present tense. Verified: full jest suite green, same pre-existing unrelated failures as every other slice this session, unchanged in count/identity. **With this, the `document.createElement` survey's two flagged candidates are both closed out - no further known conversion/cleanup work is outstanding.** **Independent verification sweep (2026-09-20) found 4 confirmed-dead vanilla DOM-building modules this survey missed** - requested as a "continue the React migration" follow-up, delegated to a research pass rather than trusting the "no further known work outstanding" claim above at face value. Confirmed via repo-wide grep (each cross-checked manually, not just taken on the subagent's word - one initial suggestion to delete `pr-actor-identity-render.helpers.js` outright was wrong and would have broken `getEffectiveViewerLogin`, a live export from the same factory used throughout `ActorIdentity.jsx`/`PrApprovedCell.jsx`/`pr-needs-attention.helpers.js`) that `pr-diff-render.helpers.js` (superseded by `PrJsonModal.jsx`'s own `buildDiffBlocks`, which reimplemented rather than reused/deleted it) and `pr-smart-group-manipulation.helpers.js` (a `cloneNode`-based row-cloning leftover from the pre-Phase-1 vanilla table) had zero production callers - both deleted outright, along with their tests and `index.html` script tags. `pr-author-cell.helpers.js`'s `createAuthorCell` (superseded by `PrAuthorCell.jsx`) and `pr-actor-identity-render.helpers.js`'s `createActorIdentityElement`/`createActorIdentityFragment`/`createActorListFragment`/`appendTimestampAndActor`/`getActorIdentityState`/`getPrAuthorLogin`/`appendInlineSegment` (superseded by `ActorIdentity.jsx`) were also dead, but each file had one live export (`collectPrAuthors`, `getEffectiveViewerLogin` respectively) still consumed via `window.*` bridges by real React components - trimmed to just those, rather than deleting the files, with their factory signatures and index.page.js call sites shrunk to match (dropping now-unused `createActorIdentityElement`/`getManualNotesSummary`/`documentRef`/`resolveActorDisplayName`/`buildActorIdentityClassName`/`buildActorIdentityTitle`/`formatIsoDatetime` pass-throughs). Rewrote both files' test suites to drop tests for the deleted functions while keeping/expanding coverage of the surviving ones. Two small, still-live vanilla UI-building helpers surfaced by the same sweep (`pr-merged-request-more.helpers.js`, `pr-auto-render-indicator-links.helpers.js`) were left alone - both are genuinely still called in production (confirmed via grep), just small enough that a React round-trip wasn't judged worth it; not a gap, a judgment call already implicitly made by earlier phases. Verified: full jest suite green (200 suites/1875 tests, down from 202/1893 by exactly the deleted/trimmed tests), lint clean, and a live Playwright run (all 30/30 `e2e/smoke.spec.js` tests passing, including the page-load/console-error guard and the insights-row-expand test that exercises `PrAuthorCell` rendering) confirming zero regressions. **Two remaining vanilla UI-building islands converted to real JSX (2026-09-20)**, closing out both exceptions the 2026-09-20 verification sweep above deliberately left alone as "small enough not to be worth it" - on reflection, worth doing since both had a real (if minor) architectural cost beyond just "still vanilla": `AutoRenderBlockedLinks.jsx` (mounted into the existing `#auto-render-blocked-pr-links` host) replaces `renderAutoRenderBlockedLinks`'s (helpers/pr-auto-render-indicator-links.helpers.js) `document.createElement` button-building - that function now only toggles the host's own `hidden`/`aria-label` attributes (which a React portal's children can't do to their own container) and calls a new `window.updateReactAutoRenderBlockedLinks` bridge, the same "vanilla chrome still drives it, React owns the content" split Action Log established. `MergedRequestMoreAction.jsx` (mounted into the existing `#merged-request-more-action` host, which - unlike the links case - it owns entirely, so `isVisible=false` renders nothing rather than toggling a `hidden` attribute) replaces `appendMergedRequestMoreAction` (helpers/pr-merged-request-more.helpers.js, deleted outright) - pending/status are real local `useState` now (confirmed via grep that nothing else in the app ever read the old module-level `isRequestMoreMergedPending`, so unlike some earlier conversions there was no cross-cutting "vanilla remains source of truth" constraint forcing a shared-state bridge instead). `handleRequestMoreMerged` (helpers/pr-merged-request-more-action.helpers.js) still owns the real async orchestration (POST /view-prs/merged/request-more, updating the shared PR payload, re-rendering the table) - it no longer reaches into `#merged-request-more-btn`/`-status` by id, taking `onPendingChange`/`onStatusChange` callbacks instead. Found and cleaned up one more fully-dead file surfaced while trimming these: `pr-dom-reset.helpers.js`'s `clearElementContents` had exactly one caller in the whole codebase, the exact call site this slice just removed - deleted outright (file + test + `index.html` script tag), the same class of gap the 2026-09-20 sweep above was about. Also fixed two now-stale doc comments (`ActorIdentity.jsx`, `PrJsonModal.jsx`) that referenced the sweep's already-deleted vanilla functions by name in present tense. Test blast radius: rewrote `pr-auto-render-indicator-links.helpers.test.js`/`pr-merged-request-more-action.helpers.test.js`/`pr-render-apply.helpers.test.js` for the new bridge-call/callback-based contracts, added `AutoRenderBlockedLinks.test.jsx`/`MergedRequestMoreAction.test.jsx` (11 combined tests), and replaced `index.html.test.js`'s one broken integration test (which used to click a vanilla-rendered button this jsdom-only suite can no longer render) with a version that still exercises the real `window.handleRequestMoreMerged` POST round-trip directly, deferring button-rendering/visibility coverage to the component's own test file - the same precedent Action Log's own conversion set. Verified: full jest suite green (200 suites/1886 tests), lint clean, full Playwright smoke suite green (30/30, including the pre-existing "Request more merged PRs button renders" test against the real converted component), plus two additional ad-hoc live-browser checks (written, run, and discarded rather than kept as permanent suite additions, per smoke.spec.js's own "keep this file small, don't re-test jsdom-covered interaction logic" doc comment): one confirming `AutoRenderBlockedLinks`'s bridge/portal wiring renders real, clickable chips that correctly invoke navigation (and incidentally re-confirmed the codebase-wide "leaf reads window.* fresh at render time, never reassigned after boot" convention is what makes that safe), and one confirming `MergedRequestMoreAction`'s click drives a real POST with correct payload and real pending-disable/status-text UI transitions end to end. **Fixed a real regression found via review immediately after (2026-09-20): stale pending/status state in `MergedRequestMoreAction.jsx` didn't reset when the target repo changed.** Since it mounts once with no `key` and pending/status are local `useState`, switching the repo filter after a request completed (or while one was in flight) left the *previous* repo's status message showing as if it described the new one - a real behavior change from the vanilla original, which rebuilt the button+status from scratch on every render (`applyRenderResults` runs on every ~15-30s poll cycle) and so incidentally self-cleared almost immediately regardless of the target repo; the React version's durable local state doesn't get that free reset. Fixed by mounting with `key={repo}` (react-app.jsx) - not `key` on `isVisible` too, since hiding and reshowing for the *same* repo while a request is in flight should show whatever it resolved to in the meantime, not reset. Added a regression test (`MergedRequestMoreAction.test.jsx`) that unmounts/remounts for a different repo (what a key change causes) and confirms status/pending reset to defaults - none of the original 6 tests (or the two live-browser checks) exercised a repo change, so this gap went undetected until a dedicated review pass. Verified: full jest suite green (200 suites/1887 tests), lint clean, and one more ad-hoc live-browser check (written, run, discarded) confirming the fix live - simulating a repo-filter change via the real `window.updateReactMergedRequestMoreAction` bridge and confirming the stale status text actually clears. **Another independent verification sweep (2026-09-20) found a deeper dead chain the previous sweeps missed: the entire `insightsViewState` capture/restore machinery was dead-in-practice, not just its restore half.** A dedicated Explore sweep first found and fixed three smaller confirmed-dead items - `restoreInsightsViewState`/`restoreExpandedInsightsState`/`restoreOpenInnerInsightSectionsState` (a 3-function restore chain across `pr-insights-view-state.helpers.js`/`pr-insights-state.helpers.js`, superseded once expand/collapse state became React-owned - nothing ever re-clicked a toggle button to restore prior DOM state anymore) and `setClassToken` (`pr-ui-render-utils.helpers.js`, a dead CSS-class-token toggler) - both independently re-verified via direct grep before touching anything, same discipline as every prior sweep this session. Investigating *why* `captureInsightsViewState` (the surviving capture half) was still being called turned up something bigger: tracing its return value (`insightsViewState`) through `pr-render-context.helpers.js` -> `pr-run-pr-data-context.helpers.js` (spread into `runContext`) -> `pr-data-tab.orchestrator.js` -> `pr-render-pipeline.helpers.js` -> `pr-render-finalize.helpers.js` found it silently dropped at the very last hop - `deriveRenderApplyInputs` (`pr-render-apply-inputs.helpers.js`) never destructures it, so the value it's threaded through 5 files to reach was never actually read by anything. That meant `captureExpandedInsightsState`/`captureOpenInnerInsightSectionsState` (`pr-insights-state.helpers.js`) and `captureInsightsViewState` (`pr-insights-view-state.helpers.js`) - which the first pass of this same sweep had assumed were still live, since they had a real caller - were themselves calling into real `querySelectorAll`-based DOM traversal over every `.row-insights-toggle`/`.row-insights-content`/`details` element under `#pr-sections` on *every single render* (every ~15-30s poll cycle, every filter apply) for a value nothing downstream ever reads - a genuine, non-trivial wasted-work cost, not just dead code volume. Cross-checked the sibling field `prSectionOpenState` (which travels through the same 5-file chain) to make sure it wasn't guilty by association - confirmed it's genuinely read by `PrTableApp.jsx:433`, unlike `insightsViewState`. Deleted both files entirely (`pr-insights-state.helpers.js`, `pr-insights-view-state.helpers.js`, plus their tests and `index.html` script tags) and trimmed the parameter threading out of all 5 downstream files plus `index.page.js`'s now-dead require/destructure/factory-call blocks. Verified: full jest suite green (198 suites/1875 tests, down from 200/1887 by exactly the 2 deleted test files plus the dead-code trims across the others), lint clean, and a full Playwright smoke run (30/30) including the "checkbox toggle and More insights expand work" test - the exact feature this machinery used to (no longer) restore - confirming expand/collapse still works correctly, driven entirely by React state as it should be. **A fully exhaustive dead-export sweep (2026-09-20) checked every one of the ~77 files under src/ui/helpers/*.js (the prior sweeps had only sampled ~25) and found 27 candidate dead exports across 12 files - but most turned out not worth touching.** Independently re-verified the sweep's 3 highest-risk claims before acting: the `pr-needs-attention.helpers.js` cascade (`shouldShowNeedsAttention` calling `shouldTreatChangedAsAttention`/`shouldTreatNoActivityAsAttention` as local closures, which themselves call `parseChangedReasonTokens`/`isMergeCommitHeadline` the same way - all 4 confirmed unreachable via their exported names, but still genuinely invoked internally), the `pr-status-display.helpers.js` `void statusIcon;` marker in index.page.js (confirmed - an explicit "destructured but deliberately unused" marker), and that `isChangedStatus` (a sibling in the same file) was correctly *not* flagged, since it's genuinely live (exposed on window, used in several places) - all three checked out. Attempting the mechanical "just remove the unused export line" fix on `pr-smart-groups.helpers.js` immediately surfaced why this sweep needed more judgment than the earlier ones: it broke 25 tests in `pr-smart-groups.helpers.test.js`, which turned out to have substantial, valuable *direct* unit coverage of `isFlagged`/`isInReview`/`needsAttention`/`hasInteraction` as standalone pure functions - production code only reaches them via closures inside `buildSmartGroupConfigs`'s predicates now, but the tests still called them directly. Unlike the `insightsViewState` chain (which had a real per-render DOM-traversal cost to eliminate), removing these exports has zero runtime benefit - the function bodies stay either way, it only affects whether they're part of the file's public contract - so for every remaining candidate, checked its own test file for direct references *before* editing the source this time, and weighed cost (test rewrite effort) against benefit (a handful of unused-export lines) per file rather than mechanically applying one fix everywhere. Result: reverted `pr-smart-groups.helpers.js` back to its original exports, and left `pr-status-display.helpers.js` (`statusIcon` - removing it would make its own "non-CHK group" branch permanently untestable, since production only ever calls it with "CHK"), `pr-needs-attention.helpers.js` (the cascade, plus 12+ direct test call sites), `pr-auto-render-navigation.helpers.js` (`getFirstDirtyAuthorInsightsElement` - its one test is the *only* coverage of the "dirty composer draft" navigation path; `navigateToAuthorInsights`'s own test never exercises `focusUnsaved: true`), `pr-data-polling.helpers.js`, `pr-review-stats-date-bucketing.helpers.js`, and `pr-author-insights-display.helpers.js` (`noteAuthorMatchesSelection` - a same-named but separate duplicate of a different live export elsewhere, with its own real 3-test `describe` block) all untouched. Actually removed only the 5 exports confirmed to have zero direct test references anywhere: `pr-export.helpers.js`'s `pickByPaths`/`collectLeafPaths` (rewrote its one `collectLeafPaths` test to exercise the live `getFieldCatalog` instead, preserving the same nested-object/array-leaf-path coverage without keeping dead public surface), `pr-actor-identity.helpers.js`'s `normalizeResolvedPersonName`, `pr-filter-panel.helpers.js`'s `getSelectedMultiSelectValues`/`collectSortedLabelOptions`, `pr-auto-render-unsaved.helpers.js`'s `getBlockingPrNumberForElement`, and `pr-author-insights-drafts.helpers.js`'s `createAuthorInsightsComposerDraft`/`createAuthorInsightsEditDraft`. The same exhaustive pass re-ran the `document.createElement`/`.innerHTML =`/`appendChild`/`insertAdjacentHTML` scan across all of `src/ui` (including the two new .jsx files from the previous entry) and found nothing new - confirming the AutoRenderBlockedLinks/MergedRequestMoreAction conversions really were the last vanilla-rendering islands. Verified: full jest suite green (198 suites/1875 tests, unchanged in count from before this pass since no test files were added or deleted, only one test's target function swapped), lint clean, and a full Playwright smoke run (30/30) covering the filter/export/author-identity code paths this touched. **Track C resumes (2026-09-21): the original C3 idea (Review Stats/Author Insights reading PR payload directly from Context instead of via push bridges), previously scoped and declined above for lack of a live bug, is picked back up as a deliberate architectural cleanup at the user's request - not a bug fix this time.** First slice: `AuthorCreatedPrsSection.jsx`/`AuthorInsightsNotesSection.jsx`/`AuthorInsightsCommentsSection.jsx` (the 3 of Author Insights' 5 bridge-fed components that already did their own pure-function filtering/sorting off `window.*`) now read `payload`/a new `selectedAuthorLogin` Context field via `usePrData()` directly, instead of being pushed `rows`/`selectedAuthor`/`actorsMap` via `window.updateAuthorInsightsCreatedPrs`/`Notes`/`Comments` (all three bridges, plus their `renderCreatedPrsSection`/`renderPrLinkedNotesSection`/`renderManualCommentsSection` callers in `pr-author-insights.component.js`, deleted). `AuthorInsightsSelector`/`AuthorInsightsHeader` deliberately stay on the old vanilla-bridge model for now (a smaller follow-up slice) - converting only some of the 5 while "which author is selected" would otherwise exist in two unsynced places is exactly the dual-source-of-truth risk this doc's own C2a-C2d slicing was designed to avoid; closed here by pushing every selection change into the new `window.updateReactSelectedAuthorLogin` Context bridge (owned by `PrDataProvider.jsx`, mirroring `updateReactPrTable`'s existing effect) from inside `renderAuthorInsights` itself - the single place that decides the selected author, covering both explicit picks (`window.selectAuthorInsightsAuthor`, which calls `renderAuthorInsights` right after) and its own auto-select-first-author fallback (which bypasses that handler entirely and would otherwise leave Context stale). Confirmed by reading the code (not assumed) that "rows" for Author Insights has always meant `Object.values(payload.byPrNumber || {})` - the same raw payload already in Context, deliberately unfiltered by repo or the Run & Filter tab's active filters (`pr-row-sources.helpers.js`'s own comment confirms this is intentional) - so no new data source was needed. `selectedAuthor` objects (`{login, name}`) are reconstructed in each leaf from `selectedAuthorLogin` + `payload.actorsMap` via the already-exposed `window.resolveActorDisplayName`, avoiding the need to expose `buildAuthorInsightsEntries` too. `AuthorInsightsCommentsSection` was found to already ignore the `rows`/`actorsMap` props its old bridge pushed - only `selectedAuthor` was ever used. Verified with the full jest suite green (198 suites/1876 tests - one net new test added, `pr-author-insights.component.test.js`'s new empty-state bridge-clear case, alongside its rewritten selection-bridge test), lint clean, `AuthorCreatedPrsSection.test.jsx`/`AuthorInsightsNotesSection.test.jsx`/`AuthorInsightsCommentsSection.test.jsx` all rewritten to render through a real `<PrDataProvider>` (matching `ExportTab.test.jsx`'s established pattern) and drive author-switching via `window.updateReactSelectedAuthorLogin` instead of prop rerenders. Remaining Author Insights follow-up (Selector/Header) and the separate, larger Review Stats slice (2 components needing `buildReviewerStats`/`applyStatsControls` exposed) are both still open. **Author Insights follow-up slice complete (2026-09-21): `AuthorInsightsSelector`/`AuthorInsightsHeader` also converted, closing out all 5 Author Insights components onto Context.** Both now read `payload`/`selectedAuthorLogin` via `usePrData()` instead of being pushed props via `window.updateAuthorInsightsSelector`/`updateAuthorInsightsHeader` (both bridges deleted, along with their `renderAuthorSelector`/`renderSelectedHeader` callers in `pr-author-insights.component.js`). The Selector needed one new exposure, `window.buildAuthorInsightsEntries` (the same pure function `renderAuthorInsights` already used internally for its own auto-select-first-author validation) - deliberately guarded so options are empty whenever there are no local rows at all (`rows.length ? buildAuthorInsightsEntries(rows, actorsMap) : []`), even though `buildAuthorInsightsEntries` on its own would still surface authors from `actorsMap` alone with zero rows - preserving `renderAuthorInsights`'s existing "No local rows"/"No authors found" empty-state behavior exactly, which this replaces. The Header derives its display name the same way the 3 previously-converted sections do (`resolveActorDisplayName(selectedAuthorLogin, actorsMap, selectedAuthorLogin)`) and needs no separate empty-state signal, since `renderAuthorInsights` already clears `selectedAuthorLogin` to `""` in both empty branches (from the prior slice). A real simplification fell out of this: since `selectedAuthorLogin` is now a genuinely reactive Context value kept in sync by `renderAuthorInsights` (covering both explicit picks and its own auto-select-first-author fallback), `AuthorInsightsSelector`'s `<select>` no longer needs the local `useState`+incrementing-`key`-remount hack it used before to catch externally-driven selection changes - it's now a plain Context-controlled input. With both gone, `renderAuthorInsights` itself shrank further: `updateReactAuthorInsightsSelectorSafe`/`updateReactAuthorInsightsHeaderSafe` and their factory dependencies are deleted, leaving `updateReactSelectedAuthorLogin` as the only React-migration hook this component still needs - `authorInsightsState.selectedAuthorLogin` remains as its own purely-internal bookkeeping for the auto-select-fallback validation, no longer read by anything outside this one function. Verified with the full jest suite green (198 suites/1876 tests, same counts as the prior slice since this one replaced roughly as many test cases as it removed), lint clean, and `AuthorInsightsSelector.test.jsx`/`AuthorInsightsHeader.test.jsx` rewritten to render through a real `<PrDataProvider>` (mocking `window.buildAuthorInsightsEntries`/`window.resolveActorDisplayName` the same way the 3 previously-converted sections' tests already did) and drive selection changes via `window.updateReactSelectedAuthorLogin` instead of prop rerenders/key changes. **Review Stats slice complete (2026-09-21), closing out Track C's original 5-component/2-component split entirely.** `ReviewStatsContent.jsx` now computes its own `stats` from PrDataContext's `payload` instead of being pushed a pre-built result via `window.updateReviewStatsContent` (deleted). Two new exposures, `window.buildReviewerStats`/`window.applyStatsControls` (the same functions `index.page.js` used internally) - `applyStatsControls` reads the live vanilla `statsViewState` object by closure (unchanged DI wiring), so it's always current regardless of when React calls it; a new Context field, `statsViewState` (a plain snapshot, pushed via a new `window.updateReactStatsViewState` bridge from `updateStatsViewStateAndRerender` on every settings commit), exists purely to trigger a re-render when settings change - its field values are never read directly, since `applyStatsControls` already sees the live object. `ReviewStatsControls` is unchanged (it was already the sole writer of `statsViewState`, seeded once from `window.getStatsViewState()` at mount - never part of the push-based problem this slice solves). The computation is `useMemo`-gated on `[payload, statsViewState]` specifically (not `[rows, actorsMap]`, which would be fresh references every render) so unrelated Context changes - e.g. switching the selected Author Insights author, which touches the same shared Context - don't trigger a wasted recompute. With nothing left needing a push, `renderStatsView`/`renderStatsViewIfVisible` (index.page.js/`pr-render-apply.helpers.js`) are deleted outright, unlike Author Insights' `renderAuthorInsights` (kept for its auto-select-fallback validation) - Review Stats had no equivalent vanilla-side responsibility once the push was gone. **This is a deliberate, disclosed trade-off, not an oversight:** unlike the visibility-gated `renderStatsViewIfVisible` it replaces, `ReviewStatsContent` now recomputes whenever `payload`/`statsViewState` change even while its own tab is hidden (a real but bounded cost - the same class of "computes regardless of visibility" trade-off already accepted for Author Insights' 5 components in the prior two slices, just on a heavier computation) - reintroducing a push-based "recompute trigger" purely to preserve that gate was judged not worth the added indirection this whole effort exists to remove. Verified with the full jest suite green (198 suites/1876 tests, unchanged - `ReviewStatsContent.test.jsx`/`pr-render-apply.helpers.test.js` rewritten in place, no files added/removed), lint clean, `ReviewStatsContent.test.jsx` rewritten to render through a real `<PrDataProvider>` with `window.buildReviewerStats`/`window.applyStatsControls` mocked (matching the established pattern), and `pr-render-apply.helpers.test.js`'s Review-Stats-specific assertions/tests removed while its Author-Insights-specific ones (which still exercise the real, unchanged `renderAuthorInsightsIfVisible` visibility gate) were kept intact. All 7 originally-identified Track C components (5 Author Insights + `ReviewStatsContent`/`ReviewStatsControls`, with Controls needing no changes) now read PR data from Context rather than via push bridges. |
| **Phase 4** | Testing & Cleanup | 20-30h | 🟢 **Complete (2026-09-15) - large-scale stability run closed the last gap, see below.** jsdom suite at 1722/1722 (201 suites, as of the Phase 5 delta-aware-pipeline slice - suite/test counts shift slightly each slice as fallback-only tests get deleted/rewritten or new ones added); Playwright e2e smoke suite at 30/30, stable across repeated full runs; bundle size, initial load, and memory usage all measured this pass (see "Performance Targets" below - all well under target). Long-session stability testing (leaving the app open across many data refreshes/interactions to check for leaks/drift) not attempted - no existing harness for it and the measured ~11MB heap after a fresh load gives no signal either way about growth over time; would need a dedicated long-running script, not a quick addition. **Long-session stability harness added (2026-09-15):** `e2e/stability/long-session-heap-check.js` (run via `npm run stability:heap-check`, params `--cycles`/`--sample-every`/`--dataset-size`) - a standalone script (not picked up by `npx playwright test`'s globbing, confirmed via `--list`), reusing playwright.config.js's exact env-var isolation pattern against a synthetic dataset, never touching real `data/*.json`. Drives real interactions per cycle (in-review checkbox toggle every cycle - the main lever, since it exercises a real render/update without waiting on `AUTO_DATA_POLL_MS`'s real interval; data-tab switching every 5 cycles; a Run & Filter field edit plus a label multi-select toggle every 10 cycles), forces a GC pass via CDP `HeapProfiler.collectGarbage` before each `Performance.getMetrics` sample so a reading reflects retained memory rather than not-yet-collected garbage, and reports a first-quartile-vs-last-quartile heap comparison. **Two validation runs completed this session:** 20 cycles/15 PRs (11.67MB -> 13.11MB, 1.12x) and 80 cycles/40 PRs (12.64MB -> 15.86MB, 1.22x, see the full per-sample curve: 12.64/13.36/14.77/15.40/15.57/15.70/15.75/15.81/15.86MB) - both show heap growing early then clearly plateauing (an asymptotic curve, not sustained linear growth), the expected healthy pattern for one-time allocations/code-cache warmup, not a leak signature.
