## 🎯 **Future Phases (Brief Overview)**

### **Phase 2: Filters & Controls** (20-30 hours) — 🟡 Started

**Goal:** Migrate filter controls to React

**Approach (per the first slice below):** incrementally, one control at a
time, the same way Phase 1 itself worked — not a Context-based rewrite of
the whole form up front. Each converted field keeps its original `id` so
every existing vanilla `getElementById`/`addEventListener` call keeps
working unmodified; only who *renders* the DOM node changes. This worked
cleanly for one text input; whether it still holds up for stateful
controls like the multi-select dropdowns (which vanilla currently builds
and repopulates from payload data — see `pr-filter-panel.component.js`) is
untested and should be re-evaluated before assuming the same pattern
applies as-is.

**✅ Done:**
- `<PrNumberFilterInput />` ("Filter by PR number(s)") — `src/ui/components/PrNumberFilterInput.jsx`,
  mounted by `mountPrNumberFilterInput()` in `react-app.jsx` into a
  `#filter-pr-numbers-root` container (`index.html`) that falls back to a
  plain `<input>` if React never mounts.
- `<ScopeFilterSelect />` ("View scope") — `src/ui/components/ScopeFilterSelect.jsx`,
  mounted the same way into `#scope-mode-root`. Confirmed the pattern
  generalizes from `<input>` to `<select>` (with one addition — see gotcha
  #1 below).
- `<AlwaysShowInReviewCheckbox />` ("Always show PRs In Review") —
  `src/ui/components/AlwaysShowInReviewCheckbox.jsx`, mounted into
  `#always-show-in-review-root`, nested inside vanilla's own
  `<label class="checkbox-row">` (unconverted) so label-click-toggles-
  checkbox and layout are unaffected. Confirmed the pattern generalizes to
  checkboxes: `setCheckbox()` in `index.page.js` now calls `element.click()`
  when restoring a persisted value that differs from the current
  `checked` state, instead of assigning `.checked` directly — the
  checkbox equivalent of `setText()`'s native-setter-plus-event trick.
- `<AttentionNoActivityModeSelect />` ("NO_ACTIVITY handling") —
  `src/ui/components/AttentionNoActivityModeSelect.jsx`, mounted into
  `#attention-no-activity-mode-root`.
- `<FilterCheckbox />` (originally named `AttentionRuleCheckbox`, renamed
  once it outgrew that scope — see below) — a single **generic,
  parameterized** component (`id`/`name`/`initialChecked` props), not one
  near-duplicate file per field, mounted via the generic
  `mountFilterCheckboxes(fields)` in `react-app.jsx`. Used for two
  unrelated batches of plain checkboxes: the five "Needs Attention rules"
  checkboxes (`attention-include-pending-comments`,
  `attention-ignore-merge-only-commits`, `attention-include-closed-merged`,
  `attention-include-draft-changed`, `attention-include-draft-no-activity`)
  and the three "Run Script options" checkboxes (`ack-changed`,
  `show-reason`, `quiet`). Use this as the template for any other batch of
  near-identical checkboxes rather than one-off components.
- `<AuthorThreadResolutionModeSelect />` ("PR author thread resolution
  policy") — `src/ui/components/AuthorThreadResolutionModeSelect.jsx`,
  mounted into `#attention-author-thread-resolution-mode-root`. Has
  dependent UI (two sibling `<details>` elements that show/hide based on
  the selected mode); needed no extra handling beyond gotcha #3's fix
  below, since the vanilla code driving that already read `.value` fresh
  each time rather than caching it.
- `<RunScriptTextInput />` — a single **generic, parameterized** component
  (`id`/`name`/`type`/`placeholder`/`initialValue` props) for the four
  plain "Run Script options" text/number fields: `repo`, `limit`,
  `merged-limit`, `jobs`. Mounted by `mountRunScriptTextInputs()` into
  their respective `#<id>-root` containers. Unlike every field above, none
  of these ever had a vanilla `"change"` listener attached in the first
  place — they're only read via `.value` when the "Run script" button is
  clicked (`persistRunScriptOptionOverrides` / `handleRunScript` in
  `index.page.js`) — so gotcha #3 below doesn't apply to this batch; only
  the restore-race handling (gotcha #1) was needed.
- `<OpenModeSelect />` ("Open mode") — `src/ui/components/OpenModeSelect.jsx`,
  mounted into `#open-mode-root`. Same reasoning as `RunScriptTextInput`
  above: no pre-existing `"change"` listener, only read on "Run script"
  click, so only the restore-race handling applied.
- `<MultiSelectCheckboxList />` ("Filter by label name(s)", `#label-list`)
  — the first multi-select dropdown converted, and structurally different
  from every field above: its *options* are rebuilt from the PR payload on
  every data (re)load (`populateIncludeLabelOptions` in
  `pr-filter-panel.component.js`), not seeded once at mount. React mounts
  directly into the existing `<div id="label-list">` container (like Phase
  1's `#pr-sections`), not a wrapper span, so the pre-existing "change"
  listener delegated on that stable container (added long before Phase 2
  existed) and the `getSelectedMultiSelectValues`/`updateMultiSelectSummary`
  DOM-querying helpers all kept working completely unmodified. Wired via a
  new optional `renderMultiSelectList(listId, items)` DI hook on
  `createPrFilterPanelComponent` — returns `false` (triggering the
  untouched vanilla DOM-building fallback) for any list id not yet
  converted or if React hasn't mounted yet, so every other multi-select and
  every existing unit test for this module keeps working unchanged. See
  gotcha #4 below for the one real bug this surfaced.
- Four more `<MultiSelectCheckboxList />` mounts, converting every
  remaining multi-select in `pr-filter-panel.component.js` the same way:
  **"Exclude by label name(s)"** (`#exclude-label-list`,
  `populateExcludeLabelOptions` — structurally identical to label-list),
  and **"Filter by PR author(s)" / "Assigned user(s)" / "PR approver(s)"**
  (`#author-list` / `#assigned-list` / `#approver-list`,
  `populateAuthorOptions` / `populateAssignedOptions` /
  `populateApproverOptions`) — these three differ from label/exclude-label
  in deriving both the checkbox `value` (a login) and its display `label`
  from `actorsMap` (`resolveActorDisplayName`) rather than using the same
  string for both, and originally used plain `${prefix}-${login}` checkbox
  ids instead of the indexed/slugified `getMultiSelectCheckboxId` scheme —
  harmless to change since nothing reads these ids directly (only
  `getSelectedMultiSelectValues`' DOM query on `:checked`, unaffected by
  id format). Same `renderMultiSelectList` DI hook, same fallback
  behavior, same `flushSync`-wrapped bridge in `react-app.jsx` (just an
  additional `MULTI_SELECT_LIST_ID_PREFIXES` entry per list — no new
  bridge code needed). E2e coverage combines all four into one batch test
  (`smoke.spec.js`) as recommended below, rather than duplicating the
  label-list test four times.
- The final four `<MultiSelectCheckboxList />` mounts — **"Allow/Deny PR
  authors to resolve threads started by"** (`#attention-author-thread-
  resolution-allow-list` / `-deny-list`, `renderActorOptionsList`) and
  **"Ignore comments/reviews from these authors"**
  (`#change-filter-ignore-comment-authors-list` /
  `-review-authors-list`, `renderChangeFilterActorList`) — complete every
  multi-select dropdown in the app. These four are the one structurally
  different case in this batch: built directly as local closures inside
  `index.page.js` itself (not `pr-filter-panel.component.js`), so the
  `window.renderReactMultiSelectList` bridge call (with the same
  handled/fallback branching as the DI hook everywhere else) is inlined
  directly into `renderActorOptionsList`/`renderChangeFilterActorList`
  rather than threaded through as an injected parameter — same bridge,
  same `flushSync` fix, different call site. Restoring these four uses two
  different override shapes worth knowing about for e2e/manual testing:
  the thread-resolution allow/deny lists are top-level array keys
  (`overrides["attention-author-thread-resolution-allow"]`), while the
  change-filter ignore-author lists nest under `overrides.changeFilters`
  (`ignoreCommentsFromAuthors`/`ignoreReviewsFromAuthors`) alongside the
  `useBuiltinMergePattern` checkbox and `ignoreCommitPatterns` textarea
  (both converted below) — see `restoreUiOptionOverrides` in
  `index.page.js`.
- `<FilterOptionSelect />` — a single **generic, parameterized** component
  (`id`/`name`/`options`/`initialValue` props, `options` an
  `{value, label}` array) for the six near-identical "Any (with/without)"
  selects: Custom comments, Other notes, PR difficulty, Rally stories,
  Rally links, Analysis of PR. Mounted by `mountFilterOptionSelects()`
  into their respective `#<id>-root` containers, seeded from
  `FILTER_OPTION_SELECT_FIELDS` in `react-app.jsx`. Same category as
  `RunScriptTextInput`/`OpenModeSelect`: none of these six ever had a
  vanilla `"change"` listener or a persisted override (only read via
  `.value` when "Apply filters (local)" is clicked), so only the standard
  restore-race handling applied - no event delegation, no `flushSync`.
  **Discovered - and then fixed - while testing this batch**: none of
  these six fields had ever actually filtered the table, a pre-existing
  bug unrelated to this conversion (`git log` showed "prDifficulty" had
  never appeared in `pr-filter-pipeline.helpers.js`'s history) -
  `deriveFilterPipelineState` read their values into
  `filterSelectionInputs` but never forwarded them into
  `buildRowFilterCriteria`, so `rowMatchesUiFilters` always saw
  empty-string criteria for all six regardless of what was selected.
  Fixed by adding `customComments`/`otherNotes`/`prDifficulty`/
  `rallyStories`/`rallyLinks`/`analysisOfPr` to
  `deriveFilterPipelineState`'s destructured params and threading them
  into its `buildRowFilterCriteriaSafe(...)` call
  (`pr-filter-pipeline.helpers.js`) - the one real call site
  (`pr-render-filter-summary.helpers.js`) already spread
  `...filterSelectionInputs` into that call, so no caller needed
  updating, just the function that was silently dropping them. Verified
  by temporarily reverting the fix and confirming both the new unit test
  (`pr-filter-pipeline.helpers.test.js`) and the updated e2e test failed
  as expected. The e2e fixture (`e2e/fixtures/user-state.json`) now gives
  PR #1 a `prDifficulty: "3"` note, the one differentiator among the three
  fixture PRs, so `"...actually filter the table"` can assert real
  filtering instead of just interaction/persistence round-tripping.
- `<FilterCheckbox />` mounted a tenth time, for **"Use built-in merge
  commit filter"** (`#change-filter-use-builtin-merge-pattern`) - the last
  checkbox in the tab. Unlike the run-script/attention-rule checkboxes,
  this one has its own special-case auto-persist-on-change behavior (a
  real PUT fires immediately on toggle, not just on "Apply filters
  (local)"), previously wired via a direct, un-delegated
  `addEventListener` - exactly the gotcha #3 shape. Fixed the same way as
  `attention-author-thread-resolution-mode`'s special case: added a
  dedicated branch to the delegated `#run-script-form` listener (checked
  *before* the generic `debouncedApplyOnChangeIds` set) instead of folding
  it into that set, since it needs to persist immediately, not just
  re-filter. Verified by temporarily removing that branch and confirming
  the new e2e test failed as expected.
- `<IgnoreCommitPatternsTextarea />` — **the last field in the Run &
  Filter tab**, "Ignore commits matching patterns (regex, one per line)"
  (`#change-filter-ignore-commit-patterns`), and the only `<textarea>`
  converted so far. Same gotcha #3 shape as the merge-pattern checkbox
  above (special-case auto-persist-on-change, added to the same delegated
  branch rather than `debouncedApplyOnChangeIds`) - verified by temporarily
  removing it and confirming the new e2e test failed. Also required
  fixing `restoreUiOptionOverrides`'s restore path for this field, which
  used a plain `textarea.value = ...` assignment instead of `setText`
  (native setter + dispatched events) - the same class of bug gotcha #1
  describes for `<input>`/`<select>`, just never hit before because this
  was the first `<textarea>` converted. `HTMLTextAreaElement` has its own
  "value" accessor property (distinct from `HTMLInputElement`'s), so
  `setNativeValueAndDispatch`'s prototype-descriptor lookup needed no
  changes to work for it. This specific ordering (restore arriving after
  React has already mounted) proved hard to force deterministically
  through the e2e suite's real network/timing - see
  `IgnoreCommitPatternsTextarea.test.jsx`'s "external native value change"
  test for the reliable, deterministic proof this technique is needed.
  **With this field converted, the entire Run & Filter tab is now
  React-owned.**

**⚠️ Real gotchas this surfaced — read before converting the next field:**
1. `index.page.js`'s `restoreUiOptionOverrides()` fetches a persisted
   filter value on page load and can resolve *before* `react-app.jsx` (a
   Vite-bundled ES module — slower to load than a small same-origin JSON
   fetch) finishes mounting. Two fixes were both needed, not just one:
   - `restoreUiOptionOverrides`'s `setText()` helper now writes through
     the native value setter and dispatches **both** a real `input` *and*
     `change` event (see `setNativeValueAndDispatch` in `index.page.js`),
     instead of a plain `element.value = ...` assignment — needed for
     when React mounts *first* and the restore arrives after, since a
     controlled React field won't otherwise notice an externally-assigned
     `.value`. `<input>` only strictly needed `input`; `<select>` needed
     `change` too, so both fire unconditionally now.
   - Each `mount*()` function reads the fallback element's *current*
     value as `initialValue` before mounting — needed for the reverse
     ordering, where restore wins the race and sets the fallback element
     before React replaces it (mounting with a hardcoded default
     `initialValue` would otherwise silently wipe out the just-restored
     value).
2. **This suite's tests are not independent** — every test shares one
   long-lived webServer and one `user-defaults.json` (see
   `playwright.config.js`'s isolation comment). Once gotcha #1 was fixed,
   restoring a persisted filter started *actually re-applying it* on page
   load (previously the restored value only looked right in the form; the
   table itself silently stayed unfiltered, since nothing had ever told
   it to re-run). That's a real correctness fix, but it means any test
   that persists a filter via "Apply filters (local)" and doesn't clean up
   afterward now silently filters the *next* test's fresh page load too.
   Every such test must reset the field back to its default and wait for
   the real persistence PUT to land (`clickApplyFiltersAndWaitForPersist`
   in `smoke.spec.js`) before finishing — a fixed `waitForTimeout` isn't
   reliable, since the persist call is fire-and-forget
   (`void persistViewFilterOptionOverrides()`). Even with cleanup, running
   these tests **in parallel** is still a lost-update race (concurrent
   GET-merge-PUT against the same file) — `playwright.config.js` sets
   `fullyParallel: false` / `workers: 1` for exactly this reason. Don't
   re-enable parallelism without addressing this.
3. **The big one — this silently broke "auto-apply on change" for every
   field converted so far, all at once, not just one.** `index.page.js`
   attaches its `"change"` → `debouncedApplyFilters` (and, for a few
   fields, persist) listeners directly to each field via
   `element.addEventListener(...)`, early during page load (a classic
   script, run *before* `react-app.jsx`'s deferred module graph finishes
   loading and mounts). `ReactDOM.createRoot(container).render(...)` then
   creates a **fresh DOM node** for that field — it does not hydrate or
   reuse the static/fallback node — silently orphaning any listener
   already attached to the old node. The field kept working when changed
   *and then* "Apply filters (local)" was clicked (that button has its
   own separate handler, unaffected), which is why every earlier test in
   this file didn't catch it - they all click that button. Changing a
   field alone, the way a real user mostly does, silently did nothing:
   no debounced re-filter, no dependent-UI update (the thread-resolution
   select's show/hide), no auto-persist. **Fixed by delegating these
   listeners on `<form id="run-script-form">`** (a stable ancestor never
   replaced by React, since React only mounts into the small per-field
   `<span id="...-root">` containers nested inside it) instead of on each
   field directly — the native `"change"` event still bubbles up to the
   form regardless of which side rendered the field that changed. See the
   delegated listener in `index.page.js` (search for
   `debouncedApplyOnChangeIds`) and in
   `pr-ui-option-scroll.helpers.js`'s `registerUiOptionPersistenceHandlers`.
   **Any future field conversion must land inside this same delegated
   listener's field-id set (or the equivalent) — never re-add a direct
   `addEventListener` on a field that Phase 2 has converted or will
   convert.**

4. **Multi-select-specific — React's non-synchronous commit clobbered a
   restored selection, and only a real-browser reload could catch it.**
   `renderPrData`'s React path (`index.page.js`) calls the vanilla
   filter-population pipeline *twice* per data load:
   `prDataTabOrchestrator.renderPrData(..., skipTableRender: true)` runs it
   once as a side effect, then `populateFilterDropdownsForCurrentPayload`
   (added earlier, for the React path's own dropdown-population needs)
   runs it again right after. Both calls read "currently checked"
   checkboxes back out of the DOM (`getSelectedMultiSelectValues`) to seed
   the *next* populate call's selections — harmless when both calls
   mutate the DOM directly and synchronously (true for every multi-select
   still on the vanilla path), but `ReactDOM.createRoot(container).render()`
   does **not** commit synchronously (React 18 batches it). Without
   forcing a synchronous commit, the second call's DOM read saw the first
   call's pre-commit (stale/unchecked) state and cleared a selection that
   had just been correctly restored, before the very first paint after a
   page reload. Fixed by wrapping the render call in `flushSync` (from
   `react-dom`, see `renderReactMultiSelectList` in `react-app.jsx`) so the
   DOM synchronously reflects each render before the function returns,
   matching the read-after-write contract every non-React caller already
   assumes. This is the first gotcha in this list the jsdom suite plus a
   single-load browser check could **not** have caught — it only manifests
   on a *second* consecutive populate call within the same page load, which
   requires either a real reload or (as used to confirm the fix) two
   Playwright script runs comparing behavior with/without `flushSync`. See
   the e2e test `"React-owned label multi-select renders options from
   payload data..."` in `smoke.spec.js`, which reloads the page and asserts
   the checkbox is still checked — confirmed to fail without `flushSync`.
   **Any future multi-select conversion must render through this same
   `flushSync`-wrapped bridge function, not a bare `root.render()`.**

Confirm all four still hold (or find the equivalent) for every future
field conversion — this is exactly the kind of vanilla/React desync bug
the "React Rendering Parity Fixes" section above catalogs, self-inflicted
this time by Phase 2 work rather than found in Phase 1's leftovers. See
the e2e tests `"React-owned PR-number filter input survives a
persisted-value restore on page load"`, `"React-owned scope <select>
survives a persisted-value restore..."`, `"changing a React-owned filter
auto-applies without clicking..."`, `"PR author thread resolution
policy select shows/hides its dependent allow/deny lists live..."`, and
`"React-owned label multi-select renders options from payload data..."`,
`"React-owned exclude-label/author/assigned/approver multi-selects
render from payload data..."`, `"React-owned thread-resolution
allow/deny and change-filter ignore-author multi-selects render and
survive a persisted restore..."`, and `"React-owned change-filter \"use
built-in merge pattern\" checkbox auto-persists on change..."` in
`e2e/smoke.spec.js`, and the corresponding `*.test.jsx` files for the
unit-level version of gotcha #1 (both directions of that race).

**Original plan (component breakdown) — superseded, kept for history:**
every field this originally sketched (and every other field in the tab
besides) is now converted; see the field-by-field "✅ Done" list above for
the actual component names and file locations rather than this stale
aspirational list.

**State management — decided (2026-09-11), not deferred-and-forgotten:**
all three original items here (Context for global state, removing vanilla
JS filter variables, migrating debounce logic to hooks) are explicitly
**not** being pursued as part of Phase 2. The incremental,
`id`-preserving approach needed no Context for any of the 35 fields
converted, precisely because vanilla stays the source of truth for
filter-application logic (`applyFiltersFromCache`/`debouncedApplyFilters`)
and every React field just mirrors it via the restore-race/event-
delegation/`flushSync` bridges documented above. "Removing the vanilla
variables" and "migrating debounce to hooks" would mean tearing out that
shared source of truth and rebuilding it as React state (almost certainly
requiring Context, since filter state is read from many unrelated
components) - a large, high-risk rewrite touching every converted field's
bridge at once, explicitly out of scope for Phase 2 and deferred to
**Phase 6 (Remove Vanilla JS)**, where it belongs alongside removing the
vanilla fallback markup itself. Revisit only if Phase 6 is actually
started, not before.

---

### **Phase 3: Other Tabs** (20-30 hours) — 🟡 Started

**Tabs to migrate, roughly in order of complexity (chosen 2026-09-11 after
looking at each):**
1. **Review Stats Tab** (`#pr-stats`) — moderate complexity: its own
   orchestrator (`review-stats-tab.orchestrator.js`, though only its
   `initialize()` is actually wired up from `index.page.js` — the rest
   of its API, `render`/`handleDateRangeChange`/`handleFilterChange`/
   `updateData`/`activateTab`/`cleanup`, is currently dead code, never
   called from anywhere), a controls component (converted, see below), a
   summary/table component, and chart visuals
   (`pr-review-stats-chart/visuals.component.js`) plus 3 aggregation/
   timeline/date-bucketing helper files. Renders a dynamic table from PR
   data - the same shape Phases 1-2 already proved out.
2. **Author Insights Tab** (`#author-insights`) — most complex: a
   dedicated component + orchestrator + 5 helper files
   (`pr-author-insights-identity/drafts/pr-link/display/data.helpers.js`).
3. **Backfill Tab** (`#backfill-card`) — simplest (mostly static markup
   with live status/log text updates via polling, no real list-rendering
   to speak of), but also the worst natural fit for React and lowest
   value - do last, if at all.

**✅ Done (Review Stats tab, slice 1 of 2 so far):**
- `<ReviewStatsControls />` — the sort/filter/minComments/topN/start-date/
  end-date controls, replacing
  `pr-review-stats-controls.component.js`'s `createStatsControls()`.
  `statsViewState` (a plain module-level object in `index.page.js`) has no
  persisted/restored override and is only ever written by these controls
  themselves - confirmed by grepping every `statsViewState.` write site
  before converting - so this mounts once with the current
  `statsViewState` as `initialState` and owns it from then on; no
  restore-race handling needed (unlike almost every Phase 2 field).
  - **The one real gotcha, and it's Phase 1's `#pr-sections` gotcha all
    over again, just in a new tab:** `renderStatsView` (index.page.js)
    used to rebuild its *entire* content — controls included — via a
    single `host.innerHTML = ""` on every stats render (the exact
    discard-and-rebuild shape the old vanilla multi-select lists had
    before Phase 2 converted those). Once React mounts into a container
    inside that subtree, an unrelated stats re-render (e.g. switching
    tabs and applying an unrelated PR filter, which cascades into
    `renderStatsView` regardless of which tab is visible) would silently
    tear the React root's DOM out from under it via that same
    `innerHTML = ""`, without ever calling `root.unmount()` - React's own
    bookkeeping would think it's still mounted while its DOM is gone.
    Fixed by giving the controls a separate static sibling container
    (`#stats-controls-root`, alongside a new `#stats-content-root` for
    everything else) in `index.html`, and updating `renderStatsView` to
    only ever rebuild `#stats-content-root` - `#stats-controls-root` is
    never touched once React has mounted into it (a `hasReactApp` check,
    same pattern as `renderPrData`'s own React-vs-vanilla branch, guards
    a vanilla fallback that still rebuilds it the old way if React never
    loaded).
  - **A second, smaller gotcha specific to this component:**
    `minComments`/`topN` must commit (call the `onChange` bridge prop,
    which mutates `statsViewState` and calls the expensive
    `applyFiltersFromCache()` to re-render) only on blur, matching the
    original vanilla control's native `"change"` handler - not on every
    keystroke, which is what React's `onChange` alone would do for a
    text-entry `<input>`. The component tracks the raw typed value
    separately (`onChange` → local state only) from the committed value
    (`onBlur` → clamped + sent to vanilla), otherwise every keystroke
    while editing would re-trigger a full stats recompute and fight the
    user's typing.
  - Verified both gotchas are real regressions the tests actually catch:
    temporarily reverted each fix and confirmed the corresponding test
    failed - see the e2e test `"React-owned Review Stats controls
    render, respond to changes, and survive an unrelated stats
    re-render"` (`smoke.spec.js`), which tags the mounted `<select>`
    DOM node with a custom marker attribute and asserts it survives an
    unrelated re-render (checking only the *displayed value* survived
    isn't enough - vanilla's rebuild reads the same `statsViewState` and
    would show the same value too, so the test's first draft passed even
    with the bug still present; only a DOM node identity check actually
    distinguishes "React survived" from "vanilla silently rebuilt an
    equivalent-looking replacement"), and
    `ReviewStatsControls.test.jsx`'s "onChange has not fired" /
    "onChange fires once on blur" tests for the second gotcha.

**✅ Done (Review Stats tab, slice 2 of 2 so far):**
- `<ReviewStatsContent />` — summary cards (with per-card "Show sources"
  native `<details>`), the chart-visuals wrapper, the reviewer table (with
  per-row "Show sources" toggle via React state, since a `<tr>` can't use
  native `<details>`), the "Showing N of M reviewers..." summary note, and
  the activity trend note - replacing
  `pr-review-stats-summary.component.js`'s `renderStatsSummaryAndTable()`
  entirely. Mounted once into `#stats-content-root` and updated via
  `window.updateReviewStatsContent(stats, rows, actorsMap)` - the same
  mount-once/update-via-bridge shape as Phase 1's
  `mountReactPrTable`/`updateReactPrTable` (pure derived display data, no
  persisted override to restore), not the restore-race pattern most of
  Phase 2 used.
  - **Chart visuals were deliberately not reimplemented in JSX.**
    `createStatsVisuals` (`pr-review-stats-visuals.component.js` +
    `pr-review-stats-chart.component.js`, ~900 lines of hand-rolled
    SVG/DOM chart building) is still called as-is; `<StatsVisuals />` just
    wraps it via a `ref` + `useEffect` that clears the container and
    appends whatever DOM node the vanilla builder returns. Rewriting that
    much custom charting logic in JSX would be a disproportionate lift for
    this slice - the same "leave it to vanilla where React's diffing
    wouldn't add real value" reasoning as Phase 2's state-management
    decision, applied here instead of to the whole tab at once.
  - **Found and fixed a real, pre-existing bug, not just a port:** the
    "View in table" button inside a stat card's expandable sources built
    its own ad hoc navigation inline - `activateDataTab("pr-data")` then a
    `setTimeout` that directly mutated `.hidden`/`aria-expanded` on the PR
    table's insights row. That's exactly the same unsafe-under-React
    pattern Author Insights' own "View in table" button already hit and
    was fixed for (see `navigateToPrInTable` in
    `pr-author-insights-pr-link.helpers.js`, which dispatches a
    `'pr-navigate-to-insights'` CustomEvent for `PrTableApp` to handle via
    its own state when React owns the table) - Review Stats' copy was
    simply never updated the same way. `<ReviewStatsContent />` calls a
    new `window.navigateToPrInTableFromStats` bridge that delegates to
    that same already-fixed helper instead of rebuilding (a third copy
    of) the broken version. Confirmed working end-to-end with a real
    browser (screenshot showing the PR data tab activated and the
    insights row genuinely expanded, not just the toggle button claiming
    so) and with the e2e test `"React-owned Review Stats content renders
    cards/table and \"View in table\" navigates to the React-owned PR
    table correctly"`.
  - Two new bridges were added narrowly rather than reusing existing
    global ones: `window.reviewStatsFormatIsoDatetime` (not
    `window.formatIsoDatetime`, which `PrDateCell.jsx` already reads with
    a much cruder fallback - reusing that name here would have changed
    Phase 1's already-shipped date formatting as an unintended side
    effect of this Phase 3 work) and `window.navigateToPrInTableFromStats`
    (not the module-level `navigateToPrInTable` name already in scope in
    `index.page.js`, which is a *different*, unrelated function from
    `pr-auto-render-navigation.helpers.js` with a different signature -
    reusing that name would have been a real naming collision, not just a
    style choice).
  - One e2e test-writing lesson worth remembering: the toggle-expanded
    assertion needed the same `toHaveJSProperty("hidden", false)` +
    `ancestor::tr`/`following-sibling::tr` technique the existing Author
    Insights "View in table" test already uses, *not*
    `expect(...).toBeVisible()` - the target PR can legitimately end up
    inside a currently-collapsed lifecycle/smart-group section depending
    on what earlier tests in this shared-webServer suite did to its
    flagged/in-review state, and `toBeVisible()` fails on that ancestor
    collapse even when the feature under test is working correctly. The
    first draft of this test passed in isolation but failed intermittently
    as part of the full suite for exactly this reason.

**✅ Done (Author Insights tab, slice 1 of several):**
- `<AuthorInsightsSelector />` — the "Author" dropdown, replacing
  `pr-author-insights.component.js`'s `renderAuthorSelector()`. Same shape
  as Phase 2's `MultiSelectCheckboxList` / Review Stats' own multi-select
  precedent, not `ReviewStatsControls`'s shape: this selector's *options*
  are rebuilt from the PR payload on every author-insights render (new
  authors can appear as data changes), so it's mounted once but updated
  via `window.updateAuthorInsightsSelector(options, selectedLogin)` with
  an incrementing `key` on every call, fully re-initializing from fresh
  props each time rather than diffing - matching the old vanilla
  behavior of discarding and rebuilding the `<select>` from scratch on
  every render.
  - **Same container-split gotcha as Review Stats, applied to a third
    container:** `renderAuthorInsights` used to rebuild the selector via
    the *same* `host.innerHTML = ""` that rebuilt the selected-author
    header and every section - given `#author-insights` (like
    `#pr-stats` before it) has no natural "no rebuild needed" boundary of
    its own, React mounting into any part of that subtree needed the
    same static-sibling-container treatment
    (`#author-insights-selector-root` / `#author-insights-content-root`)
    `renderStatsView` already got. Verified by temporarily merging the
    two containers back into one `host.innerHTML = ""` call and
    confirming the e2e test failed - and more severely than expected:
    the selector didn't just lose its selected value on a later
    re-render, it vanished from the page entirely (even before any
    re-render), because `mountAuthorInsightsSelector`'s `createRoot`
    call had already captured a reference to the original
    `#author-insights-selector-root` node, which the merged
    `innerHTML = ""` immediately detached on the very first render.
  - This is now the third time this exact class of bug has appeared
    (Phase 1's `#pr-sections`, Review Stats' `#stats-controls-root`, now
    this) - a strong signal that "does the vanilla rebuild this
    container is going into already cover something else, or could it
    ever need to?" is worth checking explicitly for *every* remaining
    Author Insights section (comments composer/list, PR-linked notes,
    created-PRs) before converting each one, not just assumed safe by
    analogy.
  - The DI contract for `createPrAuthorInsightsComponent` gained one new
    optional parameter, `updateReactAuthorInsightsSelector` (handled/
    fallback shape, same as `pr-filter-panel.component.js`'s
    `renderMultiSelectList` hook) - every existing unit test, which
    doesn't pass it, keeps exercising the unchanged vanilla fallback path.

**Approach for the rest of Review Stats, and for Author Insights /
Backfill when they're picked up:**
- Continue one slice at a time, same as Phases 1-2 - don't attempt a
  whole tab in one shot.
- Before converting any container that a vanilla render function
  currently rebuilds via `innerHTML = ""`, check whether that rebuild
  covers (or will come to cover, once React owns part of it) anything
  React has mounted into - give React-owned pieces their own static
  sibling container the vanilla rebuild is scoped away from, exactly
  like `#stats-controls-root` above and `#pr-sections` in Phase 1. This
  is the single most common way this migration has broken things so far
  (Phase 1's original #pr-sections issue, several of Phase 2's gotchas,
  and this one) - always check for it explicitly rather than assuming a
  new tab's rebuild pattern is safe by default.
- `useMemo`/`React.lazy()` as originally suggested here remain reasonable
  ideas for the heavier pieces (chart visuals, Author Insights) once
  there's an actual component to measure, not upfront.

## ✅ **Success Criteria**

**Phase 1 is complete when:**

✅ React loads without errors  
✅ All PRs render correctly  
✅ Checkboxes sync across all sections  
✅ Smart groups update immediately  
✅ Lifecycle badges appear correctly  
✅ More Insights expand/collapse works  
✅ Delta updates are faster (1.4-1.5x at 50 PRs, 3.8-3.9x at 500 PRs - see "Performance Validation" above for real numbers, not the originally-guessed 15-19x)  
✅ All 1,400+ tests pass  
✅ No visual regressions  
✅ Production build works

## 📈 **Performance Targets**

| Metric | Vanilla (measured) | React (measured) | Notes |
|--------|--------|--------|-------|
| Delta update, 1-50 PRs changed (50-PR dataset) | 44.5-51.0ms | 31.5-33.9ms | **1.4-1.5x**, flat across delta size - see "Performance Validation" above |
| Delta update, 1-50 PRs changed (500-PR dataset) | 745.1-800.6ms | 193.6-209.8ms | **3.8-3.9x**, flat across delta size |
| Bundle size | 150KB | <500KB | **Measured 2026-09-12** (after Phase 3): `npm run build:ui`'s React entry chunk (`main-*.js`, everything under `react-app.jsx` - all 20+ converted components) is 230.62 kB raw / 69.61 kB gzip, well under target. The vanilla `index.page.js`/helpers/components/orchestrators stay classic (non-module) scripts per index.html's own `<script src>` tags, so they aren't part of this bundle at all - only counted once Phase 6 migrates them into the module graph. **Re-measured 2026-09-15** (post Phase 4/5/6 completion): 232.39 kB raw / 70.19 kB gzip - a small (~0.8%) increase from incidental `PrTableApp.jsx` edits since (`pr-entry-derived-cache.helpers.js` itself is a classic script, not part of this module bundle); still well under target. **Re-measured 2026-09-24** (post ES module cleanup - `index.page.js` and all `src/ui/helpers`/`components`/`orchestrators` files converted to real ES modules and folded into the same Vite module graph, no longer classic scripts): 478.08 kB raw / 136.64 kB gzip - roughly doubled, as expected now that the vanilla code is actually part of this bundle instead of loading separately; still comfortably under target. **Re-measured 2026-09-25** (deferred-items follow-up, item 5 - see below - `React.lazy()` code-splitting for the 3 tabs never visible on initial load): eager `main-*.js` is now **430.69 kB raw / 123.77 kB gzip** (down ~47.4 kB raw / ~12.9 kB gzip, ~9.4% off the initial load), with the remaining code split into 9 on-demand chunks (~51.7 kB raw / ~18.2 kB gzip combined) fetched only the first time a user visits Review Stats, Author Insights, Action Log, Actor Names, or Export |
| Initial load | 200ms | <600ms | **Measured 2026-09-12** (Vite dev server, isolated fixture data, 3-PR dataset, headless Chromium via CDP `Performance.getMetrics`): steady-state `load` event fires at **164-174ms** (first run in a fresh dev-server process was 426ms, Vite's on-demand transform of the first request - not representative; every run after is fast, since Vite caches the transformed modules) |
| Memory usage | 10MB | <50MB | **Measured 2026-09-12**, same run: steady-state JS heap (`JSHeapUsedSize`) is **~11MB** after initial render, well under target |
| Test coverage | 98% | >95% | Maintain quality |

## 🔁 **Deferred-items follow-up (2026-09-24)**

A review of prior deferrals/declines turned up 6 items; TypeScript migration (item 1) stays out of scope, picked up separately. Status of the rest:

- **Item 2 (unify `PrTableApp.jsx`'s inline section-grouping with the shared `buildGroupedPrSections` helper): done.** `PrTableApp.jsx` now instantiates `pr-section-grouping.helpers.js`'s factory once via a lazy `useRef` singleton (so its reference-equality cache, added in the Phase 5 residual slice, actually persists across renders) and calls the real shared helper instead of a hand-duplicated inline copy. Verified: all 35 pre-existing `PrTableApp.test.jsx` tests pass unmodified, plus one new test confirming the cache is actually hit across an unrelated re-render; full jest suite (1886 tests) and full Playwright smoke suite (30 tests) both pass.
- **Item 3 (gate `ReviewStatsContent`'s computation on tab visibility): done.** Added a colocated `useIsTabPanelVisible('tab-panel-review-stats')` hook (`MutationObserver` on the panel's `hidden` attribute - no Context/React state tracks the active data tab today, since tab switching is pure vanilla DOM via `pr-data-tabs.helpers.js`'s `activateDataTab`). The `stats` `useMemo`'s deps grew to `[payload, statsViewState, isVisible]`, skipping the expensive `buildReviewerStats`/`applyStatsControls` recompute while hidden (returning the last computed value from a ref instead) and recomputing immediately the moment the tab becomes visible again - restoring the vanilla predecessor's original "skip while hidden" behavior that got dropped when this component was rewritten for Track C. Verified: all 7 pre-existing tests pass unmodified, plus 2 new tests (hidden + payload change skips the recompute; flipping `hidden` off triggers an immediate recompute); full jest suite (1888 tests) and full Playwright smoke suite (30 tests) both pass.
- **Item 4 (broader state-management cleanup): resolved** - see the Phase 2 row's "Resolved (2026-09-24)" note above; this was already completed in Phase 6, just never marked against the original deferral.
- **Item 5 (tab code-splitting): done.** Added `useIsTabPanelVisible`/`useHasTabPanelBeenVisible` (`src/ui/state/useIsTabPanelVisible.jsx` - the former promoted out of `ReviewStatsContent.jsx`, now item 3's and item 5's shared home) and wrapped the 9 components exclusive to the 5 tabs that start `hidden` in `index.html` (Review Stats' controls+content, all 5 Author Insights sections, Action Log, Actor Names, Export) in `React.lazy()`, gated on the *sticky* `useHasTabPanelBeenVisible` rather than plain visibility - several of these (`ReviewStatsControls`' sort/filter selection, `ExportTab`'s checked fields) hold local-only UI state that isn't backed by a vanilla bridge the way Author Insights' comment drafts are, so unmounting on every tab switch would have silently reset it; staying mounted forever after the first visit avoids that while still deferring each chunk's fetch/parse cost until actually needed. **Measured** (`npm run build:ui`, 2026-09-25): the eager `main-*.js` chunk dropped from 478.08 kB raw / 136.64 kB gzip to **430.69 kB raw / 123.77 kB gzip** (~9.4% off the initial load), with the deferred code split into 9 on-demand chunks (~51.7 kB raw / ~18.2 kB gzip combined) - a real, worthwhile win, bigger than the original research's "low-single-digit-percent" estimate.

**Regression found and fixed (2026-09-25, via a follow-up "any problems with these changes?" review):** `ActionLogSection`/`ActorNamesTab` each register a `window.trigger*Load` bridge on mount for the vanilla tab-click handler (`pr-management-tabs.helpers.js`'s `activateTab`) to call - before this change they were always eagerly mounted, so the bridge existed well before any click. Lazy-loading broke that: `activateTab`'s trigger call fires *synchronously* on click, always before the newly-lazy component's chunk has loaded and mounted, so it silently no-op'd - both tabs showed a stuck "never loaded" state on their first visit each page load (self-healing on a second visit, which is why it's easy to miss). No unit or e2e test caught this initially: jsdom tests mocked the trigger call directly (bypassing the race entirely) and no e2e test exercised either tab at all. Fixed by having both components call `load()` themselves on mount, not just register it - confirmed via a real repro-then-fix cycle (temporarily disabled the mount-time call, confirmed 2 new Playwright tests failed with the expected timeout, restored it, confirmed they passed) and 2 new e2e tests added specifically for this (`e2e/smoke.spec.js`, asserting the real `/view-prs/action-log`/`/view-prs/actor-name-cache`/`/view-prs/actor-login-aliases` network requests actually fire on first tab visit, not on rendered text - "No actions logged yet." is shown both when genuinely empty and when never fetched, so text alone can't distinguish the bug from correct behavior). The other 7 lazy components (Review Stats x2, Author Insights x5) were checked and confirmed NOT to have this pattern - none of them register a bridge a vanilla tab-click handler calls synchronously; Author Insights' equivalent (`updateReactSelectedAuthorLogin`) is registered by the always-mounted `PrDataProvider`, not by the lazy components themselves.

Verified (post-fix): full jest suite (1900 tests, including rewritten `ActionLogSection.test.jsx`/`ActorNamesTab.test.jsx` covering the mount-time auto-load) and full Playwright smoke suite (32 tests, including the 2 new regression tests) both pass.
- **Item 6 (`latestStoredPayload` into Context): done, narrower than originally designed.** Added `PrDataProvider.jsx`'s `window.getReactPrTablePayload` read bridge (mirrors the existing `window.updateReactPrTable` write bridge; backed by a ref so it always returns the latest payload rather than closing over a stale one). Of the 3 DI wirings originally planned to prefer it over the raw vanilla `latestStoredPayload` (`pr-apply-filters-cache.helpers.js`'s `applyFiltersFromCache`, `pr-data-tab.orchestrator.js`'s `renderPrData`, and `react-callbacks.helpers.js`'s checkbox/Ack/Apply-Label handlers), only the **first two** actually switched - the full Playwright smoke suite caught a real regression in the third: `handleCheckboxChange`/`handleAckAction`/`handleApplyLabel` all mutate `latestStoredPayload` as a synchronous side effect (via `toggleFlaggedForRow`/`toggleInReviewForRow`/the Ack/label workflows) and then immediately read it back in the *same call* to push into React - a read-your-own-write dependency the Context bridge can't satisfy, since Context's payload only updates *after* that read's return value reaches `updateReactTableSafe`. Switching it broke the checkbox-toggle smoke test (state visibly reverted right after the click); reverted that one wiring back to the raw variable, kept the other two (which only ever read, never mutate-then-immediately-reread). A reminder that "prefer the visible payload" isn't uniformly safe - it depends on whether a consumer is reading current state or completing its own write. Verified: full jest suite (1899 tests, including 3 new tests for the read bridge itself) and full Playwright smoke suite (30 tests) both pass after the correction.

---

## 🔎 **Full vanilla-to-React conversion sweep (2026-09-25)**

Requested explicitly: find *every* remaining piece of vanilla-rendered UI in the repo, for full React consistency, not just the highest-value items. Covered via 3 parallel audits - `src/ui/helpers/*.js` (158 files), `src/ui/index.page.js` (5500+ lines) plus both orchestrators plus `pr-author-insights.component.js`, and all 81 `src/ui/components/*.jsx` files (checked for ref-wrapper/container-split stragglers) plus `index.html`'s static markup - then spot-verified (2 of the more consequential claims below were independently re-checked via direct grep/read before being written down here, not just trusted from the audit).

**Bottom line: the migration is close to its end state.** All orchestration/coordination code (`pr-data-tab.orchestrator.js`, `backfill-tab.orchestrator.js`, and the bulk of `index.page.js` - polling, fetch, event wiring, bridge calls) is genuinely coordination-only with zero DOM-building, by design, not an unfinished conversion. 150 of 158 helper files are pure computation/derivation with zero DOM writes. All 81 `.jsx` components are real JSX - no lingering `useRef`+`useEffect`-delegates-to-a-vanilla-builder ("container-split") pattern remains anywhere. What's left is a short, enumerable list:

### A. Seven vanilla status/log panels (highest-value candidates)

Each is a `<pre>` element in `index.html` populated by a vanilla `.textContent =` write built from a template string - the same shape as `BackfillBadges`/`SchedulerBadges` (already React), just not yet converted. Several are literally the sibling of an already-React badge row in the same panel:

| Container (`index.html`) | Tab | Populated by (`index.page.js` unless noted) | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `#status` | Activity | `setStatusMessage()` | Also written from ~10 action-handler call sites (run/ack/clear/poll flows) |
| `#request-activity-details` | Activity | `renderRequestActivity()` | Sibling of the already-React `#request-activity-badges` |
| `#scheduler-details` | Activity | `renderSchedulerStatus()` | Sibling of the already-React `#scheduler-badges` |
| `#output` | Run & Filter | `setOutputMessage()`, text built by `pr-command-output.helpers.js` | Command/Error/Stdout/Stderr blocks, ANSI stripped |
| `#backfill-details` | Backfill | `renderBackfillStatus()` | Sibling of the already-React `#backfill-badges` |
| `#backfill-log` | Backfill | `setBackfillLogMessage()`, via `backfill-tab.orchestrator.js` | Refresh button + autoscroll checkbox are also vanilla-wired |
| `#data-meta` | PR data | `pr-render-apply.helpers.js`'s `applyRenderResults` (`meta.textContent = appliedSummaryText`) | Same `appliedSummaryText` value `AppliedFilterSummary.jsx` shows in the *Run & Filter* tab - shown here too since this tab is a different one (above the table, not the filter panel) - plausibly intentional duplication across tabs, not obviously dead, but still a trivial React conversion (mirrors the existing badge-row bridge pattern: `window.updateReactX(text)` + a tiny `<StatusPanel text={...} />`) |

### B. Filter-panel multi-select summary counts + empty-state class

`src/ui/helpers/pr-filter-panel.helpers.js` - the item *rows* of the 5 filter multi-selects (author/assigned/approver/label/exclude-label) are already React (`MultiSelectCheckboxList.jsx`). What's left vanilla is just the "`<details>` summary" text (e.g. "(3 selected)") and an `empty`/non-empty CSS class on the list container - `updateMultiSelectSummary()` (~17 lines) called from 5 `populateXOptions` functions (~230 lines total, wired at `index.page.js:2341`). Candidate: fold the count/empty-class into `MultiSelectCheckboxList.jsx` itself (it already knows its own option list).

### C. Two empty-state `<p>` builders in `pr-author-insights.component.js`

Lines ~113-116 and ~125-128 (`renderAuthorInsights`) - build a `<p class="stats-empty">` via `document.createElement`/`.textContent`/`appendChild` when the current scope has zero rows or zero authors. ~8 lines total, static text, trivial to fold into whichever component owns the Author Insights empty state today.

### D. Error/warning snackbar

`#error-snackbar-message` (`index.page.js`'s `showErrorNotification`/`showWarningNotification`) - sets `.textContent` plus toggles between two fixed CSS classes for the error/warning variant. Not yet audited for exact call-site count; a reasonable small standalone component (`<Snackbar />`) given how many action handlers likely call it.

### E. Two button-label state machines

`handleTriggerAutoRun`/`handleQuickCheck` (`index.page.js` ~2188-2320) toggle a single button's `.textContent`/`.disabled` through several states ("Triggering...", "Trigger auto run", "Checking...", "N updates found", etc.) - a state-machine button, same shape as other already-converted action buttons elsewhere in the app.

### Lower-priority, or needs investigation before converting (not just porting)

- **`src/ui/helpers/pr-auto-render-navigation.helpers.js`** (`navigateToPrInTable`, PR-row insights-expand, ~15-20 lines) - **verified via direct grep: this file has NO `isReactTableMounted` guard at all**, unlike its sibling `pr-author-insights-pr-link.helpers.js` (which does, and explicitly documents *why* - directly mutating a React-rendered row would "leave the toggle button claiming 'expanded' while the insights content never actually renders"). Since `PrTableApp.jsx` now owns `#pr-sections` in the normal running state, this code path may already be silently broken/dead rather than a genuine live feature - **investigate whether it's reachable at all before deciding whether to port it or delete it**, rather than converting it as-is.
- **`src/ui/helpers/pr-author-insights-pr-link.helpers.js`** - already mostly ported (its own header comment says the DOM-building link element itself moved to `AuthorInsightsPrLink.jsx`); the remaining vanilla branch is an explicit, guarded, documented compatibility fallback for when React hasn't mounted. Low priority.
- **`src/ui/helpers/pr-auto-render-indicator-links.helpers.js`** - 2 lines toggling `hidden`/`aria-label` on a static portal-host `<div>` (the button list itself is already `AutoRenderBlockedLinks.jsx`). This is portal-mount bookkeeping, not really a "component" to convert - leave as-is or fold into the portal-mount code.

### Confirmed dead code found during the sweep (delete, don't convert)

- **`syncSelectionCheckboxesWithInput`** (`index.page.js:3867`) walks `#pr-sections` looking for elements with class `row-select-checkbox` and sets `.checked` directly. **Verified via grep: no `.jsx` component or `index.html` markup anywhere in the current codebase renders anything with that class** - this function's tree-walk can no longer match anything, making it inert dead code (not a live bug fighting React's state, as it might look at a glance - it simply never finds a node to act on). Candidate for deletion, along with its 2 call sites (`handlePrNumbersInputChange`, `updateSelectedPrNumbers`), rather than conversion.

### Stale documentation found

- **`index.html:486-489`**'s comment on `#author-insights-content-root` still reads *"wraps the legacy vanilla builder via a ref"* - **verified false**: `AuthorInsightsCommentsSection.jsx` is real JSX with no DOM ref (its one `useRef` is an unrelated `setTimeout` handle). Leftover from an earlier version of the component; worth a one-line doc fix independent of any code change.

### Explicitly out of scope (deliberately vanilla, not conversion candidates)

- **Tab-switching chrome** (`pr-management-tabs.helpers.js`, `pr-data-tabs.helpers.js`) - `.hidden`/`aria-selected`/`.className` toggling on static elements for native event delegation; an established, deliberate pattern elsewhere in this migration (see Items 3/5 above, which build React-side visibility hooks *on top of* this vanilla chrome rather than replacing it).
- **`renderPrTableMountError`, `mountReactTable`'s pre-mount `container.innerHTML = ""` clear, `applyActivePrProgressIndicators`** - all explicitly documented in their own surrounding comments as dead-in-the-happy-path fallback/prep code for a genuine React mount failure or the React-not-yet-mounted race, not normal render paths. `renderPrTableMountError` in particular probably belongs vanilla permanently - it's the fallback for React itself failing to load, so a React port would be self-defeating.
- **`autoResizeTextarea`** (`index.page.js`) - a generic imperative DOM utility (sets `.style.height`) invoked via a ref callback *from* React components, not a rendering path of its own - same category as any `useEffect` doing `ref.current.style.x = y`.

### Confirmed clean (nothing to convert)

- `pr-data-tab.orchestrator.js`, `backfill-tab.orchestrator.js` - read in full, zero DOM-mutation patterns, pure coordination/delegation.
- All 81 `src/ui/components/*.jsx` files - no ref-wrapper/container-split stragglers (every `useRef` usage checked; the 2 real DOM refs found, in `PrJsonModal.jsx` and `ReviewerActivityChart.jsx`, are for focus-trap/scroll-lock and `ResizeObserver` measurement respectively, not content injection).
- 150 of 158 `src/ui/helpers/*.js` files - pure computation/derivation/filtering, zero DOM writes (includes every `deriveXxx`/`render*State`/`*-render-apply-inputs`/`*-summary-inputs` file, despite render-adjacent names).
- The 9 multi-select filter lists (`#label-list`, `#exclude-label-list`, etc.) and the scheduler/backfill/request-activity badge rows - confirmed already React-rendered via `window.renderReactMultiSelectList`/`window.updateReactXBadges`, just missing the `index.html` "Rendered by React" comment convention (a documentation gap, not a code gap).

### Follow-through status (implementing the sweep's findings)

- **Cleanup: done.** Deleted `syncSelectionCheckboxesWithInput` (`index.page.js`) and its 2 call sites - confirmed dead (no test coverage, `row-select-checkbox` matches nothing in current markup). Fixed the stale `index.html` comment on `#author-insights-content-root` that still described `AuthorInsightsCommentsSection` as a ref-wrapper.
- **Bug fix: done.** `pr-auto-render-navigation.helpers.js`'s `navigateToPrInTable` now takes an `isReactTableMounted` DI param and, when React owns the table, dispatches `pr-navigate-to-insights` instead of directly mutating the insights row's `hidden`/toggle-button text - mirrors `pr-author-insights-pr-link.helpers.js`'s already-proven fix for the identical hazard. Investigation confirmed this was a real, live, reachable bug (not dead code): `navigateToPrInTable` is exposed as `window.navigateToPrInTable` and called directly by `AutoRenderBlockedLinks.jsx` on click. Added 2 new tests covering both branches. Verified: full jest suite (1902 tests) and full Playwright smoke suite (32 tests) both pass.
- **The 2 empty-state `<p>` builders in `pr-author-insights.component.js`: done.** `renderAuthorInsights` no longer builds any DOM directly - it still clears `selectedAuthorLogin` via the React bridge for both empty cases (state management, kept), but the actual "No local rows available for author insights."/"No authors found in the current local data scope." messages are now rendered by `AuthorInsightsSelector.jsx` (which already computed `rows`/`options` from Context for its own empty-vs-render decision, and previously just returned `null` - now renders the matching message instead). Updated 3 existing tests to match; verified full jest suite (1902 tests) and full Playwright smoke suite (32 tests) both pass.
- **The 7 vanilla status/log panels (`#status`, `#output`, `#scheduler-details`, `#request-activity-details`, `#backfill-details`, `#backfill-log`, `#data-meta`): done.** Each now gets a `window.updateReactXText` bridge + `useState` + plain-string `createPortal` in `react-app.jsx`'s `AppRoot`, following the exact shape the badge rows (`backfillBadges`/`schedulerBadges`/`requestActivityBadges`) already used - no dedicated component needed since all 7 targets are bare `<pre>` elements with no internal markup. `index.page.js`'s direct `.textContent =` writes (including 8 more found beyond the original 7 - `toggleInReviewForRow`/`toggleFlaggedForRow`'s 9 direct `#status` writes, not previously listed) were all replaced. `#backfill-log` got the same `'viewprs:react-ready'` re-invocation treatment as backfill badges/details (it only populates on tab-visit/refresh-click, so a lost bridge race would never self-correct); the other 6 are called repeatedly during normal operation and self-heal.
  - **Real bug found and fixed along the way:** `renderRequestActivity` used to read `document.getElementById("status").textContent` to embed the current status in its own "Current status: ..." line - once `#status` became React-owned, that DOM read would be stale immediately after `setStatusMessage` calls `window.updateReactStatusText` and then synchronously calls `renderRequestActivity()` in the same function (React's state commit is asynchronous). Fixed by tracking `latestStatusText` as a plain vanilla variable instead, matching the codebase's established "vanilla variable is the source of truth" pattern (e.g. `latestStoredPayload`). A similar-looking read in `inferViewerLoginFromPage` was investigated and deliberately left reading the DOM - it's a decoupled, best-effort fallback called from a different context, not synchronously after a status-set call, so the same hazard doesn't actually apply there; confirmed via a legitimate integration test that seeds `#status` directly for this exact function.
  - **A second, more significant bug found via manual real-browser verification (jsdom tests couldn't catch it): `createPortal` does not clear a target container's pre-existing static content.** Every one of these 7 panels' `<pre>` elements had non-empty static placeholder text in `index.html` (e.g. `<pre id="status">Not run</pre>`) - React's portal doesn't replace that on mount, it only adds its own children alongside it, so every panel rendered as e.g. `"Not runNot run"` or `"Loading...Applied filters: ..."` (placeholder text with the real content stuck directly onto its end) until the OLD `.textContent =` write happened to occur again. Fixed by emptying all 7 containers' static markup in `index.html` (matching how every other React-portaled container in this codebase already starts empty). While auditing for this, found the **identical pre-existing bug in 2 already-converted containers from earlier migration phases** - `#stats-content-root` (`ReviewStatsContent`) and `#author-insights-content-root` (`AuthorInsightsCommentsSection`) - both fixed the same way.
  - Added a new Playwright regression test (`e2e/smoke.spec.js`) checking all 9 fixed containers for the stuck-placeholder signature - deliberately checks "placeholder present as a prefix but text isn't *exactly* the placeholder" rather than "doesn't start with the placeholder," since for panels like `#status`/`#output` the placeholder text is the legitimately correct, unchanged value on a fresh page load where nothing has run yet (confirmed by initially getting this wrong and having to fix the test itself - the first version flagged `#status`'s correct "Not run" value as a false positive). Verified the test actually catches the regression via a real repro-then-fix cycle, same discipline as the Item 5 lazy-loading bug earlier.
  - Also updated `src/ui/integration-tests/index.html.test.js`, which exercises `index.page.js` without `react-app.jsx`'s real `<AppRoot/>` ever mounting - added `installReactStatusTextMountBridges()` (a plain-textContent-write simulation of what the real bridges do) so its ~17 tests that read these panels' text kept working.
  - Verified: full jest suite (1902 tests) and full Playwright smoke suite (33 tests, including the new regression test) both pass.
- **The error/warning snackbar (`#error-snackbar`): done.** New `src/ui/components/Snackbar.jsx` owns the *entire* subtree (not just children of a portal target) - `index.html`'s `#error-snackbar` became an empty `#error-snackbar-root` mount point, since the outer element's own `id`/`hidden`/variant-class attributes needed React ownership, which a portal into an existing element's children can't provide. `index.page.js`'s `showErrorNotification`/`showWarningNotification`/`hideErrorNotification` became thin delegating wrappers around the `window.*` bridges this component registers, so their ~15+ existing call sites (`notifyFailureSnackbar`, `markPollSuccess`, etc.) kept working unmodified. State updates use `flushSync` (matching `react-app.jsx`'s own `renderReactMultiSelectList`) since these bridges are called from vanilla code outside any React event handler (poll failure handlers, fetch rejections) - without it, a caller reading `document.getElementById(...)` right after calling one of these (several existing integration tests do exactly this) would see a not-yet-committed DOM, caught via the integration test suite. Added `installReactSnackbarMountBridge()` to `index.html.test.js` (mounts the real component via RTL, unlike the 7 status panels' plain-text simulation, since this has real stateful behavior worth testing precisely). Manually verified in a real browser (show/hide, variant class, close button, auto-dismiss all worked correctly) and added the first-ever e2e coverage for it (`e2e/smoke.spec.js` - there was none before). Verified: full jest suite (1902 tests) and full Playwright smoke suite (34 tests, including the new snackbar test) both pass.
- **The 2 button-label state machines (`#trigger-auto-run-btn`, `#quick-check-btn`): done.** New `TriggerAutoRunButton.jsx`/`QuickCheckButton.jsx` own the disabled/label state machine; `index.page.js`'s `handleTriggerAutoRun`/`handleQuickCheck` no longer touch the DOM at all - only the fetch/branching business logic remains, exposed as `window.handleTriggerAutoRun`/`window.handleQuickCheck` for the components to call as injected `onTrigger`/`onCheck` props (matching `PrTableApp`'s `onCheckboxChange` callback-prop pattern). `handleQuickCheck` was the more involved of the two - its final label depends on the outcome (409/503/failure all revert immediately; success shows a count then reverts after a delay) - so it now returns a `{ label, resetAfterMs? }` descriptor instead of mutating `btn.textContent` itself, and the component owns the delayed-reset timer (with a regression test confirming a second click correctly cancels an earlier pending reset rather than letting it clobber the new result). Found 5 existing integration tests that click "Quick check" and assert on its resulting label/disabled state - added `installReactActionButtonMountBridges()` (real RTL mounts, same reasoning as the snackbar) to `index.html.test.js` to keep them passing. Added 8 new component tests (both buttons' default/pending/settled states) plus first-ever e2e coverage for both (`e2e/smoke.spec.js`) and manually verified both in a real browser. Verified: full jest suite (1910 tests) and full Playwright smoke suite (35 tests) both pass.
- **The multi-select summary counts + empty-state class: done - all 9 lists, not just the 5 originally scoped.** `MultiSelectCheckboxList.jsx` now owns both the "(N selected)" summary text (portaled into a sibling `<summary class="multi-select-summary">`, reading a new `data-base-label` attribute instead of the old runtime `summary.textContent.split("(")[0]` parsing) and the "empty" class on its own portal target (via a `useEffect` keyed on `options.length`, since a component can't set attributes on its own portal target from inside its own JSX). Scope grew during implementation: the original plan only accounted for the 5 lists `pr-filter-panel.helpers.js`'s `populateXOptions` functions build - investigation found 4 more (thread-resolution allow/deny, change-filter ignore-comment/review-authors) built directly in `index.page.js` via two shared functions (`renderActorOptionsList`/`renderChangeFilterActorList`) using the exact same `updateMultiSelectSummary`/`classList.add("empty")` mechanism, covered by extending `react-app.jsx`'s existing per-list container-resolution loop (already iterating all 9) rather than any new per-list code. Deleted `updateMultiSelectSummary` entirely (5 call sites in `pr-filter-panel.helpers.js`, 2 in `index.page.js`, plus a now-redundant "change" event listener call - MultiSelectCheckboxList's own React state already reacts to the same checkbox toggle) and all 7 `classList.add/remove("empty")` call sites.
  - **Hit the identical "`createPortal` doesn't clear pre-existing content" bug from item 5** (see above) in a new place: all 9 `<summary>` elements had visible fallback text alongside the new `data-base-label` attribute (intended as a no-JS default) - React's portal doesn't clear that before adding its own text, so summaries rendered as `"Filter by label name(s)Filter by label name(s)"`. Caught via the same manual real-browser verification habit this sweep established after item 5's lesson, not by any automated test. Fixed by emptying all 9 `<summary>` elements, consistent with how this sweep already handles every other portal target.
  - **Found and fixed a second, unrelated pre-existing bug while extending the item-5 regression test to cover this**: an existing e2e test's cleanup (`"React-owned thread-resolution allow/deny..."`) explicitly deleted a persisted multi-select selection via a direct PUT, but then triggered a mode-select change whose own auto-persist (`persistViewFilterOptionOverrides`) re-reads the *live, still-checked* DOM checkboxes and immediately overwrote the deletion right back - leaking a selected checkbox state into whatever test ran next against the suite's shared server. Fixed by reordering so the explicit delete-PUT is the last step, not the first.
  - Verified: full jest suite (1913 tests, including 3 new tests for the summary/empty-class behavior in `MultiSelectCheckboxList.test.jsx`, replacing 2 obsolete `updateMultiSelectSummary` tests) and full Playwright smoke suite (35 tests, including the extended regression test) both pass.

**All 7 items from the full vanilla-to-React sweep are now done.** Combined with the earlier deferred-items follow-up (items 2-6), the migration's remaining vanilla surface area is limited to what the sweep explicitly identified as deliberately-vanilla (tab-switching chrome, React-mount-failure fallbacks, generic DOM utilities invoked via refs) or flagged as needing further investigation before any further action (`pr-auto-render-navigation.helpers.js`'s sibling files, `pr-author-insights-pr-link.helpers.js`'s remaining compatibility shim, `pr-auto-render-indicator-links.helpers.js`'s portal-mount bookkeeping) - none of which are conversion candidates as-is.

### ESLint now actually covers `.jsx` files (2026-09-25)

Found via a follow-up review: `eslint.config.mjs`'s `files` patterns never included `.jsx` anywhere - ESLint's flat config only lints a file when some block's `files` pattern names it, so every one of the 81 `.jsx` components was silently skipped ("File ignored because no matching configuration was supplied") rather than actually linted, for the whole of this migration. Fixed:

- Added a dedicated `**/*.jsx` block (`sourceType: "module"`, JSX parsing enabled) and extended the existing `no-unused-vars`/import-boundary rule blocks to include `.jsx`.
- Added `eslint-plugin-react-hooks` (confirmed ESLint 10-compatible) for `react-hooks/rules-of-hooks` (error) and `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` (warn) - the two long-established, uncontroversial rules. Deliberately *not* the plugin's full v7 "recommended" preset, which folds in ~15 additional React Compiler-oriented rules (purity/immutability/set-state-in-effect/etc.) tuned for compiler memoization safety this codebase doesn't use and whose assumptions don't always fit patterns already deliberately used here (`flushSync`, `window.*` bridge reads inside effects).
- `eslint-plugin-react` (the other obvious candidate, for JSX-specific rules like `jsx-key`) could **not** be installed - its latest release's peer dependency range (`^3 || ... || ^9.7`) doesn't yet include ESLint 10, and npm refuses the install without `--force`/`--legacy-peer-deps`. Not forced, to avoid an unsupported dependency; revisit once the plugin catches up.
- Also added `dist/**` to the ignores list (missing before - hadn't been exposed until this was the first whole-project `eslint .` run of the session; the build output's bundled `import`/`export` syntax failed to parse under the default config).

**Fixed everything the newly-enabled linting found** (164 problems initially, all resolved):

- **129 files** had a genuinely unused `import React from 'react'` (Vite's `@vitejs/plugin-react` uses the automatic JSX runtime, confirmed via `vite.config.js` - `React` was never actually referenced via `React.X` in any of these). Removed programmatically from eslint's own JSON report, verified via full jest suite + a fresh production build afterward (same 185 modules, same bundle size).
- **11 stale inline `eslint-disable-next-line react/no-array-index-key`/`react/no-danger` comments** across 6 files under `src/ui/components/insights/` - now-meaningless since `eslint-plugin-react` isn't installed (nothing to disable), and were themselves causing new "rule not found" errors. Removed; the underlying code they referenced (array-index keys, `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`) was left as-is - out of scope to re-litigate without the rule active to flag it.
- **1 stale `eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps`** in `ReviewerActivityChart.jsx` - the dependency array was already complete, so the rule reported "unused eslint-disable directive." Removed.
- **2 genuine `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` warnings, both false positives, both fixed with a justified suppression instead of a code change**: `PrTableApp.jsx`'s `sections` `useMemo` (`attentionConfigVersion`) and `ReviewStatsContent.jsx`'s `stats` `useMemo` (`statsViewState`) both list a dependency that's never read inside the callback *by design* - both are deliberate "invalidation trigger" patterns (bump/change a value purely to force a recompute of something read via closure or `window.*` elsewhere), already documented in each file's own surrounding comments before this review. Removing either dependency would have silently broken real reactivity (the Needs Attention rule-config-change case, and the Review Stats settings-change case, respectively) - added an inline justification instead of removing them.
- **A handful of genuine unused-variable findings**: a stale `const repo = effectiveRepo;` alias in `PrTableApp.jsx` (dead since an earlier refactor moved every real use over to each entry's own `entry.repo` - its own surrounding comment was still describing it as in use, fixed too), an unused second parameter on `PrJsonModal.jsx`'s `formatDiffSummaryLine` (a copy-paste leftover from a sibling function that does need it), and several intentionally-unused mock-callback parameters in test files (prefixed with `_` per this config's existing `argsIgnorePattern`, rather than changed).

Verified: `npx eslint .` exits clean (0 errors, 0 warnings) project-wide; full jest suite (1913 tests), full Playwright smoke suite (35 tests), and a fresh production build all still pass after every fix.

---

## 🚧 **Phase 7: Single React entry point, no `window.*` bridges (in progress, 2026-09-26)**

A follow-up audit (3 parallel research agents) scoped what it would take to fold `index.page.js` (~5,500 lines, still owning polling, one-shot loads, user-action fetches, persistence, debounce timers, and the auto-render-blocking system) into React entirely, eliminating the ~125 app-defined `window.*` bridges connecting the two independent entry points. This is a substantially larger effort than any prior single item in this migration - being executed as 8 sequenced sub-phases (7.0-7.7), one at a time with full verification and a check-in after each, per the user's explicit preference. See the plan tool's saved plan for the full sub-phase breakdown.

### Sub-phase 7.0: actor-identity resolution moved off `window.*`, into React (done)

The original scope for 7.0 assumed most of the ~68 "Layer B" pure-function bridges (`index.page.js`'s one big `Object.assign(window, {...})` block) could just become direct ES module imports. Investigating the actual DI wiring showed this was mostly wrong: nearly every one of these functions is produced by calling a helper factory *with* dependency-injection arguments closing over `index.page.js`'s own local state (`document`, `authorInsightsState`, `statsViewState`, `pendingXFilterSelections`, sibling functions, etc.) - not standalone pure exports. Reported this back before proceeding; the user chose to expand 7.0's scope to include re-wiring the DI'd functions into React now, rather than deferring that work to later sub-phases.

First (and, for this sub-phase, only) cluster tackled: **actor-identity resolution** - `normalizeActorLogin`, `resolveActorDisplayName`, `getPreferredActorKey`, `buildRowActorsMap`, `normalizeActorLoginAliases` (`helpers/pr-actor-identity.helpers.js`), `getEffectiveViewerLogin` (`helpers/pr-actor-identity-render.helpers.js`), and `buildActorIdentityClassName`/`buildActorIdentityTitle` (`helpers/pr-actor-identity-style.helpers.js`). Chosen first because it's the most foundational cluster (nearly every other insights/author/stats helper takes one of these as a DI input) and because its one seemingly-live-state dependency (`currentActorLoginAliases`/`currentViewerLogin`, via `helpers/pr-viewer-context.helpers.js`'s `deriveViewerContext`) turned out to be derivable purely from the PR payload - already real React state in `PrDataProvider` - not from any index.page.js-only mutable variable.

**What changed:**
- `state/ActorIdentityContext.jsx` (new): a Context whose default value is the same factories called with no arguments (viewer-unaware, no aliases) - deliberately matching the permissive `window.x || (...) => ...` fallback every consuming component used to inline, so components render correctly with zero wrapping in tests that don't care about exact identity behavior.
- `state/PrDataProvider.jsx`: computes `viewerContext` (`{ currentActorLoginAliases, currentViewerLogin }`) and the full `actorIdentity` function bundle via `useMemo`, both derived straight from its own `payload` state (reusing the existing pure helper factories directly, not reimplementing them), and provides `actorIdentity` via `ActorIdentityContext`.
- `helpers/pr-viewer-login-inference.helpers.js` (new): extracted `index.page.js`'s inline `inferViewerLoginFromPage` (a last-resort fallback that scrapes `#output`/`#status` for a "Viewer : X" string) into a shared module, since React's independent viewer-context derivation needs the exact same fallback - this fixed a real regression an integration test caught (see below).
- 10 components (`ActorIdentity`, `AuthorCreatedPrsSection`, `AuthorInsightsHeader`, `AuthorInsightsNotesSection`, `AuthorInsightsCommentsSection`, `cells/PrApprovedCell`, `PrInsightsRow`, `insights/ReviewThreadsSection`, `insights/ApprovalRiskSection`, `insights/NotesSection`) switched from `window.x || fallback` to `useActorIdentity()`.
- `index.page.js`: removed these 7 property names from its `Object.assign(window, {...})` bridge (kept the local `const`s that its own internal vanilla logic still needs); deleted the now-fully-unused `buildRowActorsMap`/`buildActorIdentityClassName`/`buildActorIdentityTitle` destructured bindings and the now-unused `pr-actor-identity-style.helpers.js` import entirely.

**Bug caught by full-suite verification:** the first full jest run failed one integration test - `index.html.test.js`'s "each actor gets its matching identity styling," which sets `#status`'s text to `"Viewer : ahall236_uhg"` (simulating the DOM-scraping fallback) rather than putting `viewerLogin` on the payload. React's new independent derivation didn't know about this fallback, so it resolved an empty viewer login while vanilla's copy (still wired to the real `inferViewerLoginFromPage`) resolved correctly - a genuine regression, not a test artifact. Fixed by extracting the fallback into a shared helper both sides call, rather than duplicating or dropping it.

**Verified:** full jest suite (1913 tests, all passing), `npx eslint` clean on every touched file, full Playwright smoke suite (35 tests, all passing).

**Not yet done:** sub-phases 7.1-7.7 (init-race collapse, PR-data/polling state, user-action hooks, filter persistence, auto-render-blocking, remaining call-site cleanup, final `index.page.js` deletion) - the remaining Layer A/B bridges (~117 of ~125) are untouched.

### Sub-phase 7.0, continued: zero-dependency pure-function bridges moved to direct imports (done)

Before touching sub-phase 7.1, investigated whether it could actually be done independently - it can't yet. `index.page.js`'s `initPage()` fires `loadStoredData`/`restoreUiOptionOverrides`/`loadBackfillStatus`/`loadSchedulerStatus` immediately (not gated on React mounting), and `'viewprs:react-ready'`'s 4 listeners exist purely to retry once React catches up. The large `index.html.test.js` integration suite relies on exactly this eager-fetch behavior - it never dispatches `'viewprs:react-ready'` at all (it installs mock bridges synchronously before requiring `index.page.js` instead, so the race never manifests there). Inverting the order (wait for React before fetching, as 7.1 originally proposed) would mean that suite's hundreds of tests never load any data. This coupling means 7.1 can't be cleanly separated from 7.2 (moving the actual PR-data/polling state into React) - deferred until that sub-phase, rather than forcing a fix that would either break the integration suite or reintroduce the same dual-path complexity it's meant to remove.

Instead, continued sub-phase 7.0 with a second, lower-risk cluster: the handful of Layer B functions that come from genuinely zero-argument helper factories - no viewer/payload state involved at all, so these become plain direct ES module imports (no Context needed, unlike actor-identity):

- `isChangedStatus`, `statusClass`, `approvedClass`, `formatTitleWithIcons` (`helpers/pr-status-display.helpers.js`'s `createPrStatusDisplayHelpers()`)
- `escapeHtml`, `formatIsoDatetime`, `toCount` (`helpers/pr-formatting.helpers.js`'s `createPrFormattingHelpers()`)
