- `countPendingThreadComments` - previously a plain function defined *inline* in `index.page.js` (not in any helper module); extracted into a new `helpers/pr-thread-comments.helpers.js` first, matching this codebase's one-concern-per-file convention, then imported directly like the others.

Updated 15 components (`PrStatusCell`, `PrCheckCell`, `PrTitleCell`, `PrAttentionCell`, `PrApprovedCell`, `PrDateCell`, `PrTableApp`, `PrInsightsRow`, `ActionLogSection`, `AuthorCreatedPrsSection`, `AuthorInsightsCommentsSection`, `StatsVisuals`, `GraphCard`, `insights/ActivityEventsSection`, `insights/ApprovalRiskSection`, `insights/LinesChangedInsight`, `insights/ReviewThreadsSection` - some already touched by the actor-identity slice) and their test files. `AuthorInsightsPrDataMeta.jsx` was deliberately left untouched - its own comment already documents deferring this whole cluster as one atomic unit (Track C's concern), so `toCount`/`formatChkDisplay` stay on `index.page.js`'s bridge for that one file's sake; every other consumer has moved off.

**Bugs/gaps caught by verification, fixed as found (not deferred):**
- `AuthorInsightsPrDataMeta.jsx`'s own `formatChkDisplay(chkState, row?.failureCount)` call passes an already-*parsed* CHK state string (from `parseMarkerState`) into a function that expects the *raw* `titleDisplay` string and re-parses it via regex - a pre-existing, latent bug that only ever "worked" because the component's test always stubbed `window.formatChkDisplay` with a differently-shaped mock. Left as-is (out of scope per that file's own deferred-cluster boundary) but noted here since it'll need fixing whenever Track C's cluster is finally tackled.
- Several tests asserted on a *custom-mocked* string shape (e.g. `status-no_change` with an underscore, `fmt(2026-01-01)`) that never matched what the real function actually produces (`status-no-change` with a hyphen; a real formatted date string). These only ever passed because the component read the mock, not the real implementation - now that they read the real one, the assertions were corrected to match real output (or, for genuinely locale/timezone-dependent output like formatted dates, changed to compute the expected value by calling the real helper in the test itself, rather than hardcoding a string that would only be correct in one timezone).

**Verified:** full jest suite (1915 tests, up from 1913 - 2 new tests added for `PrTitleCell`'s marker-stripping and no-pending-comments cases), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (35 tests).

### Sub-phase 7.0, continued: needs-attention classification moved to Context (done)

Moved the last 3 stateful bridges PrTableApp.jsx read directly - `window.entryNeedsAttention`, `window.getNeedsAttentionConfig`, `window.shouldShowNeedsAttention` - onto a new `NeedsAttentionContext` (`state/NeedsAttentionContext.jsx`) fed by a new `NeedsAttentionProvider` (`components/NeedsAttentionProvider.jsx`), mounted around `PrTableApp` in `react-app.jsx`. This also let a real simplification land: `PrTableApp.jsx` previously worked around these fields living in vanilla DOM by bumping an `attentionConfigVersion` counter from a manual `document.addEventListener("change", ...)` listener, purely to give its `sections` `useMemo` something to react to (its own comment called this out as a workaround). Since the "Needs Attention rules" config fields are all already Context-native (Phase 6, `FilterStateProvider`), `attentionConfig` is now a real value the `useMemo` depends on directly - the counter and listener are gone entirely.

**Two bugs found via full-suite/e2e verification, both fixed (not deferred):**

1. **A pre-existing, newly-exposed `sections` `useMemo` had a genuinely dead `effectiveRepo` dependency.** The removed `attentionConfigVersion` line carried a blanket `// eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` that suppressed *every* issue on that dependency array, not just the intentional one - once real, `effectiveRepo` turned out to be read nowhere in the callback (its only use was removed in an earlier refactor, per that code's own comment). Removed the dead dependency once eslint could see it.
2. **A real, non-obvious React reconciliation gap**, caught only by the full jest suite + confirmed independently by a real-browser Playwright run: `NeedsAttentionProvider` sits between `PrDataProvider` and `PrTableApp` as a *stable* `children` element - created once by `AppRoot` (or a test harness) and never recreated when `PrDataProvider`'s own state updates. React's "same children element" reconciliation bailout means a `PrDataProvider`-only state change (e.g. every "Apply filters" click) was silently skipping `NeedsAttentionProvider`'s re-render entirely, even though sibling consumers of `PrDataContext` (like `PrTableApp` itself) updated correctly - console-log instrumentation confirmed `PrDataProvider`'s `setState` firing on every click while `NeedsAttentionProvider` rendered exactly once, at mount. Fixed by having `NeedsAttentionProvider` call `usePrData()` purely to subscribe to `PrDataContext` (its value is otherwise unused) - Context updates punch through the bailout for any real consumer, which forces the needed re-render. Also required teaching `attentionConfig`'s computation a `window.getFilterStateValues()`-with-DOM-fallback read (mirroring `index.page.js`'s own `getFilterStateOverrideForFieldId`/`readAttentionConfigCheckbox` pattern exactly), since the large `index.html.test.js` integration suite simulates these 6 Context-migrated fields with plain static markup rather than mounting the real portaled components - the same reason that suite's own `window.mountReactPrTable` stub needed updating to wrap with the new `NeedsAttentionProvider` too.

**Verified:** full jest suite (1914 tests - net -1 after removing a regression test whose only premise, the deleted `document.addEventListener` filter-by-id logic, no longer exists; its actual regression coverage - smart-group membership updating live - was preserved in a rewritten Context-based version), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (35 tests, including a real-browser exercise of the exact needs-attention/persisted-restore flow this change touched).

### Sub-phase 7.0, continued: review-stats cluster moved to Context (done)

Moved the whole Review Stats tab's aggregation cluster - `statsViewState` (sort/filter/topN/minComments/date-range), `getNormalizedStatsDateRange`, `isWithinStatsDateRange`, `buildReviewerStats`, `applyStatsControls`, `renderActivityTrendNote`, `bucketTimelineChartData`, `aggregateReviewerCommentsTimeline`, `aggregateReviewerApprovalsTimeline` - off ~10 `window.*` bridges and onto a new `ReviewStatsContext`/`ReviewStatsProvider` (mirroring the `NeedsAttentionContext`/`NeedsAttentionProvider` shape). Unlike needs-attention, `statsViewState` didn't need a DOM-fallback trick - `ReviewStatsControls` already owned it as real (if bridge-relayed) React state, so this sub-phase's job was mostly relocating that ownership into `PrDataProvider` (a new `setStatsViewState`) and having `ReviewStatsProvider` genuinely consume `usePrData()` for it - which, as a real Context consumption (not a pass-through), sidesteps the reconciliation-bailout class of bug needs-attention hit, confirmed by both the full jest suite and two dedicated Playwright e2e tests exercising this exact cluster end-to-end.

**Found and fixed along the way (not deferred):**
- `ReviewStatsControls`'s old `onChange` bridge (`window.updateStatsViewStateAndRerender`) had the side effect of calling `applyFiltersFromCache()` - re-running the *entire PR table's local-filter pipeline* - on every Review Stats control change, for no reason connected to Review Stats itself (Review Stats renders from its own `usePrData()` subscription, independent of that pipeline). Writing straight to Context drops this unnecessary work for free.
- `index.page.trends.test.js` reached `aggregateReviewerActivityTimeline` only via `index.page.js`'s own `__testables` export (a DI-wired instance closing over its module state) - now that the function doesn't exist there at all, rewrote all 9 of its test cases to call `pr-review-stats-timeline.helpers.js`'s factory directly (with the same real `isWithinStatsDateRange` logic index.page.js used to supply), preserving every assertion unchanged.
- `ReviewerActivityChart.jsx`'s own crude fallback bucketing (used only when `window.bucketTimelineChartData` was unset) is now dead code, since `useReviewStats()`'s default context value already provides the *real* bucketing function even when unwrapped - removed it, and updated its tests to supply an explicit one-bucket-per-date Context override where they specifically want that simplified shape (they test chart rendering/interaction, not the bucketing algorithm).
- `normalizeRowMetrics` (from the same aggregation factory) turned out to have a second, unrelated consumer (`getOpenConversationCount`, used by the "More insights" panel) - confirmed it needs none of the review-stats-specific DI params before simplifying the factory call down to just `{ toCount, asArray }` rather than deleting it outright.

**Verified:** full jest suite (1914 tests), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (35 tests, including the two Review Stats-specific tests exercising this cluster in a real browser).

**Sub-phase 7.0 status: complete.** Re-audited the filter-panel cluster (`pr-filter-panel.helpers.js`'s `populateXOptions` functions, previously listed above as the last untouched piece) before starting it and found it doesn't actually fit sub-phase 7.0's pattern: none of its exports (`getSelectedAuthorLogins`, `populateAuthorOptions`, etc.) are ever exposed on `window.*` for a React component to read - they're called only from index.page.js's own internal render-pipeline orchestration, never by React directly. Its one real state dependency, the 9 `pendingXFilterSelections` values, already moved onto `FilterStateProvider`'s Context back in Phase 6, Slice 7 (a prior session, predating this Phase 7 effort) via the same `getFilterStateValues`/`setFilterStateValue` generic bridge every other Phase 6 field uses - the module-scope `let pendingXxx` variables are just Phase 6's established fallback-when-Context-hasn't-mounted mirror, not an un-migrated Layer B bridge. So there's nothing left here matching "a window.* bridge a React component reads" - folding `populateXOptions` itself into React natively is really sub-phase 7.4's job (filter state and persistence), not 7.0's.

With that, every cluster matching sub-phase 7.0's original definition (actor-identity, the zero-dependency pure-function cluster, needs-attention, review-stats, author insights) is done. Sub-phases 7.1-7.7 (the rest of the single-entry-point work) remain undone - see the next section for 7.2 (the actual next actionable sub-phase, since 7.1 is coupled to it).

### Pre-existing bug found while auditing the author-insights cluster (fixed, 2026-09-27)

While researching `authorInsightsState`'s cross-dependency with the auto-render-blocking system (ahead of migrating this cluster), found that `index.page.js`'s `getAutoRenderBlockingState()` calls `getBlockingAuthorInsightsLogins()` with **no arguments**, but `pr-auto-render-blocking.helpers.js`'s real function takes `authorInsightsState` as its own parameter - passed through bare (an object-shorthand property) rather than wrapped in a closure supplying the live state, so it silently defaulted to `{}` on every call. Net effect: an incoming poll was **never actually deferred** for an unsaved Author Insights manual-comment draft (composer or edit-in-progress) - only for unsaved PR-notes-section fields, a separate dirty-tracking path that was wired correctly. This is a genuine, pre-existing bug, unrelated to this session's other work - not caught by `pr-auto-render-blocking.helpers.test.js`'s own unit tests, since those call the function directly with an explicit, correct `authorInsightsState` argument and never exercised the actual index.page.js wiring between it and `getAutoRenderBlockingState()`.

Fixed by wrapping the DI param in a closure: `getBlockingAuthorInsightsLogins: () => getBlockingAuthorInsightsLogins(authorInsightsState)`. No integration test currently exercises this end-to-end (would need to simulate an in-progress Author Insights comment draft *and* a concurrent poll landing) - given this whole subsystem is the very next thing sub-phase 7.0 needs to migrate to React anyway, adding bespoke coverage for the current vanilla wiring wasn't judged worth it right before that rewrite; revisit if the author-insights migration doesn't happen soon.

**Verified:** full jest suite (1914 tests, unchanged - nothing previously exercised the fixed path), `npx eslint .` clean, full Playwright smoke suite (35 tests).

### Sub-phase 7.0, continued: author-insights cluster moved to Context (done)

Moved the Author Insights tab's display/formatting/sorting cluster off ~14 `window.*` bridges and onto a new `AuthorInsightsContext`/`AuthorInsightsProvider` (same shape as `NeedsAttentionContext`/`ReviewStatsContext`): `getAuthorInsightsDisplayName`, `noteAuthorMatchesSelection`, `buildAuthorInsightsEntries`, `getAuthorInsightsSentimentLabel`/`BadgeClassName`, `getAuthorInsightsStatusBadgeClassName`, `getAuthorInsightsCreatedPrStatus`, the 3 `sortAuthorInsightsXDesc` functions, `getAuthorInsightsNoteDisplayTimestamp`, `normalizeAuthorInsightsSentiment`, `DEFAULT_AUTHOR_INSIGHTS_SENTIMENT`, `AUTHOR_COMMENT_SENTIMENT_OPTIONS`, and `getOpenConversationCount`. Confirmed via each helper module's own DI signature that none of this actually needs `authorInsightsState` itself - only a "get the current actors map" callback, which `PrDataContext`'s `payload.actorsMap` already satisfies (`authorInsightsState.latestActorsMap` was just a redundant cache of the same data). The manual-comment draft/cache CRUD functions (`getAuthorInsightsComposerDraft`, `updateAuthorInsightsEditDraft`, `getAuthorManualCommentsForLogin`, `saveAuthorManualComment`, etc.) genuinely do need `authorInsightsState` - a real mutable per-author cache the auto-render-blocking system also reads directly - so those stay on their existing `window.*` bridges as a separate, not-yet-migrated concern, still read by `AuthorInsightsCommentsSection.jsx`.

`AuthorInsightsProvider` is mounted individually around each of the 5 portaled Author Insights sections (`AuthorInsightsSelector`, `AuthorCreatedPrsSection`, `AuthorInsightsHeader`, `AuthorInsightsNotesSection`, `AuthorInsightsCommentsSection`) plus `AutoRenderBlockedLinks`, matching the per-portal wrapping style `ReviewStatsProvider` already uses - it genuinely consumes `usePrData()`/`useActorIdentity()` for its own derivation, so (unlike the original `NeedsAttentionProvider`) it's not at risk of the stable-children reconciliation bailout documented in the needs-attention writeup above.

Two PR-table-level (not Author-Insights-tab) consumers of functions in this cluster were converted to plain direct imports instead of `useAuthorInsights()`, since both are genuinely stateless and importing directly avoids requiring the whole PR table subtree to be wrapped in a Provider it doesn't otherwise need:

- `insights/ReviewThreadsSection.jsx`'s `parseSortableTime` - extracted from an inline `index.page.js` function into a new `helpers/pr-sortable-time.helpers.js` (index.page.js's own inline copy was then deleted in favor of importing the same module, removing a duplicate).
- `insights/NotesSection.jsx`'s `noteAuthorMatchesSelection` - built locally via `useMemo` from `pr-author-insights-identity.helpers.js`'s factory, with `normalizeActorLogin`/`resolveActorDisplayName` threaded in from `useActorIdentity()` rather than the factory's viewer-unaware fallback defaults, so alias resolution stays consistent with the rest of the app.

**Bug found and fixed (not deferred):** `AuthorInsightsContext.jsx`'s default value builder called `createPrAuthorInsightsDisplayHelpers()` with no arguments at all - unlike `AuthorInsightsProvider`'s real value, which correctly threads `normalizeAuthorInsightsSentiment`/`isChangedStatus`/`asArray`/`parseSortableTime`/`formatIsoDatetime`/actor-identity functions through. That factory has no internal safe-default fallback for any of its DI params (unlike most of this codebase's factories), so every consumer relying on the default Context value (i.e. every test that doesn't explicitly override it) hit a `normalizeAuthorInsightsSentiment is not a function` `TypeError` the moment `getAuthorInsightsSentimentBadgeClassName`/`getAuthorInsightsSentimentLabel` rendered. Fixed by building the default value from the same real dependencies `AuthorInsightsProvider` uses (`defaultActorIdentity`'s functions for actor resolution, plus the other zero-dependency factories already established in this migration).

Updated 8 test files (`AuthorInsightsSelector`, `AuthorCreatedPrsSection`, `AuthorInsightsNotesSection`, `AuthorInsightsCommentsSection`, `AuthorInsightsPrDataMeta`, `AutoRenderBlockedLinks`, `insights/NotesSection`, `insights/ReviewThreadsSection`) from `window.x`/`delete window.x` stubs to `AuthorInsightsContext.Provider` value overrides (or plain function args, for the two direct-import cases) - several of these tests had been silently passing against dead `window.*` stubs the components no longer read (the real default Context value happened to produce equivalent output for the specific fixtures used), so they were rewritten to actually exercise the new wiring rather than left as coincidentally-green.

Removed all 14 now-dead `window.*` bridge assignments from `index.page.js` (confirmed zero remaining non-test consumers via a project-wide grep first), plus the now-unused `DEFAULT_AUTHOR_INSIGHTS_SENTIMENT` destructured binding. `prAuthorInsightsDisplayHelpers` itself stays - it's still passed into `renderAuthorInsights` (vanilla's own not-yet-migrated selection/validation logic).

**Bug found via post-hoc self-review and fixed (not deferred):** `AuthorInsightsProvider`'s single `useMemo` bundled every returned function together, keyed on `[payload, normalizeActorLogin, resolveActorDisplayName, getPreferredActorKey]` - but most of those functions (`sortAuthorInsightsManualCommentsDesc`, the sentiment/status formatters, `DEFAULT_AUTHOR_INSIGHTS_SENTIMENT`, etc.) don't actually use `payload` or actor identity at all, so they got a fresh reference every time `payload` changed (e.g. on every poll tick), not just when their own actual inputs changed. `AuthorInsightsCommentsSection.jsx`'s per-author refresh effect has `sortAuthorInsightsManualCommentsDesc` in its dependency array (required by `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` once it became a per-render Context value) - so an unrelated poll landing while a user had a comment open for editing silently reset `editingCommentId` to `null`, closing the edit form out from under them. Fixed by hoisting the payload/identity-independent functions to module scope (built once, not per-render), leaving only `getAuthorInsightsDisplayName`/`noteAuthorMatchesSelection`/`buildAuthorInsightsEntries` - the 3 that genuinely need `payload`/actor identity - in the per-render `useMemo`. Added a regression test (`AuthorInsightsCommentsSection.test.jsx`) that mounts the real `AuthorInsightsProvider` (not a static Context mock, which can't reproduce this class of bug), opens a comment for editing, fires `window.updateReactPrTable` with unrelated payload data, and asserts the edit form stays open - confirmed it fails against the pre-fix code (edit form closes) and passes against the fix.

**Verified:** full jest suite (1915 tests, +1 for the regression test above), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (35 tests, including the 5 Author Insights-specific tests exercising this cluster in a real browser).

## 🚧 **Sub-phase 7.2, revised scope: `pollForDataChanges` extraction + centralized PR-data write path (2026-09-29)**

Sub-phase 7.2 was originally scoped (see the saved plan file) as "fold PR-data state and polling into React natively" - moving `latestStoredPayload`/`latestSelectedRepo` fully into `PrDataProvider`, wrapping `pollForDataChanges` in a `usePrDataPolling` hook, and deleting `mountReactPrTable`/`updateReactPrTable`/`getReactPrTablePayload`/`renderPrData`/`pushPayloadToReactTable`/`reactTableMounted`. Deep-reading the actual code before starting found two things the original 3-agent audit didn't catch at the line level:

1. `renderPrData` (`index.page.js`) is not a thin "push data to React" wrapper - it's the entry point for the entire vanilla side-effect pipeline (data-meta summary, filter chips, export catalog, author insights panel, stats view, and populating all 9 filter dropdowns via `deriveRenderPipelineState`). None of that has moved to React yet (sub-phases 7.3/7.4). It can't be deleted until those land.
2. `src/ui/integration-tests/index.html.test.js` calls `window.pollForDataChanges()` directly at 17 call sites and never mounts `<PrDataPolling>`. Moving the polling logic fully into a React hook and deleting the vanilla function would break this suite outright.

Given this, the full original scope isn't safely achievable in one session. Implemented a smaller, safely-scoped slice instead (see the saved plan file's "Sub-phase 7.2, revised scope" section for the full research trail, including a dedicated Plan-agent review that verified these findings and evaluated alternatives):

**Piece 1 - pure extraction of `pollForDataChanges` into a DI-factory module.** Its ~165-line body moved byte-for-byte (no logic changes) into a new `helpers/pr-data-polling-orchestration.helpers.js`, following this codebase's established `createXHelpers({...deps})` pattern (e.g. `pr-single-pr-update.helpers.js`). Every module-level variable the function used to close over directly (`supportsDataMetaPolling`, `supportsDataManifestPolling`, `lastSeenDataVersion`, `latestPrManifest`, `lastRenderedPrFingerprint`, `lastRenderedMetaFingerprint`, `lastRenderedRunStamp`, `hasDirtyPrSectionsFields`, `pendingAutoRenderPayload`) is now an injected getter/setter pair; the pure fingerprint/manifest-delta/render-decision functions it calls (`computePrDataManifest`, `getManifestDelta`, `mergeDataDeltaPayload`, `computePrDataFingerprint`, `computePrDataMetaFingerprint`, `getDataPollRenderAction`) are injected from `index.page.js`'s existing `pr-data-polling.helpers.js` instance (already wired with the real `getOrComputeEntryDerivedValue` cache) rather than duplicated. `index.page.js`'s own `pollForDataChanges` is now a thin wire-up calling the new factory - same name, same `window.pollForDataChanges` assignment, so `index.html.test.js`'s 17 direct calls needed zero changes. Added `pr-data-polling-orchestration.helpers.test.js` (9 tests) unit-testing the factory directly, covering the meta-unchanged/404-fallback/manifest-delta-unchanged/manifest-delta-changed/skip-render/queue-render-and-listen/normal-render/fetch-failure branches.

**Piece 2 - centralized the `latestStoredPayload`/`latestSelectedRepo` write path.** Added `applyLatestPrData({ payload, selectedRepo })` (next to the two variables' own declarations) that all 8 former independent write sites now call instead of assigning the pair directly - closing the actual highest-risk gap the original audit flagged ("no request-generation guard beyond content fingerprinting" for a pair written non-atomically from 8+ places). `undefined` for either field leaves it unchanged, so every call site's own existing fallback logic (e.g. `payload.repo || latestSelectedRepo`) is computed by the caller exactly as before and just passed in, rather than being reimplemented. One site needed care, not just mechanical substitution: `toggleInReviewForRow`/`toggleFlaggedForRow`'s "minimal response" branch deliberately never calls `renderPrData()` (a documented perf fix from an earlier session - the checkbox UI is already correct without a full re-render) - `applyLatestPrData` only ever updates state, never decides whether to render, so that behavior is fully preserved.

**Deliberately not done this slice** (deferred to a future session, pending a decision on how to handle `index.html.test.js`'s direct-call dependency - see the saved plan file's "open question"): building a real `usePrDataPolling` hook, switching `PrDataPolling.jsx`'s interval away from `window.pollForDataChanges`, or touching `mountReactPrTable`/`updateReactPrTable`/`getReactPrTablePayload`/`renderPrData`/`pushPayloadToReactTable`/`reactTableMounted` - all confirmed still load-bearing for the not-yet-migrated side-effect pipeline.

**Verified:** full jest suite (1924 tests, +9 for the new orchestration-helper tests), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (35 tests), plus `index.html.test.js` run in isolation (98 tests) to specifically confirm its direct `window.pollForDataChanges()` calls still behave identically.

## 🚧 **Sub-phase 7.3, revised scope: checkbox/ack/apply-label action extraction (2026-09-29, second session of the day)**

Sub-phase 7.3 was originally scoped as "move user-triggered actions into hooks" - ack/flag toggle, run-script, apply-label, backfill start/stop, request-more-merged, trigger-auto-run, and quick-check all becoming real React hooks writing directly to `PrDataProvider`. Research (2 parallel Explore agents) found this splits into two very different states, plus one genuine architectural blocker for the checkbox/ack/apply-label path specifically:

- `runSinglePrUpdate`, `handleRequestMoreMerged`, and `handleBackfillAction` are **already** fully delegated to their own DI-factory helper modules with existing unit test coverage - no extraction work needed for these when the hook-based rewrite eventually happens.
- `toggleInReviewForRow`/`toggleFlaggedForRow`/`runAckOnlyWorkflow`/`runClearOnlyWorkflow`/`runApplyLabelWorkflow` (wired through `react-callbacks.helpers.js`) were still inline in `index.page.js` and have **zero Jest-level coverage** - only `e2e/smoke.spec.js` exercises the real chain. Worse, `createReactCallbacks()` has a documented, Playwright-confirmed reason it reads `latestStoredPayload` as a bare module variable instead of through `PrDataProvider`'s Context: these handlers mutate it and read it back *synchronously in the same call* to push the fresh value into React, which `setState` can't satisfy (a prior attempt produced a real regression - the checkbox visually reverted right after the click). Building a genuine hook-based replacement here with no unit-test safety net would repeat exactly the kind of blind rewrite this migration's own discipline exists to avoid.
- `handleTriggerAutoRun`/`handleQuickCheck` are inline but simple (no `latestStoredPayload` mutation) and already tested via RTL mounting the real button components - deferred, since they depend on `notifyFailureSnackbar`/`showErrorNotification`'s callers moving into the React tree, which is sub-phase 7.6's concern.

Implemented the same kind of slice as 7.2's Piece 1: a pure, zero-behavior-change extraction, closing the Jest-coverage gap before any hook-based rewrite is attempted.

**`helpers/pr-row-checkbox-actions.helpers.js`** - `toggleInReviewForRow`/`toggleFlaggedForRow`'s bodies, moved verbatim. These two are structurally near-identical but were **not** deduplicated: re-reading both in full surfaced real grammatical differences in their status messages ("Enabling in-review for #X" vs "Flagging #X" - the flagged variant never says "for"), so a shared template risked a subtle wording bug in what's meant to be a zero-risk extraction. Kept as two separate functions; deduplicating them is a deliberate future follow-up, not bundled into this pass.

**`helpers/pr-ack-label-actions.helpers.js`** - `runAckAction`, `runAckOnlyWorkflow`, `runClearOnlyWorkflow`, `runApplyLabelAction`, `runApplyLabelWorkflow`, all in one module since they form one cohesive DI-factory instance (the workflow functions call the action functions internally, same as before). `runAckOnlyWorkflow`/`runClearOnlyWorkflow`/`runApplyLabelWorkflow` each have two callers preserved unchanged: the React checkbox/apply-label-select path via `react-callbacks.helpers.js`, and the vanilla "Run & Filter" tab's own form buttons (`handleAckOnly`/`handleClearOnly`/`handleApplyLabelClick`, still in `index.page.js`, now calling the same-named thin wrappers).

Both `index.page.js` wire-ups are thin: same function names, same `window.*`/DI call sites elsewhere in the file (notably `createReactCallbacks()`'s wiring), unchanged. `runAckAction`/`runApplyLabelAction` aren't re-exported at the `index.page.js` level at all - nothing outside the new factory ever called them directly (only the workflow functions did), so eslint's `no-unused-vars` correctly flagged keeping them destructured as dead weight.

Added `pr-row-checkbox-actions.helpers.test.js` (9 tests) and `pr-ack-label-actions.helpers.test.js` (13 tests) - the first Jest-level coverage this exact code path has had, covering the minimal-delta/full-payload/no-data-fallback/failure/exception branches for checkbox toggles, and the success/warnings/auth-failure/network-failure/validation branches for ack/clear/apply-label.

**Deliberately not done this slice:** `react-callbacks.helpers.js`, the `window.handleCheckboxChange`/`handleAckAction`/`handleApplyLabel` bridges, `handleTriggerAutoRun`/`handleQuickCheck`, and the three already-delegated handlers - all confirmed out of scope for the reasons above. The actual hook-based rewrite (deferred to a future session) will very likely find the synchronous-read-after-write workaround isn't needed at all once these become genuine React event handlers calling `PrDataProvider`'s own setter directly - worth confirming that reasoning before committing to the design.

**Verified:** full jest suite (1946 tests, +22 for the two new test files), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (35 tests, including the checkbox-toggle e2e test that exercises this exact extracted path), plus `index.html.test.js` run in isolation (98 tests).

## 🚧 **Sub-phase 7.4: multi-select filter options to React, plus a real persistence-gap fix (2026-09-30)**

Revisited sub-phase 7.2 first (data polling) and found its remaining scope (a real `usePrDataPolling` hook) still isn't safely achievable: most of the polling state (`lastSeenDataVersion`, `latestPrManifest`, `pendingAutoRenderPayload`, `hasDirtyPrSectionsFields`) is shared with the not-yet-migrated auto-render-blocking system (sub-phase 7.5) - a hook owning this state privately would fork it from the vanilla copy. Left 7.2 as-is and moved to sub-phase 7.4 (filter state and persistence) instead, per the user's explicit choice.

Research found filter **values** are already fully on `FilterStateContext` (Phase 6) - every actual control is a React component. What wasn't migrated: the **option lists** for the 9 multi-select filter fields (author/assigned/approver/label/exclude-label/thread-resolution allow-deny/change-filter ignore-comment-review-authors), still computed imperatively by vanilla `populateXOptions` functions and pushed into React via an imperative `window.renderReactMultiSelectList` bridge.

**Moved option computation to React:**
- New `state/FilterOptionsContext.jsx` + `components/FilterOptionsProvider.jsx` (matching `ReviewStatsContext`/`ReviewStatsProvider`'s established shape) - derives all 9 option lists via `useMemo` straight from `PrDataContext`'s payload/`ActorIdentityContext`, moving the dedup/sort logic out of the deleted vanilla functions verbatim. The 4 actor-map-based lists (thread-resolution allow/deny, change-filter ignore-comment/review-authors) are genuinely identical derivations in the deleted code (same `actorsMap`, same sort) - computed once as `actorOptions` and shared across all 4, rather than recomputing the same result 4 times.
- New `helpers/pr-filter-label-extraction.helpers.js` - extracted `extractRowLabelNames`/`normalizeFilterToken`/`getLabelName` out of `index.page.js` (previously inline, not in any importable module), so both `index.page.js` and the new Provider import the same logic.
- New `components/MultiSelectListPortals.jsx` (extracted into its own module rather than inlined in `react-app.jsx`, specifically so `index.html.test.js`'s own test harness - which never loads `react-app.jsx` itself, since that file auto-mounts `<AppRoot/>` as an import-time side effect - can import and reuse this exact component instead of hand-duplicating its logic a second time, matching this sub-phase's own "extract once, reuse" plan). Replaces the old `window.renderReactMultiSelectList` bridge/`multiSelectStates` state: for each of the 9 lists, reads currently-checked DOM values, seeds `checked` via new `helpers/pr-multi-select-checked-state.helpers.js`'s `seedCheckedState` (existing-checked-wins-over-pending, same as the deleted vanilla functions), and reuses the existing `createMultiSelectRenderCache` (moved, not reimplemented) so an unchanged list still doesn't force a remount on every poll tick.
- Deleted from `pr-filter-panel.helpers.js`: `populateIncludeLabelOptions`/`populateExcludeLabelOptions`/`populateAuthorOptions`/`populateAssignedOptions`/`populateApproverOptions`/`collectSortedLabelOptions`, and all their now-dead DI params - kept `getSelectedXLogins`/the 6 "Any" filter getters/`renderManagementFilterSummary`/`setupMultiSelectDropdownClosing` (different concern, untouched). Deleted from `index.page.js`: the 2 inline `populateAuthorThreadResolutionActorOptions`/`populateChangeFilterActorOptions` functions and `renderMultiSelectListSkipUnchanged` (now fully dead there). `pr-filter-options.helpers.js`'s `populateFilterOptions` is now a permanent no-op shell, called with zero arguments - its caller (`deriveViewerFilterSetup`, part of `renderPrData`'s still-vanilla pipeline) can't be deleted yet per sub-phase 7.2's own finding, so this stays wired in as an inert call target, a candidate for outright removal in sub-phase 7.7's final cleanup.

**A genuine architectural gap found and solved, not just a test-compatibility workaround:** the 9 lists' restore-time "pending selections" used to live in a single module-scope variable inside `index.page.js` (readable by the vanilla `populateXOptions` functions in the same file). Once option computation moved to React (a separate ES module with no shared closure scope), that channel no longer worked. Routing pending selections through `FilterStateProvider`'s generic `window.getFilterStateValues`/`window.setFilterStateValue` bridge was rejected - that bridge's mere existence flips every OTHER migrated field's restore behavior too (`setFilterStateOverrideForFieldId` skips a field's DOM-mutation fallback the moment `window.setFilterStateValue` exists, regardless of which key is being written), which would have broken ~25 unrelated restore paths. Built a dedicated new module, `helpers/pr-pending-multi-select-selections.helpers.js`, backed by a window-level store instead: `getPendingMultiSelectSelection`/`setPendingMultiSelectSelection`, plus `subscribeToPendingMultiSelectSelections` - a write doesn't cause a React re-render by itself (a plain object mutation), and a real, reproducing integration-test failure during this slice confirmed the obvious alternative ("the next unrelated `renderPrData`/payload-driven re-render will naturally pick it up") doesn't actually work: `renderPrData` re-running with the *same* payload object reference doesn't change `FilterOptionsProvider`'s `useMemo` dependency, so `filterOptions` never gets a new reference to trigger `MultiSelectListPortals`'s effect. The subscribe callback bumps a local tick state instead, forcing a deterministic re-seed on every pending-selection write, regardless of timing.

**A real, independently-fixable bug found and fixed:** the 6 `FilterOptionSelect` "Any (with/without)" fields (`filterCustomComments`/`filterOtherNotes`/`filterPrDifficulty`/`filterRallyStories`/`filterRallyLinks`/`filterAnalysisOfPr`) were missing from **both** `persistUiOptionOverrides` and `restoreUiOptionOverrides` (not just persist, as first suspected - checked both directly) - a real, undocumented gap, not the same thing as their separate, deliberate exclusion from auto-apply-on-change (`react-app.jsx`'s `FILTER_OPTION_SELECT_FIELDS` comment documents that exclusion explicitly; this persistence gap had no such documentation anywhere). Fixed by adding all 6 to `getUiOptionDefaults()`, `FILTER_STATE_FIELD_MAP`, `persistUiOptionOverrides`'s `textIds`, `restoreUiOptionOverrides`'s matching restore list, and `persistViewFilterOptionOverrides`'s field list - all 5 already-generic call sites, no new logic needed. Left the apply-on-select-vs-"Apply filters (local)"-button UX inconsistency (`ROADMAP.md`) untouched, as planned - this fix only changes whether a value survives a reload, not when it takes effect.

**A real `flushSync` reentrancy bug found via a real-browser Playwright run (not caught by jsdom):** `MultiSelectListPortals`'s initial implementation copied `window.renderReactMultiSelectList`'s old `flushSync(() => setRenderState(...))` pattern verbatim. In the old code this was safe - the bridge was called synchronously from *vanilla* code outside any React lifecycle. In the new code the same call happens *inside* a `useEffect`, and when that effect fires as part of a parent component's own commit (confirmed via 3 real `smoke.spec.js` failures showing "Warning: flushSync was called from inside a lifecycle method"), it's a genuine reentrancy bug. Audited every reader of these lists' checked values (`getSelectedAuthorLogins`/`persistUiOptionOverrides`/`getFormBody`/etc.) and confirmed none of them run synchronously right after this effect any more - they're all separate, later, user-triggered events - so the same-tick guarantee `flushSync` existed for doesn't apply here. Fixed by using a plain `setRenderState` call instead.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2021 tests, +2 for this session), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (37 tests, re-run after the `flushSync` fix specifically), plus `index.html.test.js` run in isolation (104 tests, +2 for the new persistence-gap round-trip coverage).

## 🚧 **Sub-phase 7.5: PR Notes dirty-state to Context, revised scope (2026-09-30)**

Next in Phase 7's own sequence: the "auto-render-blocking" system, which defers an incoming poll's re-render while the user has unsaved work in progress (a PR Notes field edit, or an Author Insights manual-comment draft), showing an indicator with links to jump to the unsaved field. Research (2 Explore agents + 1 Plan agent, re-verified directly against source) found this splits into pieces of very different risk, the same shape as sub-phases 7.2/7.3's own "attempt the full thing, find the real entanglement, land a smaller safe slice" pattern - so this sub-phase lands only the PR Notes piece.

**PR Notes' dirty signal was a clean migration.** `NotesSection.jsx`'s `hasChanges` is computed in the render body but was only ever reported outward via a `useEffect([hasChanges])` (always post-commit, never a same-tick read) plus a `data-has-unsaved-notes` DOM attribute vanilla code scanned for. New `state/NotesDirtyContext.jsx` + `components/NotesDirtyProvider.jsx` (closer in shape to `FilterStateProvider.jsx` than `NeedsAttentionProvider.jsx`, since it genuinely *owns* writable state descendants call a setter on, rather than only deriving a value from upstream Context - no reconciliation-bailout trick needed, since the state change originates inside the Provider itself). Holds a `Set` of dirty PR numbers, not a single boolean - confirmed via `PrTableApp.test.jsx` that `expandedInsights` is a composite-key map, so multiple `NotesSection` instances can be dirty simultaneously, and the deleted `getUnsavedNotesSections()` already handled that correctly; a single boolean would have been a real regression. Publishes `window.getDirtyNotesPrNumbers()` - a brand-new, single-purpose bridge, not `FilterStateProvider`'s generic one (whose mere existence changes ~25 unrelated fields' restore behavior, per sub-phase 7.4's own finding - no such risk here, since nothing else reads or writes this name).

`pr-auto-render-unsaved.helpers.js`'s `getUnsavedNotesSections` (DOM-element based) became `getUnsavedNotesPrNumbers` (PR-number-string based, fed by the new bridge); `getFirstUnsavedElementForPrNumber`'s Notes-branch simplified to a direct `data-pr-number` lookup instead of filtering through the old attribute. `pr-auto-render-state.helpers.js`'s matching DI param renamed to match. The sibling `getDirtyTrackedFields()`/`data-original-value` mechanism was confirmed fully dead in production (zero producers anywhere) during this audit - left alone, unrelated cleanup.

**Explicitly deferred, with concrete reasons (not just "seems hard"):**
- **Author Insights draft dirty-state** → real React state: `authorInsightsState` is mutated and read back *synchronously in the same event-handler tick* (not an effect) by 3 independent consumers (`pr-auto-render-blocking.helpers.js`, `pr-auto-render-navigation.helpers.js`, `AuthorInsightsCommentsSection.jsx` itself) - the same regression class sub-phase 7.3 explicitly avoided for `latestStoredPayload`'s synchronous read-after-write requirement. Revisit only alongside a `react-callbacks.helpers.js`-style rewrite.
- **The render-blocking enforcement itself** (`pendingAutoRenderPayload`/`hasDirtyPrSectionsFields`, `getPendingAutoRenderAction`/`getDataPollRenderAction`, `flushPendingAutoRender*`) - still entangled with `renderPrData`'s still-vanilla side-effect pipeline (sub-phase 7.2's own finding, re-confirmed here) - can't become a real hook until that pipeline moves. It's otherwise untouched; it just reads the Notes dirty count from the new bridge instead of a DOM scan.
- **`AutoRenderBlockedLinks.jsx`'s `window.navigateToPrInTable`/`navigateToAuthorInsights` bridges** - entangled with still-vanilla tab-switching and Author Insights rendering, not a Context-readiness problem; moving them now would be pure code motion with no risk reduction.

**New coverage closing a real gap:** no test previously exercised the actual wiring between a real `NotesSection` instance and the vanilla bridge end-to-end (`NotesSection.test.jsx` only ever stubbed `window.recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields`) - the same class of gap that let the `getBlockingAuthorInsightsLogins` bare-shorthand-property bug (sub-phase 7.0) go unnoticed. Added `components/NotesDirtyProvider.test.jsx` (4 tests, mounting the real Provider + real `NotesSection`) and a new Playwright test (`e2e/smoke.spec.js`, since this feature had zero prior e2e coverage) confirming the same wiring works in a real browser.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2032 tests, +11), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (38 tests, +1 new), plus `index.html.test.js` run in isolation (104 tests, unchanged count - `NotesDirtyProvider` wired into its existing `mountReactPrTable` stub alongside `NeedsAttentionProvider`).

## 🚧 **Sub-phase 7.6: extract handleTriggerAutoRun/handleQuickCheck(All) into DI-factory helpers (2026-10-03)**

Next in Phase 7's sequence: "remaining call-site cleanup." Its one documented pointer (line 1290, from sub-phase 7.3's own writeup) turned out to be **stale**: it claimed `handleTriggerAutoRun`/`handleQuickCheck` were blocked on "`notifyFailureSnackbar`/`showErrorNotification`'s callers moving into the React tree" - but `Snackbar.jsx`, `TriggerAutoRunButton.jsx`, and `QuickCheckButton.jsx` were already created and portaled into the live React tree in an earlier commit (`2c05d8b`), *before* that sentence was even written. Same class of finding as sub-phase 7.4/7.5's "renderPrData blocks everything" framing turning out to be partly outdated after closer inspection - re-verified directly against source (1 Explore agent) rather than taken at face value.

The actual, narrower, accurate scope: these 3 handlers (`index.page.js`) were already wired to real React buttons, but their own fetch/try-catch workflow bodies were still inline, unlike every sibling action already extracted into a dedicated `createXHelpers({...deps})` DI-factory module with its own unit tests (`pr-row-checkbox-actions.helpers.js`, `pr-ack-label-actions.helpers.js`, `pr-single-pr-update.helpers.js`, `pr-merged-request-more-action.helpers.js`). Confirmed via grep: no dedicated unit test exercised their own branching logic before this - only `index.html.test.js`'s integration suite (real button clicks against mocked fetch) and the button components' own RTL tests (which mock the callback prop entirely, never reaching the real fetch logic).

Pure "move the body, add the tests" extraction, zero behavior change:

- `handleQuickCheck`/`handleQuickCheckAll` → extended the existing `pr-quick-check-actions.helpers.js` (already home to `buildQuickCheckOutcome`/`collectAllLoadedPrsByRepo`/`toRepoRequests` from the earlier "Quick Check All" feature work) with `runQuickCheckWorkflow`/`runQuickCheckAllWorkflow`, moved verbatim from their respective handler bodies. New DI params: `postJson`, `getFormBody`, `getLatestStoredPayload` (a getter, since `latestStoredPayload` is a mutable module variable - same pattern `pr-ack-label-actions.helpers.js` already uses for it), `markPrsBusy`/`clearPrsBusy` (previously read as bare `window.markPrsBusy?.(...)` inside the inline body - now injected with safe no-op defaults, matching this module's existing DI discipline for every other dependency).
- `handleTriggerAutoRun` → new `pr-trigger-auto-run-action.helpers.js` (a standalone single-action module, matching `pr-single-pr-update.helpers.js`/`pr-merged-request-more-action.helpers.js`'s precedent, since it's an unrelated action from quick-check).
- `index.page.js`'s own `handleTriggerAutoRun`/`handleQuickCheck`/`handleQuickCheckAll` are now one-line wrappers calling into these, preserving the exact same `window.handleX = (...args) => handleX(...args)` bridge shape every sibling handler in this file uses.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2047 tests, +15: 15 new tests for `runQuickCheckWorkflow`/`runQuickCheckAllWorkflow` plus the new `pr-trigger-auto-run-action.helpers.test.js`), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (38 tests, including the existing real-browser click-through of all 3 buttons - proves zero behavior change), plus `index.html.test.js` run in isolation (104 tests, unchanged).

## 🚧 **Sub-phase 7.3 follow-up: removed the synchronous `latestStoredPayload` readback via return values (2026-10-03)**

Sub-phase 7.3's own writeup deferred turning `handleCheckboxChange`/`handleAckAction`/`handleApplyLabel` (`react-callbacks.helpers.js`) into real React-native logic, citing a documented, Playwright-confirmed regression: these three handlers mutate `latestStoredPayload` and then read it back *synchronously in the same call*, to push the just-mutated value into React - a `setState`-backed Context read can't satisfy that (Context only updates *after* the read, so reading it synchronously hands back the pre-mutation value - exactly what broke previously: "the checkbox visually reverted right after the click").

Research (1 Explore agent, re-verified directly against source - read `react-callbacks.helpers.js`, `pr-row-checkbox-actions.helpers.js`, and `pr-ack-label-actions.helpers.js` in full before designing anything, given the prior-regression history) found the blocker's *mechanism* is real, but the conclusion that a shared module variable is required was stale: the vanilla action functions already compute the new payload into a local variable before writing it to `latestStoredPayload` - they just didn't return it. Nothing else read `latestStoredPayload` again in the same synchronous stack before a yield point; the only consumer of the readback was the immediate React push itself.

**Fix: every exit point of the underlying vanilla action functions now returns `{payload, selectedRepo}` on success or `null` on failure**, instead of writing only to the shared variable:
- `toggleInReviewForRow`/`toggleFlaggedForRow` (`pr-row-checkbox-actions.helpers.js`) - all 6 exit points per function (missing-PR-number guard, failed response, minimal-delta success, full-prData success, fallback-reload success, catch block).
- `runSingleAckRequest`/`runChunkedAckRequest`/`runAckAction`/`runAckOnlyWorkflow`/`runClearOnlyWorkflow` and their apply-label mirrors (`pr-ack-label-actions.helpers.js`) - same shape throughout, propagated via `return await ...` through each `try`/`finally`. Needed one new DI dependency (`getLatestStoredPayload`) for the `loadStoredData`-fallback branches specifically - safe because that read happens strictly *after* `loadStoredData`'s own internal `applyLatestPrData` write has already completed (a plain synchronous JS assignment, not a `setState`), not the same-tick-as-mutation hazard this fix removes.
- `react-callbacks.helpers.js`'s three handlers now use the returned value directly and no longer read back `latestStoredPayload` at all - that getter is removed entirely from this file's DI surface. `getLatestSelectedRepo` stays (a separate, legitimate pre-call fallback read, not part of the hazard).

**Scope decision, made deliberately:** did not convert the three handlers into real React hooks in this pass, even though the blocker that originally deferred it is now resolved. Confirmed by reading `PrDataProvider.jsx` directly: `window.updateReactPrTable` merges `payload`+`selectedRepo`+`visiblePrNumbers` with "undefined = leave unchanged" semantics, while the Provider's own exposed `setPayload` only touches `payload` - a hook calling `setPayload` directly would need to replicate that merge or thread new setters through the Provider, a separately-scoped change. The three handlers also sit under `PrTableApp.jsx`'s busy-state decorators and `markPrsBusy`/`clearPrsBusy`/`markPrsQueued`/`clearPrsQueued` window bridges, which would need re-plumbing too. Given the documented prior regression in exactly this area, the smaller, additive fix was judged the right scope for this pass.

**Verified, given the high-risk history:** full jest suite (2050 tests, +18), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (38 tests - critically including "checkbox toggle and More insights expand work", the exact historical-regression scenario, and the chunked bulk Ack/Apply-label test), `index.html.test.js` run in isolation (104 tests, unchanged - confirmed return-value-agnostic). Explicit self-review pass completed: re-read the full diff and traced the synchronous call stack by hand for all three handlers, confirming `window.getReactPrTablePayload` is never consulted anywhere in the new code (grepped to verify) and every new `return null`/`return {...}` point is reached correctly. Note: verification relied on the automated Playwright suite (a real browser via the dev server) rather than a separate interactive manual click-through, since this session has no interactive browser tool beyond the automated test runner.

## 🚧 **Sub-phase 7.2 follow-up: `#repo` Context-value override, plus two dead-end findings (2026-10-04)**

Researched two candidate "next slices" for Phase 7; both turned out to be dead ends, recorded here so a future session doesn't re-research either from scratch.

**Author Insights draft dirty-state** (deferred in sub-phase 7.5, citing the same regression class sub-phase 7.3 avoided for `latestStoredPayload`): investigated whether that blocker was similarly stale. It isn't - `authorInsightsState` is a plain mutable object, so a synchronous mutate-then-read has no staleness hazard today (unlike `latestStoredPayload`, which was backed by `setState`). No live bug exists to fix here; the deferral is purely architectural (the "no `window.*` bridges" goal). Worse, `getBlockingAuthorInsightsLogins` scans dirty drafts across *every* author login and every abandoned in-progress edit comment, not just whichever login is currently rendered (only one login's forms are ever mounted at a time) - a `NotesDirtyProvider`-shaped fix (a component reports its own dirty boolean via `useEffect`) can't reconstruct that cross-login picture without either continuously re-scanning via the existing vanilla function (no real `window.*`-reduction benefit, since it just adds a bridge in the other direction) or moving the actual draft value storage into real Provider state - which cascades into `pr-auto-render-navigation.helpers.js`'s "jump to my unsaved draft" feature needing a parallel redesign (it reads those same draft values to find which DOM element to focus). Decided: skip. Not worth the redesign risk for a slice with no bug behind it.

**Filtering computation to Context** (`deriveRenderFilterSummaryState`, called from `pr-data-tab.orchestrator.js`'s `renderPrData`): re-audited `renderPrData`'s full pipeline first and found most of sub-phase 7.2's original blocker list (data-meta, filter chips, author-insights, stats view, all 9 filter dropdowns) is now stale - already migrated by sub-phases 7.0/7.4. What's left and genuinely still vanilla is this filtering chain, whose output (`filteredRows`) is what the React table actually renders as `visiblePrNumbers` - not a side computation. Converting it to a reactive `useMemo` Context derivation would make filter changes apply live instead of only on an explicit trigger (today's behavior, and the literal subject of an existing `ROADMAP.md` item, "Fix consistency on when filters are applied"). That's a real user-visible behavior change, not just an internal refactor - not a call to make as a side effect of an architecture-cleanup slice. Decided: don't do the reactive conversion.

**What did land**, genuinely small and safe: `renderPrData` hard-reads `#repo` and `#filter-pr-numbers` DOM elements. `filterPrNumbers` already prefers a `FilterStateProvider` Context value over its DOM read when available (`getFilterStateValue`); `#repo` had no equivalent, even though `selectedRepo` already exists as real Context state in `PrDataProvider`. Added the same override for `repo`:
- `state/PrDataProvider.jsx`: a new dedicated read bridge, `window.getReactPrTableSelectedRepo`, mirroring the existing `window.getReactPrTablePayload` bridge exactly (a ref kept in sync, so the `[]`-effect closure never goes stale). A new bridge rather than changing `getReactPrTablePayload`'s return shape, since two existing call sites already depend on it returning the payload directly.
- `orchestrators/pr-data-tab.orchestrator.js`: new optional `getSelectedRepoOverride` DI param (safely defaulted to always-`undefined`, so every existing test keeps working unmodified), preferred over `repoInput.value.trim()` when it returns a string - same handled/fallback shape as `filterPrNumbersRaw`'s existing override, right next to it. The `!repoInput || !filterPrNumbersInput` DOM-element-existence guard is untouched - only which *value* is read from `repoInput` changed.
- `index.page.js`: wired `getSelectedRepoOverride: () => window.getReactPrTableSelectedRepo?.()` into the existing `createPrDataTabOrchestrator({...})` call.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2055 tests, +5), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (38 tests), `index.html.test.js` run in isolation (104 tests, unchanged - this suite drives `renderPrData` through real `#repo` DOM elements with no `PrDataProvider` mounted alongside it, so the override correctly resolves to `undefined` there, preserving its existing behavior exactly).

## 🚧 **`PrInsightsDisplayContext`/`PrInsightsDisplayProvider`: "More insights" panel off `window.*` (2026-10-04)**

Next Phase 7 slice after the `#repo` override above - a fresh inventory (via a research agent, re-verified directly against source before implementing, since two of its "zero-dependency" claims turned out to be wrong) found the "More insights" expandable row panel's formatting/badge-class cluster as the largest remaining clean candidate: 22 `window.X || fallback` reads across `PrInsightsRow.jsx` and 4 `components/insights/*` sections, structurally identical to the 4 clusters already migrated this way (`ActorIdentityContext`, `NeedsAttentionContext`, `ReviewStatsContext`, `AuthorInsightsContext`).

**Corrected after direct verification (not taken on faith from the research/plan agents):**
- `buildPrPeopleOptions` and `getOpenConversationCountWithMe` were both flagged as "zero-dependency" - both are actually actor/viewer-identity-dependent (confirmed by reading their bodies: the former calls `resolveActorDisplayName` from its closure, the latter reads `currentViewerLogin`/`inferViewerLoginFromPage()`, the exact chain `ActorIdentityContext`'s `getEffectiveViewerLogin(row)` already provides).
- `ActivityEventsSection.jsx`'s `buildActivityEvents(pr)` is a module-level plain function, not a hook - it read `window.buildFallbackActivityEvents`/`window.buildActivityEventKey` directly specifically *because* it can't call a hook. Fixed by having the component itself call the new hook and pass those two functions in as a parameter.
- `ReviewThreadsSection.jsx`'s `useHelpers()` is already a valid hook (calls `useActorIdentity()` internally) - adding `usePrInsightsDisplay()` inside it needed no restructuring.
- Two components outside the 5 migrated also read 3 of these names directly: `AuthorInsightsPrDataMeta.jsx` (`parseMarkerState`, `getViewedFilesSummary`) and `cells/PrApprovedCell.jsx` (`getOpenConversationCountWithMe`) - their `window.*` bridges stay, confirmed via project-wide grep, not just the 5 target components. A documented follow-up opportunity (migrating those two would let the remaining 3 bridges go too), not an oversight. **Resolved by two later sessions** (`PrApprovedCell.jsx` off `window.*`, 2026-10-05; the "Track C" closeout, 2026-10-09 - see those entries below) - this note is kept for its own history but is stale as a "still open" item; re-confirmed via direct read (2026-10-11) that neither component reads `window.*` at all anymore.
- `getAuthorThreadResolutionPolicy`/`readReviewConversationsUiState`/`writeReviewConversationsUiState` stayed explicitly out of scope - a DOM-scan dependency (sub-phase 7.4's deliberate choice not to track multi-select "currently checked" state in Context) and a module-scope `Map` (persisting across remounts) respectively, each a separate, real design decision.

**What moved:** 6 new helper modules (`pr-markdown-render`, `pr-activity-events`, `pr-approval-duration`, `pr-notes-people-options`, `pr-open-conversation-count`, `pr-viewed-files-summary`.helpers.js) - pure extractions of what were previously plain inline `index.page.js` functions, each with its own unit tests. A new `state/PrInsightsDisplayContext.jsx` + `components/PrInsightsDisplayProvider.jsx`, mirroring `AuthorInsightsProvider.jsx`'s *fixed* shape (identity-independent functions hoisted to module scope, built once; only the 5 identity-dependent ones rebuilt in a `useMemo` keyed on the actor-identity functions) - avoiding the exact stale-dependency-array bug that cluster hit originally. Mounted once in `react-app.jsx` alongside `NeedsAttentionProvider`/`NotesDirtyProvider`, wrapping `<PrTableApp>`. 18 of the 22 `window.assign` entries were deleted from `index.page.js` at the time (the other 4 - `parseMarkerState`/`getViewedFilesSummary`/`getOpenConversationCountWithMe`/`getAuthorThreadResolutionPolicy` - stayed, either for the two out-of-scope consumers above or the explicitly-deferred case). **Update (2026-10-11):** 3 of those 4 (`parseMarkerState`/`getViewedFilesSummary`/`getOpenConversationCountWithMe`) are gone too now, per the two later sessions noted above - only `getAuthorThreadResolutionPolicy` remains, for its own still-valid, separate reason.

**Test updates:** `PrInsightsRow.test.jsx`/`ApprovalRiskSection.test.jsx` needed real fixes (not just mechanical rewiring) - both had `window.*` stubs whose custom output diverged from the real functions' actual output (e.g. `formatDurationMinutes(90)` stubbed as `"90m"` vs. the real `"1h 30m"`), caught immediately by the full test suite once the components switched to reading real Context defaults. Fixed by wrapping renders in `<PrInsightsDisplayContext.Provider value={{...defaultPrInsightsDisplay, ...overrides}}>` and either keeping a deliberate override (where the test's own intent is "badges render with some class," not "badges use the exact real algorithm," already covered by `pr-insight-badge-class.helpers.test.js`) or supplying the specific override a test actually needs. The other 3 component test files' stale `window.*` stubs were simply dead (nothing asserted on their specific output), so those were just removed.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2092 tests, +43), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (38 tests, including "checkbox toggle and More insights expand work" exercising this exact cluster in a real browser), `index.html.test.js` run in isolation (104 tests, unchanged - confirmed this suite never referenced any of the 22 names, so is unaffected either way).

## 🚧 **`ReviewConversationsUiStateContext`/`Provider`: retiring `reviewConversationsUiStateByKey` (2026-10-05)**

`ReviewThreadsSection.jsx` used `window.readReviewConversationsUiState`/`window.writeReviewConversationsUiState` to remember a user's per-PR "Review conversations" filter-mode and summary-cards-toggle choice across its own mount/unmount (collapsing/re-expanding a row's "More insights" panel resets its local `useState`). Backed by `index.page.js`'s module-scope `reviewConversationsUiStateByKey = new Map()`.

No `window.*` bridge published at all (unlike `NotesDirtyProvider`) - nothing outside this component ever needs to read it. **A real coverage gap closed:** no existing test proved the toggle actually survived a real remount - `ReviewThreadsSection.test.jsx` only ever rendered one instance per test. New `components/ReviewConversationsUiStateProvider.test.jsx` mounts the Provider once and uses `rerender` to collapse/re-expand the *same* section instance (a fresh `render()` per "remount" would instead create a brand-new Provider/Map each time and prove nothing - caught this exact mistake in the test's first draft, when two tests failed for the right reason).

## 🚧 **Direct-import `PrTableApp.jsx`'s row-sorting functions; delete 2 dead bridges (2026-10-05)**

Re-inventoried the remaining `Object.assign(window, {...})` entries after this session's last 3 slices. `normalizeRows`/`sortRowsByPrNumberDesc`/`sortRowsByDateFieldDesc` (`index.page.js`) turned out to be genuinely zero-dependency pure row-sorting functions (their one real dependency, `parseSortableTime`, was already a direct-importable export from sub-phase 7.0) - the exact "zero-dependency pure-function bridge → direct import" pattern from that original sweep, simpler than every Context-based slice this session since no Provider is needed at all. New bare-export `helpers/pr-row-sorting.helpers.js` (matching `pr-sortable-time.helpers.js`'s own precedent - no `createXHelpers()` wrapper, since there's nothing to inject), imported directly by `PrTableApp.jsx` in place of the 3 `window.X || fallback` reads. The function definitions stay in `index.page.js` (still used by its own internal DI wiring); only the window bridges are gone.

**A real risk identified during planning, confirmed and fixed during implementation:** `PrTableApp.test.jsx` only installed real sort implementations on `window` inside two specific blocks; every other test relied on the old no-op fallback. Switching to an always-real import broke exactly the one test that *spied on* `window.sortRowsByPrNumberDesc`'s call count (to verify `pr-section-grouping.helpers.js`'s reference-equality cache skips re-sorting on an unrelated re-render) - a plain ES import can't be reassigned the way a `window.*` global can. Fixed with `jest.mock('../helpers/pr-row-sorting.helpers.js', ...)`, keeping the real implementations for every export and wrapping only `sortRowsByPrNumberDesc` in a `jest.fn()` - the same "`jest.mock` is the equivalent of swapping a `window.*` global" pattern this file already used for `pr-section-config.helpers.js`/`pr-smart-groups.helpers.js`. Every other test in the file needed no changes at all, confirming the pre-implementation risk analysis (no test asserted on row order outside the sort-focused tests) was correct.

**Also found and fixed while re-reading this cluster (bundled in, not a separate commit):** `isInReviewEnabled`'s `window.*` bridge (a standalone assignment plus an `Object.assign` entry) was confirmed **dead** for the React side - `PrTableApp.jsx`'s `checkNeedsAttention` never reads it (a past bug, already fixed; `PrTableApp.test.jsx`/`index.html.test.js` both carry regression tests proving exactly this). Its real consumer is `index.page.js`'s own internal "alwaysShowInReview" filter, which calls the local function directly, not through `window` - deleted the bridge, kept the function. `isFlaggedEnabled` turned out to be **even more dead** than planned: eslint caught (correctly) that it had *zero* callers at all once its bridge was removed - no internal "alwaysShowFlagged" filter exists, unlike its sibling. Deleted the function definition entirely, not just the bridge.

**Remaining known candidate, still not attempted:** `getSelectedPrNumbers`/`updateSelectedPrNumbers` (`cells/PrSelectionCell.jsx`) - genuinely shares a DOM input (`#pr-numbers`) with vanilla form serialization (`getFormBody`), a real entanglement between a React checkbox cell and a vanilla text field read synchronously on form submit, not a simple bridge swap. **Resolved (2026-10-09)** - see "`#pr-numbers` off its own one-off bridge, closing the last 'remaining known candidate'" below; this note is stale as a "still open" item, kept only for its own history.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2118 tests, +8), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (38 tests), `index.html.test.js` run in isolation (104 tests, unchanged - confirmed via grep no reference to any of the changed names).
