## 🚧 **SSE-backed activity drawer, plus retiring the scheduler-status poll (2026-10-05)**

Not a Phase 7 bridge-cleanup slice - a new feature, requested directly: a collapsible drawer showing all in-progress background activity (the three in-process scheduled jobs, backfill, in-flight user requests, and the bulk ack/apply-label queue), pushed live rather than polled. Two explicit constraints shaped the design: **push, not poll** (Server-Sent-Events, not a new/extended polling interval), and **be honest about what's real** - there is no backend job queue anywhere, just one depth-1 "deferred retry" flag (`quickCheckSkippedWhileAutoRunInProgress`), so the drawer represents that as a "waiting on X" status rather than inventing a queue position. The ONE place a genuine ordered queue already exists is the frontend's chunked bulk ack/apply-label batch (`pr-ack-label-actions.helpers.js`) - that's the only section allowed to show a numbered "chunk N of M" position.

**A premise correction caught before implementing:** initial planning (via a research agent) assumed `react-app.jsx` still used independent per-section React roots, which would have forced a `CustomEvent`-based bridge to get state into a separately-mounted drawer. Verified directly against source instead - `react-app.jsx` has exactly one `ReactDOM.createRoot()` call and 43 `createPortal` usages (the multi-root doc comment near the top of that file is stale, describing a design the file no longer uses). This meant Context reaches every portaled section already, so the drawer's state-sharing problem (with `PrTableApp.jsx`'s busy/queued tracking) could be solved with a lifted Provider instead of a new cross-root primitive.

**Backend (new):** `helpers/view-prs-job-events-helpers.js` (a module-scoped `EventEmitter`-based pub/sub; every envelope it builds bundles the full `scheduler: getViewPrsSchedulerPublicState()` object, not just the specific job's own fields - this is what lets the frontend fully retire the old scheduler poll without reimplementing its rendering), `helpers/view-prs-events-route-helpers.js` (pure SSE frame formatting - deliberately no `id:` field, since there's no server-side replay buffer to honor), `routes/view-prs-events-routes.js` (`GET /view-prs/events`, a raw streaming handler - NOT `createSyncHandler`, which calls `res.status(500).json(...)` on error and would throw `ERR_HTTP_HEADERS_SENT` once a stream has started). `getViewPrsSchedulerPublicState()` gained 3 fields it was missing (`isQuickCheckInProgress`, `quickCheckSkippedWhileAutoRunInProgress`, `isMergedDrainInProgress` - the last needed a new scheduler-state flag, since no in-progress tracking existed for the merged-queue drain at all) so a client connecting mid-run sees accurate state immediately, not "idle" until the next event.

**Emit points, mirroring each job's existing guard conditions exactly (app.js):** `runViewPrsAutoRefresh`'s previously-silent concurrency guard now emits `skipped{reason:"already-in-progress"}` (the single highest-value honesty fix here - a dropped manual trigger was invisible before this), plus circuit-open/missing-deps/manual-cooldown skips and a start/finish pair. `runViewPrsQuickCheck` emits `deferred{waitingOn:"autoRefresh",willRunAfter:true}` on the one path that genuinely retries (the catch-up quick-check `runViewPrsAutoRefresh`'s own `finally` fires), and `skipped{willRunAfter:false}` on the other blocker (a concurrent quick-check, which is a true drop) - this distinction is the honesty contract's single most important test assertion. `runViewPrsMergedQueueDrain` emits a nested `start → (autoRefresh's own start/skip/finish) → finish` sequence, honestly showing when a drain accomplished nothing because auto-refresh was already running. Backfill was deliberately NOT wired into SSE - it's a detached OS process with no in-process lifecycle to hook, and the server doesn't self-poll it; adding a watcher to make it push-capable would be inventing new machinery that doesn't exist today, which the "honest" principle rules out. It stays on its existing 5s poll.

**Frontend (new):** `helpers/pr-job-events.helpers.js` (pure reducer - `skipped` never changes a job's `status`, `deferred` sets `waitingOn` without touching status, any non-`deferred` quickCheck event clears `waitingOn`, a snapshot is always authoritative regardless of `seq`), `state/JobEventsContext.jsx` + `components/JobEventsProvider.jsx` (owns the single `EventSource`, with `onerror` branching on `readyState` - `CONNECTING` means the browser is already retrying, `CLOSED` means it won't and this component must reconnect itself with backoff - plus a staleness watchdog for a buffering proxy that connects but delivers nothing silently), `components/ActivityDrawer.jsx` + `ActivityDrawerSchedulerSection.jsx` + `ActivityDrawerQueueSection.jsx` (the four sections; the scheduler section's copy for a deferred quick-check is exactly "Waiting on auto refresh - will run as soon as the refresh finishes," never a position or count). New top-right toggle/panel CSS, sitting at z-index 1100/1150 - above the error snackbar (1000) but below the PR JSON modal (2000) and the auto-render-blocked indicator (1200, left on top deliberately: it's an actionable warning, the drawer is dismissable).

**The one real queue, made honest, not reinvented:** `pr-ack-label-actions.helpers.js`'s existing `chunks` array (already real, already ordered) is surfaced via 4 new bridges (`beginBulkActionBatch`/`markBulkActionChunkInFlight`/`markBulkActionChunkDone`/`finishBulkActionBatch`, backed by the pure `helpers/pr-bulk-action-batches.helpers.js`), called from both `runChunkedAckRequest` and `runChunkedApplyLabelRequest` around their existing `runWithConcurrencyLimit` call (which already passes a chunk index to its worker - no new plumbing needed there). A batch under 2 chunks is never tracked at all, matching that file's own existing single-chunk skip - no fabricated one-item queue.

**`busyPrNumbers`/`queuedPrNumbers` lifted out of `PrTableApp.jsx`** into new `state/PrActivityQueueContext.jsx` + `components/PrActivityQueueProvider.jsx` (same Set-of-composite-keys shape, same `markPrBusy`/`clearPrBusy`/`markPrsBusy`/etc. semantics, moved verbatim) - necessary because the drawer's queue section is a sibling portal, not a descendant of `PrTableApp`, so the two need to share state via a common ancestor Provider rather than `PrTableApp`'s own local `useState`. `PrTableApp.test.jsx`'s shared `renderPrTableApp` helper now wraps every render in `<PrActivityQueueProvider>` - fixed all 8 tests that had asserted on the bridges `PrTableApp` used to register itself, with no other test changes needed.

**Polling retirement (a follow-up ask mid-implementation, not in the original ask):** audited all 4 `PrDataPolling.jsx` intervals against what each actually does. The scheduler-status poll (`pollSchedulerStatus`, 30s) was fully retired - it polled exactly the state SSE now pushes, so `JobEventsProvider` calls the existing `window.renderSchedulerStatus` (a new bridge, wrapping the unchanged `renderSchedulerStatus` function) directly on every SSE frame instead, reusing 100% of the existing badge/details-text/`pr-active-progress-update` rendering logic. The PR-data poll (`pollForDataChanges`, 30s) could **not** be fully retired - it's the only mechanism that catches changes from *other* browser tabs/sessions (e.g. someone else acking a PR), which has no SSE hook and deliberately so (the session that initiates a mutation already learns its own result synchronously). Instead, `JobEventsProvider` now triggers `window.pollForDataChanges()` immediately on an `autoRefresh`/`mergedQueueDrain` "finish" SSE event, collapsing the wait for those two triggers from up to 30s to near-instant, while leaving the interval as a slower safety net for everything else. The backfill poll (5s, external process) and the 1s request-activity clock tick (pure client-side, no server round-trip) are unchanged - there's nothing server-side to push for either. `PrDataPolling.test.jsx` and `index.page.memory-leak-prevention.test.jsx` updated to assert `pollSchedulerStatus` is never called automatically anymore (the function itself stays callable - `index.html.test.js` still calls it directly).

**Verified:** full jest suite (2199 tests, +81), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (38 tests). Also manually verified against the real server (not just the standalone-Express route test): started `src/server/server.js` directly, `curl`'d `/view-prs/events` and confirmed the exact snapshot shape including the 3 new scheduler-state fields, then triggered a real `POST /view-prs/quick-check` while watching a second concurrent SSE connection - observed the real `start` → `finish` event pair with the bundled scheduler state updating live in between, confirming the backend wiring end-to-end beyond what the isolated route tests alone could prove.

### Follow-up: self-review fixes (2026-10-05/06)

A self-review of the feature above (asked for directly, not part of the original ask) found 6 issues, all fixed in the same session:

1. **A chunk's failure was indistinguishable from success in the drawer.** `runChunkedAckRequest`/`runChunkedApplyLabelRequest` (`pr-ack-label-actions.helpers.js`) called `markBulkActionChunkDoneSafe` unconditionally in each chunk's `finally`, even on the path where `failedChunks.push(...)` had just run - the code already knew a chunk failed, but never told the drawer. Added a `"failed"` chunk state (`pr-bulk-action-batches.helpers.js`'s `markChunkDone` now takes `{ ok }`, defaulting to `true` for backward compatibility), a `failedCount` alongside `doneCount` in the view model, and a visibly distinct "failed" row + "(N failed)" batch-header annotation in `ActivityDrawerQueueSection.jsx`. `ActivityDrawer.jsx`'s toggle count now subtracts both `doneCount` and `failedCount` when counting chunks still outstanding.

2. **The SSE "scheduler" frame (activePrNumbers progress) had no rate limit, and its client-side handler is expensive.** `syncSchedulerActivePrNumbers` (app.js) fires on every `incrementActivePrNumber`/`decrementActivePrNumber` call - roughly twice per PR touched by a refresh - and `JobEventsProvider` calls `window.renderSchedulerStatus` (a full `.pr-number-cell` DOM scan) on every frame it receives, unconditionally. For a large multi-repo refresh this could re-scan the whole table many times a second - worse than the old 30s-poll baseline during exactly the window when responsiveness matters most. Fixed with a leading+trailing throttle inside `createViewPrsJobEventsHelpers` (new `schedulerStateThrottleMs`, default 250ms, env-configurable - see #4): the first call in a quiet period fires immediately, and any calls within the window coalesce into one trailing call carrying the latest state, so the rate is bounded without ever dropping the final update.

3. **Dead code:** `getJobEventsViewModel` in `pr-job-events.helpers.js` was defined, exported, and never called anywhere. Removed.

4. **`heartbeatIntervalMs`/`maxClients` (the SSE route) and the new throttle above had no env-var override**, unlike nearly every other interval/threshold in this codebase. Added `VIEW_PRS_EVENTS_HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL_MS`, `VIEW_PRS_EVENTS_MAX_CLIENTS`, `VIEW_PRS_SCHEDULER_STATE_THROTTLE_MS` to `config/app-config.js`, following the exact `Math.max(floor, parseInt(env.X || default, 10) || default)` pattern every sibling value there already uses.

5. **CSS fragility:** the drawer's fixed header and fixed scrollable body were two independent `position: fixed` elements kept from overlapping only by a hand-matched `padding-top: 56px` on the panel - any future header-height change would silently break that sync. Replaced with a flex-column `.activity-drawer-panel` (fixed, full height) containing a non-shrinking `.activity-drawer-panel-header` and a `flex: 1 1 auto; overflow-y: auto` `.activity-drawer-panel-content` - the header's real height now reserves its own space automatically.

6. **No keyboard affordances:** the drawer had no Escape-to-close and didn't move focus anywhere on open. Not a true modal (nothing behind it goes inert, no focus trap), but added both as cheap, expected behavior: focus moves to the close button on open, and a `keydown` listener (added/removed with the panel's own open state) closes it on Escape.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2212 tests, +13), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (38 tests), and a repeat of the real-server manual check (server boots cleanly with the 3 new env-configurable values wired through, `/view-prs/events` still serves the correct snapshot).

### Follow-up: a second review pass, 5 more fixes (2026-10-06)

A second review of the feature (again asked for directly) found 5 more issues, all fixed in the same session:

1. **`#scheduler-badges`/`#scheduler-details` had no fallback once SSE fully replaced the old poll.** If the SSE connection ever actually failed in some deployment (a buffering reverse proxy, an unsupported browser, a blocked route) those elements would just go stale forever outside the drawer, which has its own connection banner but only renders while a user has it open. Fixed by threading connection liveness all the way into the existing rendering function: `renderSchedulerStatus(schedulerRaw, { isLive })` (`index.page.js`) now prepends a "Live updates offline - showing last known state" badge/line when `isLive: false`. `JobEventsProvider.jsx` keeps a `lastSchedulerRef` of the last real scheduler object it received, and a dedicated effect (watching `connection`/`isStale`) re-renders that cached object with `isLive: false` the moment the connection goes down or the staleness watchdog trips - without needing a new SSE frame to trigger it, since by definition none arrive while offline.
2. **Zero real-browser coverage of the drawer itself.** Added 2 Playwright tests to `e2e/smoke.spec.js`: one opens the drawer and confirms the real `EventSource` actually connects through the real server *and* Vite's dev proxy (manually verified via `curl` through the proxy too - confirmed it passes SSE through untouched) with no connection-down banner, all three job rows present, and Escape-to-close working; the other clicks "Quick check" and waits for the drawer's Quick check row to show "Running" - proving a real push round-trip, not a poll. Both are placed *before* the pre-existing "Trigger auto run and Quick check..." test: that test's own "Trigger auto run" click starts a real, fire-and-forget auto-refresh that - with no real GitHub network/auth in this sandbox - stays `isAutoRunInProgress: true` for a long time afterward, which would make a quick-check test running later in the same shared-server suite see it immediately skipped instead of ever reaching "Running". (Confirmed by hitting this exact problem when the new tests were first placed at the end of the file - moving them earlier fixed it without touching the pre-existing test.) Also discovered mid-debugging: the underlying quick-check script can hang indefinitely on `gh` network calls in an offline sandbox, so the "Running" assertion deliberately never waits for the job to actually *finish* - `runViewPrsQuickCheck`'s `start` emit fires synchronously before any of that network work begins, which is enough to prove the push pipe works without depending on `gh`/network availability.
3. **Escape closed two unrelated things at once.** `ActivityDrawer`'s `keydown` listener (on `window`) and `PrJsonModal`'s pre-existing one (on `document`) both react to Escape with neither calling `stopPropagation()` - if the drawer was open underneath an opened PR JSON modal, Escape closed both instead of just the focused modal. Fixed by adding `event.stopPropagation()` to `PrJsonModal`'s Escape branch (it's the true modal - focus trap, body scroll lock - so it should consume the key before a non-modal overlay underneath it ever sees it), verified with a new cross-component test rendering both together.
4. **3 new env vars were undocumented.** Added a short "Activity" section to README.md (what the drawer shows, which parts are push vs. poll) plus `VIEW_PRS_EVENTS_HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL_MS`/`VIEW_PRS_EVENTS_MAX_CLIENTS`/`VIEW_PRS_SCHEDULER_STATE_THROTTLE_MS`, matching the existing inline-env-var-list convention used for the scheduler's own tuning vars just above it.
5. **A small always-on cost on the refresh hot path, independent of whether anyone's watching.** `emitSchedulerStateChanged` (`view-prs-job-events-helpers.js`) built a full scheduler envelope and ran throttle timer bookkeeping on every call regardless of whether any SSE client was connected - which is the common case for most of this process's life. Added an early return when `emitter.listenerCount(...) === 0` (checked before any of that work starts, not just before the cheap `emitter.emit` call at the very end).

**Verified:** full jest suite (2218 tests, +6), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (40 tests, +2 - all passing in the corrected order), and the real-server/real-Vite-proxy manual `curl` checks described in #2 above.

### Follow-up: removing the Status tab's now-redundant scheduler/request-activity display (2026-10-06)

Asked directly, after the feature shipped: with the activity drawer now showing live scheduler-job and in-flight-request status, what on the Status tab is redundant? Investigated the actual `index.html` structure rather than going from memory - found two genuinely duplicate display blocks and one piece of real information that would have been silently lost by removing them naively.

**Removed, as pure duplication:**
- The "Request Activity" card (`#request-activity-badges` + `#request-activity-details`) - the exact same `AppRoot` state the drawer's "In-flight user actions" section already renders, with no action buttons attached to lose.
- The "Auto Refresh Scheduler" badges/details (`#scheduler-badges` + `#scheduler-details`) - the same `renderSchedulerStatus` call that now drives the drawer's "Scheduled background jobs" section, which is strictly richer (per-job running/idle, live via SSE, honest "waiting on X"). The "Trigger auto run" button in that same block stayed - it's a control, not a display.

**Explicitly kept, despite looking similar:** the Backfill tab's own badges/details (paired with Start/Stop/Refresh buttons and the log viewer - removing them would leave someone managing backfill with no on-page feedback), `#status`/`#output` (unrelated to scheduler/backfill - they show the result of whatever action a user just ran), the Action Log tab (persistent history, not live status), and the per-row spinner/"Update queued" badge (per-PR, not aggregate - the drawer doesn't cover this at all).

**A real feature-parity gap caught before deleting anything:** the old `getSchedulerBadges` (`pr-activity-badges.helpers.js`) computed an "Update queued: N open, M merged/closed" badge - PRs quick-check had already flagged as changed but the merged-queue drain hadn't picked up yet - and nothing in the drawer showed this. Rather than silently drop it, extended `pr-job-events.helpers.js`'s reducer to carry `pendingOpenCount`/`pendingMergedClosedCount` (read off every envelope's own bundled scheduler object, not just a drain-specific one, so it stays current regardless of which job most recently emitted) and surfaced it on the drawer's Merged/closed drain row.

**Cascading cleanup once the display blocks were gone:** `renderSchedulerStatus`/`renderRequestActivity` (`index.page.js`) both shrank to just their still-needed side effects (the per-row progress indicator, `#request-activity-badges` - which the drawer still consumes as a prop via `AppRoot` state, unlike the removed details text). The `isLive` plumbing added in the previous follow-up (`JobEventsProvider.jsx`'s `lastSchedulerRef` + its "connection went down, re-render stale" effect, `renderSchedulerStatus`'s `isLive` parameter) became dead code once there was no `#scheduler-badges`/`#scheduler-details` left to mark stale - removed entirely, since the drawer already owns its own connection-state display. `latestStatusText` (a module-level variable in `index.page.js`) turned out to have been read only by the removed request-activity details line - deleted along with its now-pointless write-only tracking comment. `getSchedulerBadges` had zero remaining callers once its one call site was gone - deleted from `pr-activity-badges.helpers.js` along with its tests, keeping `getRequestActivityBadges` (still used).

**Verified:** full jest suite (2212 tests), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (40 tests, including the "no React-portaled status/log panel shows its old static placeholder text..." regression guard and both activity-drawer tests from the previous follow-up).

### Follow-up: pushing "data changed" to other tabs, closing most of the remaining PR-data poll gap (2026-10-06)

Asked directly: of the other polls still running (1s request-activity tick, 5s backfill, 30s PR-data), which can also move to SSE? Audited each on its own merits rather than assuming:

- **The 1s request-activity tick isn't a poll at all** - it never talks to the server, it's a local clock re-rendering elapsed-time text for this tab's own already-known in-flight requests. Nothing to convert.
- **The 5s backfill poll is technically convertible but wouldn't actually remove polling** - backfill is a detached OS process with no callback/IPC mechanism, so making it "push" would mean the *server* starts polling the PID/log file instead of the browser - relocating the poll, not eliminating it. Left as-is, consistent with the original feature's "be honest about what's real" reasoning for excluding it from SSE in the first place.
- **The 30s PR-data poll was a genuine candidate.** It stayed partially poll-based specifically because it's the only mechanism that catches a data change made by a *different* tab or user - ordinary mutation routes had no SSE hook. Audited every code path that actually writes to the stored PR data file (not assumed from memory) to find the ones still missing instrumentation.

**What writes the data file, and what was already covered:** `dataVersion` is just the file's own `mtimeMs:size`, so any write bumps it, regardless of mechanism. `runViewPrsAutoRefresh`/`runViewPrsQuickCheck`/`runViewPrsMergedQueueDrain` (the scheduler jobs) were already instrumented. Four more call paths write the file and weren't:
- `POST /ack` - via `runAckRefreshes`, a per-PR loop calling the script directly (not the already-instrumented `runViewPrsAutoRefresh`, a wrong assumption corrected during the audit). Only when the request is a real ack/ackClear operation, not a checkbox-only (flagged/inReview) one, which never touches PR data at all.
- `POST /labels/apply` - via `patchStoredPrLabels`, a direct Node-side file patch (no script invocation at all - a third, distinct write mechanism alongside the script-based one and the scheduler jobs).
- `POST /run` - the generic manual "Run script" action (also used for the "↻ Update" single-PR button via `--pr`).
- `POST /merged/request-more` - via `runRequestMoreRefreshes`, another per-PR script loop.

**Design:** a new job-agnostic `data-changed` SSE event (`view-prs-job-events-helpers.js`'s `emitDataChanged`, parallel to the existing `emitSchedulerStateChanged` - same zero-subscriber skip, no throttle needed since these are discrete user actions rather than a tight per-PR loop), bundling the current scheduler state like every other envelope for consistency. Each of the 4 routes calls it only when a write actually happened - after `refreshedPrs.length > 0` for ack/apply-label/request-more, unconditionally on `/run`'s success path. The requesting tab already has its own fresh result from the route's own response; this is purely for every *other* connected tab. `JobEventsProvider.jsx` routes `data-changed` through the exact same frame handler already used for `job`/`scheduler` events, adding one more condition to the existing "should I trigger `pollForDataChanges` immediately" check - no new reducer state needed. The 30s interval itself stays as a slower fallback, since this still isn't every conceivable write path (e.g. anything bypassing these 4 routes entirely).

**A test-writing mistake caught before it shipped:** an early test assumed `/labels/apply` with no matching stored row would leave `appliedPrs` empty - wrong. `appliedPrs` reflects the GitHub-side label apply succeeding; `patchStoredPrLabels` finding no matching row is a separate, later failure captured in `refreshErrors`, which is what `emitDataChangedSafe` is actually gated on. Fixed the assertion rather than the (correct) production code.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2224 tests, +12), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (40 tests). Also checked directly against a real, isolated server (not just mocked integration tests): confirmed a checkbox-only ack produces no `data-changed` frame on a live SSE connection. Did not attempt a live positive-path trigger for ack/apply-label/request-more against this sandbox - each shells out to a real `gh` call with no GitHub network/auth available here, and an earlier manual quick-check attempt in this same environment hung rather than failing fast; the mocked integration tests already exercise the positive path precisely (per-route, per-condition) without that risk.

### Follow-up: closing the "not all actions are shown" gap, plus a Recent Activity section (2026-10-06)

Asked directly: not all user actions showed up in the drawer at all. Investigation found one real bug and two real gaps, all fixed in the same session:

- **Apply-label's activity tracking was silently broken.** `pr-ack-label-actions.helpers.js` already called `beginRequestActivitySafe("labelApply")`, but `"labelApply"` had never been added as a key to `index.page.js`'s `requestActivityCounters`/`requestActivityStartedAtMs` objects. `setRequestActivityCounter`'s `hasOwnProperty` guard silently dropped every increment/decrement for any undeclared key, so the counter never moved and nothing ever rendered - no error, just quiet data loss. Fixed by adding the key to both objects, and additionally refactored `getActiveRequestActivityEntries()` from 5 hand-written near-duplicate `if` blocks into one generic iteration over a new `REQUEST_ACTIVITY_LABELS` map, so a new key with no matching display case can't silently recur.
- **Checkbox toggles (flagged/in-review) and Notes/author-comment saves had zero activity tracking at all** - not broken, just never wired up, so a slow network request for either looked identical to nothing happening. Wired both through the same existing `beginRequestActivity`/`finishActivity` pattern: `pr-row-checkbox-actions.helpers.js` took `beginRequestActivity` as a new DI param (both `toggleInReviewForRow`/`toggleFlaggedForRow` start/stop it around their existing try/finally); `NotesSection.jsx` and `index.page.js`'s `saveAuthorManualComment`/`updateAuthorManualComment` read a new `window.beginRequestActivity` bridge the same way `NotesSection.jsx` already reads `window.postJson` - the established convention for a React component making its own direct fetch outside a vanilla helper factory.
- **Added a "Recent Activity" section** (`ActivityDrawerRecentActivitySection.jsx`) so a request that already finished doesn't just vanish - the existing "In-flight user actions" section only shows what's running *right now*. Two honestly-separate lists rather than one merged timeline: "Scheduled jobs" (left) reuses `pr-job-events.helpers.js`'s existing `recentFinished` reducer field - tracked since the original feature but never rendered anywhere until now - and gets a real SSE-sourced `ok`/`error` outcome for free; "Actions from this tab" (right) is a new, lighter-weight history (`recordRecentRequestActivity`, capped at 10, fed by every `beginRequestActivity` call site including the newly-wired ones) that only knows an action *finished*, not whether it succeeded - deliberately not threading real success/failure through 8+ existing try/catch shapes for a history list, since each of those actions already has its own failure snackbar at the moment it happens.

**A test-fixture gap caught by the new section itself:** `ActivityDrawer.test.jsx`'s hand-built `idleContextValue` fixture had never included `recentFinished`, which only mattered once something actually read it - 5 of 8 tests started throwing `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'length')`. Fixed both the fixture (added `recentFinished: []`) and the component itself (`Array.isArray(...) ? ... : []` defensive guard), the second of which turned out to be necessary on its own: `PrJsonModal.test.jsx` has the same fixture gap in an unrelated fixture and needed no edit at all once the component guarded itself.

**A second, unrelated regression caught by the full suite, not the targeted one:** `window.beginRequestActivity = ...` was `index.page.js`'s first-ever unconditional top-level `window.*` reference - every other such assignment lives inside a function body, only touching `window` when actually called. Two pre-existing integration tests (`index.page.labels.test.js`, `index.page.notifications.test.js`) `require()` the module directly under Jest's default node environment (no `jsdom` pragma, since they'd never needed one), so the bare module-load-time reference threw `ReferenceError: window is not defined`. Fixed by guarding the assignment with `typeof window !== "undefined"`, matching the pattern already used internally for other environment-sensitive bridges.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2231 tests, +7), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (40 tests, unchanged - this round added no new browser-observable surface that wasn't already covered by existing drawer/checkbox/notes tests).

### Follow-up: a per-repo-aware priority dispatcher, replacing the 3 scheduler setIntervals (2026-10-06)

Motivated by two things raised directly: wanting per-repo customizable scheduling cadence/priority (impossible under 3 global timers + flat in-progress booleans), and a real priority-inversion bug this surfaced - `quickCheck` already fast-follows an immediate `autoRefresh` the moment it detects an **open** PR changed, but never did the same for **closed/merged** changes, which only sat flagged until `mergedQueueDrain`'s own fixed interval (30 min default - actually *longer* than the blind 15-min `autoRefresh` sweep it was competing with).

**Core change:** `src/server/helpers/view-prs-dispatcher-helpers.js` (new) owns a registry of per-`(repo, taskType)` entries (`quickCheck`/`autoRefresh`/`mergedDrain` x every repo `getViewPrsAutoRefreshRepos()` resolves), each with its own `priority`/`intervalMs`/`nextDueAt`, selected by one concurrency-budget-aware `pickNextBatch` (priority desc, then oldest-overdue). `app.js`'s `runDispatcherTick()` replaces the three independent `setInterval`s with one, grouping selected entries **by task type** (not by repo) before calling the existing, unmodified `runViewPrsQuickCheck`/`runViewPrsAutoRefresh`/`runViewPrsMergedQueueDrain` - critical, not cosmetic: those three functions are single-flight by design (their own `isXInProgress` guards assume at most one call at a time), so firing one call per *repo* concurrently would trip their own guards constantly once more than one repo's entries are due in the same tick, which is the common case, not an edge case.

**The actual bug fix:** `runViewPrsQuickCheck`'s existing fast-follow (open-PR change -> immediate `autoRefresh`) is reimplemented as `bumpEntryUrgent(repo, "autoRefresh")` + an immediate dispatch tick (same latency as today's direct call) - and the identical treatment is now ALSO applied to `pendingMergedClosed` -> `bumpEntryUrgent(repo, "mergedDrain")`, closing the gap that motivated this whole round.

**Two real correctness bugs found and fixed during implementation, not just at design time:**
- **Overlapping-tick double-execution:** a bump's own "trigger an immediate tick" call could re-select an entry the OUTER tick's sequential loop hadn't marked `isRunning` yet (since groups are marked running one at a time, not all upfront), running the same entry twice within one conceptual selection round. Fixed with a simple module-level re-entrancy guard (`isDispatcherTickInFlight`) - a bump that arrives mid-tick just waits for the tick after, since the bumped entry's `nextDueAt` is already set regardless.
- **Cross-test state leakage via the real, on-disk scheduler-state file:** `persistViewPrsSchedulerState` now writes per-repo entries (run history only - `priority`/`intervalMs` are deliberately never persisted, always re-derived live from `schedulerRepoConfig` so a config edit takes effect with no restart), and `initializeScheduler`'s own `readViewPrsSchedulerState()` call reads that same real temp file back on every invocation - meaning one test's successful dispatcher run could resurrect stale `isRunning`/`nextDueAt`/`lastFinishedAt` data into a *later* test that had just reset its in-memory state, since the file itself was never cleaned between tests. Fixed by having the test suite's own reset helpers delete the on-disk file too, not just the in-memory object.

**Circular-dependency resolution:** the dispatcher helpers need `getViewPrsAutoRefreshRepos` from the scheduler helpers; the scheduler helpers' `getViewPrsSchedulerPublicState` needs the dispatcher's derived fields (`isXInProgress`/`lastXAt` now computed from the registry instead of flat fields, plus the new `dispatcherQueue` array) - broken with a plain mutable forward-reference box (`dispatcherHelpersRef`) in `app.js`, assigned once the dispatcher is actually constructed, read lazily by closure from inside the scheduler helpers (both factories only ever get *called* well after both exist, since this is all synchronous module-load-time wiring).

**Config:** per-repo `priority`/`*IntervalMs` overrides go in a new `schedulerRepoConfig` key inside the *existing* `/view-prs/user-defaults` GET/PUT JSON blob - no new settings route or UI form this round (confirmed with the user as the deliberate scope for v1); the drawer shows each repo's effective priority/cadence read-only as part of the new section below. Two new env vars: `VIEW_PRS_DISPATCHER_GH_PROCESS_BUDGET` (default 8 - bounds how many `(repo, taskType)` entries `pickNextBatch` selects into one tick, per a fixed per-task-type cost estimate; a backlog beyond that just spills into the next ~5s tick instead of being created in one unbounded batch) and `VIEW_PRS_DISPATCHER_TICK_INTERVAL_MS` (default 5000); `VIEW_PRS_AUTO_INTERVAL_MS` also gained an env override for consistency with quick-check/merged-drain, which already had one. The `--jobs` flag passed to the underlying script is *not* derived from this budget - it's still the separate, pre-existing `viewPrsQuickCheckAllJobs` constant, unaffected by this round (an earlier draft of this note overstated this; see the follow-up below for why the stacking risk it was meant to solve turned out to already be a non-issue here).

**Frontend:** `pr-job-events.helpers.js`'s reducer gained a job-agnostic `dispatcherQueue` field (same extraction pattern as the existing pending counts). New `ActivityDrawerDispatcherSection.jsx` - a sibling to `ActivityDrawerQueueSection.jsx` (the bulk-chunk queue), not a rewrite of `ActivityDrawerSchedulerSection.jsx` (which keeps its own, narrower job: per-job-type status, not ordering) - renders the dispatcher's real `running -> due (priority desc) -> scheduled (soonest first)` order exactly as given, trusting the server's own sort.

**A real environment limitation found, not worked around:** Playwright's e2e server sets `VIEW_PRS_DISABLE_SCHEDULER_STARTUP=1` specifically to avoid real `gh`/network calls during tests - meaning `initializeScheduler()` (the only thing that seeds the dispatcher's registry) never runs there, so the new "Dispatcher queue" section is correctly, honestly empty (and hidden) in every e2e test. An assertion expecting it to be populated was added, found to fail for exactly this reason, and removed rather than worked around - the empty state here is the honest one, not a bug, and there's no existing e2e mechanism to safely populate the registry without risking a real `gh` call.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2268 tests: 734 backend + 1413 frontend + 121 others, all passing), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (40 tests, including the pre-existing real-SSE drawer test, now exercising the dispatcher's wiring end-to-end against a real running server with the scheduler disabled). A genuinely real-`gh`-dependent manual check (does a live, scheduler-enabled server actually drain a merged/closed PR within one tick instead of 30 minutes) was not attempted in this sandbox, consistent with this session's standing "no real `gh` network access here" constraint - the equivalent behavior is covered instead by `app.scheduler.test.js`'s mocked-script integration tests, which exercise the exact same production code path.

### Follow-up: a review pass found 4 real gaps between the dispatcher's documentation and its actual implementation (2026-10-07)

Asked directly to look for problems; a fresh adversarial read of the dispatcher code (as opposed to the implementation/testing pass that built it) turned up 4 real issues, none caught by the test suite because they're gaps between intent and wiring, not logic bugs within what was actually wired:

1. **`reserveGhSlots`/`releaseGhSlots` were fully built and unit-tested in isolation, but never called from anywhere.** The plan (and a code comment in `app.js`) described manual routes occupying a budget slot so the background dispatcher throttles around them - `getReservedGhSlots()` always returned `0` in practice, since no route ever called either function. Fixed by wiring both into every real `gh`-calling route in `view-prs-mutation-routes.js` (`/run`, `/run-auto`, `/quick-check`, `/quick-check-all`, `/ack`, `/merged/request-more`, `/labels`, `/labels/apply`) via the same injected-with-a-safe-fallback pattern `emitDataChangedSafe` already uses. `/run`/`/ack` reserve before their script call and release via `.finally()` on the existing promise chain (releasing the instant the actual child process exits, not after the subsequent bookkeeping, which does no `gh` work); the try/catch-shaped routes reserve before the try and release in a `finally`; `/run-auto`'s fire-and-forget call reserves immediately and releases once its own background promise settles. New regression tests in `app.routes.test.js` spy on the real `dispatcherHelpers.reserveGhSlots`/`releaseGhSlots` methods (not a full override - `runDispatcherTick` and the urgency-bump call sites still need the rest of that object) to confirm each route reserves and releases exactly once, including on failure and for a checkbox-only ack.
2. **The plan document overstated what the gh-process budget actually does.** It claimed `VIEW_PRS_DISPATCHER_GH_PROCESS_BUDGET` was "a single ceiling that `pickNextBatch` derives both concurrency and per-task `--jobs` from" - only the first half was ever implemented. The `--jobs` flag is still the separate, untouched `viewPrsQuickCheckAllJobs` constant. Corrected in the Config section above, with a note on why the stacking risk that line was describing turned out to already be handled by the sequential-task-type-group fix from the original round (at most one task type's `gh` processes run at a time now, which bounds the real worst case more directly than deriving `--jobs` ever would have).
3. **Silent, permanent task-type starvation if the budget is misconfigured.** `VIEW_PRS_DISPATCHER_GH_PROCESS_BUDGET` only floors at 1 in `app-config.js`, with no check against the dispatcher's own per-task-type cost estimates (4, for `autoRefresh`/`mergedDrain`). Set it to 1-3 and those two task types would never be selected again - `cost <= remaining` permanently false, forever, with nothing anywhere surfacing why background refresh/drain had silently stopped (`quickCheck`, cost 1, would keep running, masking it). Fixed in `app.js`: an `effectiveDispatcherGhProcessBudget` is clamped up to the highest per-task-type cost if the configured value would starve anything, with a `console.warn` naming the exact clamp that happened - makes the footgun impossible by construction rather than just documenting the risk. New isolated test file (`app.dispatcher-budget-clamp.test.js`, using `jest.resetModules()` + a fresh `require` before setting the env var, same precedent as `app.insights-hook-script.test.js`) confirms both the clamp-and-warn path and the no-op path when the configured value is already safe.
4. **Redundant disk reads on every tick.** `getOrInitRegistry()`'s existing-entry branch called `recomputeEntryConfig` -> `resolveEntryConfig` -> `getSchedulerRepoConfig()` (a synchronous `readUserDefaults()` file read) once per `(repo, taskType)` pair - 3 reads per repo, every ~5 seconds, for the life of the process. Fixed by having `getOrInitRegistry()`/`migrateLegacyPersistedState()` fetch `getSchedulerRepoConfig()` once per call and pass the result down, rather than `resolveEntryConfig` re-fetching it itself. New test confirms the config function is called exactly once per `getOrInitRegistry()` call regardless of repo count.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2278 tests: 742 backend + 1415 frontend + 121 others, all passing), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide.

### Follow-up: two more review findings, one of them a genuine resource leak (2026-10-07)

Asked directly for anything else that could go wrong; a second fresh pass over the now-fixed dispatcher turned up two more real, previously-unflagged issues:

1. **Removing a repo from tracking never cleaned up its dispatcher entries - they ran forever.** `getOrInitRegistry()` only ever added entries for repos in `getViewPrsAutoRefreshRepos()`'s current result; nothing ever pruned an entry whose repo had dropped out of that list (env var changed, stored PR data pruned). A stale entry would keep being selected, keep consuming gh-process budget, keep erroring with capped backoff if access was gone - and since `persistViewPrsSchedulerState` persists every entry with no filtering, it survived restarts too. A genuine unbounded leak across budget, memory, and disk. Fixed: `getOrInitRegistry()` now prunes any entry whose repo is no longer in the current tracked set, immediately after the add/recompute pass - except an entry still `isRunning`, which is left alone and pruned on the *next* call once it actually finishes (never yanked out from under its own in-flight run). New tests cover both the prune-on-drop case and the "running entry survives until it finishes, its non-running siblings don't wait for it" case.
2. **The manual-route reservation fix from the previous round undercounted `/run-auto`'s real concurrency.** `/run-auto` is the one manual route that calls `runViewPrsAutoRefresh` with no repo scope, fanning out across up to `viewPrsPrDiffConcurrency` (4) repos *in parallel* - the same multi-repo worker pool the dispatcher's own `autoRefresh` entries use. Reserving the flat `1` every other manual route reserves (correct for them - re-audited `/run` specifically and confirmed it's always exactly one `callRunViewPrsScript` invocation regardless of args, never the multi-repo path, so its own `1` was already accurate) under-represented `/run-auto`'s actual gh-process usage to the budget. Fixed by having `/run-auto` reserve the dispatcher's own `autoRefresh` cost estimate (4) instead of the default, via a new `runAutoGhReservationCost` param threaded from `app.js` (reusing the same cost map the dispatcher itself uses, rather than a second, disconnected guess). New test confirms the exact reserved/released amount, not just that reservation happened.

**A claim from the previous follow-up corrected, not just a new fix added:** that entry had said "the dispatcher's own reservation fix undercounts `/run` and `/run-auto`" - re-auditing `/run`'s actual code path during this fix showed that was only half right. `/run` never uses `runViewPrsAutoRefresh`'s parallel worker pool; it's always a single script invocation, so its flat `1` reservation was already correct and needed no change.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2281 tests, +3), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (40 tests).

## 🚧 **`#pr-numbers` off its own one-off bridge, closing the last "remaining known candidate" (2026-10-09)**

Closes the gap flagged at the end of the "row-sorting functions" slice above: `getSelectedPrNumbers`/`updateSelectedPrNumbers` (`index.page.js`), bridged onto `window.*` for `PrSelectionCell.jsx`'s bulk-select checkboxes, read/wrote the "#pr-numbers" field directly as a DOM input - the one field in the "Run Script options" group (`repo`/`limit`/`merged-limit`/`jobs`, all already Context-backed via `ContextRunScriptTextInput.jsx` since Phase 6) never migrated, specifically because of this entanglement: `getFormBody()` reads it via a real `new FormData(form)` call at "Run script" submission time, not through any JS code this migration could also update.

**Confirmed the entanglement doesn't actually block the obvious fix.** `repo`/`limit`/`jobs`/`mergedLimit` already prove the pattern works: `ContextRunScriptTextInput` renders a genuine, real DOM `<input>` (React doesn't float it in memory), so `FormData(form)` reads it correctly today for all 4 with zero special-casing - `getFormBody()` already includes `formProps.repo`/`formProps.limit`/etc. read exactly this way. Migrating `#pr-numbers` onto the same component needed no changes to `getFormBody()` at all.

**What moved:**

- `index.html`: the static `<input id="pr-numbers">` replaced with a `<span id="pr-numbers-root">` portal target, matching its 4 siblings.
- `react-app.jsx`: `FILTER_STATE_TEXT_FIELDS` gained a 5th entry (`key: 'prNumbersInput'` - `prNumbers` was already taken by the *separate*, unrelated `filter-pr-numbers` Context key, confirmed via grep before picking the name).
- New `helpers/pr-selected-pr-numbers.helpers.js` (bare exports, no DOM dependency, matching `pr-row-sorting.helpers.js`'s own precedent): `getSelectedPrNumbersFromInput`/`toggleSelectedPrNumber`, the pure parse/toggle logic that used to live in `updateSelectedPrNumbers`.
- `PrSelectionCell.jsx`: reads/writes the same `prNumbersInput` Context value via `window.getFilterStateValues()`/`window.setFilterStateValue()` - the *generic* bridge `FilterStateProvider` already exposes for vanilla code, not a new one-off bridge - rather than `useFilterState()`. Deliberate: this cell mounts once per visible table row, and a real Context subscription would re-render every row's checkbox on every keystroke in any of the 30+ other filter fields, not just this one. Kept `defaultChecked` (not `checked`), preserving the exact same one-way, read-once-at-mount behavior the old `window.getSelectedPrNumbers`/`updateSelectedPrNumbers` bridge had - a live two-way sync was considered and deliberately not added, since it wasn't present before either and the performance tradeoff above argues against ever making this cell Context-reactive.
- `index.page.js`: `getPrNumbersInput`/`getSelectedPrNumbers`/`setSelectedPrNumbers`/`handlePrNumbersInputChange`/`updateSelectedPrNumbers` deleted outright (zero other callers, confirmed via grep); `"pr-numbers"` added to `FILTER_STATE_FIELD_MAP`, `getUiOptionDefaults()`, and the 3 persist/restore id lists (`persistUiOptionOverrides`'s `textIds`, `restoreUiOptionOverrides`'s matching list, `persistRunScriptOptionOverrides`) - the same 4-list pattern every sibling field in this group already follows. The 2 direct `addEventListener("input"/"change", handlePrNumbersInputChange)` calls removed too - that handler's one effect (clearing a whitespace-only value) had zero test coverage and would have become a hidden-DOM-mutation bug once the field was React-controlled (writing to `.value` directly without going through `onChange` - exactly the class of bug this migration exists to avoid), so it was dropped rather than ported.

**Test-harness update, not a behavior change:** `index.html.test.js` never mounts React - it simulates every Context-backed field by injecting the exact markup React would have rendered into each `-root` span (`injectRunFilterFieldElements`). Added the matching `pr-numbers-root` entry; all 8 pre-existing tests touching `#pr-numbers` (`getFormBody`/autofill-hint assertions) passed unmodified once that one injection existed, confirming - as expected - that none of them actually depended on `getSelectedPrNumbers`/`updateSelectedPrNumbers` at all, only on the raw DOM input.

**New coverage:** `pr-selected-pr-numbers.helpers.test.js` (the pure toggle logic), `PrSelectionCell.test.jsx` (rewritten from `window.*` stubs to `window.getFilterStateValues`/`setFilterStateValue` stubs, plus a new "no provider mounted yet" default-to-unchecked case the old bridge-fallback version didn't have), and a new Playwright test exercising the real entanglement end-to-end: checks PR #1's row-select checkbox in a real browser, switches to the Run & Filter tab, and confirms the real `#pr-numbers` `<input>` now reads "1" - the exact mechanism `getFormBody()`'s `FormData` read depends on.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2352 tests, +9), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide (the new helper module needed adding to `eslint.config.mjs`'s ES-module allow-list, same as every prior bare-export helper), full Playwright smoke suite (41 tests, +1).

With this, every `window.*` bridge flagged as a "remaining known candidate" across this session's Phase 7 slices is closed. What's left on `index.page.js`'s `Object.assign(window, {...})` block (per that block's own inline comments) is either a documented, deliberate deferral (Author Insights draft dirty-state, the render-blocking enforcement pipeline, `AuthorInsightsPrDataMeta.jsx`'s Track C cluster, `ExportTab.jsx`'s intentionally-DOM-based `<details>`-state helpers) or a genuine remaining entanglement not yet investigated this session (none currently identified) - a fresh inventory pass, not assumed from this list, would be the right next step before picking the next slice.

### Follow-up: `applyNonCredentialFieldHints()` never actually ran against any of these 5 fields in production (2026-10-09)

A review pass on the slice above found this wasn't a new regression - it predates this session's own change and affected `repo`/`limit`/`merged-limit`/`jobs` too, just newly extended to a 5th field by migrating `pr-numbers` the same way. `applyNonCredentialFieldHints()` is called once, synchronously, from `initPage()` - which completes before react-app.jsx's own module graph has necessarily finished loading, so these 5 React-portaled fields may not exist in the DOM yet on that first pass; `getOptionalElementById` silently finds nothing, with no retry (unlike the backfill status/log bridges a few lines above it, which already re-fire on `'viewprs:react-ready'` for exactly this reason). `index.html.test.js` couldn't have caught this for any of the 5 - its test harness injects the equivalent markup synchronously, before `initPage()` ever runs, so the race never manifests there.

**Fixed** by adding a third `'viewprs:react-ready'` listener (`index.page.js`) alongside the two existing backfill ones, re-running `applyNonCredentialFieldHints()` once React has actually mounted. Confirmed this is a real, previously-broken behavior (not a hypothetical) by temporarily disabling the new listener and re-running the new Playwright test below - it failed with `autocomplete=""` instead of `"off"` on a real page load, then passed again once restored.

**A review pass caught the new test only covering 5 of `NON_CREDENTIAL_HINT_FIELD_IDS`'s 6 entries** - `filter-pr-numbers` (`PrNumberFilterInput.jsx`, Phase 2) is React-portaled too and subject to the identical race, correctly covered by the fix itself (which re-runs the whole function, not a per-field list) but missing from the test's own assertions. Extended the test to check all 6.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2352 tests, unchanged - this fix has no jest-level coverage, consistent with the sibling backfill bridges' own lack of it; the race it closes isn't reproducible under `index.html.test.js`'s synchronous-injection harness), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (42 tests, +1 - a new test asserting all 6 `NON_CREDENTIAL_HINT_FIELD_IDS` fields' `autocomplete`/`data-lpignore` attributes after a real page load, which is the only way to exercise this race at all).

- `collectPrAuthors` - the one name in this batch that genuinely needed real work, not a direct import: it takes `getPreferredActorKey` as a DI param, so `PrAuthorCell.jsx` now calls `createPrAuthorCellHelpers({ getPreferredActorKey })` itself, threaded from `useActorIdentity()` via a `useMemo` - same pattern `insights/NotesSection.jsx`'s `noteAuthorMatchesSelection` wiring already established. Confirmed this doesn't introduce a new per-row Context-subscription performance risk (the class of concern that shaped `PrSelectionCell.jsx`'s own earlier migration): `PrAuthorCell.jsx` already renders `<ActorIdentity>` as a child, which was *already* subscribing to `ActorIdentityContext` on every row - adding a second subscription at the parent doesn't change that re-render profile.

Kept the factory exactly as it was and added a fully separate bare `postJson` export (using the real global `fetch`) for React's use instead of risking that test's isolation.

## 🚧 **"Track C" wasn't actually one atomic unit - closed, plus a real latent bug fixed (2026-10-09)**

Re-investigated the "Track C" cluster (`toCount`/`parseMarkerState`/`formatChkDisplay`/`getViewedFilesSummary`, deferred as one atomic unit in `AuthorInsightsPrDataMeta.jsx` across several earlier entries) by tracing each name to its actual source factory, rather than taking the earlier "atomic unit" framing at face value - the same "re-verify directly against source" discipline sub-phases 7.4/7.6 already used once each to find a stale blocker claim. All 4 turned out to be genuinely zero-dependency:

- `toCount` - `createPrFormattingHelpers()`, zero args, already directly imported by several other components.
- `formatChkDisplay` - the same zero-arg `createPrStatusDisplayHelpers()` whose sibling exports (`isChangedStatus`/`statusIcon`) are already direct imports elsewhere.
- `getViewedFilesSummary` - `createPrViewedFilesSummaryHelpers({ toCount })`, the *exact* composition `PrInsightsDisplayProvider.jsx` already builds at module scope for its own use.

Confirmed via grep this file was the only remaining `window.X` consumer for all 4, so no other component needed updating. Built all 3 (not `parseMarkerState` - see the bug below) directly at module scope in `AuthorInsightsPrDataMeta.jsx` itself, matching `PrInsightsDisplayProvider.jsx`'s own precedent - no Context/hook needed, so no new per-row subscription either.

**A real, previously-masked bug fixed, not just carried over:** sub-phase 7.0's own writeup had already flagged this and deferred the fix "whenever Track C is tackled." The old call was `formatChkDisplay(chkState, row?.failureCount)`, where `chkState` was the *already-parsed* marker value (e.g. `"PASS"`) from a separate `parseMarkerState(row?.titleDisplay, 'CHK')` call. But `formatChkDisplay`'s real signature takes the *raw* `titleDisplay` string and does its own `/\[CHK:([^\]]+)\]/` extraction internally (the identical regex `parseMarkerState` had just redundantly applied) - and takes only one argument, not two. Passing an already-extracted bare state like `"PASS"` through that same regex never matches (no brackets to find), so this silently rendered `"-"` for every PR, in production, the entire time - masked only because this component's own test stubbed `window.formatChkDisplay` with a differently-shaped mock that didn't reproduce the real function's actual parsing. Fixed by calling `formatChkDisplay(row?.titleDisplay)` directly; `parseMarkerState` is no longer needed in this component at all as a result (its own import/factory call in `index.page.js` was removed entirely too, having no other internal caller).

**New regression coverage:** two new test cases proving the fix - a `titleDisplay` with a real `[CHK:PASS]` marker now renders `"CHK: ✅ PASS"` (confirmed this fails against the pre-fix code, always `"-"`, and passes against the fix), and one with no marker correctly still renders `"CHK: -"`. The existing "status/approved/CHK/conversations" test's `getViewedFilesSummary` mock was also replaced with real `viewedFilesCount`/`changedFilesCount` fixture fields, since the real function is no longer mockable via `window.*`.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2376 tests, +2), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (43 tests, unchanged - the existing Author Insights notes-section test already exercises this component against real PR rows in a real browser).

With this, every name flagged across this session's "fresh inventory" passes is resolved - `recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields`/`getAvailableRepoLabels`/`runSinglePrUpdate` remain the only 3 confirmed-genuinely-deferred names on `index.page.js`'s bridge, alongside `getAuthorThreadResolutionPolicy` and the Export tab/auto-render-blocked-links entanglements (both pre-existing, re-confirmed, not new).

## 🚧 **`getAvailableRepoLabels` closed - consolidating two bridges for one list into one Context (2026-10-09)**

Re-investigated `getAvailableRepoLabels` (left deferred in the previous entry as "closes over a mutable module-level variable") rather than taking that framing as final. It's true the underlying data (`availableRepoLabels`, fetched by `refreshAvailableRepoLabels()` via `GET /view-prs/labels`) is genuinely mutable, async, vanilla-owned state - but reading further found this exact data already had a **second**, independent `window.*` bridge for the same list: `window.updateReactApplyLabelOptions`, a push into `AppRoot`'s own local `useState`, feeding only the Run & Filter tab's `ApplyLabelSelect.jsx` dropdown. `PrActionsCell.jsx`'s per-row dropdown used the *other* bridge (`window.getAvailableRepoLabels`, a pull). Two separate delivery mechanisms for one underlying list, neither aware of the other, is exactly the kind of entanglement this migration's Context-based approach exists to collapse - not a reason to leave both in place.

**What moved:** new `state/RepoLabelsContext.jsx` + `components/RepoLabelsProvider.jsx` (same shape as `NotesDirtyContext`/`NotesDirtyProvider`), mounted wrapping `FilterStateProvider`'s entire subtree in `react-app.jsx` (needs to reach both `ApplyLabelSelect`'s portal and `PrTableApp`'s `PrActionsCell` - siblings, not an ancestor/descendant pair, same reasoning `PrActivityQueueProvider` already documents for its own wrapping scope). `index.page.js`'s `refreshAvailableRepoLabels()` keeps the actual fetch and its own module-scope cache unchanged - `populateApplyLabelSelect()` now pushes into the Context via a new dedicated bridge (`window.setAvailableRepoLabels`) instead of the old `window.updateReactApplyLabelOptions`, matching `NotesDirtyProvider`'s own "a dedicated bridge, not `FilterStateProvider`'s generic one" reasoning - this is genuinely new, distinct state, not an existing `FILTER_STATE_FIELD_MAP` field. `ApplyLabelSelect.jsx` reads `useRepoLabels()` directly now instead of taking a `labels` prop (it's logically still a descendant of the Provider in React's component tree even though `createPortal` renders it to a different DOM location - Context resolution follows the component tree, not the portaled DOM position). `PrActionsCell.jsx` reads the same hook instead of the old pull bridge. `AppRoot`'s own `applyLabelOptions` local state and its `window.updateReactApplyLabelOptions` effect are both retired entirely.

**A performance consideration checked, not assumed safe:** `PrActionsCell.jsx` is a per-row component - adding a Context subscription there raises the same re-render-frequency question `PrSelectionCell.jsx`'s own `FilterStateContext` migration (and `PrAuthorCell.jsx`'s `collectPrAuthors` one) already had to answer. `availableRepoLabels` only changes on a repo switch or an explicit "Reload labels" click - nowhere near `PrDataContext`'s per-poll-tick or `FilterStateContext`'s per-keystroke change frequency - so this is a low-risk addition, not a repeat of the high-frequency case `PrSelectionCell.jsx` deliberately avoided subscribing to.

**New coverage closing a real gap:** neither old bridge had any test proving one update reached its own consumer, let alone both at once. `RepoLabelsProvider.test.jsx` mounts the real Provider with both real consumers together (matching `NotesDirtyProvider.test.jsx`'s own "mount the real wiring, not a mock" precedent), proving one `window.setAvailableRepoLabels` call reaches both, that `PrActionsCell.jsx`'s existing-label exclusion still works alongside it, and that a non-array argument falls back safely. A new Playwright test (this whole cluster had zero prior e2e coverage - confirmed by grep) overrides the suite's existing empty-labels route mock, clicks the real "⟲ Labels" button, and confirms both the Run tab's and a real PR row's own dropdown show the same fetched labels, with that row's pre-existing fixture label (`"enhancement"`, confirmed in `e2e/fixtures/data.json`) correctly excluded from its own dropdown but still present in the Run tab's.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2381 tests, +5), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (44 tests, +1).

Only `recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields` and `runSinglePrUpdate` remain as confirmed-genuinely-deferred names on `index.page.js`'s bridge now, alongside `getAuthorThreadResolutionPolicy` and the Export tab/auto-render-blocked-links entanglements.

## 🔎 **`runSinglePrUpdate` properly scoped - confirmed genuinely blocked, not stale (2026-10-09)**

Asked directly to scope `runSinglePrUpdate` for a real React-native rewrite (a research-first pass, matching sub-phase 7.3's own "read everything before designing" discipline) - given this session's track record of finding several *other* "deferred" claims stale under closer inspection (Track C, `getAvailableRepoLabels`), this one got the same scrutiny rather than being assumed hard from its own prior one-line note.

**Traced every DI param of `createPrSinglePrUpdateHelpers`** (`pr-single-pr-update.helpers.js`): most are trivially resolvable now - `postJson`/`defaultRepo` are already bare exports elsewhere in this session's own work, `stripAnsi`/`getGithubAuthFailureHint`/`formatCommandOutputWithAuthHint` are plain pure functions. The real blocker is the function's own final step: `renderPrData(latestData, repo, {...})`.

**Confirmed fresh, not inherited, that `renderPrData` is still genuinely load-bearing:** read its *current* body directly rather than trusting sub-phase 7.2's now-2-weeks-old note that it's "the entry point for the entire vanilla side-effect pipeline." Much of what that note described has since moved to React (Author Insights, Stats View, the 9 filter dropdowns) - but `renderPrData` still unconditionally calls `prDataTabOrchestrator.renderPrData(...)` on every invocation, and that call is where the **PR filtering pipeline** (scope/PR-number/label/author/assigned/approver filters) still lives, fully vanilla. Its output (`filteredRows`) becomes `visiblePrNumbers` - the exact prop that tells the React table which PRs to actually show. Calling `PrDataProvider`'s own `setPayload` directly (skipping `renderPrData` entirely, the obvious "make it a hook" move) would mean the filter pipeline never re-runs on a single-PR update - the table would silently stop respecting the user's active filters the moment one PR refreshes.

**This is the same wall, not a new one:** sub-phase 7.2's own follow-up entry already investigated converting this exact filtering chain to a reactive `useMemo` and explicitly declined - not because it's hard, but because it would make filters apply *live* instead of only on an explicit "Apply filters" click, a real user-visible behavior change this migration's own discipline says isn't a call to make as a side effect of an architecture-cleanup slice. `runSinglePrUpdate` hits that identical, already-deliberately-parked decision from a different angle.

**No partial/consolation slice found worth doing either:** swapping just `postJson`/`defaultRepo` into direct imports wouldn't close the `window.runSinglePrUpdate` bridge itself (the function still needs calling that way from `PrTableApp.jsx`'s `handleUpdatePrBusy`) or meaningfully simplify anything on its own - not worth a slice with no real payoff.

**Conclusion: the original deferral was accurate.** Recorded here, with the exact mechanism (not just "it's complicated"), so a future session doesn't re-research this from scratch - and so that if the filtering-to-Context behavior-change decision is ever revisited (a product decision, not an engineering one), `runSinglePrUpdate`, `recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields`, and sub-phase 7.1/7.2's remaining scope would all likely unblock together, not independently.

Extracted into new bare-export `helpers/pr-viewed-files-state.helpers.js`; `PrStatusCell.jsx` imports it directly instead of reading `window.getViewedFilesState`.
