**Verified:** full jest suite (2384 tests, +4), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (44 tests, unchanged).

## 🚧 **ROADMAP.md's checkbox on-failure-revert bug: re-investigated, found already stale (2026-10-10)**

Picked up ROADMAP.md's documented bug ("In Review/Flagged checkbox's on-failure revert is dead code") to fix. The documented mechanism was real as far as it went: `handleCheckboxChange`'s `mockCheckbox = { checked }` object is genuinely never read back, so `toggleInReviewForRow`/`toggleFlaggedForRow`'s own `checkbox.checked = !nextValue` revert-on-failure never reaches the real, fully-controlled checkbox.

Implemented the documented fix (re-add a `getLatestStoredPayload` getter to `handleCheckboxChange`'s failure branch only, to force a re-push of the unchanged payload) and wrote a Playwright regression test for it (`e2e/smoke.spec.js`, "a failed In Review toggle reverts the checkbox..."). Following this session's "verify the test actually catches the regression" discipline, disabled the fix (`if (false && currentPayload)`) and reran the test - **it still passed.**

Root cause: `toggleInReviewForRow`/`toggleFlaggedForRow` already call `setStatusTextOnly` unconditionally (both before the fetch and again on failure), which calls `window.updateReactStatusText` - wired in `react-app.jsx` to a local `useState` setter inside `AppRoot`. `PrTableApp` is rendered as a non-memoized child of `AppRoot` (via an inline `createPortal(...)` call re-evaluated every `AppRoot` render), so this pre-existing status-text update already triggers a cascading re-render that re-derives `isInReview`/`isFlagged` from the unchanged `payload` on every toggle attempt, success or failure - independently of any extra re-push. Confirmed empirically by stashing the entire fix and rerunning the Playwright test against the true original, unmodified code: it still passed.

**Conclusion: the ROADMAP.md claim was accurate about the mock-object mechanism but stale about the user-visible bug** - the checkbox never actually gets stuck, because an unrelated pre-existing side effect already forces the correcting re-render. Reverted the fix (`react-callbacks.helpers.js`, its tests, and `index.page.js`'s `stateGetters` addition) as redundant - same "don't keep code whose only job is duplicating an existing effect" reasoning as elsewhere in this migration. Kept the Playwright test, since it's a legitimate behavioral regression test (checkbox must not get stuck) regardless of which mechanism satisfies it. ROADMAP.md's item marked `[x]` with this finding recorded inline.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2384 tests, unchanged), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (45 tests, +1).

## 🚧 **Live filtering: the PR table's row-filtering is now a reactive Context derivation (2026-10-10)**

Implemented the filtering-to-Context conversion sub-phase 7.2/7.5 both explicitly parked (a real, user-visible behavior change - filters now apply live instead of only on an explicit "Apply filters" click - asked for directly, not a side effect of a cleanup). Landed as 2 sequential slices, each independently verified, per this migration's established discipline.

**Slice A - lifted the 5 row-filtering multi-selects' checked-state into Context.** The one genuine gap: every other filter criterion (scope, PR-number, alwaysShowInReview, the 6 "Any" filters) was already real Context state in `FilterStateProvider`; the include/exclude-label/author/assigned/approver multi-selects' checked-state lived only in each `MultiSelectCheckboxList` instance's own local `useState`, with no way for a reactive derivation to read "currently checked." New `state/RowFilterSelectionContext.jsx`/`RowFilterSelectionProvider.jsx` own this as real Context state; `MultiSelectCheckboxList.jsx` became mode-aware (optional `checkedSet`/`onToggle` props - the 4 non-row-filtering lists are untouched, still local state); `MultiSelectListPortals.jsx` mirrors its existing `seedCheckedState` seeding pass into the new Context for just those 5 lists, reusing that logic as-is. Mounted as a shared ancestor in `react-app.jsx` wrapping both the filter-portals tree and the PrTableApp portal (sibling portals, same reason `RepoLabelsProvider` already wraps both). No visible behavior change in this slice by itself - pure state relocation.

**Slice B - the actual live derivation.** Extracted `rowMatchesUiFilters` (previously inline in `index.page.js`) into its own DI'd module, `helpers/pr-row-match-filters.helpers.js`, matching the sibling `pr-scope-selection.helpers.js`/`pr-row-filtering.helpers.js` pattern - same closures wired into the vanilla side unchanged. New `helpers/pr-visible-pr-numbers.helpers.js` composes the existing pure helpers (`resolveScopedRows`/`buildRowFilterCriteria`/`applyRowUiFilters`/`rowMatchesUiFilters`) into one `computeVisiblePrNumbers` function. New `state/useVisiblePrNumbers.jsx` is the hook that wires it to Context - called directly inside `PrTableApp` (not through an intermediate "bridge" component pushing into `PrDataProvider`'s state), deliberately avoiding the stable-children reconciliation-bailout bug class `NeedsAttentionProvider` had to work around previously. Builds `collectAssignedUsers`/`collectApproversFromRow` from `useActorIdentity()`'s Context-derived functions (already reactive, from sub-phase 7.0) instead of index.page.js's own mutable `currentActorLoginAliases`/`currentViewerLogin` module `let`s, which aren't reachable from React. `PrTableApp.jsx` now calls `useVisiblePrNumbers()` instead of reading `visiblePrNumbers` off `usePrData()`.

**A real design tension found and resolved:** `useVisiblePrNumbers` initially called the throwing `useFilterState()` hook - correct for the real app (always has a `<FilterStateProvider>` ancestor) but broke `integration-tests/index.html.test.js`'s isolated `PrTableApp`-only mount, which deliberately never mounts one (it exercises `index.page.js`'s vanilla DOM-read pipeline directly). Tried mounting a `FilterStateProvider` there too - reverted after it broke 6 unrelated persisted-restore tests elsewhere in that same huge shared test file, since `FilterStateProvider` installs *global* `window.getFilterStateValues`/`setFilterStateValue` bridges that `restoreUiOptionOverrides` and other DOM-fallback call sites then prefer over the plain DOM writes those tests assert on directly - with no real input components mounted in that harness to keep Context in sync with the DOM. Fixed properly by having `useVisiblePrNumbers` read `FilterStateContext` directly (not the throwing hook) with safe "no filters active" defaults, matching `NeedsAttentionContext`/`ActorIdentityContext`'s own established permissive-default pattern - works correctly with or without a real Provider ancestor. One `index.html.test.js` test (`"...PR-number filter takes precedence"`) genuinely couldn't be satisfied by the reduced harness any more (PR-number-filter precedence over the table specifically needs Context reachability this harness doesn't wire up) - adjusted its table-visibility assertion to match what IS reachable there (the 4 multi-select filters alone), with the precedence behavior itself covered where it's actually reachable (a unit test in `pr-visible-pr-numbers.helpers.test.js`, and new Playwright coverage against the real, fully-wired app).

**Deliberately out of scope, documented not silently dropped:** the "Applied filters" chip/summary text (`AppliedFilterSummary.jsx`) still only updates on the old debounced/click cadence - it's fed via a separate `window.renderReactFilterSummary` → local `useState` push bridge, not Context, and has its own non-pure `renderSchedulerStatus` side effect baked into `deriveRenderSummary`. May lag the now-live table briefly; converting it is a separable follow-up. The vanilla pipeline's own `visiblePrNumbers` computation (`index.page.js` ~3109-3141) and `window.updateReactPrTable`'s 3rd argument are now dead for the table but left wired rather than ripped out (documented inline at `PrDataProvider.jsx`'s state field) - removing them is naturally scoped together with `runSinglePrUpdate`/`recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields` finally becoming real React-native hooks, now unblocked by this change (a single-PR payload update can go straight through `PrDataProvider`'s existing `setPayload` and let `useVisiblePrNumbers` recompute automatically) but not bundled into this change.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2427 tests, +43), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (47 tests, +2 - a PR-number-filter-live test and a multi-select-live test, both proving the table updates with zero "Apply filters" click).

**2 real bugs found via code review right after this landed, both fixed:**

1. **`isInReviewEnabled` was reimplemented incorrectly in `useVisiblePrNumbers.jsx`** (`Boolean(row?.inReview)` instead of `index.page.js`'s real, string-tolerant version). `inReview` is stored as a **string** throughout the real data pipeline (`view-prs-data-helpers.js`'s own `inReview: "false"` default; `view-prs-mutation-route-helpers.js`'s `toTrimmedString` on every write) - `Boolean("false")` is `true` in JS, so this wasn't a theoretical edge case, it was the *normal* data shape. Net effect: with `alwaysShowInReview` checked, the new live-filtering React path treated essentially every PR as "in review," short-circuiting every other active filter (label/author/assigned/approver/the 6 "Any" filters) for rows that should have been excluded - a real correctness regression the vanilla/click-apply path never had, since it still called the real, unmodified function. The same wrong assumption had been copy-pasted into both new unit test files' fixtures, which is exactly why neither caught it. Fixed by copying the real function's exact logic verbatim; added explicit string-`"false"`/string-`"true"` regression tests to both test files (`pr-row-match-filters.helpers.test.js`, `pr-visible-pr-numbers.helpers.test.js`) that would have caught this immediately.
2. **No debounce on the new live derivation's one free-text input.** `PrNumberFilterInput.jsx` writes to Context on every keystroke (only the old *side-effect* trigger was ever debounced, not the value itself) - `useVisiblePrNumbers` read that raw, undebounced value directly, so typing a multi-digit PR number (e.g. "12") visibly flickered through wrong intermediate matches (briefly showing PR #1 before showing PR #12), a real user-visible UX regression no existing or new test caught (all assertions checked only the *final* state after `fill(...)`, never an intermediate frame). Fixed with a new, generically-reusable `state/useDebouncedValue.jsx` hook (same 150ms as `FilterStateProvider`'s own debounce), applied *only* to `filterPrNumbers` - every other field this hook reads is a discrete click/selection (scope, the 5 multi-selects, the 6 "Any" selects), not prone to the same rapid-fire-keystroke churn, so applying this generically to every field wasn't needed or done.

**Verified again after both fixes:** full jest suite (2434 tests, +7), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (47 tests, unchanged - confirmed Playwright's own auto-retrying assertions absorb the new 150ms debounce fine).

## 🚧 **Scheduler state lifted into Context - the one missing piece for AppliedFilterSummary's live conversion (2026-10-10)**

Looked into converting `AppliedFilterSummary`'s chip/summary text (the one deliberately-deferred exception from the live-filtering work above) to a live Context derivation too. Found it needs one more piece of infrastructure first: the chip text's scheduler-summary portion (`Auto every Nm | Last manual: ... | Last auto: ...`) is sourced from `latestSchedulerState`, a vanilla module-level `let` in `index.page.js` - not reactive anywhere, even though `JobEventsProvider.jsx` already receives this exact data live over SSE. It just never held onto it: every snapshot/job/scheduler frame it receives only ever got forwarded through a `window.renderSchedulerStatus` bridge call, one-way, into the vanilla variable.

Landed just this one piece, as its own bounded, independently-useful slice (matches this migration's "don't cram multiple concerns into one slice" discipline - the actual chip-text hook itself, and swapping `AppliedFilterSummary` off its `window.renderReactFilterSummary` push bridge, is still a separate follow-up, not done here):

- `helpers/pr-job-events.helpers.js` - new `extractSchedulerSummary(scheduler, previous)`, matching the exact same pattern as the existing `extractDispatcherQueue`/`extractOpenAutoCircuitRepos`/`extractPendingCounts` (same "read off every envelope's own bundled scheduler object" category). Extracts `intervalMinutes`/`manualCooldownMinutes`/`lastManualRunAt`/`lastAutoRunAt`/`lastAutoSkipReason`/`lastAutoError` - the exact fields `pr-applied-summary.helpers.js`'s `buildAppliedSummaryViewModel` needs for its scheduler-summary string. Field-by-field "keep previous value if this envelope didn't carry it" merge (not a blanket replace), since job-agnostic `scheduler`-type envelopes and per-job envelopes don't always carry every field. Wired into `getInitialJobEventsState`/`applyJobEventsSnapshot`/`applyJobEvent`'s existing state shape as a new `schedulerSummary` object, alongside the other scheduler-snapshot fields.
- `JobEventsContext.jsx` needed no changes - its default value already spreads `getInitialJobEventsState()`, so `schedulerSummary` is included automatically; any `useJobEvents()` consumer can now read `schedulerSummary` reactively.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2438 tests, +4 new + the existing 21 `pr-job-events.helpers.test.js` tests confirmed still passing unchanged, proving this was a pure additive change), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (47 tests, unchanged - including the activity-drawer/real-SSE test, confirming the drawer's own existing scheduler-badge consumers are unaffected).

## 🚧 **"Applied filters" chip/summary text is now live too, closing out the last documented exception from the live-filtering work (2026-10-10)**

Finished the follow-up flagged above. `AppliedFilterSummary`'s chip/summary text now updates the instant any filter changes, with no "Apply filters (local)" click and no debounced-pipeline round-trip - the last remaining place the live-filtering slices still deferred to the old cadence.

**`pr-visible-pr-numbers.helpers.js`'s `computeVisiblePrNumbers` now returns `{ visiblePrNumbers, scopeLabel }`** instead of a bare array - `scopeLabel` (e.g. "all stored rows"/"needs attention rows") falls out of the exact same scope-resolution pass for free, needed for the chip text's "scope=..." entry. Updated its own test file and `state/useVisiblePrNumbers.jsx`'s single call site accordingly - zero behavior change for the table itself.

**`state/useVisiblePrNumbers.jsx` split into two exports**: the shared derivation is now `useFilteredPrRows()` (returns the full `{ visiblePrNumbers, scopeLabel }`), with `useVisiblePrNumbers()` (unchanged for `PrTableApp.jsx`'s sake) narrowed to a one-line wrapper returning just `.visiblePrNumbers`. Avoids running the whole scope+filter composition twice per render for two different consumers.

**New `state/useAppliedFilterSummary.jsx`** composes `useFilteredPrRows()` + `RowFilterSelectionContext`'s checked multi-selects (run through the existing, reusable `pr-selected-filters.helpers.js`'s `buildSelectedFiltersViewModel` for the comma-joined display strings) + `FilterStateContext`'s `openMode`/`alwaysShowInReview` + `useJobEvents().schedulerSummary` (the piece lifted into Context in the prerequisite slice above) - into the existing, reusable `pr-applied-summary.helpers.js`'s `buildAppliedSummaryViewModel`, unchanged.

**`react-app.jsx`**: removed the `window.renderReactFilterSummary` registration effect and its backing `filterSummary` local `useState` entirely (genuinely dead now, unlike the "leave it wired" choices elsewhere in this migration - nothing else reads this local state). New `AppliedFilterSummaryLive` wrapper component (hooks can't be called mid-JSX) calls `useAppliedFilterSummary()` and renders the existing `AppliedFilterSummary` presentational component with its result. `index.page.js`'s own vanilla computation of the same text still runs and still calls the now-nonexistent `window.renderReactFilterSummary` - its own existing `typeof === "function"` guard makes that a harmless no-op, left wired per this migration's established "don't rip out the vanilla computation mid-slice" pattern (same choice as `visiblePrNumbers`'s own now-dead vanilla computation) - decoupling `deriveRenderSummary`'s other side effect (`renderSchedulerStatus`, still needed for the per-row progress indicator/`#request-activity-badges`) from the chip-text computation specifically is a separate, future cleanup.

**`JobEventsProvider` widened** from "just the activity-drawer portal" (a prior comment on this exact line predicted this) to wrap everything `RowFilterSelectionProvider` does - `useAppliedFilterSummary` is now consumed by both the `prTable` portal and the `appliedFilterSummary` portal, sibling portals relative to the drawer, the same cross-portal-sharing reason every other Provider at that nesting level is already this wide.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2443 tests, +5 new - `useAppliedFilterSummary.test.jsx`), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (48 tests, +1 - a new test confirming the chip text updates live for both the PR-number filter and a multi-select, with no "Apply filters" click, including a real-browser check that the activity drawer's own real-SSE test still passes after widening `JobEventsProvider`'s scope).

**1 real bug found via code review right after this landed, fixed:** `useAppliedFilterSummary.jsx` read `values.filterPrNumbers` raw/undebounced for the chip text's `pr-numbers=...` entry, while `rowsCount` came from `useFilteredPrRows()`, which debounces that same value internally (the earlier debounce fix). While typing a multi-digit PR number, the two could visibly disagree with each other for up to 150ms (e.g. `pr-numbers=12 | Rows: 1`, since `Rows` still reflected the old, settled filter) - directly contradicting this hook's own doc comment ("this text never disagrees with what's actually shown"). Fixed by exporting `FILTER_PR_NUMBERS_DEBOUNCE_MS` from `useVisiblePrNumbers.jsx` and debouncing `filterPrNumbers`-for-display in `useAppliedFilterSummary.jsx` the exact same way, so both numbers settle together. Added a fake-timers regression test asserting the mid-debounce text never shows a mismatched pair - confirmed it fails without the fix (reproduces the exact `pr-numbers=12 | Rows: 1` bug) and passes with it.

**Verified again after the fix:** full jest suite (2444 tests, +1), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (48 tests, unchanged).

**A second, more significant finding from the same review pass:** `JobEventsContext`'s `schedulerSummary` default (`getInitialJobEventsState`) is a hardcoded placeholder (`intervalMinutes: 15, lastAutoRunAt: null, ...`), not real data - until this connection's first real SSE `snapshot` event arrives, `useAppliedFilterSummary`'s chip text would briefly show that placeholder (e.g. "Last auto: -") instead of the truth. The vanilla pipeline never had this gap, because `deriveRenderSummary` (`pr-render-summary.helpers.js:40`) reads `payload?.scheduler` directly - a field the server already embeds in every `/view-prs/data` response (`view-prs-data-response-helpers.js:25`, the exact same `getViewPrsSchedulerPublicState()` shape the SSE `scheduler` envelope carries) - and `payload` is already real, reactive `PrDataProvider` state with correct data from the very first fetch. Not a reason to revert the SSE-based `schedulerSummary` work, though - SSE genuinely updates *between* polls, which fits this round's "live" theme better than data only as fresh as the last 30s poll. Fixed by having `JobEventsProvider.jsx` fall back to `payload.scheduler` (normalized through the same, now-exported `extractSchedulerSummary`) only until `lastSnapshotAt` confirms a real SSE frame has arrived - from then on the live SSE value is preferred, same as before. Also had to switch from the throwing `usePrData()` hook to reading `PrDataContext` directly (same permissive pattern `useVisiblePrNumbers.jsx` already uses) - `JobEventsProvider`'s own existing test file mounts it standalone, with no `<PrDataProvider>` ancestor.

**Verified again:** full jest suite (2447 tests, +3 - including a test proving the fallback is correctly superseded once a real snapshot arrives), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (48 tests, unchanged).

## 🚧 **2 more window.\* bridges closed: `#data-meta` and the merged-request-more-action button are now live too (2026-10-10)**

Asked to continue Phase 7. Scoped `runSinglePrUpdate`/`recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields` first (both still deferred per the "properly scoped" entry above) - since the live-filtering work made the vanilla render pipeline's own `visiblePrNumbers` dead for the table, that entry's own closing note suggested those two might now be unblocked. A research pass (tracing `prDataTabOrchestrator.renderPrData`'s actual current side effects, not trusting the orchestrator's own stale doc comment) found the picture is more mixed than hoped: filter-dropdown population, filter chips/summary, and `visiblePrNumbers`/`filteredRows` are indeed all dead/no-ops already - but `runSinglePrUpdate` specifically calls `loadStoredData()` unconditionally right after its own direct `renderPrData` call (a pre-existing, separate redundancy, out of scope to fix here), and `loadStoredData()` itself always re-invokes the full orchestrator pipeline regardless of what the first call does - so bypassing the orchestrator in `runSinglePrUpdate`'s own first call wouldn't actually remove the orchestrator from that flow. Not a clean win; left `runSinglePrUpdate`/`recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields` deferred, unchanged.

Found a genuinely clean, independently-useful slice instead, in the same category as the chip-text conversion: of the orchestrator's two remaining real (non-dead) side effects, both turned out to be trivially convertible to live Context derivations themselves, the same way the chip text was:

- **`#data-meta`** (`window.updateReactDataMetaText`) turns out to already show the *exact same string* as the "Applied filters" chip text - both were fed the same `appliedSummaryText` by the vanilla pipeline's `applyRenderResults` (`pr-render-apply.helpers.js:85`/`:87-90`). `DataMetaTextLive` (`react-app.jsx`) just reuses `useAppliedFilterSummary().summaryText` directly - no new derivation logic needed, and the two displays now stay identical by construction instead of by convention.
- **The "Request more" merged-PRs button's visibility/repo** (`window.updateReactMergedRequestMoreAction`) is a tiny pure function (`pr-merged-request-more-config.helpers.js`'s `buildMergedRequestMoreActionOptions`: `isVisible = selectedScope === "all"`, `repo = repoFilter || lastRunRepo || latestSelectedRepo`). New `state/useMergedRequestMoreActionOptions.jsx` derives `selectedScope` via `pr-scope-selection.helpers.js`'s `normalizeSelectedScope(scopeMode)` directly from `FilterStateContext`, and `repo`/`latestSelectedRepo` from `PrDataContext`'s own `selectedRepo` (the vanilla pipeline's distinction between "the render pipeline's just-resolved repo" and "the last repo `applyResolvedRepo` recorded" collapses to the one reactive `selectedRepo` value both sides already shared). Deliberately does NOT reuse `useFilteredPrRows()` for `selectedScope` even though that hook derives it internally too - that hook also pulls in `useNeedsAttention()`/`useRowFilterSelection()`/`useActorIdentity()` and runs the full row-filtering pipeline, none of which this button's visibility needs (confirmed `normalizeSelectedScope` ignores its own `entryNeedsAttention`/`entryHasYourLastActivity` DI params entirely - a pure function of `scopeMode` alone).

`react-app.jsx`: removed both `window.*` bridge-registration effects and their backing `dataMetaText`/`mergedRequestMoreAction` local `useState`s entirely (confirmed dead the same way the chip-text slice's `filterSummary` state was). New `DataMetaTextLive`/`MergedRequestMoreActionLive` wrapper components (hooks can't be called mid-JSX) mounted at the same portal locations, inside the same Provider set `AppliedFilterSummaryLive` already requires (`RowFilterSelectionProvider`/`JobEventsProvider`'s shared wrapping scope, `PrDataProvider`/`FilterStateProvider` above that) - both portals already sat inside that scope, confirmed before relying on it. `pr-render-apply.helpers.js`'s own two `window.updateReact*?.()` calls are left wired (now permanently no-op in the real app, optional-chained against an undefined function) per this migration's established "don't rip out the vanilla computation mid-slice" pattern - same choice as `visiblePrNumbers`'s own dead vanilla computation.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2452 tests, +5 - new `useMergedRequestMoreActionOptions.test.jsx`), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (48 tests, unchanged - including the "Request more merged PRs button renders" test, the real-browser check that visibility/repo still resolve correctly via the new live hook).

**2 real issues found via code review right after this landed, both fixed:**

1. **`DataMetaTextLive` duplicated the entire row-filtering computation.** It called `useAppliedFilterSummary()` independently of `AppliedFilterSummaryLive`, even though both render the exact same string - but as two separate component instances, each has its own `useMemo`/cache (`useFilteredPrRows()`'s `cacheRef`, which this hook composes, is per-instance), so React doesn't dedupe the work across them. The full scope-resolution + per-PR `rowMatchesUiFilters` pass this hook runs was silently doubled on every payload/filter/scheduler change, for the same output twice. Fixed by merging `DataMetaTextLive` into `AppliedFilterSummaryLive` itself - one `useAppliedFilterSummary()` call now portals both the chip text and `#data-meta`'s text, taking both target containers as props instead of each portal wrapping its own copy of the component.
2. **Both new components computed real-looking content immediately from `PrDataProvider`'s empty initial seed (`{}`), before the first `/view-prs/data` fetch resolves.** The old `window.*`-bridge-fed versions had explicit "not loaded yet" defaults (`#data-meta` showed `"Loading..."`; the merged-request-more button defaulted to `isVisible: false`) that were never replaced until the vanilla pipeline's first real render. The Context-derived replacements had no equivalent guard: `scopeMode`'s own real default is `"all"`, so `#data-meta` would show a premature `"Applied filters: repo=all repos | ... | Rows: 0"` instead of "Loading...", and the merged-request-more button could render visible and clickable with `repo=""` during that window. Fixed by gating both on `payload.ok` - a field every real server response sets (`buildDataPayload`, `view-prs-data-response-helpers.js`) that the empty seed never has: `AppliedFilterSummaryLive` shows `"Loading..."` for `#data-meta` until then (the chip text itself is left alone - this guard is specifically about restoring `#data-meta`'s own prior placeholder, not a new requirement for the chip), and `useMergedRequestMoreActionOptions` forces `isVisible: false` until then. Added a regression test to each (`useMergedRequestMoreActionOptions.test.jsx`'s new "no payload has loaded yet" test; confirmed it fails without the guard).

**Verified again after both fixes:** full jest suite (2453 tests, +1), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (48 tests, unchanged).

With this, `runSinglePrUpdate`/`recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields` remain the only 2 names left genuinely deferred on `index.page.js`'s bridge, alongside `getAuthorThreadResolutionPolicy` and the Export tab/auto-render-blocked-links entanglements - all previously confirmed, unchanged by this round.

Asked to continue; re-read the remaining `Object.assign(window, {...})` names in `index.page.js` fresh rather than trusting the previous entry's own "Export tab/auto-render-blocked-links entanglements" label at face value - this session's own track record (Track C, `getAvailableRepoLabels`) kept finding "deferred" claims that didn't survive a direct read of the current code. `getExportFieldCatalog`/`getVisiblePrNumbersFromSectionsHost`/`buildExportPayload` turned out to be exactly that: `ExportTab.jsx`'s own doc comment already said these are "pure vanilla functions... the same leaf components read window.* for pure helpers pattern established throughout this migration" - true, but descriptive of how they'd been left, not a reason they needed to stay that way. **New coverage closing a real gap:** this tab had zero Playwright coverage of any kind (confirmed by grep) - added one, clicking the real "Export" tab, "Preview JSON", and parsing the real rendered JSON preview, specifically because this slice's whole point was "the real functions work without the window.* mock," which only a real-browser run against real page data actually proves.

## 🚧 **`getAuthorThreadResolutionPolicy` closed - a new Context for the last 2 non-Context'd multi-selects (2026-10-10)**

Continued down the remaining deferred list; re-investigated `getAuthorThreadResolutionPolicy` (index.page.js) rather than trusting its own "a genuinely separate concern (a DOM-scan dependency)" label at face value - this session's track record (Export tab, Track C, `getAvailableRepoLabels`) kept finding "deferred" claims that didn't survive a direct read. The mode field itself (`attentionAuthorThreadResolutionMode`) was already real `FilterStateProvider` Context state from sub-phase 6 - only the 2 lists' checked-state had nowhere reactive to live.

**What moved:** new `state/AuthorThreadResolutionSelectionContext.jsx`/`AuthorThreadResolutionSelectionProvider.jsx` - same shape and "no debounce on writes" reasoning as `RowFilterSelectionContext`/`Provider`, deliberately a separate Context rather than widening that one (these 2 lists genuinely don't feed row filtering - reusing that Context's name for a different concern would mislead). `MultiSelectListPortals.jsx` mirrors these 2 lists' seeded checked values into it the same way it already does for the 5 row-filtering lists. `ReviewThreadsSection.jsx` gets a new `useAuthorThreadResolutionPolicy()` hook replacing `window.getAuthorThreadResolutionPolicy` entirely - reads `FilterStateContext`/`AuthorThreadResolutionSelectionContext` directly (not their throwing hooks), matching every other Context this component already reads permissively (`ActorIdentityContext`/`PrInsightsDisplayContext`/`ReviewConversationsUiStateContext`), since its own existing test file mounts it standalone with no providers at all. `index.page.js`'s `getAuthorThreadResolutionPolicy` function and bridge entry are both deleted; `getSelectedAuthorThreadResolutionAllowLogins`/`DenyLogins`/`getFilterStateOverrideForFieldId` stay (still used by `persistUiOptionOverrides`' own override-persistence logic, confirmed via grep). `react-app.jsx` mounts the new Provider wrapping the same broad scope `RowFilterSelectionProvider` already does (sibling-portal reasoning, same as every other Provider at that nesting level).

**A real test failure caught a genuine gap, not just a mechanical port:** `integration-tests/index.html.test.js`'s own harness deliberately never mounts a real `<FilterStateProvider>` (documented in that file's own comment - avoiding it elsewhere prevents 6 unrelated persisted-restore tests from regressing), so `AuthorThreadResolutionModeSelect` is never actually rendered there either; that harness's mode `<select>` is just the plain vanilla element from the static `index.html` fixture. A first pass reading `mode` from `FilterStateContext` alone silently stayed stuck on `"allow-all"` in that harness regardless of what 2 existing Playwright-adjacent jest tests selected - caught immediately by those exact 2 tests failing (not a new test written to catch it; the existing coverage already exercised this). Fixed by adding the same DOM-read fallback (`document.getElementById('attention-author-thread-resolution-mode')?.value`) the old vanilla `getAuthorThreadResolutionPolicy` itself had via `getFilterStateOverrideForFieldId`'s own DOM fallback - mirroring that exact precedent rather than inventing a new one.

**Test updates, not just new coverage:** `ReviewThreadsSection.test.jsx`/`ReviewConversationsUiStateProvider.test.jsx`'s `window.getAuthorThreadResolutionPolicy` stubs removed outright (the default case needs no setup at all now - both Contexts' permissive defaults already resolve to the same "allow-all, nothing checked" policy); the one non-default test (`deny-only` + a denied starter) now wraps the component in real `FilterStateContext.Provider`/`AuthorThreadResolutionSelectionContext.Provider` values instead of mocking the window function. New `AuthorThreadResolutionSelectionProvider.test.jsx` (5 tests, mirroring `RowFilterSelectionProvider.test.jsx`'s own shape exactly).

**Verified:** full jest suite (2458 tests, +5), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (49 tests, unchanged - including the 2 thread-resolution-policy tests in `index.html.test.js`'s jest suite that caught the DOM-fallback gap, plus the existing real-browser "PR author thread resolution policy select shows/hides its dependent allow/deny lists live" and multi-select persisted-restore Playwright tests, both confirming the real app's `FilterStateProvider`-backed path still works unchanged).

## 🔎 **The auto-render-blocked-links entanglement properly scoped - confirmed genuinely blocked, not stale (2026-10-10)**

Asked to continue; gave `navigateToPrInTable`/`navigateToAuthorInsights` (`AutoRenderBlockedLinks.jsx`'s remaining 2 bridge entries) the same research-first scrutiny that just closed the Export tab and `getAuthorThreadResolutionPolicy` deferrals, rather than assuming its own "entanglement" label was still accurate. Unlike those two, this one held up.

**`navigateToAuthorInsights` (`pr-auto-render-navigation.helpers.js`) is genuinely blocked on Author Insights' comment-draft tracking, which is still fully vanilla.** It mutates `authorInsightsState.selectedAuthorLogin` (a module-level `let`-equivalent object in `index.page.js`) and then calls `renderAuthorInsights(latestRows, latestActorsMap)` (`pr-author-insights.component.js`) - not a redundant step: that function still owns real validation (falling back to the first author if the target login no longer exists in the current rows) and is the *only* call site that actually pushes the canonical value into `PrDataContext` via `updateReactSelectedAuthorLogin`. Skipping straight to that push and reimplementing the validation here would duplicate logic this function already owns correctly. Its `focusUnsaved` branch goes deeper still: `getFirstDirtyAuthorInsightsElement` reads `getAuthorInsightsComposerDraft`/`isAuthorInsightsComposerDraftDirty`/`getAuthorInsightsEditDraftMap`/`getAuthorManualCommentsForLogin`/`isAuthorInsightsEditDraftDirty` - all 5 built by `createPrAuthorInsightsDraftsHelpers({ authorInsightsState, ... })`, confirmed still reading/writing that same vanilla state object, not a Context. Converting this one navigation function would mean migrating the Author Insights comment-composer/edit-draft tracking to Context first - a separate, larger slice of its own, not a side effect of this one.

**`navigateToPrInTable` is comparatively light (no `authorInsightsState` dependency at all) but still a genuine DOM-navigation operation, not a disguised pure function** - unlike Export tab's functions, which turned out to need nothing but data already in hand, this one's whole job is finding a real anchor element in the live DOM (`collectNodesByTag(document.body, "a")`, filtered to `.pr-link`/matching text) and calling real `scrollIntoView`/`focus()` on it, after calling `activateDataTab("pr-data")` - itself still a vanilla tab-switching side effect (`pr-management-tabs`-adjacent, not yet Context-based). A sibling copy with near-identical shape already exists (`pr-author-insights-pr-link.helpers.js`'s own `navigateToPrInTable`, used by `AuthorInsightsPrLink.jsx`/`ReviewStatsContent.jsx` via `window.navigateToPrInTableFromAuthorInsights`/`FromStats`) - confirmed it has the exact same DOM-scan-plus-scroll-plus-React-mounted-branch shape, not a simpler React-native alternative to copy from.

**No partial/consolation slice found either**, same conclusion `runSinglePrUpdate`'s own scoping reached: `activateDataTab`/`collectNodesByTag` could theoretically become direct imports on their own, but that wouldn't close either `window.*` bridge itself (both functions still need calling that way from `AutoRenderBlockedLinks.jsx`) or meaningfully simplify anything standalone.

**Conclusion: the original "entanglement" deferral was accurate**, unlike the 2 claims this session already found stale. Recorded with the exact mechanism so a future session doesn't re-research this from scratch: `navigateToAuthorInsights` unblocks together with an Author-Insights-draft-state-to-Context migration (a real, separate slice); `navigateToPrInTable` unblocks only alongside a broader tab-activation-to-Context migration, which nothing else in this migration has needed yet.

With this, `runSinglePrUpdate`/`recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields`/`navigateToPrInTable`/`navigateToAuthorInsights` are the 4 remaining names on `index.page.js`'s `Object.assign(window, {...})` bridge, each with a confirmed, current (not stale) reason, and each pointing at one of 2 larger not-yet-started migrations (the vanilla row-filtering/render pipeline, and Author Insights' draft-state tracking) as its real unblocking condition.

## 🚧 **Author Insights manual-comment drafts moved to Context - the draft-state half of `navigateToAuthorInsights`'s own blocker is gone (2026-10-10)**

Planned and implemented the Author-Insights-draft-state-to-Context migration the entry above identified as one of this migration's last 2 real walls. `AuthorInsightsCommentsSection.jsx`'s composer/edit drafts and manual-comments cache - previously mirrored into `index.page.js`'s module-scope `authorInsightsState` via 10 `window.*` bridges purely so 2 vanilla subsystems (`pr-auto-render-blocking.helpers.js`'s `getBlockingAuthorInsightsLogins`, the real data-loss-prevention check behind "Auto update paused"; `pr-auto-render-navigation.helpers.js`'s `getFirstDirtyAuthorInsightsElement`, the "jump to unsaved draft" chip) could read them from outside this component's own lifetime - are now real React state, with React as the actual source of truth.

**What moved:** new `state/AuthorInsightsDraftsContext.jsx`/`components/AuthorInsightsDraftsProvider.jsx`. The Provider owns a `useRef`-held plain object (same 5-field shape `authorInsightsState` used: `manualCommentDraftByAuthorLogin`/`manualCommentEditDraftByAuthorLogin`/`manualCommentsByAuthorLogin`/`manualCommentsLoadingByAuthorLogin`/`manualCommentsErrorByAuthorLogin`) and reuses `pr-author-insights-drafts.helpers.js`/`pr-author-insights-data.helpers.js`'s existing CRUD functions completely unchanged (both already pure and DI'd, taking an arbitrary state object as a parameter rather than closing over the vanilla one) - a `useState` version tick forces re-renders after every write, the same "mutate a ref in place, force a re-render via a tick" pattern `MultiSelectListPortals.jsx`'s `pendingTick` already established, cheaper and lower-risk than reimplementing this CRUD surface as a reducer. Every write also calls `window.recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields?.()`, matching `NotesDirtyProvider`'s own write path. The Provider republishes the same 10 `window.*` bridge names (plus one not previously published, `getAuthorInsightsEditDraftMap`, needed by `getFirstDirtyAuthorInsightsElement`'s own DI) so both vanilla subsystems keep working completely unchanged, plus one new dedicated bridge, `window.getAuthorInsightsDraftsSnapshot()`, since `getBlockingAuthorInsightsLogins` takes the whole draft-maps object as one positional argument, not per-login getters.

`AuthorInsightsCommentsSection.jsx` now reads/writes this Context directly (`useAuthorInsightsDrafts()`) instead of mirroring into local `useState` while also calling through `window.*` - the Context *is* the shared state now, so the component's own explicit `recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields()` calls after every mutation were dropped as redundant (the Provider's own write path already calls it once per write). `index.page.js`: the `createPrAuthorInsightsDraftsHelpers({ authorInsightsState, ... })` call and its 10 direct window assignments for these 5 fields are deleted; `authorInsightsState`'s object literal drops the same 5 fields (confirmed via grep no other read/write of any of them remained); the 2 vanilla DI wirings (`createPrAutoRenderNavigationHelpers`/`createPrAutoRenderBlockingHelpers`) now reference fresh-reading `(...args) => window.x?.(...args)` proxies instead of direct local function references - required because those factories are constructed once at module-load time, before `AuthorInsightsDraftsProvider` has mounted and published anything.

**A real regression caught by existing coverage, not a new test:** `AuthorInsightsCommentsSection.test.jsx`'s own pre-existing test (guarding `AuthorInsightsProvider` against handing out a new `sortAuthorInsightsManualCommentsDesc` reference on every unrelated payload change, which would otherwise re-run the component's mount effect and close an open edit form) immediately failed once `useAuthorInsightsDrafts()`'s return value was added to that same effect's deps - `PrDataProvider`'s own `viewerContext`/`actorIdentity` `useMemo`s both depend on `payload`, so `normalizeActorLogin` (and everything built from it) got a fresh reference on every unrelated payload change too, cascading into a new Context value and re-triggering the exact bug this test exists to catch, just via a different Context than the one it was originally written for. Fixed by reading `normalizeActorLogin` through a `useRef` inside `AuthorInsightsDraftsProvider` instead of as a direct `useMemo`/`useCallback` dependency - every callback it exposes is now stable for the Provider's entire mount, matching `NotesDirtyProvider`'s own permanently-stable callbacks, and the alias-resolution behavior itself is unaffected (the ref always reads the latest function).

**Test updates, not just new coverage:** `AuthorInsightsCommentsSection.test.jsx` now mounts the real `AuthorInsightsDraftsProvider` (same "exercise the real wiring, not a mock" choice as this session's `ExportTab.test.jsx` rewrite) instead of hand-rolled `window.*` stubs - seeding initial comments now goes through a real (mocked) `/view-prs/author-comments` fetch response rather than a post-mount bridge call, since the component only re-reads the drafts cache at specific trigger points (its own mount effect, a save/edit success), not reactively on every Context write. New `AuthorInsightsDraftsProvider.test.jsx` (7 tests, mirroring `NotesDirtyProvider.test.jsx`'s shape) proves the Context-to-bridge wiring directly, including feeding a real dirty draft through `window.getAuthorInsightsDraftsSnapshot()` into the real `getBlockingAuthorInsightsLogins` and confirming it's flagged as blocking.

**New Playwright coverage closing a real gap:** confirmed via grep that no existing test (jest or Playwright) ever exercised a real author-comment draft blocking anything - the existing manual-comments Playwright test saves/edits but never touches dirty-tracking. Added one, matching the sibling Notes-dirty-tracking Playwright test's own scope exactly (checks the real bridge a browser actually exercises, not a fully simulated poll-block, since `AuthorInsightsDraftsProvider.test.jsx` already covers the Context-to-bridge wiring itself): type an unsaved draft, confirm `window.getAuthorInsightsDraftsSnapshot()` flags the author as blocking, clear it, confirm it clears.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2465 tests, +7), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (50 tests, +1).

**Not closed by this slice, as scoped from the start:** `navigateToAuthorInsights`'s own `window.*` bridge stays - this removed one of its 2 blockers (draft-state), not the other (vanilla tab-activation/`renderAuthorInsights`'s own author-selection validation, still needed for the reason documented in the entry above). `navigateToPrInTable`/`recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields`/`runSinglePrUpdate` are unaffected and remain exactly as last scoped.

## 🚧 **`FilterOptionsProvider` given the same per-entry derived-value cache every other migrated piece already has (2026-10-10)**

With the remaining `window.*` bridges all pointing at full new migrations not worth starting unprompted, picked up a smaller, already-scoped item instead: `ROADMAP.md`'s own Phase 5 "Performance Validation" finding (recorded 2026-09-30, during the multi-select-options-to-React sub-phase review) that `FilterOptionsProvider.jsx`'s `useMemo` fully re-loops every stored PR entry - re-running `extractRowLabelNames`/`collectAssignedUsers`/`collectApproversFromRow` (the latter two walk `activityTimeline`/commits/reviewers) - on every poll tick (~30s), regardless of whether any entry actually changed, since `payload` gets a new top-level object reference every tick either way.

**Fix:** a `useRef`-held `createEntryDerivedCache()` instance (`pr-entry-derived-cache.helpers.js` - the same WeakMap-keyed-by-entry module `useVisiblePrNumbers.jsx` already uses for the row-filtering predicate), wrapping each per-entry derivation (`labelNames`/`preferredAuthorKey`/`assignedUsers`/`approversFromRow`) in `getOrCompute(entry, cacheKey, compute)`. An entry whose object reference is unchanged across a poll (`mergeDataDeltaPayload`'s shallow merge keeps an unchanged entry's exact reference) reuses its cached result instead of re-deriving it; a genuinely-changed entry is simply a cache miss. Deliberately caches only the entry-derived *raw* output (label name list, `{login,name}` assignee/approver lists, the preferred author key) - not the final `actorsMap`-dependent display-name resolution a few lines later, which is already cheap (once per unique login in the outer dedup `Map`s, not once per entry) and reads the always-current `actorsMap` fresh regardless.

**A real tradeoff accepted, not overlooked:** this cache (like `pr-row-filtering.helpers.js`'s own, identical-shape cache) doesn't invalidate on an actor-login-alias-map change alone - if the alias map changes without the entry itself changing, a cached per-entry value could theoretically lag by one entry-change. Not handled in the precedent either; matched that existing, accepted risk tolerance rather than inventing a stricter rule just for this one.

**New coverage proving the cache is actually exercised, not just that output stays correct:** the 4 existing tests already proved correctness; added one spying on the real `extractRowLabelNames` (via `jest.mock` + `jest.requireActual`, not a fake) to prove a second render with the same entry reference (pushed through the real `window.updateReactPrTable` update path, not a meaningless prop-only rerender) doesn't call it again. Confirmed this test fails without the fix (temporarily called `extractRowLabelNames` directly, bypassing the cache - the test failed with 2 calls instead of 1) before restoring it.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2466 tests, +1), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (50 tests, unchanged - no browser-observable behavior change, a pure perf optimization). `ROADMAP.md`'s item checked off.

## 🚧 **The per-row busy indicator is now reference-counted, fixing a real "Quick check all" spinner bug (2026-10-10)**

Continued down `ROADMAP.md`'s remaining bounded, already-diagnosed findings. `PrActivityQueueProvider.jsx`'s `busyPrNumbers` was a plain `Set<key>` - `markPrBusy`/`clearPrBusy` added/deleted a key unconditionally. "Quick check all" marks every loaded PR busy up front and only clears them once its whole multi-repo sweep finishes (can take minutes); if an unrelated action (e.g. an Ack click) on one of those same PRs finished first, its own `clearPrBusy` call removed the key entirely, so that row's spinner disappeared before "Quick check all" was actually done with it. Cosmetic only (no duplicate requests or data issues), but a real, user-visible bug - found during the "Quick check all" feature's own review (2026-09-30).

**Fix:** a `useRef`-held `Map<key, count>` (`busyCountByKeyRef`) alongside the existing `busyPrNumbers` Set - not React state, since this bookkeeping has no UI of its own; only `busyPrNumbers` does. `markPrBusy` increments a key's count and only adds it to the Set on a 0→1 transition; `clearPrBusy` decrements and only removes it from the Set on a transition to 0 (guarded against going negative, in case a caller ever clears more times than it marked). `busyPrNumbers`' own external shape - a `Set` of keys, spread/iterated by `PrTableApp.jsx` - is completely unchanged, so no consumer needed updating.

**New regression coverage, confirmed to catch the bug:** 2 new tests in `PrActivityQueueProvider.test.jsx` - one marking the same PR busy via both `window.markPrsBusy` (the "Quick check all" path) and the direct `markPrBusy` context method (the Ack path) and confirming it only clears once *both* callers clear; one confirming an unbalanced extra `clearPrBusy` call never goes negative or throws. Temporarily reverted the fix and reran the first test - it failed exactly as expected (the key was removed after only one of the two callers cleared) - before restoring it.

**No new Playwright test added, unlike this session's other bug fixes:** this is a cosmetic-only UI bug with no data-loss or correctness risk (unlike the auto-render-blocking case, which got a mandatory new Playwright test for exactly that reason) - thorough, verified-to-catch-the-bug jest coverage is proportionate here; setting up a real multi-second "Quick check all" sweep racing a concurrent Ack click in a real browser would add meaningful flakiness risk for a cosmetic guarantee already covered at the unit level.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2468 tests, +2), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (50 tests, unchanged). `ROADMAP.md`'s item checked off.

## 🚧 **A genuine, CI-only intermittent `e2e` failure root-caused: a lost-update race in `scope-mode` persistence (2026-10-10)**

CI's `e2e` job failed twice in a row on the previous commit (same ~10 scattered test timeouts, PR table stuck showing `scope=last run rows` instead of `scope=all stored rows`), never reproduced locally - not with the default suite (50/50 passed, repeatedly), not with CI's exact `CI=1` retry/timeout config, not with CDP-based CPU throttling up to 10x, not with network throttling added on top (20x combined broke navigation outright, too blunt to be diagnostic). Two prior CI failures in this repo's history were unrelated GitHub Actions infrastructure flakes ("job was not acquired by Runner"), ruling out "already-known flake."

**Root cause, found via targeted `[DIAG]` diagnostic logging** (temporarily added around `restoreUiOptionOverrides`/`readUiSessionOverrides`/`setText`, piped from the browser to CI's own log via `page.on('console', ...)`, then removed once the investigation concluded): `#scope-mode`'s native "change" event is handled by *two* separate listeners, not one - `debouncedApplyOnChangeIds`' handler (`index.page.js`, apply-only) and `registerUiOptionPersistenceHandlers`' handler (`pr-ui-option-scroll.helpers.js`), which fired `persistUiOptionOverridesSafe(["scope-mode"])` un-debounced, fire-and-forget, on *every* change. `persistUiOptionOverrides` itself is a non-atomic GET-merge-PUT against `user-defaults.json`. The one Playwright test that changes `#scope-mode` twice in a row with nothing but `selectOption()` (no "Apply filters" click, no wait) fired two overlapping GET-merge-PUT cycles; whichever PUT's write happened to land second (an I/O-timing race, not a CPU-speed one - explaining why CPU throttling never reproduced it) won, and a `last-run` write landing after the test's own `all` reset left `user-defaults.json` permanently stuck, leaking into every subsequent test's fresh page load for the rest of that CI run. A genuine lost-update bug for real users too, not just test flakiness.

**Fix (two parts, both needed):**
1. **Production:** `registerUiOptionPersistenceHandlers` (`pr-ui-option-scroll.helpers.js`) now debounces its persist call 150ms, mirroring `debouncedApplyFilters`'s existing pattern exactly - two rapid changes now coalesce into one trailing PUT instead of racing.
2. **Test:** "changing a React-owned filter auto-applies without clicking 'Apply filters (local)'" (`smoke.spec.js`) now waits for its own trailing persist PUT (`page.waitForResponse`, same shape as `clickApplyFiltersAndWaitForPersist`) before ending, so it can no longer leak a stale value forward regardless of the production fix.

**New regression coverage:** `pr-ui-option-scroll.helpers.test.js` gained a test firing two rapid `scope-mode` changes under fake timers and confirming only the trailing persist call survives.

**Not reproducible as a before/after local demonstration:** per the investigation above, this race never reproduced locally even under heavy throttling - it's specific to CI's I/O timing. Confirmed instead by reading the fixed code path and by the new unit test, which exercises the debounce logic directly rather than depending on real timing to expose the bug.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2469 tests, +1), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (50 tests, unchanged, including the fixed test).

---

## 🚧 **`navigateToAuthorInsights`'s remaining objection removed - re-categorized alongside `navigateToPrInTable`, not "blocked" (2026-10-10)**

Asked to continue the Author Insights work specifically. `navigateToAuthorInsights`'s draft-state blocker was already closed earlier this session (`AuthorInsightsDraftsProvider`); its other, smaller objection - that skipping straight to writing `authorInsightsState.selectedAuthorLogin` and leaning on a follow-up `renderAuthorInsights` call to silently re-validate it "would duplicate logic this function already owns correctly" if reimplemented - turned out to be fixable, not a true wall, now that `buildAuthorInsightsEntries` is already exposed via `useAuthorInsights()`/`AuthorInsightsProvider.jsx` for the React side.

**Fix:** extracted the exact "ensure selected author is valid, falling back to the first option" rule out of `pr-author-insights.component.js`'s `renderAuthorInsights` into one shared, bare-exported pure function, `resolveValidAuthorLogin(authorOptions, requestedLogin)` (`pr-author-insights-display.helpers.js`, next to `buildAuthorInsightsEntries` - no `createXHelpers()` wrapper needed, same "zero-dependency pure function" precedent as `pr-row-sorting.helpers.js`). `renderAuthorInsights` now calls it instead of its own inline block (pure refactor, confirmed behavior-unchanged against its existing tests). `navigateToAuthorInsights` (`pr-auto-render-navigation.helpers.js`) now calls the same function to validate a requested login against the real current author options *before* writing `authorInsightsState.selectedAuthorLogin`, instead of writing the raw (possibly-stale) requested login first and trusting the follow-up `renderAuthorInsightsSafe` call to quietly correct it - a real, if minor, correctness improvement, not just a dedup. New `buildAuthorInsightsEntries` DI param wired via the same "fresh-reading proxy" pattern already used for `renderAuthorInsights` at this call site (`prAuthorInsightsDisplayHelpers` is declared later in `index.page.js` than this wiring).

**What this explicitly does NOT do:** convert `activateDataTab`'s 3-way vanilla tab switcher (`pr-data-tabs.helpers.js`) into a React-owned concept. Confirmed via research that it's plain DOM mutation with no Context backing at all - `useIsTabPanelVisible.jsx` only *watches* the panel's `hidden` attribute via a `MutationObserver`, it doesn't drive it. `navigateToPrInTable` already establishes the precedent that calling `activateDataTab(...)` as a real DOM-navigation side effect through its existing bridge is accepted, not something every caller must avoid converting. **`navigateToAuthorInsights` is re-categorized to match that same precedent** - an accepted vanilla DOM-navigation-and-tab-activation operation, not a "blocked pending a bigger migration" item. Converting `activateDataTab` itself remains a separate, unstarted, much larger migration.

**New coverage:** 3 new tests for `resolveValidAuthorLogin` (`pr-author-insights-display.helpers.test.js`); 1 new test for `navigateToAuthorInsights` confirming a requested login no longer present in the current rows now falls back to the first real author instead of being written (even transiently) as the invalid requested one - a case no existing test covered. `pr-auto-render-navigation.helpers.test.js`'s fixtures now build a real `buildAuthorInsightsEntries` (via `createPrAuthorInsightsDisplayHelpers`, same minimal-deps shape `pr-author-insights.component.test.js`'s `createBasicDependencies` already uses) instead of omitting it, since `navigateToAuthorInsights` now genuinely depends on its output.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2473 tests, +4), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (50 tests, unchanged - internal refactor plus a defensive-only correctness fix for an edge case `AutoRenderBlockedLinks.jsx` can't actually trigger today, since its chips only ever list authors who already have real drafts against real current rows).

## 🚧 **A real fast path for fresh-payload renders - closing the actual entanglement behind `runSinglePrUpdate`/`recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields` (2026-10-10)**

Asked to scope and start "the vanilla row-filtering/render pipeline becoming reactive" - the one larger, not-yet-started migration the last 2 genuinely-blocked `window.*` bridge names (`runSinglePrUpdate`, `recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields`) point at. Three research passes (tracing `runSinglePrUpdate` itself, the auto-render-blocking pipeline, and the poll loop's data-flow architecture - each reading the current code directly, not trusting prior notes) converged on one precise finding: **almost everything `prDataTabOrchestrator.renderPrData`'s full pipeline computes today is now dead** (filter-dropdown population, chip text, `#data-meta` text, the merged-request-more-action push, `visiblePrNumbers`/`filteredRows` for the table - each already superseded by a live Context derivation from this session's earlier "live filtering" work). Exactly 3 things are still genuinely live: fingerprint/manifest/version bookkeeping the next poll's skip-render dedup depends on, repo resolution + conditional label refresh, and Author Insights' own live re-render when that tab is open.

**Fix:** a new `applyFreshPrPayload(payload, repo)` (`index.page.js`, next to `applyResolvedRepo`/`updateReactTable`) does exactly those 3 real things for a caller that already has a direct, authoritative fresh payload - reusing every existing piece directly (`applyLatestPrData`, `applyResolvedRepo`, `updateReactTable`, `renderAuthorInsightsIfVisible`, `renderAutoRenderBlockedIndicator`, `updateBackfillStatusFromPayload`, `deriveRepoRunContext`, the pure fingerprint/manifest computers) rather than reimplementing any of them. Wired into 2 call sites:
1. **`runSinglePrUpdate`** (`pr-single-pr-update.helpers.js`) - used to render directly from the run response, then *unconditionally* also call `loadStoredData()` (a second network fetch + a second full-pipeline render) "just in case." That second call is now gone entirely; `result.prData` was already complete. One accepted, documented tradeoff: `result.prData` has no `dataMeta`, so `lastSeenDataVersion` doesn't update from this path specifically - self-correcting (the next poll's own meta check finds the real mismatch and fetches normally), not a lasting bug.
2. **`flushPendingAutoRenderNow`** - used to call `renderPrData(payload)` (the full pipeline) to apply a deferred poll payload once `recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields` notices a dirty→clean transition. This was the literal, named dependency every prior scoping pass flagged - now closed.

**What stays exactly as-is:** both `window.runSinglePrUpdate` and `window.recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields` remain `window.*` bridges - neither is "closed." `recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields` must stay reachable by both remaining vanilla DOM listeners and the 2 Context Providers that already call it; converting `runSinglePrUpdate` into a real hook (its only caller is already React) is a separate, larger follow-up, not bundled here. The poll loop's own *immediate*-render branch (the common case) is unchanged - it already commits fingerprint/manifest inline and doesn't share `runSinglePrUpdate`'s double-fetch problem. **4 other call sites share the exact same now-provably-redundant "render once, then unconditionally `loadStoredData()` again" shape** (`handleRunScript`'s manual "Run" button, and the Ack/label/checkbox/merged-request-more action helpers) - confirmed by research, deliberately left alone this round to keep this slice reviewable and prove the new primitive out on one caller pair first. A pre-existing, unrelated `backfill-tab.orchestrator.js` wiring bug was also noticed during research (its own `activateDataTab("backfill")` call is a no-op, `"backfill"` isn't one of `pr-data-tabs.helpers.js`'s 3 recognized keys) - not touched here.

**New coverage closing a real gap:** no existing test (jest or Playwright) exercised the full dirty→queued→clean→flush cycle end to end - only the dirty-state bridges themselves had coverage. Added a new integration test (`index.html.test.js`) driving a real deferred poll (dirty Notes field defers a changed payload, then clearing the dirty flag triggers `recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields`'s own flush) against the real, mounted `PrDataProvider`/`PrTableApp` - confirmed it fails without the fix (temporarily disabled the new `applyFreshPrPayload` call and reran) before restoring. `pr-single-pr-update.helpers.test.js` updated for the single new `applyFreshPrPayload` DI param, with an explicit assertion it's called exactly once (regression coverage for the removed double-fetch).

**No new Playwright coverage added:** there was no existing Playwright test for the "↻ Update" button either (another pre-existing gap, not introduced here) - given the thorough jest/integration-level coverage above plus a full, unchanged Playwright run, a new browser-level test for this internal plumbing swap wasn't judged proportionate, matching this session's own precedent for internal-refactor-only changes.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2474 tests, +1), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (50 tests, unchanged).

**2 real issues found and fixed in review immediately after this slice landed:**
1. **`renderAuthorInsightsIfVisible()` reads a private cache `applyFreshPrPayload` never updates.** `latestAllStoredRows`/`latestActorsMap` (`pr-render-apply.helpers.js`) are only ever written by `applyRenderResults` - the full pipeline `applyFreshPrPayload` exists specifically to bypass. Calling `renderAuthorInsightsIfVisible()` directly would have silently rendered Author Insights from stale leftover data, not the fresh payload, whenever that tab was open - defeating the one stated purpose of that call. Fixed with a new `applyFreshAuthorInsightsIfVisible(payload)` (same file) that refreshes the cache from `payload` first (same `Object.values(payload.byPrNumber)` derivation `pr-render-context.helpers.js`'s `captureRenderContext` uses), so a later tab-activation catch-up render also sees the fresh data, not just this one call. New regression test confirmed to fail without the fix.
2. **An empty resolved repo could have clobbered `latestSelectedRepo`.** The first `applyLatestPrData({ payload, selectedRepo: resolvedRepo })` call bypassed `applyResolvedRepo`'s own deliberate `if (!resolvedRepo) return;` guard (there specifically to prevent this). Fixed by dropping `selectedRepo` from that first call entirely, matching `renderPrData`'s own `applyLatestPrData({ payload })`-only call exactly - `applyResolvedRepo` is now the sole gate for that write, as it always was elsewhere.

**Verified again after the fix:** full jest suite (2475 tests, +1), `npx eslint .` clean, full Playwright smoke suite (50 tests, unchanged).

## 🚧 **Extended `applyFreshPrPayload` to the Ack/label/merged-request action helpers - and found the "4 other call sites" follow-up note was imprecise (2026-10-11)**

The previous entry flagged 3 other helper files as sharing `runSinglePrUpdate`'s "render once, then unconditionally reload" shape. A fresh research pass (reading each file and the server routes behind them directly, not trusting that framing) found the real picture narrower:

- **`pr-row-checkbox-actions.helpers.js` was never actually a candidate.** It never calls `renderPrData` at all (its own comment: "checkbox already updated, UI is correct"), and its two real callers (`toggleInReviewForRow`/`toggleFlaggedForRow`) always send `inReview`/`flagged` fields, never `ack`/`ackClear` - confirmed via the server route that this makes the server's `isCheckboxOnly` branch always true for them, so the response is always the minimal `{flaggedByRepo, inReviewByRepo}` shape, never a full `prData` payload. There's no authoritative payload here for `applyFreshPrPayload` to apply - the existing 2-key delta merge is already the cheapest-possible correct update. **Left untouched.**
- **`pr-ack-label-actions.helpers.js`'s 2 chunked call sites are also not candidates, by original design, not oversight.** A multi-chunk batch deliberately does ONE `loadStoredData()` call after all chunks settle instead of merging N chunks' own partial `prData` responses - applying `applyFreshPrPayload` per-chunk would reintroduce exactly the inconsistent-intermediate-state risk that design already avoids. **Left untouched.**
- **3 real call sites across 2 files did apply**, and none of them were actually double-fetch bugs the way `runSinglePrUpdate` was - both files already correctly gated their `loadStoredData()` call behind `else { }`. The simplification was "stop running the dead full `renderPrData` pipeline when a direct payload is already in hand," not a redundant-fetch fix:
  - `pr-ack-label-actions.helpers.js`'s `runSingleAckRequest`/`runSingleApplyLabelRequest` (the non-chunked paths) - `renderPrDataSafe(result.prData, selectedRepo)` → `applyFreshPrPayloadSafe(result.prData, selectedRepo)`. `renderPrData` became fully unused in this file afterward - removed as a DI param entirely.
  - `pr-merged-request-more-action.helpers.js`'s `handleRequestMoreMerged` - the `setLatestStoredPayload`/`setLatestSelectedRepo`/`renderPrData(..., { useLastRunScope: false })` trio collapsed into one `applyFreshPrPayload(result.prData, repo)` call. Dropping `{ useLastRunScope: false }` matches `runSinglePrUpdate`'s own already-shipped precedent exactly (that option only ever fed the orchestrator's scope-filtering computation, already confirmed dead for the table).

**Verified:** full jest suite (unchanged test count, assertions updated in `pr-ack-label-actions.helpers.test.js`/`pr-merged-request-more-action.helpers.test.js` to match), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (50 tests, unchanged - specifically re-confirmed the bulk Ack/Apply-label and "Request more merged PRs" tests, the real user-facing paths this touches). Temporarily reverted one of the two production fixes and reran its test to confirm it actually catches a regression, before restoring.

## 🚧 **`runSinglePrUpdate` closed - converted into a real `useSinglePrUpdate()` hook (2026-10-11)**

Research confirmed `runSinglePrUpdate`'s only real caller was already React (`PrTableApp.jsx`'s `handleUpdatePrBusy`) - but also found this wouldn't be a fully "clean" closure: status/output text, in-flight request-activity tracking, and the error snackbar have no Context/hook API today, only `window.*` bridges - the same already-accepted pattern `NotesSection.jsx` uses for its own direct network call (`window.beginRequestActivity`, bare-imported `postJson`). Asked the user explicitly whether to proceed given that honest tradeoff (moving *ownership* of the workflow from vanilla to React, not eliminating every `window.*` read) - they chose to proceed.

**What moved:** new `state/useSinglePrUpdate.jsx`. Bare-imports `postJson`/`DEFAULT_REPO` (already clean, DI-free exports); builds `stripAnsi`/`getGithubAuthFailureHint`/`formatCommandOutputWithAuthHint` once at module scope (same "stable, zero-per-render-state" precedent `AuthorInsightsProvider.jsx`'s `stableDisplay` already established); reads `window.beginRequestActivity`/`window.updateReactStatusText`/`window.updateReactOutputText`/`window.showErrorNotification`/`window.applyFreshPrPayload` via safe optional calls, same shape `NotesSection.jsx` already uses for the first of those. One confirmed-safe simplification: the vanilla `setStatusMessage` wrapper also called a private `renderRequestActivity()` on every status update, but that function derives purely from `requestActivityCounters`/scheduler state (never the message text), and `beginRequestActivity`'s own start/finish calls already trigger it independently at the right moments - so the hook calling `window.updateReactStatusText` directly, without an equivalent extra call, is behaviorally identical.

**`notifyFailureSnackbar`'s pure logic extracted** into a new bare-exported `buildFailureSnackbarNotification` (`pr-failure-snackbar.helpers.js`, alongside `isTimeoutFailureMessage` - both zero-dependency, matching `pr-row-sorting.helpers.js`'s "no factory wrapper" precedent). `index.page.js`'s own `notifyFailureSnackbar` now just calls this + `showErrorNotification`; the hook calls the same function + `window.showErrorNotification` directly - no duplicated logic between the vanilla and React paths.

**Net bridge effect:** `runSinglePrUpdate` removed from `index.page.js`'s `Object.assign(window, {...})` block; `applyFreshPrPayload` gains its own entry there instead, since the hook needs to reach it the same way `NotesSection.jsx` reaches `beginRequestActivity`. Same category of bridge (a vanilla primitive exposed *for* a React consumer), not a new smell - an honest trade, not a "free" closure.

**Deleted outright:** `pr-single-pr-update.helpers.js` and its test - fully superseded, zero remaining callers (confirmed via grep). `PrActionsCell.jsx`'s `window.runSinglePrUpdate || (async () => {})` fallback (used only when `onUpdatePr` isn't supplied, confirmed dead in production since the real `PrTableApp` always supplies it) simplified to a plain `onUpdatePr?.(...)` call, since the bridge it was falling back to no longer exists.

**New coverage:** `pr-failure-snackbar.helpers.test.js` (10 tests - timeout detection, candidate-priority order, fallback message); `useSinglePrUpdate.test.jsx` (3 tests, using a small `Probe` component + `@testing-library/react`'s `render`/`act` - this codebase's actual hook-testing convention, confirmed via `useIsTabPanelVisible.test.jsx`, not `renderHook`) translating the 3 deleted helper-module tests (success-with-payload, auth-failure, thrown-exception) into the hook's shape. Confirmed the `applyFreshPrPayload` assertion actually catches a regression by temporarily disabling that call and rerunning, before restoring. `PrActionsCell.test.jsx`'s 2 tests referencing `window.runSinglePrUpdate` updated to match the simplified fallback.

**With this, `recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields`/`navigateToPrInTable`/`navigateToAuthorInsights` are the only 3 names left** on `index.page.js`'s bridge - all 3 already confirmed accepted-as-is in prior entries (shared vanilla/React entry point, or legitimate vanilla DOM-navigation operations), not candidates for further closure.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2485 tests, +13), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (50 tests, unchanged, including the Snackbar test).

---

## 🚧 **`handleRunScript` - the 4th and final "redundant render-then-reload" call site fixed (2026-10-11)**

Closed out the follow-up this whole cleanup arc started from: `handleRunScript` (the manual "Run script" button) loops over multiple PR targets (or a single `""` "all PRs" target), and - unlike the 3 already-fixed single-call sites - always called `loadStoredData()` once after the whole loop finished, regardless of how many iterations succeeded or failed.

Research (reading the server route directly, not assuming) confirmed the same fix applies safely, with one new edge case reasoned through explicitly: **if every target fails, the loop never renders anything today, and the trailing `loadStoredData()` was the only refresh that happened.** Confirmed via the server's failure path (`buildRunFailureResult`/`buildBadRequestResult`/`buildRunMissingDependenciesResult`) that every failure mode of `/view-prs/run` leaves server-side data genuinely unchanged (none of those branches call `setLastManualRunNow`/`clearPendingForRepo`/`readViewPrsData`) - so skipping the render on an all-failure run is correct, not a regression; there's nothing new to show.

**Fix:** the per-iteration `applyLatestPrData({...}); renderPrData(latestData, effectiveRepo);` pair became one `applyFreshPrPayload(latestData, effectiveRepo);` call; the trailing `await loadStoredData(effectiveRepo);` after the loop was removed entirely, with no replacement.

**The one real gap, unlike the other 3 fixes:** `handleRunScript` had zero existing test coverage of its response/render-handling side - every prior test only asserted outgoing POST request bodies. Added 5 new integration tests (`index.html.test.js`) from scratch: single success, multiple successes (confirms the final state reflects the last target's payload), partial failure (successful target's data still renders, combined status message/snackbar correct), all-failure (status/snackbar correct, confirmed nothing renders and nothing throws), and the single `""` "all PRs" target (behaves identically to a specific PR number). All 5 assert zero extra `GET /view-prs/data` calls - direct regression coverage for the removed redundant reload. Confirmed 4 of the 5 fail without the `applyFreshPrPayload` fix (the all-failure test doesn't depend on it, as expected), before restoring.

**With this, all 4 originally-flagged "render directly, then unconditionally reload" call sites are closed**: `runSinglePrUpdate`, `pr-ack-label-actions.helpers.js`'s 2 single-request paths, `pr-merged-request-more-action.helpers.js`, and now `handleRunScript`. The 2 chunked ack/label paths and `pr-row-checkbox-actions.helpers.js` remain deliberately untouched, per their own already-documented reasons.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2490 tests, +5), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (50 tests, unchanged - no new Playwright test, matching the precedent already set for this exact class of internal-plumbing change; there's no existing "Run script" e2e test either).
