## 🚧 **Fixed the `backfill-tab.orchestrator.js` wiring bug noticed during earlier research (2026-10-11)**

A prior entry noted, as an unrelated aside, that `backfillTabOrchestrator.activateTab()`'s own `activateDataTab("backfill")` call was a no-op (`"backfill"` isn't one of `pr-data-tabs.helpers.js`'s 3 recognized keys). Asked to fix it; investigation found the real shape was different from that one-line note: `activateTab()` had **zero callers anywhere** (confirmed via grep) - not just a broken call, dead code. The real Backfill tab click handler lives entirely in `pr-management-tabs.helpers.js`'s own `activateTab("backfill")`, which already correctly switches panel visibility/aria-selected - but, unlike the Action Log/Actor Names tabs, never triggered a status/log refresh on activation, since the orchestrator method that was meant to do that was never wired in. Presented both findings (broken-and-dead vs. a real missing-refresh gap) and asked how to proceed - user chose to fix both.

**Fix:** removed the broken `activateDataTab` call/DI param from `backfill-tab.orchestrator.js`'s tab-activation method entirely - tab-switching chrome was never this function's job, `pr-management-tabs.helpers.js` already owns it correctly. That method now does only what it should have: `if (isInitialized) { void loadStatus({ includeLog: true }); }` - and was renamed from `activateTab` to `onTabActivated` (matching `pr-data-tabs.helpers.js`'s own established naming for this exact "called when a tab becomes active" shape) since it no longer activates anything itself, only reacts to activation. New `onBackfillTabActivated` callback DI added to `createPrManagementTabsHelpers` (`pr-management-tabs.helpers.js`), called when the backfill tab is clicked, matching the existing `loadActionLog`/`loadActorNameCache` on-activation pattern exactly. `index.page.js` wires `onBackfillTabActivated: () => backfillTabOrchestrator.onTabActivated()` - no lazy-proxy indirection needed, `backfillTabOrchestrator` is already declared earlier in the file than this wiring.

**New/updated coverage:** removed the stale `backfill-tab.orchestrator.test.js` test asserting the now-deleted broken `activateDataTab` call; its sibling "loadStatus is triggered" test is unaffected (updated to call `onTabActivated`). Added 3 new tests to `pr-management-tabs.helpers.test.js`: backfill tab click triggers `onBackfillTabActivated`, a non-backfill tab click doesn't, and a missing `onBackfillTabActivated` doesn't throw. Confirmed the first of these fails without the new wiring, before restoring.

**Follow-up review caught one more thing:** a later pass over this same slice found the top-of-file JSDoc still described this method as `activateTab - Activate this tab`, even though it never did that - fixed alongside the rename above.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2492 tests, +2 net), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (50 tests, unchanged, including the 2 existing tests that click into the real Backfill tab).

---

## 🚧 **Deleted `pr-data-tab.orchestrator.js`'s 5 dead public methods - the second half of the sibling-orchestrator unused-API-surface pattern (2026-10-11)**

A fresh sweep following the backfill fix found `pr-data-tab.orchestrator.js` - authored off the same "orchestrator" template (confirmed via git log: identical JSDoc structure, `isInitialized` pattern, IIFE module shape) - has the same issue, but worse: 5 dead public methods instead of 1 (`activateTab`, `handleDataRefresh`, `handleFilterChange`, `loadData`, `cleanup`), confirmed via grep to have zero real callers anywhere outside their own unit tests. `index.page.js` only ever calls this orchestrator's `initialize()`/`renderPrData()`.

**Unlike the backfill fix, this one needed no new wiring** - research confirmed every one of the 5 is pure superseded duplicate scaffolding, not a missing-behavior gap:
- `activateTab` just called `activateDataTab("pr-data")` - already called directly everywhere tab-switching for this tab actually happens (the real click handler inside `initDataTabs()`, itself called from this orchestrator's own `initialize()`; plus 3 other direct "jump to this tab" call sites in `index.page.js`). `onTabActivated` (the real "what happens on activation" hook) is already wired centrally inside `activateDataTab` itself - nothing missing, unlike backfill's genuine refresh-on-activate gap.
- `handleDataRefresh` just called this orchestrator's own `renderPrData` - already called directly at the 2 real places a refresh happens.
- `handleFilterChange` just called `applyFiltersFromCache()` - already called directly via the more capable debounced wrapper (`debouncedApplyFilters`) that's the actual production path.
- `loadData` just called `loadStoredData(repo)` - already called directly at startup, with real error handling (`.catch(...)`) this wrapper never had.
- `cleanup` only reset `isInitialized`, which had no externally observable effect once its only 2 "consumers" (the `isInitialized` guards inside `handleDataRefresh`/`handleFilterChange`) are also gone. The real polling/visibility lifecycle is fully owned by `<PrDataPolling />` in `react-app.jsx`, unrelated to this flag.

**Fix:** deleted all 5 method definitions, their "Public API" return entries, and the 3 DI params (`applyFiltersFromCache`/`loadStoredData`/`activateDataTab`) that became unused once their one real consumer was gone (`initDataTabs` stays - `initialize()`, a real method, uses it). Updated the top-of-file JSDoc to match. `index.page.js`'s own wiring for this orchestrator dropped the same 3 now-redundant params - the underlying functions themselves are untouched and keep being called directly everywhere else in the file; only their pointless pass-through into this one orchestrator instance is gone.

**Testing:** pure dead-code deletion with a confirmed-working real implementation already covering every case elsewhere - no new coverage needed. Deleted the 8 tests exercising the 5 removed methods (`pr-data-tab.orchestrator.test.js`), trimmed the "all methods defined"/"are functions" assertions to match. Confirmed no other test in the file referenced them even indirectly.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2484 tests, -8 as expected), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (50 tests, unchanged) - zero behavior change, exactly as intended for a pure dead-code removal.

## 🚧 **`pr-render-apply.helpers.js`'s merged-request-more-action plumbing retired - and a genuine false positive caught before it shipped (2026-10-11)**

Scoped (via research) the next layer of `applyRenderResults`'s own comment-flagged dead calls: `window.updateReactDataMetaText`/`window.updateReactMergedRequestMoreAction`, both described as "now permanently-no-op... in the real app" since `react-app.jsx` no longer registers either - `AppliedFilterSummaryLive`/`MergedRequestMoreActionLive` derive both live from Context instead. Confirmed independently (not just trusting the comment): `MergedRequestMoreActionLive`'s backing hook (`useMergedRequestMoreActionOptions.jsx`) imports `pr-merged-request-more-config.helpers.js` directly, with zero dependency on anything `index.page.js` computes - genuinely dead there.

**`window.updateReactDataMetaText` was NOT actually dead, despite reading that way.** Removed it first alongside the merged-request-more cleanup, ran the full jest suite, and got 7 failures - all `index.html.test.js` assertions on `#data-meta`'s text content. Root cause: that integration-test harness never mounts the real `AppliedFilterSummaryLive` component, so its own `wireTextBridge("updateReactDataMetaText", "data-meta")` stub is the *only* thing populating `#data-meta` anywhere in that whole suite. The comment's claim was true for the real app, but didn't account for this test harness's own simulation still depending on the old contract. Restored the call and its test coverage; left `updateReactMergedRequestMoreAction` removed (no test depended on it the same way - the regression was specific to `#data-meta`, confirmed by rerunning the full suite clean once only that one call was restored).

**Fix (final scope):** deleted `buildMergedRequestMoreActionOptionsSafe`'s computation and the `window.updateReactMergedRequestMoreAction?.()` call from `applyRenderResults`; dropped the now-unused `buildMergedRequestMoreActionOptions` param/local; `index.page.js`: removed its `buildMergedRequestMoreActionOptions` instantiation (`pr-merged-request-more-config.helpers.js`'s own factory - confirmed its only other consumer, `useMergedRequestMoreActionOptions.jsx`, imports the module directly and never went through this bridge) and its now-dead import. Also deleted `index.page.js`'s own already-known-dead `visiblePrNumbers` computation inside `renderPrData` (lines computing it from `renderPipelineResult.filteredRows` and threading it into `updateReactTable`/`mountReactTable`) - a prior slice's own comment (the `applyFreshPrPayload` entry, 2026-10-10) had already identified this exact computation as dead ("each already superseded by a live Context derivation elsewhere"), and independently confirmed here: `PrTableApp.jsx` reads `visiblePrNumbers` from `useVisiblePrNumbers()` (a live Context-derived hook), never from `usePrData()`'s own `visiblePrNumbers` state field that this computation used to feed.

**Updated a stale comment** (above `renderPrData`'s React-rendering-path pipeline call) that still described filter dropdowns as a side effect of this pipeline - corrected during the earlier `populateFilterOptions` entry, left as-is here.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2488 tests, net -1: the merged-request-more test deleted, the data-meta test's coverage kept), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (50 tests, unchanged).

## 🚧 **`PrDataProvider.jsx`'s dead `visiblePrNumbers` state field removed - the riskier follow-up from the previous entry, done carefully (2026-10-11)**

The previous entry's research flagged this as a separate, slightly riskier follow-up (touches `react-app.jsx`, `PrDataProvider.jsx`, and 3 test files) rather than bundling it in - worth doing once its sole feeder (`index.page.js`'s `visiblePrNumbers` computation) was already confirmed dead and deleted. Re-verified independently before touching anything, given the near-miss the previous entry already had with a false "dead" claim: `react-app.jsx`'s real `<PrDataProvider>` mount hardcodes `initialVisiblePrNumbers={null}` (never a real value), and `PrTableApp.jsx` reads filtering exclusively from `useVisiblePrNumbers()` (a Context-derived hook with zero dependency on this Provider's state) - confirmed via direct reads, not just trusting the prior research. Also ruled out a false-positive match: `pr-export.helpers.test.js`'s own `visiblePrNumbers` references are `buildExportPayload`'s own unrelated parameter, not this state field.

**Fix:** removed `visiblePrNumbers` entirely from `PrDataProvider.jsx` - the `initialVisiblePrNumbers` prop, the state field, and `window.updateReactPrTable`'s 3rd-argument handling; updated its header JSDoc. `react-app.jsx`: dropped `initialVisiblePrNumbers={null}` from the real mount and the 3rd argument from its own `window.updateReactPrTable?.()` call; updated a comment that cited the (now actually deleted, not just "left wired") `visiblePrNumbers` computation as a precedent for a different still-existing no-op bridge. `index.html.test.js`/`PrTableApp.test.jsx`: dropped the now-nonexistent prop from their own mount-bridge/render harnesses (confirmed first that no test in either file ever passed a meaningful value through it). `react-app.test.jsx`: deleted its 2 dedicated unit tests for this field's own undefined-vs-null merge semantics - that specific bug class is moot once the field doesn't exist - and updated a comment citing those tests as an example.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2486 tests, -2 as expected), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (50 tests, unchanged).

## 🗺️ **Full migration roadmap: what's left, including deferred work (2026-10-11)**

Asked directly: what's remaining, including deferred work, and what's the plan to finish it. Three parallel research passes built a precise current-state inventory (not re-reading history and assuming it's still accurate - several entries above turned out stale). Net finding: **the migration is closer to done than `index.page.js`'s 4,239 lines vs. `react-app.jsx`'s 1,000 suggests.** Most of the remaining bulk is either real business logic with no DOM dependency that could run from a hook just as easily as a vanilla function, or bootstrap/bridge plumbing that only exists because the last few vanilla UI pieces haven't moved yet. No undiscovered "big missing feature."

**What's actually left, verified:**

1. **3 real `window.*` bridges**, each blocked on a specific still-vanilla subsystem: `recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields` (the render-blocking pipeline, sub-phase 7.5's one unclosed piece), `navigateToPrInTable` (+2 sibling copies - vanilla tab-activation + a live-DOM anchor scan), `navigateToAuthorInsights` (its remaining half - `renderAuthorInsights`'s still-vanilla author-selection validation). `applyFreshPrPayload`/`beginRequestActivity`/`debouncedApplyFilters`/the action-handler bridges are a different category (vanilla primitives exposed *for* React, same as `postJson`) - not blockers.
2. **2 genuinely vanilla-only UI pieces with zero React ownership:** both tab bars (management-tabs + data-tabs, raw `<button>`s toggled via `classList`/`aria-selected`/`hidden`), and the auto-render-blocked indicator panel's show/hide + "Discard unsaved" logic.
3. **1 tab with no real JSX at all:** Backfill - buttons/orchestration are vanilla (status/log text panels are already React text-portals).
4. **The Run Script form's 6 trigger buttons** - plain buttons calling still-vanilla handlers (`handleRunScript`/`handleAckOnly`/etc.); the fields they read are already fully React-owned, so this is about the buttons/handlers, not the fields.
5. **UI option persistence** (~700 lines) - fetches/writes `/view-prs/user-defaults`, pushing into `FilterStateProvider` via `window.setFilterStateValue` rather than owning the fetch/persist itself.
6. **Polling/activity-tracking function bodies** - scheduling already lives in React (`<PrDataPolling/>`/`<JobEventsProvider/>`); the function bodies they call are still vanilla.
7. **A DI-forwarding pattern** in the ack-label/quick-check action factories (`markPrsBusy`/`clearPrsBusy`/etc., each a pure `window.X?.()` forward).

**Explicitly NOT part of this migration, confirmed still-correctly-scoped-elsewhere:** virtual scrolling (`ROADMAP.md`, a real separate feature, "only if users have 200+ PRs regularly"); Phase 4/5's formal testing/perf passes (ongoing incremental improvement, not a blocking gap). One correction found along the way: the Review Stats chart's old "~900 lines of hand-rolled SVG, left vanilla" note is now **stale** - it was rewritten as real JSX (`StatsVisuals.jsx`/`ReviewerActivityChart.jsx`) in an earlier session.

**The plan: 8 sequenced phases**, same bounded-slice-plus-full-verification discipline as every entry above, ordered by leverage (phases unblocking multiple downstream items first) and risk (proven Provider patterns before novel ones):

- **Phase A - Tab-switching chrome → `TabStateProvider` Context.** Highest leverage: unblocks `navigateToPrInTable` (+2 siblings) and half of `navigateToAuthorInsights`'s blocker. Keep `pr-management-tabs.helpers.js`/`pr-data-tabs.helpers.js`'s pure "which tab should be active" logic, just change who calls it (Context setter instead of direct DOM mutation) - the same pattern this whole migration has used throughout.
- **Phase B - Auto-render-blocking/dirty-field pipeline → `AutoRenderBlockingProvider`.** Closes `recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields`. Mirrors `NotesDirtyProvider.jsx`/`AuthorInsightsDraftsProvider.jsx` exactly - those already proved this pattern for the *feeder* side; this is the same for the *consumer/enforcement* side.
- **Phase C - Backfill tab → real JSX.** New `useBackfillActions()` hook over the already-pure `createPrBackfillActionHelpers`, same shape as `useSinglePrUpdate.jsx`.
- **Phase D - Run Script form triggers → React.** Hooks over the already-pure ack-label/run-script DI factories; `getFormBody()`'s `FormData(form)` read goes away since every field is already Context-owned.
- **Phase E - UI option persistence → Context-owned.** Debounced POST on change inside `FilterStateProvider` itself, replacing the push-based restore.
- **Phase F - Polling/activity-tracking bodies → hooks.** Called directly by `<PrDataPolling/>`/`<JobEventsProvider/>`, removing the `window.*` indirection now that scheduling already lives there.
- **Phase G - Retire the busy/queued/bulk-batch DI-forwarding params**, once D's hooks exist, via direct calls into `PrActivityQueueProvider`'s Context.
- **Phase H - Final retirement.** With A-G done, `index.page.js` has no DOM to wire up and no bridge left to expose - `initPage()`/the `Object.assign(window,...)` block/`mountReactTable`/`updateReactTable` all become dead, deletable the same way `ensureDefaultFilterValues`/`populateFilterOptions` were earlier today. `react-app.jsx`'s own mount becomes the sole entry point. Sub-phase 7.7's original vision, finally reachable.

**Scope note:** several of these phases (A, B, D especially) are themselves multi-session efforts at this session's own observed slice size - working through them one bounded slice at a time, verifying and documenting each, stopping between phases for redirection rather than barreling through unattended.

> **This 8-phase list is superseded for anything past Phase F** - see "🗺️ Reconciling every deferred item from Phases A-F back into the plan" further down for the corrected sequence (Phases A-F each shipped a narrower slice than described here in at least one respect; the corrected list adds D.2/F.2/G.1/G.2 to actually close those gaps instead of leaving them deferred indefinitely).

## 🚧 **Phase A, slice 1: data-tabs bar → React + `TabStateProvider` (2026-10-11)**

First concrete implementation slice of the roadmap above. Scoped to just the data-tabs bar (PR Data/Review Stats/Author Insights) - 3 buttons, already fully exported/tested as a standalone module - not the 6-button management-tabs bar, which becomes a separate follow-up slice.

**Hard constraint that shaped the whole approach:** `state/useIsTabPanelVisible.jsx` (used by several already-migrated components) is a `MutationObserver` watching each panel's `hidden` DOM attribute, not a Context read; `pr-render-apply.helpers.js`'s own vanilla `isTabPanelVisible` reads `panel.hidden` directly too. Neither could be touched, so the design reuses the real DOM-mutation logic rather than replacing it: `pr-data-tabs.helpers.js`'s exported `activateDataTab(key)` is completely unchanged - the new Context just becomes a second, React-visible "who's active" source of truth that calls into it as a side effect, the same "React decides, but still performs the real DOM write" pattern `AlwaysShowInReviewCheckbox.jsx` already established.

**What moved:** new `state/TabStateContext.jsx`/`components/TabStateProvider.jsx` (plain `useState`, matching `NotesDirtyProvider.jsx`'s shape, not the ref+version-tick pattern - this state genuinely originates in React now). `setActiveDataTab(key)` updates React state and calls `window.activateDataTab?.(key)` - a new one-line bridge (`index.page.js`, right next to the real `activateDataTab` instance) exposing the existing vanilla function *for* this Provider, same category as `applyFreshPrPayload`/`beginRequestActivity`, not a new entanglement. A `window.setActiveDataTab` write bridge (same shape as `FilterStateProvider`'s `window.setFilterStateValue`) lets vanilla-only callers trigger a switch *through* Context instead of bypassing it. New `components/DataTabButton.jsx` - one generic component for all 3 buttons (matching `ContextFilterCheckbox.jsx`'s "no field-specific behavior" shape), rendering the exact same `<button>` markup/attributes the static HTML used to, same `id`s. `index.html`: the 3 static `<button>`s replaced with empty `<span id="tab-*-root">` portal targets (matching `#filter-pr-numbers-root`'s precedent exactly - confirmed by reading it first rather than guessing).

**4 external callers redirected** from calling the vanilla `activateDataTab` directly to going through `window.setActiveDataTab` instead, so the React-rendered buttons' active-state styling never desyncs from the real active tab: `pr-auto-render-navigation.helpers.js`'s `navigateToPrInTable`/`navigateToAuthorInsights` DI wiring, and the two `window.navigateToPrInTableFromStats`/`FromAuthorInsights` bridges (all 3 in `index.page.js`).

**`index.page.js`'s own button-wiring retired for this bar:** `prDataTabOrchestrator`'s `initDataTabs` DI param is now `() => {}` - the real `initDataTabs()`'s `.onclick` wiring would have double-fired alongside React's own `onClick` once the buttons became React-rendered. `initDataTabs` itself (and its own tests) are completely untouched, only this one call site stops using it; the now-unused `initDataTabs` destructure was removed too (caught by `eslint`'s `no-unused-vars`).

**2 real test fixes in `index.html.test.js`** (confirmed via grep this is the ONLY test file referencing these 3 button ids by role/name - everywhere else was either untouched or already covered): "shows key management and data tabs from static HTML" dropped its now-inapplicable "PR data" tab assertion (that bar isn't static HTML anymore); "switches the active data tab when a user clicks Review statistics" was removed outright - this jsdom-only suite never loads `react-app.jsx`, so there's no vanilla fallback left to click, same precedent an already-existing comment in that file documents for a prior, similarly-shaped removal. Equivalent real coverage already exists in Playwright (`e2e/smoke.spec.js`'s "lifecycle section expand/collapse and tab switching work" already clicks all 3 data-tab buttons in a real browser) - confirmed this test actually catches a regression by temporarily stripping `TabStateProvider`'s `window.activateDataTab?.()` call and rerunning just that test (failed with a clear "expected visible, received hidden" diff) before restoring.

**New coverage:** `TabStateProvider.test.jsx` (5 tests - bridge publish/cleanup, both read/write directions, no-throw without the vanilla bridge installed) and `DataTabButton.test.jsx` (3 tests - default active state, click switches tabs, the real `window.activateDataTab` call).

**Not closed by this slice, as scoped from the start:** the management-tabs bar (6 buttons) stays fully vanilla - separate follow-up slice. `navigateToPrInTable`/`navigateToAuthorInsights` are NOT removed from `index.page.js`'s `window.*` bridge by this slice - they still do real DOM-anchor-scan/author-selection-validation work beyond the tab switch; only their internal `activateDataTab` call moved off the vanilla function.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2493 tests, +8 new, net after the 1 removed/1 trimmed integration test), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (50 tests, unchanged - real existing coverage already exercises this).

## 🚧 **Phase A, slice 2: management-tabs bar → React, extending `TabStateProvider` (2026-10-11)**

Converts the other, bigger tab bar - management-tabs (Activity/Run & Filter/Backfill/Action Log/Actor Names/Export, 6 buttons) - same proven approach as slice 1. `pr-management-tabs.helpers.js`'s `activateTab` had zero external callers (unlike `activateDataTab`'s 4), so no caller-redirection step was needed here - the scope turned out to be elsewhere.

**A real structural wrinkle found during implementation, not anticipated by the plan:** `activateTab` was nested *inside* `initManagementTabs()` as a closure capturing its element references once - not a top-level, re-querying function like `pr-data-tabs.helpers.js`'s `activateDataTab`. Since `initManagementTabs()` was about to stop being called in production, `activateTab` had to be extracted to the top level first (re-querying elements every call, matching the sibling file's shape) before it could be exposed to React at all - a real refactor, not just "export it unchanged" as planned. Behavior-preserving, confirmed via the file's own 11 existing tests passing unmodified.

**The scope turned out much bigger than planned in one specific place, found only during implementation:** grepping `index.html.test.js` for the plan's anticipated "~2-3 affected tests" (slice 1's actual count) instead found the "Run & Filter" tab clicked as test setup in **55+ places**, plus 7 more for "Backfill" and 3 pure tab-switch tests to remove - that jsdom-only suite (which never loads `react-app.jsx`) uses these clicks throughout as routine navigation to reach panel content, not just to test the switch itself. Flagged this to the user before proceeding, given the material scope difference from what was approved - confirmed to proceed with the full bulk fix.

**The bulk fix itself needed a second structural change, also found only empirically:** replacing each `await user.click(screen.getByRole("tab", {name: "Run & Filter"}))` with a direct `window.activateManagementTab("script")` call (the same new bridge, invoked directly instead of via a simulated click) seemed sufficient, but `activateTab`'s own guard required the 3 *button* elements to exist (not just the panels) before doing anything - and buttons no longer exist as plain elements in this suite (replaced by empty portal roots, since it never mounts `react-app.jsx`). Fixed by relaxing the guard to require only the 3 panels, with every button mutation made conditionally guarded - matching the conditional-guard pattern the 3 "optional" tabs (action-log/actor-name-cache/export) already used. Confirmed this relaxation is genuinely load-bearing (not just convenient) via temporary-revert-and-rerun: reverting to the button-requiring guard reproduced 45 failures.

**A second, independent timing issue, also only found empirically:** 5 "change detection filters" tests still failed after the guard fix - not because of the tab switch, but because the PR table's own React mount is asynchronous (triggered by a data fetch), and the old `await user.click(...)` happened to provide enough of an async yield for that mount to complete before assertions ran; the bare synchronous bridge call doesn't. Fixed with a `waitFor(...)` immediately after each affected `window.activateManagementTab("script")` call, polling for the actual condition each test needed (list populated, or a specific restored selection) rather than an arbitrary delay.

**Fix:** `activateTab` exported from `pr-management-tabs.helpers.js` (alongside the pre-existing `initManagementTabs`). `TabStateContext.jsx`/`TabStateProvider.jsx` extended with `activeManagementTab`/`setActiveManagementTab` (one shared Provider for both bars, not a second one - matching the original roadmap's own `{ activeManagementTab, activeDataTab }` shape) plus a `window.setActiveManagementTab` write bridge, published for symmetry even though nothing calls it yet. New `components/ManagementTabButton.jsx` (generic, mirrors `DataTabButton.jsx`). `index.html`: all 6 static `<button>`s replaced with empty `<span id="tab-*-root">` portal targets. `react-app.jsx`: 6 new container lookups + portals, inside the same `<TabStateProvider>` slice 1 already mounts. `index.page.js`: `window.activateManagementTab` bridge added next to the `activateTab` wiring; the `initManagementTabs();` call removed from `initPage()` entirely (a direct call, not a DI param like slice 1's `initDataTabs` swap-to-no-op).

**New coverage:** `ManagementTabButton.test.jsx` (3 tests, mirrors `DataTabButton.test.jsx`) and 5 new tests extending `TabStateProvider.test.jsx` with the management-tab equivalents of its existing data-tab tests. `pr-management-tabs.helpers.test.js`'s 11 existing tests unmodified.

**Not closed by this slice, as scoped from the start:** same as slice 1 - `recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields`/`navigateToPrInTable`/`navigateToAuthorInsights` stay on `index.page.js`'s `window.*` bridge, unaffected by this tab bar's conversion.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2497 tests, net +4 after the integration-suite fixes above), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (50 tests, unchanged - real existing coverage, including tests that click "Run & Filter"/"Backfill"/"Action Log"/"Actor Names"/"Export" in a real browser, already exercises this).

## 🔎 **Phase B scoped: `recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields` is NOT closeable the way Phase A's tab bridges were - correction to the roadmap (2026-10-11)**

Started Phase B (the roadmap's next item: the auto-render-blocking pipeline → Context, closing the last real `window.*` bridge). Research found the roadmap's own framing was wrong in one important way: Phase A's tab bridges were closeable because their *writes* originated from a React action (a button click). `pendingAutoRenderPayload`'s writes originate from the **vanilla polling orchestrator** (a background poll arriving), and the "when is it safe to apply" timing (debounce, wait-for-blur) is also vanilla - there is no React-originated write to relocate. Structurally, `window.recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields` is the same legitimate bridge category as `window.applyFreshPrPayload`/`window.beginRequestActivity` (one side notifying the other of a state change), just in the reverse direction - not a blocker the way the tab bridges were.

**What research actually found, confirmed via direct reads, not assumption:**
- Of the 3 signals feeding `hasDirtyPrSectionsFields`, 2 (`unsavedNotesCount` via `NotesDirtyProvider.jsx`, `blockingAuthorInsightsLogins` via `AuthorInsightsDraftsProvider.jsx`) are already fully React-owned, reached only via a bridge read.
- The 3rd (`dirtyFieldCount`, a `querySelectorAll('[data-original-value]')` DOM scan) is **confirmed dead in production** - grepped the entire `src/ui` tree; nothing anywhere writes `data-original-value`. Always zero. Not touched in this slice (no urgency), but worth knowing for whoever eventually cleans it up.
- The indicator panel is already a mix: its "jump to this PR/author" links (`#auto-render-blocked-pr-links`) are already real JSX (`AutoRenderBlockedLinks.jsx`) - only the outer panel's show/hide, status text, and the "Discard unsaved" button remain vanilla.

**Asked the user how to proceed given this correction** - offered the scaled-back version (convert just the indicator panel's rendering via a push bridge, the proven lower-risk pattern, leaving the bridge permanently in place) vs. investigating whether the bigger redesign (moving `pendingAutoRenderPayload`/flush-timing into React, which actually *would* close the bridge for real) is feasible anyway. Chose to investigate.

**Investigation found the bigger redesign is architecturally sound but currently under-tested, not simply risky-and-therefore-off-the-table:** `getPendingAutoRenderAction` (`pr-data-polling.helpers.js`) - the pure decision function choosing apply-now/wait-for-blur/wait-for-clean/none - is DI-free and already directly unit-tested for all 4 branches, trivially portable. The real risk is concentrated entirely in `flushPendingAutoRender`'s imperative shell: a bare `setTimeout(0)` that self-attaches a one-time native `blur` listener on `document.activeElement`, with no cancellation path - and **this exact mechanism had zero test coverage anywhere** (jest or Playwright) before this entry; only the pure decision function and the sibling "wait-for-clean" path were tested. Moving this into a `useEffect` without first having a regression net would risk landing a behavior change (StrictMode double-invoke, listener timing) completely undetected.

**Chose to add that test coverage first**, as its own bounded step, before attempting the bigger move. New integration test (`index.html.test.js`): "a poll that clears while a text field is focused waits for blur before applying, instead of clobbering the focused field" - exercises the real mechanism end-to-end (focuses a real `<input>`, defers a poll via the existing dirty-Notes-field trick, clears dirty while still focused to reach the wait-for-blur branch, confirms the payload does NOT apply while focused, fires a real `blur` event, confirms the self-attached listener re-fires and the payload then applies). Confirmed this test actually catches a regression by temporarily removing the blur-listener attachment and rerunning - it failed with a clear timeout waiting for the payload to apply - before restoring.

**Verified:** full jest suite (2498 tests, +1), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (50 tests, unchanged) - pure test-coverage addition, no production code changed.

**Next step for Phase B, not done in this slice:** with real coverage of the blur-wait mechanism now in place, the bigger redesign (moving `pendingAutoRenderPayload`/`hasDirtyPrSectionsFields`/the flush-timing decision into a new `AutoRenderBlockingProvider`, with the vanilla polling orchestrator reading/writing through bridges) can be attempted with an actual safety net. Not yet started.

---

## ✅ **Phase B: `pendingAutoRenderPayload`/flush-timing moved into `AutoRenderBlockingProvider` (2026-10-11)**

Did the bigger redesign the previous entry set up a safety net for. Kept the exact "who notifies whom" structure unchanged - `NotesDirtyProvider.jsx`/`AuthorInsightsDraftsProvider.jsx` still call `window.recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields?.()` exactly as before. What changed is what that function does internally: instead of mutating vanilla module variables and writing DOM directly, `index.page.js`'s `recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields` now computes blocking state via the unchanged `getAutoRenderBlockingState()`/`computeHasDirtyPrSectionsFields()` helpers and pushes it into a new Provider via `window.pushAutoRenderBlockingState?.(state)`.

**New:** `state/AutoRenderBlockingContext.jsx` + `components/AutoRenderBlockingProvider.jsx` (owns `pendingAutoRenderPayload` + the derived blocking-state fields, runs the apply/wait-for-blur/wait-for-clean decision via the already-tested `getPendingAutoRenderAction`), `components/AutoRenderBlockedStatusText.jsx` + `components/AutoRenderBlockedApplyButton.jsx` (the two pieces of the indicator panel that became real JSX).

**A portal-nesting conflict, found during implementation:** `AutoRenderBlockedLinks.jsx` was already portaled into `#auto-render-blocked-pr-links`, a static sibling child inside `#auto-render-blocked-indicator`. Making the whole panel React-owned would mean the new Provider's own `createPortal` destroying that sibling's target on every render. Resolved by leaving the outer panel wrapper and `#auto-render-blocked-pr-links` as static HTML, with the panel's `hidden` attribute toggled as a small, deliberate vanilla-DOM-write side effect inside the Provider's own `useEffect` - only the status text and "Discard unsaved" button became real portaled JSX, each into its own new root span in `index.html`.

**Scope grew beyond the plan's file list, found only during implementation, not flagged separately since it was a natural extension of the approved design:** `applyFreshPrPayload`'s 2 call sites, `prDataTabOrchestrator`'s DI, and - most significantly - `pr-data-polling-orchestration.helpers.js` itself directly called the old `renderAutoRenderBlockedIndicator`/`flushPendingAutoRender` for its "queue-render-and-listen" case. Fixed by recognizing that once the Provider's own reactive effect handles the blur-wait decision uniformly, "queue-render" and "queue-render-and-listen" become equivalent at that call site - simplified to just queueing the payload either way, with `pr-data-polling-orchestration.helpers.test.js` updated accordingly (the renamed test now asserts the orchestrator itself does *not* attach a blur listener, since the Provider does).

**A real regression, caught only by the full Playwright suite, not jest:** the first working version used plain `useState` + `flushSync` for the two write bridges (`window.setPendingAutoRenderPayload`/`window.pushAutoRenderBlockingState`), modeled after `FilterStateProvider.jsx`'s own precedent - needed because a write followed immediately, in the same synchronous call stack, by a bridge read (`pr-data-polling-orchestration.helpers.js`'s `pollForDataChanges()` reading `getHasDirtyPrSectionsFields` right after a push) would otherwise see a stale pre-write value. This passed the full jest suite (jsdom) cleanly, but the real-browser Playwright run hit `Warning: flushSync was called from inside a lifecycle method` on 4 of 50 tests - because `window.pushAutoRenderBlockingState` can also be invoked from *inside* `NotesDirtyProvider.jsx`'s own `useEffect` (via `window.recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields?.()`), and React disallows `flushSync` from inside a lifecycle method; Playwright's harness fails any test whose console captures a warning. **Fixed** by recognizing this state doesn't genuinely originate inside this component's own React tree the way `NotesDirtyProvider`'s own dirty-count state does - it's pushed in from outside via a bridge, same category `AuthorInsightsDraftsProvider.jsx`'s own header comment already describes for its own state. Rewrote the Provider to use that exact precedent: a `useRef`-held plain object mutated directly (synchronous, no React commit required for a subsequent read to see it) plus a plain `setState` version-tick bump to trigger a re-render for UI purposes - no `flushSync` anywhere. Confirmed fixed by rerunning the full Playwright suite: 50/50 passing (was 46/50).

**Dead code deleted in this slice:** `renderAutoRenderBlockedIndicator`, `flushPendingAutoRenderNow`, `forceApplyPendingAutoRender`, `flushPendingAutoRender`, the module-scope `pendingAutoRenderPayload`/`hasDirtyPrSectionsFields` variables, and the vanilla click-wiring for `#auto-render-blocked-apply-btn`.

**Not closed by this slice, as scoped from the start:** `window.recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields` itself stays a real, callable bridge - this was never going to close it (see the prior entry's correction). `getDirtyTrackedFields`'s dead DOM scan (confirmed dead, zero live writers of `data-original-value`) and extending `AuthorInsightsDraftsContext`'s own exposed shape to subscribe reactively instead of via a bridge read remain explicit follow-ups, not done here.

**New coverage:** `AutoRenderBlockingProvider.test.jsx` (7 tests), `AutoRenderBlockedStatusText.test.jsx`/`AutoRenderBlockedApplyButton.test.jsx` (2 tests each).

**Verified:** full jest suite (262 suites, 2509 tests), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (50/50 passing).

## ✅ **Phase C: Backfill tab's 4 buttons + checkbox → real JSX (2026-10-12)**

Converts the last remaining fully-vanilla tab control surface - the Backfill tab's Start/Stop/Refresh-status buttons, the log-refresh button, and the auto-scroll checkbox (`#backfill-badges`/`#backfill-details`/`#backfill-log` were already React text/badge portals from earlier phases; only the interactive controls were still raw vanilla elements).

**The real disabled-state source of truth turned out richer than a simple "request in flight" flag, found via a direct read before assuming a `TriggerAutoRunButton.jsx`-style local-pending design would work:** `pr-backfill.helpers.js`'s `getBackfillStatusViewModel` already computes `buttonState: { startDisabled, stopDisabled, refreshDisabled, refreshLogDisabled }` from **both** `isBackfillActionPending` **and** `backfill.running` (Start stays disabled while already running, Stop stays disabled while it isn't, independent of any in-flight request) - `index.page.js`'s `renderBackfillStatus` already applied this to all 4 buttons via direct `.disabled = ...` writes, on every status render (initial load, post-action, tab activation, auto-poll). A naive local-`useState`-pending design would have silently dropped the `backfill.running` half of this logic.

**Fix:** pushed `buttonState` into React as a 3rd piece of status-derived state in `react-app.jsx`'s `AppRoot`, alongside the `backfillBadges`/`backfillDetailsText` state that already existed there - fed by a new `window.updateReactBackfillButtonState` bridge, called from `renderBackfillStatus` right alongside the 2 existing sibling calls. `renderBackfillStatus`'s own direct `startButton.disabled = ...` (and 3 siblings) and the `getOptionalElementById("backfill-start-btn")` lookups are gone - this bridge replaces them. New `components/BackfillControls.jsx` (Start/Stop/Refresh-status, matching `TriggerAutoRunButton.jsx`'s callback-injection shape but reading `disabled` from the injected `buttonState` prop rather than deriving it locally) and `components/BackfillLogControls.jsx` (the auto-scroll checkbox + "Refresh log" button). The checkbox is deliberately uncontrolled (`defaultChecked`, not `checked`) - `pr-ui-option-scroll.helpers.js`'s `shouldAutoScrollBackfillLog` reads `#backfill-log-autoscroll`'s live `.checked` DOM value at call time, not from pushed state, so an uncontrolled checkbox with the same id satisfies that unchanged; `autoScrollBackfillLogToBottom`'s direct `#backfill-log`'s `.scrollTop` mutation stays exactly as-is (React doesn't own scroll position).

**A real behavior gap found and fixed while removing the old `setButtonDisabled`-by-id DI from `pr-backfill-actions.helpers.js`'s `handleBackfillAction`:** the original code disabled all 4 buttons *synchronously, immediately* at the top of the function (before the POST even resolves) - replacing that with only the post-response `updateBackfillStatusFromPayload` push would have left a short window where a second click could fire before the UI reflected the in-flight action. Fixed by adding an immediate `setBackfillButtonState({...all disabled})` call at the top (new DI param, replacing `setButtonDisabled`), and keeping the existing catch-all `{...all enabled}` fallback in the `finally` block's own inner `catch` (for when the final status reload itself fails and there's no fresh view model to push) - both covered by new/updated tests in `pr-backfill-actions.helpers.test.js`.

**Dead public API surface found and deleted, same pattern as `pr-data-tab.orchestrator.js`'s earlier cleanup:** `backfillTabOrchestrator`'s `handleAction`/`loadStatus`/`refreshStatus` had zero real callers - the vanilla button click handlers always called `handleBackfillAction`/`loadBackfillStatus`/`loadBackfillLogTail` directly, bypassing the orchestrator entirely. Deleted `performAction`/`refreshStatus` outright and stopped exporting `loadStatus` (kept as an internal function - `initialize`/`onTabActivated` still call it directly). `backfill-tab.orchestrator.test.js` rewritten to exercise `loadStatus`'s behavior (including its own error-rendering path) through `onTabActivated()` instead of calling the now-private function directly; the dedicated `handleAction`/`refreshStatus` test blocks were removed, not rewritten, since that behavior no longer exists in the orchestrator at all.

**New coverage:** `BackfillControls.test.jsx`/`BackfillLogControls.test.jsx` (3-4 tests each - disabled-state from props, click/change callbacks, uncontrolled-checkbox default). `index.html.test.js`: new `installBackfillControlsBridge()` (mirrors `installAutoRenderBlockingProviderBridge`'s shape - a small local harness component owns the `buttonState` subscription, portals the 2 real components into their root spans) - the existing role-based "Start backfill"/"Stop backfill" click tests and their disabled-state assertions needed no changes, since they already interacted via role/name rather than raw DOM ids.

**Not closed by this slice** (correction added 2026-10-12, see the "Reconciling every deferred item" entry below - this was missed when first written): the roadmap's own Phase C description called for "a new `useBackfillActions()` hook over the already-pure `createPrBackfillActionHelpers`" - what actually shipped converted only the trigger buttons/checkbox (`BackfillControls.jsx`/`BackfillLogControls.jsx`), still reaching `handleBackfillAction`/`refreshBackfillStatus`/`refreshBackfillLogTail`/`autoScrollBackfillLogToBottom` via `window.*` bridges, same gap as Phase D's own buttons ended up with. The Run Script form's own triggers (Phase D) and UI option persistence (Phase E) remain separate, not-yet-started-at-the-time phases.

**Verified:** full jest suite (264 suites, 2507 tests), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (50/50 passing).

---

## ✅ **Phase D: Run Script form's 6 trigger buttons → React (2026-10-12)**

Converts the 6 remaining vanilla buttons whose business logic was already fully self-contained and whose fields were already fully React-owned (Context-backed inputs/selects) - `run-script-btn`/`ack-only-btn`/`clear-only-btn`/`apply-label-btn`/`refresh-labels-btn` (all in the Run Script form) and `apply-filters-btn` (a separate Visibility Filters card).

**A simplifying finding confirmed before implementing, not assumed:** a grep for `.disabled =`/`setButtonDisabled` against all 6 ids found zero matches anywhere - unlike Phase C's backfill buttons, none of these 6 have ever had disabled/pending visual state; clicking "Run script" again mid-run has always been possible (the business logic has its own internal guards where it matters, e.g. `refreshAvailableRepoLabels`'s `isFetchingRepoLabels` early-return, unchanged). This ruled out a `TriggerAutoRunButton.jsx`-style local disabled/label state machine as out-of-scope new behavior, and confirmed a single generic, fully stateless component (matching `DataTabButton.jsx`'s "no field-specific behavior" shape) was the correct, risk-minimized conversion for all 6.

**New `components/RunScriptActionButton.jsx`** - `{ id, label, title, className, onClick }`, nothing else. **8 new one-line `window.*` bridges** in `index.page.js` (same "vanilla primitive exposed *for* React" pattern as `window.handleTriggerAutoRun`): `persistRunScriptOptionOverrides`/`handleRunScript`/`handleAckOnly`/`handleClearOnly`/`handleApplyLabelClick`/`refreshAvailableRepoLabels`/`persistViewFilterOptionOverrides`/`applyFiltersFromCache` - no business logic touched, every function was already fully self-contained. `index.html`: 6 one-to-one swaps of `<button id="X">Label</button>` for `<span id="X-root"></span>`, with every sibling/wrapper left untouched (several of these buttons share a `form-actions` div with already-React-portaled siblings like `#quick-check-btn-root` - no restructuring needed, matching `#trigger-auto-run-btn-root`'s own minimal-footprint precedent). `react-app.jsx`: 6 new container lookups + portals; `run-script-btn`'s and `apply-filters-btn`'s `onClick` each compose 2 bridge calls, matching their original inline handlers exactly.

**Scope discovery, found via grep before writing any test code, not during cleanup afterward:** the jsdom `index.html.test.js` suite (which never mounts `react-app.jsx`) interacts with 2 of these 6 heavily by role/name - `{ name: "Apply filters (local)" }` appears **43 times**, `{ name: "Run script" }` **8 times** (51 total; the other 4 buttons have zero role-based references there). Addressed with a new `installRunScriptActionButtonsBridge()` (mirrors `installBackfillControlsBridge()`'s precedent, simpler here since these buttons are stateless - a direct array of 6 portals, no wrapper component/state needed). All 51 existing tests passed unmodified once the bridge existed, confirming they only ever depended on role/name, not raw DOM ids.

**New coverage:** `RunScriptActionButton.test.jsx` (3 tests - props render correctly, click calls `onClick`, safe no-op without one).

**Not closed by this slice, as scoped from the start:** none of the 8 underlying business-logic functions changed - this was purely a trigger-ownership move. Phase E (UI option persistence → Context-owned) and beyond remain separate, not-yet-started phases.

**Verified:** full jest suite (265 suites, 2510 tests), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (50/50 passing).

## ✅ **Phase E: UI option persistence moves to FilterStateProvider (2026-10-12)**

The biggest, riskiest slice in the roadmap - ~700 lines of `getUiOptionDefaults`/`FILTER_STATE_FIELD_MAP`/`persistUiOptionOverrides`/`restoreUiOptionOverrides`/`readUiSessionOverrides`/`writeUiSessionOverrides` moved from vanilla `index.page.js` into `state/FilterStateProvider.jsx`, which now owns UI option persistence directly.

**Checked in with the user twice before implementing, given real findings each time:** confirmed persistence only ever fired at explicit trigger points (not on every keystroke) - the user chose to preserve those exact trigger points/bridge names (a product decision) while explicitly freeing the *mechanics* from needing to match the old implementation (a request to not force stale timing hacks to survive the move). The old restore flow's `flushSync`/`queueMicrotask`/dual-restore-pass choreography - needed only because vanilla code was reaching into React from outside - became unnecessary once restore became a plain "fetch then setState" effect running inside the Provider's own lifecycle.

**New `helpers/pr-ui-option-persistence.helpers.js`** - `getUiOptionDefaults`/`FILTER_STATE_FIELD_MAP`/`readUiSessionOverrides`/`writeUiSessionOverrides`, moved verbatim (no DOM dependency). `FILTER_STATE_FIELD_MAP` stays shared, not duplicated - `index.page.js`'s own `getFilterStateOverrideForFieldId` (still needed by `getNeedsAttentionConfig`/`getOpenModeFilter`/etc.) imports the same instance.

**`FilterStateProvider.jsx` extended** with a mount-only restore effect (one `setValues` merge for every `FILTER_STATE_FIELD_MAP`-mapped override, plus direct `setPendingMultiSelectSelection` calls for the 9 multi-select keys - that store is deliberately `window`-level already, so calling it from here is no different than the old vanilla call) and `persistRunScriptOptions`/`persistViewFilterOptions` callbacks, exposed as `window.persistRunScriptOptionOverrides`/`window.persistViewFilterOptionOverrides` - the **same bridge names** the old vanilla functions used, so all 6 real call sites (2 Phase D buttons, 3 vanilla "change" listener branches, 1 debounced scope-mode persist in `pr-ui-option-scroll.helpers.js`) needed zero changes at the call site itself, only DI wiring updates where they referenced the old function by name.

**A real, confirmed-dead redundancy found and removed while porting the diffing logic:** the old `persistUiOptionOverrides` wrote `change-filter-use-builtin-merge-pattern`'s value to *two* places in the persisted JSON (a top-level key and `changeFilters.useBuiltinMergePattern`) - restore read both, but always derived from the same source, so they were always consistent; collapsed to the one location restore actually treats as canonical.

**The real architectural blocker, found only by running the full suite, not assumed up front:** `index.html.test.js` (the ~9000-line jsdom suite) had an existing comment explaining it deliberately never mounted `FilterStateProvider`, specifically to avoid this exact problem - its own filter-field inputs are plain static HTML (no real Context-backed component), so mounting the Provider would make every Context-preferring reader ignore the suite's own raw DOM-write test simulations. With persistence now living *inside* that Provider, there was no vanilla implementation left to test in isolation - mounting it became unavoidable. Fixed by mounting `FilterStateProvider` once (after `fetchMock`/`global.fetch` are wired - mounting it earlier, as first attempted, raced the Provider's own restore-on-mount fetch against test fixture setup and silently missed every `userDefaultsOverrides` fixture) and adding a `syncFilterStateFromElement(element, contextKey)` test helper, called right after each test's own DOM-mutating simulation of a field change, at the ~15 call sites across ~10 tests that assert on what actually got persisted or restored - mirroring what the real app's own field components do via their real `onChange`. Two tests asserting directly on a static DOM element's value (reading what restore used to write via `setNativeValueAndDispatch`) were updated to read `window.getFilterStateValues()` instead - the real source of truth post-migration.

**A second-order risk found via the full suite catching 3 *new* failures after the first fix attempt:** seeding every `FILTER_STATE_FIELD_MAP` key with its real default in the test harness (to fix one test expecting "untouched checkbox = no override") broke 3 *other*, previously-passing tests - `scope-mode`/`filter-pr-numbers` are deliberately DOM-only in this reduced harness (no Context-backed component mounted for them, by the suite's own prior design), and seeding them made the data-meta summary's Context-preferring reader silently ignore those tests' own DOM-only changes. Fixed by seeding *only* `changeFilterUseBuiltinMergePattern` (confirmed via grep that no test anywhere manipulates that one field directly, so there's nothing for a correct seeded default to mask) instead of every field.

**New coverage:** `pr-ui-option-persistence.helpers.test.js` (8 tests), extended `FilterStateProvider.test.jsx` coverage via its own existing suite (unchanged assertions continue to pass, confirming the pre-existing `flushSync`-based bridges were untouched).

**Not closed by this slice, as scoped from the start:** the 9 multi-select lists' *persist*-side DOM reads (`getSelectedAuthorLogins()` etc., real checkbox `:checked` state) are untouched - reached from the Provider via injected `window.*` bridges instead of called directly in vanilla code, same category as every other "vanilla primitive exposed for React" bridge in this migration.

**Verified:** full jest suite (266 suites, 2518 tests), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (50/50 passing) - including every real-browser "...survives a persisted-value restore"/"...auto-persists on change and survives a restore" test, the actual end-to-end proof this migration didn't change observable behavior.
