## ✅ **Phase F: `pollForDataChanges`'s render-tracking state moves into `PrDataProvider` (2026-10-12)**

Same scope-correction pattern as Phases B and E: the roadmap's one-line Phase F description ("polling/activity-tracking bodies → hooks") covered 4 functions with very different risk profiles. Research + a user check-in narrowed this slice to `pollForDataChanges`'s own state specifically - `pollBackfillStatus`/`pollSchedulerStatus` (tiny, self-contained; `pollSchedulerStatus` deliberately stays `window`-bridged since `index.html.test.js` calls it directly) and `renderRequestActivity`/`beginRequestActivity` (tied to a *different* shared-state cluster used by 10+ unrelated vanilla action handlers) are explicitly out of scope for this slice.

**What moved:** the 7 module-scope `let`s `pollForDataChanges`'s factory depended on (`supportsDataMetaPolling`/`supportsDataManifestPolling`/`lastSeenDataVersion`/`latestPrManifest`/`lastRenderedPrFingerprint`/`lastRenderedMetaFingerprint`/`lastRenderedRunStamp`) moved into a plain `useRef` inside `PrDataProvider.jsx` (no version-tick/re-render needed - nothing ever renders from this state, it's pure diffing bookkeeping). `PrDataProvider.jsx` now builds `pollForDataChanges` itself via the same unchanged, pure `createPrDataPollingOrchestrationHelpers` factory, exposed to `<PrDataPolling />` **directly via Context** (no `window.*` indirection - the actual Phase F goal) and *also* still as `window.pollForDataChanges` (kept only because `index.html.test.js` calls it directly at 17+ call sites and never mounts `<PrDataPolling />` - same "stays callable for tests" precedent `window.pollSchedulerStatus` already established). `<PrDataPolling />` now calls it via `usePrData()` instead of the bridge.

**A 4th consumer surfaced during implementation, beyond the 2 (`applyFreshPrPayload`, `prDataTabOrchestrator`) found during research:** `loadStoredData` (the initial-page-load GET handler) *also* writes `lastSeenDataVersion`/`lastRenderedRunStamp` directly. All 3 vanilla write sites now redirect through one new combined bridge, `window.pushPrDataPollingTrackingState(partial)` - same "who writes when" structure preserved, matching the Phase E precedent. `lastSuccessfulRenderedCheckAt` stays fully vanilla (it's `prDataTabOrchestrator`'s own tab-activation-catch-up concern, never actually read by `pollForDataChanges` itself - a scope narrowing, not an oversight).

**A real chicken-and-egg bug found via the full suite, not assumed up front:** `loadStoredData` calls its 2 setters *before* calling `renderPrData` - but `renderPrData` is what eventually mounts `<PrDataProvider>` (via `mountReactTable`), so on every page's very first load, that write always races a bridge (`window.pushPrDataPollingTrackingState`) that doesn't exist yet, silently dropping the initial `dataVersion`/`runStamp` seed (confirmed by a real failing test: "skips full data fetch when polling sees an unchanged data version" - the dropped seed made the first poll always see a "changed" version and fetch unnecessarily). Fixed by seeding `trackingRef`'s initial value directly from `initialPayload` (the same prop `PrDataProvider` already receives and already uses to seed its own `payload` state) - the exact same `dataMeta.dataVersion`/`lastRun.updatedAt` extraction `pr-stored-data-load.helpers.js` itself uses, so the two stay equivalent without needing the race-prone bridge call for this specific case.

**Also needed:** 2 existing jsdom test files that mounted `<PrDataPolling />` standalone (`PrDataPolling.test.jsx`, `index.page.memory-leak-prevention.test.jsx`) needed wrapping in a minimal `<PrDataContext.Provider>` now that `pollForDataChanges` comes from `usePrData()` instead of a bare `window.*` read; and 7 `index.html.test.js` assertions checking `typeof window.pollForDataChanges === "function"` synchronously right after page-init needed a `waitFor` wrapper, since that bridge is now set inside a React effect (async relative to `initTestPage`) instead of at vanilla module-load time (sync).

**New reverse bridges** (vanilla primitives exposed *for* `PrDataProvider.jsx`, same category as every other one in this migration): `window.computePrDataManifest`/`getManifestDelta`/`mergeDataDeltaPayload`/`computePrDataFingerprint`/`computePrDataMetaFingerprint`/`getDataPollRenderAction` (the pure helpers, still wired with the real `getOrComputeEntryDerivedValue` cache - not duplicated), `window.getLatestStoredPayload`/`applyLatestPrData`/`renderPrData`/`markPollSuccess`/`showPollFailureWarning`.

**Not closed by this slice, as scoped from the start:** `pollBackfillStatus`/`pollSchedulerStatus`/`renderRequestActivity`/`beginRequestActivity` remain vanilla, `window`-bridged functions - a deliberate scope correction, not an oversight (see this entry's own opening paragraph).

**Verified:** full jest suite (266 suites, 2518 tests), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (50/50 passing).

---

## 🗺️ **Reconciling every deferred item from Phases A-F back into the plan (2026-10-12)**

Asked directly: across Phases A-F, where was scope narrowed or work deferred, and is each deferral correctly reflected as *remaining* work somewhere later in the plan, rather than silently dropped? Went back through every "Not closed by this slice" note. Two kinds turned up: items that are **permanently** out of scope for a specific, confirmed reason (correctly closed, not deferred), and items that are **genuinely still pending** and need a landing spot later in the sequence. This entry is that landing spot - nothing below is new work *decided* here, it's existing deferred work being made visible again instead of staying buried in a slice's own writeup.

**There is no "permanently out of scope" category (standing policy, set 2026-10-12 by direct user instruction).** Three earlier corrections already walked this back piece by piece - the "vanilla primitive exposed for React" bridges, then the test-only bridges, then (below) the last 2 stragglers found during Phase D.2's own review - but the pattern kept repeating because the category itself was the bug: every time something got filed as "permanent," it turned out to just be a migration nobody had scoped yet. So as of now, nothing gets filed there again. Every `window.*` bridge and every DOM-reached-via-bridge piece of state gets an actual landing spot below, even the ones that look like they need a real architectural change (a Provider-tree reorder, a ref replacing a DOM query) to close. "Hard to close soon" is not the same as "permanent," and this plan does not conflate the two anymore.

**Formerly-miscategorized items, now with real landing spots:**
- `getDirtyTrackedFields`'s `data-original-value` DOM scan (Phase B) - re-confirmed zero live writers anywhere in `src/ui`. Not "permanent dead code to maybe delete someday" - dead code with no caller is just a deletion, and a deletion is a 1-line change with no reason to wait. **Landing spot: folded into Phase H's cleanup sweep** (below) rather than its own phase, since it's a deletion, not a migration, but it still gets tracked and done, not left floating indefinitely.
- The 9 multi-select lists' *persist*-side DOM reads (`getSelectedAuthorLogins()` etc., Phase E) - 7 of these 9 already have a reactive Context mirroring this same checked-state (`RowFilterSelectionContext` for label/exclude-label/author/assigned/approver; `AuthorThreadResolutionSelectionContext` for the 2 thread-resolution lists) - `FilterStateProvider`/`PrDataProvider` just can't reach it today because both sit *above* those Providers in `react-app.jsx`'s tree, not below. The remaining 2 lists have no Context at all yet. **Landing spot: new Phase G.3** (below) - reorders the Provider tree so `FilterStateProvider`/`PrDataProvider` sit below `RowFilterSelectionProvider`/`AuthorThreadResolutionSelectionProvider`, gives the 2 Context-less lists one, and replaces every persist-side bridge read with a direct Context read.
- The "vanilla primitive exposed *for* React" bridges (`window.recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields`/`window.applyFreshPrPayload`/`window.beginRequestActivity`/etc.) - they exist only because the *logic on the other side* of each bridge - `applyFreshPrPayload`'s payload-merge, `beginRequestActivity`'s counter bookkeeping, `recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields`'s dirty-field diffing - is still vanilla. Once the vanilla side each of these reaches into is itself converted (Phases D.2/F.2/G.1/G.2 do that piece by piece), the bridge has nothing left to reach. **Landing spot: Phase H**, which already was "final retirement" but now explicitly includes removing these specific globals once their vanilla-side dependents are gone, not just the ones named in Phase H's original description.
- The remaining test-only bridges (`window.pollSchedulerStatus`, `window.pollForDataChanges`, and any others matching the "a test still pokes `window.X` directly" shape). **Landing spot: Phase H** rewrites those test assertions to render through React and call via hooks/Context, same as every other test in this migration, then deletes the bridge.
- **Found during Phase D.2's own review (2026-10-12), same correction applies:** `window.autoScrollBackfillLogToBottom` (real DOM scroll-position + checkbox-checked read, `BackfillLogControls.jsx`'s own auto-scroll toggle) and `window.refreshAvailableRepoLabels` (its fetch-dedup state is shared with `index.page.js`'s `renderPrData`, which auto-refetches labels whenever the selected repo changes) were almost filed as permanent in Phase D.2's own write-up - same mistake, caught before it stuck. **Landing spots: new Phase G.4** (`autoScrollBackfillLogToBottom` - replace the DOM query with a ref owned directly by `BackfillLogControls.jsx`, so the scroll-on-new-log-line behavior lives in the component that renders both the checkbox and the log container, no bridge needed) and **new Phase G.5** (`refreshAvailableRepoLabels` - move `renderPrData`'s automatic repo-change-triggered refetch into `RepoLabelsProvider.jsx` itself, as an effect watching the selected repo via Context, so there's one React-owned implementation instead of a vanilla one a React trigger has to reach through a bridge).

Target end state, unchanged in spirit but now actually enforced: **zero `window.*` bridges left anywhere in the codebase**, and zero bridge-reached DOM state that a Provider could instead own directly.

**Genuinely deferred, still pending - added to the forward plan below:**

1. **`navigateToPrInTable` (+2 sibling bridges)/`navigateToAuthorInsights`'s remaining vanilla halves** - from the original "what's actually left" inventory's item 1, only ever half-addressed. Phase A closed the *tab-switch* call inside each (now goes through `TabStateProvider` via `window.setActiveDataTab`), but the rest of each function is still fully vanilla DOM work that was never picked back up: `navigateToPrInTable`'s own anchor-scan/`scrollIntoView`/`focus`/unsaved-element-detection (`pr-auto-render-navigation.helpers.js`), and `navigateToAuthorInsights`'s still-vanilla author-selection validation against `renderAuthorInsights`'s own option list. No phase since A has touched either. **Landing spot: new Phase G.1** (below), since it's the same "vanilla DOM-manipulation primitive, callable from both React and vanilla" shape Phase G's own busy/queued retirement work will already be elbow-deep in.
2. **`AuthorInsightsDraftsContext`'s own exposed shape** - still only a bridge-read snapshot (`window.getAuthorInsightsDraftsSnapshot()`), not a reactive subscription; explicitly flagged in Phase B's own entry as a separate follow-up and never revisited since. **Landing spot: new Phase G.2.**
3. **Phases C and D's original visions weren't actually completed, the same way in both cases** - the roadmap described Phase C as "a new `useBackfillActions()` hook over the already-pure `createPrBackfillActionHelpers`" and Phase D as "hooks over the already-pure ack-label/run-script DI factories"; what actually shipped for *both* converted only the trigger buttons (confirmed via a direct check while writing this entry: no `useBackfillActions()` file exists anywhere in the repo), leaving `handleBackfillAction`/`loadBackfillStatus`/`loadBackfillLogTail`/`autoScrollBackfillLogToBottom` (Phase C) and `handleRunScript`/`runAckOnlyWorkflow`/`runClearOnlyWorkflow`/`runApplyLabelWorkflow`/`refreshAvailableRepoLabels` (Phase D) all as vanilla DI-factory functions, reached via `window.*` bridges exactly as before. Phase C's own entry wrongly claimed "Not closed: nothing" - corrected in place now that this was found. This matters beyond either phase itself: **Phase G's own stated prerequisite is "once D's hooks exist"** - which never happened for D *or* C, so Phase G has been sitting on an unmet precondition since Phase F's own investigation first noticed the Phase D half of this. **Landing spot: new Phase D.2**, sequenced immediately before Phase G below - the real hooks (`useBackfillActions()`, `useRunScript()`, `useAckLabelActions()`, etc., over the existing pure factories, same shape `useSinglePrUpdate.jsx` already proved) that Phase G actually needs.
4. **Phase F's other 3 functions** - `pollBackfillStatus`/`pollSchedulerStatus`/`renderRequestActivity`/`beginRequestActivity` were explicitly scoped out of Phase F (tied to shared state well beyond polling), but the original roadmap named all 4 as Phase F's target, not just `pollForDataChanges`. **Landing spot: new Phase F.2** (below) - `renderRequestActivity`/`beginRequestActivity`'s own request-activity-counter state (used by 10+ action handlers) is the real remaining complexity here, comparable in shape to Phase F's own `trackingRef` work, now that there's a proven pattern for it.

**Updated phase sequence (supersedes the "8 sequenced phases" list above for everything from here on):**

- Phases A-F: done (see their own entries above - Phase C's "Not closed" note has been corrected in place to reflect item 3 below).
- **Phase D.2 - Backfill/Run Script/ack-label business logic → real hooks. DONE (2026-10-12).** `useBackfillActions()`, `useRunScript()`, `useAckLabelActions()`, `useRefreshAvailableRepoLabels()` (all 4 in `state/`) now own the Start/Stop/Refresh-status/Refresh-log/Autoscroll/Run-script/Ack-only/Clear-only/Apply-label/Refresh-labels triggers - `BackfillControls.jsx`/`BackfillLogControls.jsx`/`RunScriptActionButton.jsx`'s instances (react-app.jsx) call each hook's returned function directly now, not a `window.*` bridge. `useBackfillActions`/`useAckLabelActions` build their own instance of their existing pure DI-factory (`createPrBackfillActionHelpers`/`createPrAckLabelActionsHelpers`), matching `useSinglePrUpdate.jsx`'s precedent; `useRunScript`/`useRefreshAvailableRepoLabels` had no factory to wrap (confirmed - they were always plain top-level functions in `index.page.js`), so (also matching `useSinglePrUpdate.jsx`'s own precedent for pieces with no factory) `useRunScript` inlines `handleRunScript`'s logic directly, while `useRefreshAvailableRepoLabels` stays a deliberate thin pass-through to the existing `window.refreshAvailableRepoLabels` bridge - its state is genuinely shared with an automatic second caller inside `index.page.js`'s own `renderPrData` (re-triggered on repo change), so forking an independent copy would risk the same kind of divergence bug Phase F had to fix for `pollForDataChanges`'s tracking state.
  - **New reverse bridges added** (vanilla primitives exposed *for* the new hooks, same category as every other one in this migration): `window.getBackfillTrackingState`/`setSupportsBackfillLogPolling`/`setIsBackfillActionPending`/`updateBackfillStatusFromPayload`/`setBackfillLogMessage`/`renderBackfillStatus` (so `useBackfillActions`'s own factory instance reads/writes the exact same module-level state `index.page.js`'s own instance uses for the backfill tab orchestrator/initial auto-load, instead of forking a second copy), and `window.getFormBody`/`summarizeAckRefreshWarnings`/`loadStoredData` (same reasoning, for `useRunScript`/`useAckLabelActions`).
  - **Test-only bridges removed, not kept "for tests" (per direct user instruction, 2026-10-12):** `window.handleBackfillAction`/`refreshBackfillStatus`/`refreshBackfillLogTail`/`handleRunScript`/`handleAckOnly`/`handleClearOnly`/`handleApplyLabelClick` are deleted from `index.page.js` entirely - `index.html.test.js`'s own `BackfillControlsTestHarness`/`installRunScriptActionButtonsBridge` (which used to duplicate react-app.jsx's `window.*`-calling wiring, since that suite never mounts react-app.jsx itself) were rewritten to call the same 4 hooks instead, so nothing but the hooks' own internal DI reads these names anymore. `window.autoScrollBackfillLogToBottom`/`window.refreshAvailableRepoLabels` stay bridged *for this slice* - correctly scoped out of D.2 itself (see this entry's own opening paragraph) - but per the "no permanent category" policy (above), both now have real landing spots: **Phase G.4**/**Phase G.5**, not "kept forever."
  - **Verified:** full jest suite (270 suites, 2528 tests, including 4 new hook test files), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (50/50 passing).
- **Phase F.2 - `renderRequestActivity`/`beginRequestActivity`/`pollBackfillStatus`/`pollSchedulerStatus` → `PrDataProvider`. DONE (2026-10-12).** The request-activity counter/history/scheduler-state bookkeeping (`requestActivityCounters`/`requestActivityStartedAtMs`/`recentRequestActivityEntries`/`latestSchedulerState` - the last one wasn't named in this entry's own one-line description but was tightly coupled, so it moved too) now lives in `PrDataProvider.jsx`'s own `activityRef`, inlined directly (no new `helpers/` factory - there wasn't a pre-existing one to wrap, same precedent as Phase D.2's `useRunScript`/`useRefreshAvailableRepoLabels`). `pollBackfillStatus`/`pollSchedulerStatus` moved alongside it as thin guard+wrapper functions, still delegating to vanilla `loadBackfillStatus`/`loadSchedulerStatus` (both keep other real vanilla callers - the backfill tab orchestrator/initial auto-load, the quick-check workflow's forced scheduler refresh - untouched by this phase) via 2 new reverse bridges.
  - **`index.page.js`'s 14 real call sites of `beginRequestActivity(key)`/`renderRequestActivity()` all kept working unchanged**, with zero call-site edits: both became thin same-named proxy consts (`const beginRequestActivity = (key) => (window.beginRequestActivity || (() => () => {}))(key)`) forwarding to the `window.*` bridges `PrDataProvider` now installs - far simpler than redirecting all 9 vanilla call/DI sites individually, and the same no-op-before-mount fallback shape `useSinglePrUpdate.jsx`/`useRunScript.jsx`/etc. already use for this exact bridge.
  - **A 3rd read site of `latestSchedulerState` turned up mid-implementation**, beyond the 2 this entry's own research already knew about: `prDataTabOrchestrator`'s `stateGetters.getLatestSchedulerState` - deleting the bare module `let` without fixing this would have been a `ReferenceError` on the next tab-switch render. Fixed with one more reverse bridge (`window.getLatestSchedulerState`), same category as everything else here - exactly the kind of hidden consumer this phase's own scope note said to watch for.
  - **`window.beginRequestActivity`/`window.pollSchedulerStatus` stay bridged** - 5 real React consumers (`useSinglePrUpdate.jsx`/`useRunScript.jsx`/`useBackfillActions.jsx`/`useAckLabelActions.jsx`/`NotesSection.jsx`) already read the first directly, and `index.html.test.js` is the second's only remaining caller (needed a `waitFor` fix there, same shape Phase F already needed for `pollForDataChanges`) - per the "no permanent bridges" policy, neither is permanent, just out of this phase's own scope (Phase H's job).
  - **`window.pollBackfillStatus`/`window.renderRequestActivity` removed outright** - confirmed no caller besides `<PrDataPolling />`, which now reads both from `usePrData()` Context instead (same pattern `pollForDataChanges` already established in Phase F). `PrDataPolling.test.jsx`/`index.page.memory-leak-prevention.test.jsx`'s existing Context-forwarding test harness (added in Phase F for `pollForDataChanges`) got 2 more forwarding entries, so every existing assertion kept working unchanged.
  - **Verified:** full jest suite (270 suites, 2533 tests, including 5 new `PrDataProvider.test.jsx` cases), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (50/50 passing). One pre-existing, environment-level flaky test (`check-open-pr-updates.test.js`, a bash-script integration test unrelated to this phase) was observed failing under full-suite load and passing in isolation and on the pre-F.2 baseline - confirmed not a regression via repeated runs.
- **Phase G - Retire the busy/queued/bulk-batch DI-forwarding params in `useAckLabelActions.jsx`. DONE (2026-10-12).** The one-line description undersold the real blocker: `useAckLabelActions()` is called from `AppRoot`'s own function body (`react-app.jsx`), and `PrActivityQueueProvider` was mounted *inside* `AppRoot`'s own returned JSX - making `AppRoot` the Provider's ancestor, never its descendant (a component can never be a descendant of something it itself renders). No restructuring inside `AppRoot`'s JSX could fix that - the actual fix was a Provider-tree reorder, the same shape Phase G.3 (below) already anticipates: `react-app.jsx`'s `mountAppRoot()` now wraps `<AppRoot />` itself in `<PrActivityQueueProvider>` at the root `.render()` call, and the old, now-redundant inner `<PrActivityQueueProvider>` (which only wrapped the drawer/`PrTableApp` portals) was removed. That made `AppRoot` - and everything it calls directly, including `useAckLabelActions()` - a genuine Context descendant.
  - `PrActivityQueueProvider.jsx`'s own Context `value` only exposed 4 of the 8 relevant functions before this phase (`markPrBusy`/`clearPrBusy`/`markPrQueued`/`clearPrQueued` - the 4 bulk-batch mutators, `beginBulkActionBatch`/`markBulkActionChunkInFlight`/`markBulkActionChunkDone`/`finishBulkActionBatch`, had no Context equivalent at all, only a `window.*` one). Added all 4 as stable `useCallback`s, exposed via Context alongside the window bridges (which stay - see below).
  - `useAckLabelActions.jsx` now calls `usePrActivityQueue()` directly instead of reading `window.markPrsBusy`/`beginBulkActionBatch`/etc. The 4 busy/queued Context functions are singular/per-PR (`markPrBusy(prNumber, repo)`) where the factory wants plural/array-taking (`markPrsBusy(prNumbers, repo)`) - wrapped in the same per-PR-loop shape `PrActivityQueueProvider.jsx`'s own window-bridge effect already used, not forwarded 1:1. The 4 bulk-batch functions matched the factory's expected shape exactly - direct passthrough.
  - **Confirmed out of scope, staying bridged** (not this phase's job): `index.page.js`'s own separate `createPrAckLabelActionsHelpers` instance (feeds `react-callbacks.helpers.js`'s per-row Ack/Apply-Label path) and `pr-quick-check-actions.helpers.js`'s `markPrsBusy`/`clearPrsBusy` DI (also from `index.page.js`) - both are vanilla JS with no render-tree position, structurally unable to ever become Context consumers no matter how the Provider tree is reorganized. They stay `window.*`-bridged until Phase H retires `index.page.js` entirely - the 8 window bridges themselves were *not* removed by this phase, only given a Context-reachable alternative for the one caller (`useAckLabelActions.jsx`) that could actually use it.
  - **Verified:** full jest suite (270 suites, 2535 tests, including a new real-`PrActivityQueueProvider` busy-state test in `useAckLabelActions.test.jsx` and a Context-vs-window-bridge parity test in `PrActivityQueueProvider.test.jsx`), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (50/50 passing, including the 2 specs that exercise the busy-indicator/bulk-queue UI directly - the real regression check for a Provider-tree reorder like this).
- **Phase G.1 - `navigateToPrInTable`/`navigateToAuthorInsights`'s remaining vanilla halves → React. DONE (2026-10-13).** Both were genuine DOM-navigation operations (find a node, `scrollIntoView`/`focus()` it), not disguised pure functions - the tab-switch half was already closed (Phase A). New hooks `state/useNavigateToPrInTable.jsx`/`state/useNavigateToAuthorInsights.jsx` replace `window.navigateToPrInTable`/`window.navigateToAuthorInsights`, called directly by `AutoRenderBlockedLinks.jsx`'s chips.
  - `navigateToPrInTable`'s "which row" lookup was already duplicated in React - `PrTableApp.jsx`'s existing `pr-navigate-to-insights` CustomEvent listener (added for Author Insights' "View in table" link) does the same `sectionsRef`-based data lookup the vanilla anchor-scan did. Extended that same listener (no new event, no new wiring elsewhere) to track a pending navigation target and, once its insights row actually commits, scroll/focus the right node via a scoped `document.querySelector` against the `data-pr-number`/`data-repo` attributes `PrNumberCell.jsx` already renders - same selectors vanilla used, just run from inside the component that owns that markup instead of a cross-cutting helper scanning the whole document.
  - `navigateToAuthorInsights`'s validation rule (`resolveValidAuthorLogin`), option list (`useAuthorInsights().buildAuthorInsightsEntries`), and write target (`selectedAuthorLogin`) were all already reachable from React with zero new Context needed - only a Context-exposed setter was missing (`PrDataProvider.jsx` gained `setSelectedAuthorLogin`, mirroring `setPayload`/`setStatsViewState`). No vanilla `authorInsightsState` mutation needed anymore for this path.
  - Both functions' `focusUnsaved` branches (the behavior `AutoRenderBlockedLinks.jsx` always requests) resolve a specific DOM node via the same `querySelector` chain vanilla used, against data attributes (`data-pr-number`, `data-author-login`, `data-draft-kind`) the real components already render - no ref-map needed, a scoped query was simpler and sufficient.
  - `helpers/pr-auto-render-navigation.helpers.js` (and its test file) had no other caller once this landed - deleted outright, along with `createPrDomVisibilityHelpers`'s own instantiation in `index.page.js` (its only 2 exports, `expandAncestorDetailsElements`/`ensureInsightsRowVisibleForElement`, had no other caller either) and `getFirstUnsavedElementForPrNumber`'s destructure (same reason) - a real functional retirement, not just an un-bridging.
  - **Added real e2e coverage after the fact (2026-10-13, on request):** no existing spec drove `AutoRenderBlockedLinks`' own chips directly (the 2 "View in table" specs exercise the *sibling* `pr-author-insights-pr-link.helpers.js` implementation, untouched by this phase) - added one. Writing it caught a real bug the unit/jsdom tests couldn't see: `AutoRenderBlockedLinks.jsx`'s portal (inside `JobEventsProvider`) sat *outside* `TabStateProvider`'s boundary in `react-app.jsx`, so `useNavigateToPrInTable`/`useNavigateToAuthorInsights`'s `useTabState()` call silently resolved to the Context's no-op default instead of the real Provider - the tab never actually switched in production, only the row-expand/scroll/focus half worked. Same "a sibling portal needs to be a real descendant, not just visually adjacent" bug class Phase G's own `PrActivityQueueProvider` move fixed - `TabStateProvider`'s own boundary in `react-app.jsx` was widened to also wrap `AutoRenderBlockedLinks.jsx`'s portal (it's cheap, pure-state Context, so widening it costs nothing). The new e2e test also had to use the *real* current payload for its `window.setPendingAutoRenderPayload` setup (not a fake stub) - a dirty field's blur (switching tabs away from it) triggers `AutoRenderBlockingProvider`'s own real "apply once blurred" behavior, and a fake/empty payload would have actually been applied and wiped the whole table; a harmless same-data payload avoids that while still exercising the real code path.
  - **Verified:** full jest suite (271 suites, 2538 tests, including new `useNavigateToPrInTable.test.jsx`/`useNavigateToAuthorInsights.test.jsx`, a rewritten `AutoRenderBlockedLinks.test.jsx`, and 2 new `PrTableApp.test.jsx` cases), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (51/51 passing, including the new e2e spec, confirmed stable across repeated full-suite runs - it's sensitive to shared-webServer test-order pollution, same as a few other specs in this file, so it scopes every locator to the PR's own lifecycle section rather than a bare PR-number match).
- **Phase G.2 - `AuthorInsightsDraftsContext` exposes its dirty-draft maps reactively. DONE (2026-10-13).** Narrower in practice than the one-line roadmap description implied: `AuthorInsightsDraftsProvider.jsx` already built a stable `getDraftsSnapshot` callback (feeding the `window.getAuthorInsightsDraftsSnapshot` bridge) but never added it to the Context's own memoized `value` - so there was no way for a React consumer to read the dirty-draft maps at all, only the bridge. Added `getDraftsSnapshot` to the Context `value` (and a matching no-op default in `AuthorInsightsDraftsContext.jsx`), so a future real consumer can call `useAuthorInsightsDrafts().getDraftsSnapshot()` reactively.
  - **No real consumer migrated** - the only real caller of the bridge is `index.page.js`'s `getBlockingAuthorInsightsLogins` wiring, feeding `recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields` (called from many non-React event handlers) - fully vanilla with no render-tree position, the same "structurally unable to ever become a Context consumer" category as other confirmed-out-of-scope callers elsewhere in this plan. `getDirtyTrackedFields` (the roadmap's "adjacent" reference) turned out to be unrelated dead code sitting next to that caller in the same options object, not something that calls the bridge - it stays slated for Phase H's own cleanup sweep. The `window.getAuthorInsightsDraftsSnapshot` bridge itself stays, out of scope until `index.page.js` retires in Phase H.
  - **Secondary cleanup:** fixed 3 stale doc comments (`AuthorInsightsDraftsContext.jsx`, `AuthorInsightsCommentsSection.jsx`, `index.page.js`) left over from Phase G.1, which had deleted `pr-auto-render-navigation.helpers.js` and its `getFirstDirtyAuthorInsightsElement` export outright but left comments in these 3 files still describing it as a live consumer.
  - **Verified:** full jest suite (271 suites, 2539 tests, including a new Context-vs-bridge parity test in `AuthorInsightsDraftsProvider.test.jsx`), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (51/51 passing). No new e2e coverage added - this phase added a capability, not a user-facing behavior change.
- **Phase G.3 - Close the 9 multi-select lists' persist-side DOM reads. DONE (2026-10-13).** Reordered `react-app.jsx`'s Provider tree so `RowFilterSelectionProvider`/`AuthorThreadResolutionSelectionProvider`/a new `ChangeFilterActorSelectionProvider` now wrap `PrDataProvider`/`FilterStateProvider` (previously the other way around) - `FilterStateProvider.jsx`'s own `buildOverrides()` calls `useRowFilterSelection()`/`useAuthorThreadResolutionSelection()`/`useChangeFilterActorSelection()` directly now, replacing every `window.getSelectedAuthorLogins()`-shaped DOM-scan bridge read. `ChangeFilterActorSelectionContext`/`Provider` (mirroring `RowFilterSelectionContext`/`Provider` exactly) gives the last 2 of the 9 lists (the change-filter actor lists) a Context for the first time; `MultiSelectListPortals.jsx` wires them in the same way as the other 7. `index.page.js`'s separate `getFormBody()` call (run-script submission, not persistence) keeps reading the same DOM-scan bridges unchanged - genuinely vanilla with no render-tree position, confirmed out of scope.
  - **The real work was a test-harness restructuring, not just the Provider-tree reorder.** `index.html.test.js` never mounts real `react-app.jsx` - it hand-built 2 *independent* `rtlRender` trees (`installFilterStateProviderBridge()` for `FilterStateProvider` alone, `window.mountReactPrTable` for `PrTableApp`/`MultiSelectListPortals`), which could never share a Context instance no matter how their DOM output nested. Rather than add a test-only bridge (conflicts with this session's "no permanent bridges" policy) or leave the behavior unconverted, merged them into one persistent root: `installFilterStateProviderBridge()` now mounts `FilterStateTestRootHarness` (owns all 3 selection Providers + `FilterStateProvider`), and `window.mountReactPrTable` triggers a `createPortal` from *inside* that same tree (via a module-level `mountReactPrTableSlotController`, wrapped in `act()` to keep the mount synchronous) instead of starting a second, disconnected `rtlRender` tree. The one other `installReactTableMountBridge()` call site outside the main `initTestPage()` flow needed its own `installFilterStateProviderBridge()` call added, in the same relative position (after that test's own fetch mock is wired) as the main flow already required.
  - **Fallout fixed across 15 test files** that rendered `FilterStateProvider` without the 3 selection-Context ancestors it now unconditionally requires (all 3 hooks throw without a real Provider) - 10 simple `renderWithProvider`-shaped files wrapped in place, 3 files (`useAppliedFilterSummary.test.jsx`, `useMergedRequestMoreActionOptions.test.jsx`, `PrTableApp.test.jsx`) needed their existing `RowFilterSelectionProvider` nested *inside* `FilterStateProvider` flipped to wrap it instead (matching the real reorder), plus `FilterStateProvider.test.jsx` itself and the new `ChangeFilterActorSelectionProvider.test.jsx`.
  - **One genuine async-settling fix, not a bug in this phase's own code:** a pre-existing test (`"given user-defaults with change filters, when page loads, then selections are restored"`) asserted synchronously on the 2 newly-Context-controlled lists' restored checked state - now that `MultiSelectCheckboxList` reads `checkedSet` from Context for these 2 lists too (previously fully local state, no Context write at all), the pending-selection-restore path's own "clear after consuming" step re-triggers one more seeding pass before the Context write commits to the DOM, same transient window an existing thread-resolution restore test already wraps in `waitFor` - wrapped this test's 2 assertions in `waitFor` the same way, confirmed via a temporary debug trace (pending store/seeding-effect inputs logged across both effect runs) before concluding it was a timing settle, not a real data-loss bug.
  - **Verified:** full jest suite (272 suites, 2545 tests, including the new `ChangeFilterActorSelectionProvider.test.jsx` and a new `FilterStateProvider.test.jsx` case asserting `buildOverrides()`'s output actually reflects live sibling Context writes, not just that it doesn't crash), confirmed stable across 2 full runs; `npx eslint .` clean project-wide; full Playwright smoke suite (51/51 passing, including the spec that exercises thread-resolution/change-filter multi-select persisted restore directly - the real end-to-end regression check for this reorder).
- **Phase G.4 - `autoScrollBackfillLogToBottom` → a ref owned by `BackfillLogControls.jsx` itself. DONE (2026-10-13).** `BackfillLogControls.jsx` didn't actually render the log container before this - `#backfill-log` was a separate sibling portal `react-app.jsx`'s `AppRoot` owned directly, which is exactly why scroll-sync needed a DOM-query bridge in the first place (no single component owned both the checkbox and the log container to read/write via refs). Now it owns both: `logText`/`logContainer` (the raw `#backfill-log` DOM node) are passed in, `BackfillLogControls` renders the log portal itself, holds `autoscrollCheckboxRef` on its own checkbox, and reuses the same pure `shouldAutoScrollBackfillLog`/`getBackfillScrollTop` functions (`pr-backfill.helpers.js`) the old bridge used - called directly instead of through `window.autoScrollBackfillLogToBottom`. `useBackfillActions.jsx` no longer returns `autoScrollBackfillLogToBottom` and no-ops its own DI value for it (redundant now that `BackfillLogControls` self-triggers).
  - **A real design bug found and fixed during implementation, not just a test-timing issue:** the first attempt keyed the scroll-sync `useEffect` on `logText` itself - broke immediately because a log refresh that happens to fetch *identical* text (a common, real case - polling an unchanged log) never changes `useState`'s value, so React bails out of re-rendering and the effect silently never fires for that refresh. The old bridge call was unconditional, with no such gap. Fixed by adding a separate `backfillLogUpdateTick` counter (`react-app.jsx`, bumped on every `window.updateReactBackfillLogText` call regardless of whether the text actually changed) and keying the effect on that instead - caught by the one real end-to-end jest test for this behavior (`index.html.test.js`), not by the new unit tests (which didn't yet simulate "same text, new refresh" until updated alongside the fix).
  - **Confirmed out of scope, staying bridged:** `window.autoScrollBackfillLogToBottom` itself (and `pr-ui-option-scroll.helpers.js`'s factory) - `index.page.js`'s own vanilla backfill tab orchestrator/initial-auto-load still has a live, non-React caller for it. `window.getBackfillTrackingState()`'s `isBackfillRunning` read also stays bridged inside `BackfillLogControls.jsx` (just inlined, not routed through the closed factory) - threading it into real `AppRoot` state would mean changing `renderBackfillStatus`'s payload shape, a different bridge's concern.
  - **Verified:** full jest suite (272 suites, 2549 tests, including a rewritten `BackfillLogControls.test.jsx` with real scroll-behavior assertions replacing the old callback-fired-once test), confirmed stable across 2 full runs; `npx eslint .` clean project-wide; full Playwright smoke suite (51/51 passing, including the spec that checks `#backfill-log` for stale placeholder text).
- **Phase G.5 - `refreshAvailableRepoLabels`'s automatic repo-change trigger → `RepoLabelsProvider.jsx`. DONE (2026-10-13).** `RepoLabelsProvider.jsx` is a real descendant of `<PrDataProvider />` (`react-app.jsx`'s `PrDataProvider > FilterStateProvider > RepoLabelsProvider` nesting), so it now reads `selectedRepo` directly via `usePrData()` and triggers `window.refreshAvailableRepoLabels(selectedRepo)` itself (via its own `lastTriggeredRepoRef`, when the repo is non-empty and differs from the last one it triggered for) - replacing `index.page.js`'s `applyResolvedRepo()`, which used to decide this (comparing against its own `labelsFetchedForRepo` module variable). `labelsFetchedForRepo`/`shouldRefetchLabelsForRepo` had no other caller - deleted outright (including their 5 `index.page.labels.test.js` unit tests, now testing a deleted function), not kept "just in case."
  - **Scope boundary, deliberate:** only the *trigger* moved - `refreshAvailableRepoLabels` itself (the actual fetch, its own `isFetchingRepoLabels`/`availableRepoLabels` cache, `resolveRepoForLabelsFetch`) and the `window.refreshAvailableRepoLabels` bridge stay exactly as before. The manual "↻ Labels" button (`useRefreshAvailableRepoLabels.jsx`, called from `AppRoot`'s own function body - an *ancestor* of `RepoLabelsProvider`, not a descendant) still reaches through that bridge - converting it to read a Context directly would require `RepoLabelsProvider` to *also* wrap `AppRoot` itself, which would in turn require `PrDataProvider` to move there too (since `RepoLabelsProvider` needs to stay `PrDataProvider`'s descendant to read `selectedRepo` reactively) - a much bigger, differently-scoped Provider-tree reorder this phase's own wording didn't ask for. `FilterStateProvider.jsx`'s own restore-path caller of the bridge is likewise untouched.
  - **Verified:** full jest suite (272 suites, 2548 tests, including 4 new `RepoLabelsProvider.test.jsx` cases exercising the repo-change trigger directly - mount-with-repo, mount-with-no-repo, same-repo-rerender-no-refetch, repo-change-refetches), confirmed stable across 2 full runs; `npx eslint .` clean project-wide; full Playwright smoke suite (51/51 passing, including the spec whose own labels dropdown depends on the initial auto-fetch still working through this new trigger path). No e2e spec exercises an automatic repo-*change* refetch specifically (confirmed via research) - this phase's new trigger-decision logic is verified by the new unit tests only; not claiming e2e coverage that doesn't exist.
- **Phase H - Final retirement.** Reachable only once D.2/F.2/G/G.1/G.2/G.3/G.4/G.5 are all done, not just A-F. Expanded scope (2026-10-12 correction, see above): also removes every "vanilla primitive exposed for React" `window.*` bridge whose vanilla-side dependent has by then been converted (`recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields`/`applyFreshPrPayload`/`beginRequestActivity`/etc.) - these are not permanent, just blocked until their counterpart logic migrates. Phase H **also rewrites the remaining `window.X`-poking assertions in `index.html.test.js`** (`pollSchedulerStatus`, `pollForDataChanges`, and any others of this shape still around by then) to render through React and call via hooks/Context instead, then deletes those bridges too - this is not optional or deferred further, it's in scope for Phase H itself. Phase H also deletes `getDirtyTrackedFields`'s dead `data-original-value` DOM scan as part of its own cleanup sweep, now that nothing is left floating as "permanent." Target end state, with no exceptions carved out anymore: zero `window.*` bridges left anywhere in the codebase.
  - **🔀 Split into sub-phases (2026-10-13, per direct user instruction), same discipline as Phase G → G.1-G.5.** A research pass before starting found Phase H isn't a bounded slice the way every phase before it was - it's effectively "finish the rest of the migration": 60 `window.*` bridges still live in `index.page.js`, ~70+ direct `window.X`-poking call sites across `index.html.test.js`, and each of the 3 "vanilla primitive exposed for React" bridges named above (`recomputeDirtyPrSectionsFields`/`applyFreshPrPayload`/`beginRequestActivity`) still has a live, necessary vanilla-side caller - closing any of them means migrating real remaining logic (the `#pr-sections` dirty-field DOM-delegation, the request-more-merged/ack-label workflows' remaining vanilla paths, etc.), not deleting glue. `index.page.js` itself is still 3238 lines, roughly 30-40% of which is still load-bearing vanilla logic, not bridge declarations. Phase H is now a sequence of sub-phases (H.1, H.2, ...), each getting the same bounded-slice-plus-full-verification treatment as every G.x phase - not attempted as one giant change.
  - **Phase H.1 - delete `getDirtyTrackedFields`'s dead `data-original-value` DOM scan. DONE (2026-10-13).** Confirmed via grep: nothing anywhere in `src/ui` ever sets `data-original-value`, so the scan (`pr-auto-render-unsaved.helpers.js`) always returned `[]` in production. Deleted outright, along with 2 functions that turned out to cascade from it: `getBlockingPrNumberForElement` (called only from inside the now-dead scan's own consumers) and `getFirstUnsavedElementForPrNumber` (already noted dead in `index.page.js`'s own Phase G.1 comment - "had no caller left besides the now-deleted `navigateToPrInTable`/`navigateToAuthorInsights`" - but the function itself was left behind until now). `getOptionalElementById`/`readElementAttribute` DI params dropped from the factory (no longer used by anything in it); `getBlockingPrNumbers` simplified to wrap its one remaining source (`getUnsavedNotesPrNumbers`) with the same Set-dedup+sort behavior.
    - `pr-auto-render-state.helpers.js`'s `getAutoRenderBlockingState()` return shape keeps its `dirtyFieldCount` field (real consumers read it: `AutoRenderBlockingProvider.jsx`, `pr-auto-render-indicator.helpers.js`) - now hardcoded to `0`, exactly matching its actual runtime value before this change (the DI param that fed it is gone, not the field itself).
    - **Verified:** full jest suite (272 suites, 2546 tests - 2 removed for the deleted `getFirstUnsavedElementForPrNumber` coverage, replaced by one tighter test of `getBlockingPrNumbers`' dedup behavior), confirmed stable across 2 full runs; `npx eslint .` clean project-wide; full Playwright smoke suite (51/51 passing). The "interval fires the poll" behavior was already covered elsewhere with real fake timers (`PrDataPolling.test.jsx`/`index.page.memory-leak-prevention.test.jsx`, both against a *mocked* `pollForDataChanges`), which made `index.html.test.js` calling the real async orchestration function directly look like a legitimate, deliberate test seam for precise multi-poll sequencing control (one test runs 3 sequential polls, each needing a different fetch-mock swapped in between, asserting intermediate state) rather than technical debt the way `pollSchedulerStatus`'s dead bridge was. Flagged this tradeoff to the user before implementing; the user chose to do the rewrite anyway.

The 9 tests themselves were wrapped in a new nested `describe("data polling (via real PrDataPolling interval)", ...)` with its own `beforeEach`/`afterEach` installing/tearing down `jest.useFakeTimers({ doNotFake: ["Date"] })` (scoped to timers only - this suite's fixtures/assertions rely on real `Date`/ISO timestamps elsewhere). All 12 `await window.pollForDataChanges()` call sites became `await act(async () => { await jest.advanceTimersByTimeAsync(DATA_POLL_MS); })`; the 7 `typeof window.pollForDataChanges === "function"` readiness checks were dropped (either removed outright where a later `waitFor`/`screen.findByRole` already covered readiness, or replaced with a `.pr-row` existence check).

DONE (2026-10-14).** The one bridge-closing candidate H.4's research actually found (`useAckLabelActions.jsx` already builds the same `createPrAckLabelActionsHelpers` instance the bulk buttons use, via Context instead of window bridges) - but research surfaced it wasn't a simple prop redirect: the real per-row handlers (`handleAckAction`/`handleApplyLabel`) were built by vanilla `createReactCallbacks()` (`index.page.js`, called once at table-mount time) and threaded as fixed props through `window.mountReactPrTable` into `AppRoot`'s local state. `AppRoot` already calls `useAckLabelActions()` itself, but **`AppRoot` is `PrDataProvider`'s ancestor, not its descendant** (its own JSX renders `<PrDataProvider>`) - the same "can't be a descendant of something it renders" constraint Phase G's own `PrActivityQueueProvider` move already hit once - so `AppRoot` can't reach `setPayload` to push a fresh table payload after an action.

One of those redesigns caught a secondary bug in the *test's own mock data*, not production: a mocked `prData: {}` (empty object) wiped the whole table payload, breaking the busy-indicator assertion for unrelated reasons - fixed by mocking `prData: null` instead (triggers the real "fall through, keep existing payload" path `applyFreshTablePayload` already handles correctly). `index.html.test.js`'s own parallel harness needed no behavioral changes - it already exercises the real Ack/Apply-Label click end-to-end against a real `fetchMock`, and `PrActivityQueueContext`'s safe default (not throwing without a Provider) meant no new Provider-wrapping fallout there either, confirmed by running its full 105-test file unchanged.

No equivalent existed in `PrTableApp.test.jsx`'s redesigned tests (only the `isAcked=false` case was covered). Added `given onAckAction with isAcked=true..., then it posts an ackClear request, not ack` to close that gap.

  - **Phase H.7 - the author-thread-resolution allow/deny `<details>` visibility toggle → a headless React component. DONE (2026-10-16).** Replaces `index.page.js`'s vanilla `updateAuthorThreadResolutionRuleVisibility()` (and its `window.updateAuthorThreadResolutionRuleVisibility` bridge, called from `FilterStateProvider.jsx`'s own mount/change effect and from a delegated native "change" listener) with `components/AuthorThreadResolutionRuleVisibility.jsx`, a headless (renders `null`) component reading `attentionAuthorThreadResolutionMode` from `FilterStateContext` directly, mounted as a plain child inside `FilterStateProvider`'s subtree (`react-app.jsx`'s `buildFilterStateFieldPortals()`) - same category as `PrDataPolling.jsx`.

Root cause: `react-app.jsx`'s `computeStaticContainers()` resolves `MultiSelectListPortals.jsx`'s own portal targets (`document.getElementById(listId)`) **synchronously, once, before any React commit** - the 2 list `<div>`s only existed once *this* component's own portal had committed, a chicken-and-egg ordering `npx jest`'s jsdom-only tests never caught (no real paint/commit-order timing), but a real browser did immediately. Fixed by reverting the 2 `<details>`/list `<div>`s to stay exactly as-is, static markup in `index.html` (never React-rendered) - the new component instead mutates `hidden`/`open` on those same static nodes via `getElementById`, inside a `useEffect` keyed on `mode`, the same DOM write the vanilla function did, just driven by Context instead of a bridge call. This is the right scope for this phase: the bridge and the vanilla function are gone; the static-markup/portal-timing dependency those 2 lists have is a separate, larger concern (not touched here).
    - **Verified:** full jest suite (276 suites, 2571 tests, including new `AuthorThreadResolutionRuleVisibility.test.jsx`), confirmed stable; `npx eslint .` clean project-wide; full Playwright smoke suite (55/55 passing) - critically, **after** the first (broken) version had already passed the full jest suite cleanly, confirming again (same lesson as Phase G.1's portal-boundary bug) that this migration's jsdom unit suite cannot catch real DOM-commit-order/portal-timing bugs - only Playwright does, which is why it stays mandatory for every phase touching real DOM structure, not just a "nice to have."
  - **Phase H.8 - `window.setStatusMessage`/`window.updateReactStatusText`'s React-side callers → a `StatusTextContext`. DONE (2026-10-16).** `statusText` (the `#status` panel) used to be a plain `useState('Not run')` inside `AppRoot` (react-app.jsx), fed only by a `window.updateReactStatusText` bridge install effect - meaning `useSinglePrUpdate`/`useRunScript`/`useBackfillActions`/`useAckLabelActions`/`useRowCheckboxActions`/`AutoRenderBlockingProvider`/`PrDataProvider` all had to bounce through that bridge (or the composed `window.setStatusMessage`, which also called `renderRequestActivity()`) just to update a *sibling* piece of React state, purely because `AppRoot` is each of those callers' ancestor, not their descendant (same "can't be a descendant of something it renders" constraint Phase G's `PrActivityQueueProvider` move and Phase H.5 already hit). New `state/StatusTextContext.jsx` + `components/StatusTextProvider.jsx` (mirroring `PrActivityQueueProvider`'s own shape and reasoning exactly) now wraps `<AppRoot />` from `mountAppRoot()`, making every one of those 7 callers a genuine Context descendant; each now calls `useStatusText().setStatusText(message)` directly, still composed with the untouched `window.renderRequestActivity?.()` bridge call where the original behavior needed it (`useRunScript`/`useBackfillActions`/`useAckLabelActions`/`AutoRenderBlockingProvider`/`PrDataProvider` - preserves the mid-operation activity-badge refresh the old `window.setStatusMessage` composition provided; `useSinglePrUpdate`/`useRowCheckboxActions` only ever called the text-only bridge, so no composition was needed for those 2). `window.setStatusMessage`/`window.updateReactStatusText` themselves stay - `index.page.js`'s own `setStatusTextOnly`/several vanilla DI factories (`pr-merged-request-more-action`, `pr-apply-filters-cache`, the backfill tab orchestrator's vanilla instance, `pr-data-polling-orchestration`) still call them directly with no React tree position of their own; `StatusTextProvider` just becomes what *answers* the bridge now, not `AppRoot`.
    - **A second, confirmed-in-practice multi-root bug, not just a theoretical risk:** the first attempt had `StatusTextProvider` `createPortal`-ing `statusText` into `#status` (matching `AuthorThreadResolutionRuleVisibility`'s and `AppRoot`'s own prior precedent for these text panels) - broke 4 `index.html.test.js` assertions immediately (`"Not runNot runAck only completed"` instead of `"Ack only completed"`). Root cause: that suite never mounts the real `<AppRoot/>` - it hand-builds **3 separate, independent `rtlRender` trees** that each need this Provider (`BackfillControlsTestHarness`, `RunScriptActionButtonsTestHarness`, the main persistent `FilterStateTestRootHarness`), and unrelated React portals from different roots don't reconcile against each other's children in the same DOM node - each instance's portal just appended its own text node instead of one replacing the other. Fixed by having `StatusTextProvider` write `#status.textContent` directly in a plain `useEffect` instead of a portal - a DOM assignment overwrites unconditionally regardless of which instance last ran, matching the "later install wins" precedent this same test file's own `wireTextBridge` helper already relies on for other panels. Confirmed via research *before* writing this fix that this was a real, reproducible failure mode, not a one-off - flagged to the user as a scope correction (G.3-sized test-harness risk) before implementing, per their explicit go-ahead to proceed anyway.
    - **A 7th caller found during cleanup, folded in as the same pattern:** `PrDataProvider.jsx`'s own `pollForDataChanges` DI (`setStatusMessage: (...args) => window.setStatusMessage?.(...args)`, feeding `pr-data-polling-orchestration.helpers.js`'s "Auto-updated from latest run..." messages) wasn't in the original 6-caller inventory - found via a post-fix grep sweep for remaining `window.setStatusMessage`/`window.updateReactStatusText` reads. `PrDataProvider` is mounted inside `AppRoot`'s own returned JSX (a genuine descendant, not an ancestor-position caller), so closing it needed no Provider-tree change, just the same `useStatusText()` + `window.renderRequestActivity?.()` composition as the other mid-operation callers.
    - **Verified:** full jest suite (276 suites, 2571 tests - all 7 affected hook/provider test files updated to wrap their `Probe`s in a real `StatusTextContext.Provider` instead of mocking the old bridge, net same test count since this was a seam swap, not new coverage), confirmed stable; `npx eslint .` clean project-wide; full Playwright smoke suite (55/55 passing) - this run was only against the already-fixed `textContent` version; the multi-root concatenation bug was caught by `index.html.test.js` itself (not Playwright, which has no equivalent multi-root test harness to expose it) before Playwright ever ran against the broken portal version.

DONE (2026-10-19).** Same shape as `StatusTextProvider.jsx` (Phase H.8): the Provider now *answers* `window.getBackfillTrackingState`/`setSupportsBackfillLogPolling`/`setIsBackfillActionPending`/`updateBackfillStatusFromPayload`/`setBackfillLogMessage`/`renderBackfillStatus` (the remaining 6 of the cluster's original 8 bridges, after H.13 deleted the 7th, `autoScrollBackfillLogToBottom`) instead of index.page.js - both index.page.js's own vanilla `loadBackfillStatus` instance and `useBackfillActions.jsx`'s hook now read/write one real shared source of truth through the exact same bridge names (neither needed to change which bridges they call - only who answers them moved), instead of two independently-drifting copies. `BackfillLogControls.jsx`'s own direct `window.getBackfillTrackingState()` read converted to a real `useBackfillStatus()` Context read (a genuine descendant, since the Provider wraps `<AppRoot/>` from `mountAppRoot()`).

Fixed by reading/writing through refs inside the bridge functions (always-current, no effect-dependency lag), while `useState` stays only for the Context value `BackfillLogControls.jsx` reads reactively - a different consumer with different, correctly-reactive requirements.

DONE (2026-10-19), after explicit user sign-off on the resulting UX change (Needs Attention toggles go live-instant, matching every other Context-migrated filter field).** A dedicated research pass (prompted by the empirical "stub applyFiltersFromCache and run the suite" finding from the prior entry) found the premise behind that finding was itself wrong: the Needs Attention rule engine, per-row icon (`PrAttentionCell`), and smart-group membership were *already* 100% live React/Context - the vanilla pipeline's only remaining real role was incidentally triggering `NeedsAttentionProvider`'s re-render (via a bare `usePrData()` subscription used purely as a "punch through React's stable-children bailout" hack), not computing anything itself. The actual fix: subscribe to `FilterStateContext` directly (`useContext`, same shape `useVisiblePrNumbers.jsx`'s own `useFilteredPrRows()` already uses for its fields), dropping the `usePrData()` punch-through entirely - correct Context propagation makes it unnecessary. **Sizing was revised down** from the audit's original 2-4x estimate to roughly one typical sub-phase, once research established the "feature port" framing was wrong - this was a surgical Provider rewrite, not a feature build.
    - **A real, hard-to-diagnose test-infrastructure bug found and fixed during implementation, not predicted by research:** `index.html.test.js`'s `injectRunFilterFieldElements()` pre-seeds these 6 fields' DOM containers with plain static markup (no React) before `installFilterStateProviderBridge()` portals the real Context-backed components into the same containers - and does not clear that static markup first. `document.getElementById()` for these 6 field ids therefore found the *static* element, not the real React one, in this suite specifically - meaning even a genuine `user.click()`/`user.selectOptions()` interaction (not just a raw `.checked =` write) never reached `FilterStateContext` for these fields in this test file. The old `NeedsAttentionProvider`'s DOM-read fallback had been silently relying on this exact static element the whole time, despite its own comment describing that fallback as a rare edge case - it was actually the primary mechanism several tests depended on. Fixed by adding this suite's own existing `syncFilterStateFromElement` call after every interaction with these 6 fields (3 tests, `attention toggles update icons and persist non-default overrides`/`attention toggle changes update row icons and persist non-default overrides`/`draft attention icon logic follows include-draft toggles`), same pattern already used elsewhere in this file for other DOM-only-presenting fields - not a `NeedsAttentionProvider` bug, a pre-existing test-harness quirk this phase's change was the first to expose.

The prior entry's framing of it as "itself testing 'Needs Attention' smart-group membership" was inaccurate - corrected here.

The 1 still-failing test is exactly the 4th one above (thread-resolution-policy warning text) - it does *not* fail for a Needs-Attention reason (confirmed passing unmodified, see above), but it *does* still depend on the stubbed pipeline for something else (its own "Warning: N thread incorrectly resolved" summary text). So `applyFiltersFromCache`/`debouncedApplyFilters` are narrower than previously known but still not fully dead - the Review Conversations thread-resolution-warning feature is now the one remaining confirmed real dependency, not Needs Attention.

Porting *that* feature to a live derivation too (same shape as this phase, and as the original row-filter work) is the next concrete, bounded step toward fully retiring these 2 bridges - smaller than the original "2-4x" estimate implied, now that 2 of its 3 known dependents (row filters, Needs Attention) are already closed.
  - **Phase H.19 - cluster 1's last known dependent closed: `ReviewThreadsSection.jsx`'s "Warning: N thread incorrectly resolved" summary now recomputes on a live Context change, not just on the vanilla pipeline's re-render. DONE (2026-10-19).** A dedicated research pass (the same empirical-and-code-reading method as H.18) found `useAuthorThreadResolutionPolicy()` itself was *already* a correct, fully live Context derivation (mode from `FilterStateContext`, allow/deny lists from `AuthorThreadResolutionSelectionContext`) - the real bug was one level up: `ReviewThreadsSection`'s own `computeReviewThreadsData` memo (`useMemo(..., [pr, actorsMap])`, with an `eslint-disable-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps`) never listed the live policy as a dependency, so toggling the mode select or an allow/deny checkbox re-rendered the component (a genuine Context consumer) but the memo kept returning its stale cached value regardless - only a `pr`/`actorsMap` reference change (previously only driven by the vanilla `renderPrData` pipeline) ever forced a recompute. No real computation needed porting; `pr-data-tab.orchestrator.js` (what `applyFiltersFromCache` actually runs) has zero references to "thread"/"resolution"/"resolved" at all.
    - **The fix:** `useHelpers()` now also returns the live `authorThreadResolutionPolicy` object directly (not just the `getAuthorThreadResolutionPolicy()` closure `computeReviewThreadsData` calls), and the memo's dependency array was narrowed to `[pr, actorsMap, helpers.authorThreadResolutionPolicy]` instead of the whole `helpers` object - `helpers` itself is a fresh object literal every render (`useHelpers()` isn't memoized), so depending on it whole would make the memo pointless, while `authorThreadResolutionPolicy`'s own internal `useMemo` (keyed on `[mode, checkedByListId]`) only changes reference when the policy genuinely changes. User-visible effect: this warning text now updates instantly when the policy mode/allow/deny lists change, instead of only after "Apply filters (local)" - the same category of fix as H.18, applied one level higher in the same component tree.
    - **A second, real test-infrastructure bug found during verification, same class as H.18's:** the one jest test exercising this ("given both allow and deny starter lists include the same actor, when policy mode switches, then only the active mode list is enforced") drives the mode `<select>` via `document.getElementById("attention-author-thread-resolution-mode")` - a 7th field `index.html.test.js`'s `injectRunFilterFieldElements()` pre-seeds with static markup, the exact same collision H.18 fixed for the 6 attention-* fields, just never covered for this one. Confirmed real (not cosmetic) because fixing the memo first exposed it as a genuine failure: `user.selectOptions()` on this field never reached `FilterStateContext`, so the mode change relied entirely on the vanilla pipeline forcing a re-render that re-read a DOM fallback - which the corrected, Context-accurate memo no longer does. Fixed with the same `syncFilterStateFromElement` calls this file already uses elsewhere (4 call sites in this one test).
    - **Empirically re-verified cluster 1's own "stub `applyFiltersFromCache`/`debouncedApplyFilters`, run the full suite" check** (same method as H.18) against both fixes together: full `index.html.test.js` run (105/105) with both stubbed to no-ops - zero failures, confirming this was genuinely the last of the 3 real dependents (row filters, Needs Attention, this warning text) this pipeline still had. Stub cleanly reverted afterward (`git diff` empty on `index.page.js`).
    - **Verified:** full jest suite (`src/ui`: 227 suites/1659 tests, all passing; full repo run surfaced 8 pre-existing, unrelated failing suites in `src/script`/`src/dependencies`/`src/server` - confirmed via `git stash` to fail identically with this phase's changes fully reverted, so pre-existing to this environment, not a regression); `npx eslint .` clean on every touched file; full Playwright smoke suite run (48/55 passing - the 7 failures, including a `500` from `/view-prs/backfill`, also confirmed via the same `git stash` method to be pre-existing and unrelated to this change, not caused by it). No new Playwright coverage added for the live-update behavior itself: `e2e/fixtures/data.json` has no PR with review threads at all, so exercising this specific fix in a real browser would need new fixture data, not just a new test - out of proportion to this slice; the jest fix is directly and thoroughly covered there instead.
    - **Not closed by this slice:** cluster 1's remaining bridges (`computePrDataManifest`/`getManifestDelta`/`mergeDataDeltaPayload`/`computePrDataFingerprint`/`computePrDataMetaFingerprint`/`getDataPollRenderAction`/`getLatestStoredPayload`/`applyLatestPrData`/`renderPrData`/`markPollSuccess`/`showPollFailureWarning`) are still untouched - closing *those* is a real, substantial port (the `renderPrData`/polling pipeline itself), not a bridge-renaming exercise, unlike the last 2 sub-phases. `applyFiltersFromCache`/`debouncedApplyFilters` have zero remaining *known* dependents now (row filters, Needs Attention, and this warning text are all confirmed closed) - but that's a different claim from "the whole pipeline is dead," since `renderPrData` itself still does real, unported work (the manifest/fingerprint/delta machinery above). A secondary, smaller inconsistency noted but left untouched: `AuthorThreadResolutionRuleVisibility.jsx` reads `values.attentionAuthorThreadResolutionMode` with no DOM-read fallback (unlike `useAuthorThreadResolutionPolicy()`'s own hook) - not the cause of any current failure (it's always mounted inside a real `<FilterStateProvider>` in the real app), just a minor asymmetry if a future harness ever needs to exercise it standalone.
  - **Phase H.20 - cluster 6 closed: `window.getFormBody` and its DOM/FormData scan deleted outright, replaced by `state/useFormBody.jsx` reading `FilterStateContext`/`RowFilterSelectionContext` directly. DONE (2026-10-19).** A dedicated research pass (same method as H.18/H.19) confirmed the plan's own prior correction (2026-10-16) was right: every field `getFormBody()` read was already Context-backed, and the only real blocker was architectural - `useRunScript`/`useAckLabelActions`/`useQuickCheckActions` (all invoked from `AppRoot`'s own function body in `react-app.jsx`) need `useFilterState()`/`useRowFilterSelection()` to resolve without throwing, which requires `AppRoot` to be a genuine *descendant* of both Providers, not their ancestor as before (they used to wrap `AppRoot`'s own returned JSX instead) - the same "component can't be a descendant of something it renders" constraint Phase G's own `PrActivityQueueProvider` move and Phase G.3's own selection-Provider move already ran into, just one level further out this time.
    - **The fix:** `RowFilterSelectionProvider`/`AuthorThreadResolutionSelectionProvider`/`ChangeFilterActorSelectionProvider`/`FilterStateProvider` moved from inside `AppRoot`'s returned JSX to wrapping `<AppRoot/>` itself, from `mountAppRoot()` (`react-app.jsx`) - their children (`PrDataProvider` and everything below it) stay exactly where they were, now rendered as plain descendants instead of textually-nested children, same "delete the now-redundant inner tag" move Phase G made for `PrActivityQueueProvider`. `FilterStateProvider`'s `initialValues` (pure, no-DOM-dependency defaults) was extracted into its own standalone `buildFilterStateInitialValues()` function so `mountAppRoot()` can compute it without needing `AppRoot`'s own `containers` state. New hook `state/useFormBody.jsx` re-derives `getFormBody()`'s exact old return shape (`repo`/`prNumbersInput`/`prNumberList`/`prNumbers`/`prNumber`/`limit`/`mergedLimit`/`jobs`/`openMode`/`label`/`excludeLabel`/`author`/`ackChanged`/`showReason`/`quiet`) from `values`/`checkedByListId` instead of `FormData` - `useRunScript`/`useAckLabelActions`/`useQuickCheckActions` all call it instead of `window.getFormBody?.()`. `getSelectedIncludeLabelNames`/`getSelectedExcludeLabelNames`/`getSelectedAuthorLogins` (index.page.js) stay - still genuinely needed by cluster 1's `createPrFilterSelectionInputsHelpers` instance, this was just their one other caller. `getFormBody()` itself, its `window.getFormBody` bridge, and 2 now-dead locals it was the last caller of (`toBoolean`, the local `parsePrNumbersInput` wrapper) are deleted outright.
    - **A genuine new risk class, not just a repeat of G.3's own experience (confirmed inert, but worth naming):** this is the first move in this plan's history where a Provider already *inside* another Provider gets lifted *above* it, reversing their relative ancestor/descendant order - `PrDataProvider` stays inside `AppRoot` (this cluster doesn't move it), so `FilterStateProvider` goes from being its descendant to being its ancestor. Confirmed inert by reading both files: neither depends on the other's Context.
    - **Real test fallout, found exactly as the research pass predicted:** `index.html.test.js`'s `RunScriptActionButtonsTestHarness`/`ActionButtonsTestHarness` used to each mount via their own, independent `rtlRender` tree (`installRunScriptActionButtonsBridge`/`installReactActionButtonMountBridges`, both deleted) - once their hooks call `useFilterState()`/`useRowFilterSelection()`, they need to be genuine descendants of `FilterStateTestRootHarness`'s own Providers instead (the same "merge into the one persistent root" move G.3 already made for `MountedPrTableTree`), so both are now rendered as plain children inside that same tree (`ActionButtonsTestHarness` keeps its own isolated `PrActivityQueueProvider` instance around it, same as before). 3 separate hook unit tests (`useRunScript.test.jsx`/`useAckLabelActions.test.jsx`/`useQuickCheckActions.test.jsx`) stubbed `window.getFormBody` directly and needed rewriting to seed real `FilterStateContext`/`RowFilterSelectionContext.Provider` values instead - a fallout class G.3 didn't have (its own bridge had no per-hook unit test stubbing it).
    - **A second, real test-infrastructure bug found during verification, same class as H.18/H.19's:** 7 tests in `index.html.test.js` drive the Run Script form's scalar fields (`repo`/`pr-numbers`/`ack-changed`/`show-reason`/`quiet`) via `document.getElementById(...)` + `user.type()`/`user.click()`, asserting on the resulting POST payload. These happened to keep passing under the OLD `getFormBody()` by accident of `FormData`'s own duplicate-key resolution (the real, React-portaled input - appended *after* the pre-existing static one `injectRunFilterFieldElements()` seeds - won by document order inside `new FormData(form)`'s iteration, even though `document.getElementById` itself resolves to the static one first). The new Context-based `useFormBody()` has no such accidental fallback, so these 7 tests' `document.getElementById`-driven interactions needed the same `syncFilterStateFromElement` calls H.18/H.19 already established elsewhere (plus 1 more, a sibling passing test fixed defensively for the same latent gap, confirmed via its own assertions not actually depending on the broken field).
    - **Verified:** full jest suite (278 suites/2574 tests, all passing - 3 hook test files rewritten in place, `index.html.test.js` updated in place, no new/removed test files); `npx eslint .` clean project-wide; full Playwright smoke suite (55/55 passing, exercising Run script/Ack/Clear/Apply label/Quick check/Trigger auto run end-to-end in a real browser - the closest real coverage this repo has for this exact area). The 8 previously-failing server/script jest suites and 7 previously-failing Playwright tests (both pre-existing environment issues, a missing `C:\Program Files\Git\bin` PATH entry on this machine, unrelated to any code in this plan) are also now fixed, persisted to the User PATH - full-repo jest and Playwright are both 100% green for the first time this plan records.
    - **Not closed by this slice:** clusters 1 (`renderPrData`/polling pipeline's own manifest/fingerprint/delta machinery) and 3 (tab panel visibility) remain exactly as sized in the "Remaining-effort audit" below.
  - **Phase H.21 - cluster 3 started: `useIsTabPanelVisible`/`useHasTabPanelBeenVisible` now read `TabStateContext` directly instead of watching a panel's `hidden` attribute via `MutationObserver`. DONE (2026-10-19).** A dedicated research pass (same method as H.18-H.20) found the "cross-cutting, touches most of the component tree" framing in the audit below was accurate for the *bridges'* blast radius but not for the content layer: every tab panel's contents are already fully React-rendered, and `TabStateProvider`/`TabStateContext` (Phase A) already track `activeDataTab`/`activeManagementTab` as real state - the 2 vanilla bridges (`activateDataTab`/`activateManagementTab`) are the *only* thing still physically writing each panel's `hidden` attribute, and nothing in the content layer needed new wiring to close this slice specifically. This hook (the "deferred-items follow-up" precursor the research called out as a safe, zero-production-risk first slice) was the one piece not already blocked on anything - its own header comment ("no Context tracks which tab is active today") had been stale since Phase A, it just hadn't been pointed at `TabStateContext` yet.
    - **A real regression, caught by Playwright, not jsdom - the single most important finding this phase:** the fix alone broke 9 real-browser tests (Author Insights/Action Log/Actor Names/Export content never appearing). Root cause: `react-app.jsx`'s `AppRoot` function body calls `useHasTabPanelBeenVisible()` 5 times (`hasReviewStatsBeenVisible` etc.) to gate lazy-mounting those tabs' contents - but `<TabStateProvider>` was still nested *inside* `AppRoot`'s own returned JSX (Phase G.1's own widening, see that comment), not wrapping `<AppRoot/>` itself. `AppRoot`'s body therefore was never a descendant of its own `TabStateProvider` child, so `useTabState()` silently resolved to `TabStateContext`'s own safe-default value (`activeDataTab: 'pr-data'`, forever) instead of the real live state - the *exact* "component can't be descendant of what it renders" constraint Phase H.20 just ran into for `FilterStateProvider`, this time for `TabStateProvider`. Every jsdom test happened to side-step this (each wraps its own `Probe` in a real `Provider` directly), which is why it only surfaced in Playwright. **Fixed the same way as H.20:** `<TabStateProvider>` moved from inside `AppRoot`'s JSX to wrapping `<AppRoot/>` at `mountAppRoot()` (innermost of the now-7 Providers wrapped there, since it needs none of the others). This is now the *second* confirmed instance of this bug class in 2 consecutive sub-phases - worth treating as a standing checklist item for any future Context consumer called from `AppRoot`'s own body, not just a one-off surprise each time.
    - **Real test fallout:** `useIsTabPanelVisible.test.jsx` rewritten to seed `TabStateContext.Provider` values (2 of the hook's own real recognized panel ids) instead of creating/mutating arbitrary DOM elements - the hook now throws on an unrecognized panel id (a deliberate tightening: the old DOM-based version silently defaulted to "visible" for a nonexistent element, which this phase found was masking the fact that several *other* tests relied on that exact silent fallback, not a deliberate contract). `useBackfillTabAutoRefresh.test.jsx` and `ReviewStatsContent.test.jsx`'s "tab-visibility gating" describe block rewritten the same way (seed/change Context instead of a DOM element's `hidden` property). `ReviewStatsContent.test.jsx`'s own `renderContent()` helper (used by every *other*, non-gating test in that file) needed an explicit `TabStateContext.Provider` default (`activeDataTab: 'review-stats'`) - those tests had been implicitly relying on the old hook's "no panel element in the DOM at all → default visible" fallback, which no longer exists once the hook reads Context instead; the explicit default now states that assumption directly instead of leaving it to an accidental side effect of never creating the DOM element. One of those tests (`"...dispatches pr-navigate-to-insights"`) additionally needed `setActiveDataTab`/`setActiveManagementTab` no-ops added to that same default Context value - `useNavigateToPrInTable.jsx` calls `setActiveDataTab('pr-data')` unconditionally, which threw (`undefined is not a function`) before dispatching its own event, silently swallowing the assertion under test.
    - **Verified:** full jest suite (278 suites/2574 tests, all passing); `npx eslint .` clean project-wide; full Playwright smoke suite (55/55 passing - confirmed fixed from an initial 46/55 after the `TabStateProvider` move, not just re-run for luck).
    - **Not closed by this slice:** the 2 vanilla bridges (`activateDataTab`/`activateManagementTab`) themselves, and the 9 panels' `hidden`-attribute ownership, are untouched - this was deliberately scoped to just the safe precursor the research identified. The research's own finding stands: the remaining piece is genuinely concentrated, hard-to-split risk, concretely one thing - **~60 scattered `window.activateManagementTab(...)` call sites inside `index.html.test.js`** (a harness that never mounts the real `TabStateProvider`/buttons) depending on the bridges' `hidden`-toggling side effect happening synchronously with no React tree present. A new small sync component/hook (e.g. `TabPanelVisibilitySync`, mounted once inside the now-outer `<TabStateProvider>`) is the landing spot for the panels' own `hidden` ownership; `pr-data-tabs.helpers.test.js`/`pr-management-tabs.helpers.test.js` (pure-jsdom, no React, asserting the bridges' DOM-mutating behavior directly) would also need rewriting/deleting at that point.
  - **Phase H.22 - cluster 3 closed: `activateDataTab`/`activateManagementTab` (and `pr-data-tabs.helpers.js`/`pr-management-tabs.helpers.js` outright) deleted, replaced by `components/TabPanelVisibilitySync.jsx` reading `TabStateContext` reactively. DONE (2026-10-19).** New headless component (same shape `AuthorThreadResolutionRuleVisibility.jsx` already established) owns all 9 tab panels' `hidden` attribute via 2 effects (one per tab bar) keyed on `activeDataTab`/`activeManagementTab`, mounted as `<AppRoot/>`'s own sibling inside `<TabStateProvider>` (`mountAppRoot()`) - not nested inside `AppRoot`'s JSX, learning directly from H.21's own regression rather than repeating it. `TabStateProvider.jsx`'s `setActiveDataTab`/`setActiveManagementTab` no longer call the (now-deleted) bridges as a side effect - the Context state change is the only thing anything needs to react to now. Button `className`/`aria-selected` writes (the other half of what the 2 deleted functions did) were already fully redundant with `DataTabButton.jsx`/`ManagementTabButton.jsx`'s own Context-derived styling - confirmed via direct read before deleting, not assumed.

A new local `activateManagementTabForTest(key)` helper (same panel-id/key map as `TabPanelVisibilitySync.jsx`'s own, just a plain DOM write with no Context) replaces the deleted bridge - a single find-and-replace of the identifier across all ~60 call sites, not 60 individual edits, since every call site already had the right shape.

Added 3 new jest tests each to `ActionLogSection.test.jsx`/`ActorNamesTab.test.jsx`, driving `TabStateContext` directly: re-activating reloads, mounting already-active loads exactly once (catching the double-fire bug above, not just describing it), and deactivating triggers nothing.
