A further ~15 of this file's own private factory instances/functions feeding only this chain (`entryNeedsAttention`/`entryHasYourLastActivity`, `getEffectiveViewerLogin`, `collectRequestedReviewers`/`collectAssignedUsers`/`collectApproversFromRow`, `getNeedsAttentionConfig` and its 2 reader helpers, `FILTER_STATE_FIELD_MAP`/`getFilterStateOverrideForFieldId`, `shouldAlwaysShowInReviewRows`, the 11 `getSelected*`/`get*Filter` getters (their one other claimed caller, `getFormBody()`, was itself deleted in H.20 - a stale comment this phase also fixed), `rowMatchesUiFilters`, `normalizeRows`/`sortRowsByPrNumberDesc`/`sortRowsByDateFieldDesc`/`parseCsvTokens`, `isInReviewEnabled`, `isChangedStatus`, `getPreferredActorKey`, `lastSuccessfulRenderedCheckAt`) are deleted too - each confirmed via grep to have no remaining reader once the chain above them was gone, not assumed from the research pass alone.

But `currentActorLoginAliases` has a second, indirect reader: `normalizeActorLogin`'s own default parameter (`helpers/pr-actor-identity.helpers.js`'s `getActorLoginAliases` DI) falls back to it whenever a caller doesn't pass aliases explicitly - and `getAuthorInsightsDisplayName`/`getBlockingAuthorInsightsLogins` (both real, live) call `normalizeActorLogin` exactly that way.   - **Phase H.24 - `window.loadSchedulerStatus`/`window.getSupportsSchedulerPolling` deleted outright. DONE (2026-10-20).** **Correction to this cluster's own prior framing:** the audit below described this bridge as closing "once that poll path is itself owned by a Provider," matching the shape `pollBackfillStatus`/`pollForDataChanges` already established - but by the time this phase started there was no automatic poll path left to own at all (retired in Phase H.2, SSE replaced it), and `loadSchedulerStatus`'s only state (`supportsSchedulerPolling`, the 404 dedup flag) had zero remaining reader anywhere (`window.getSupportsSchedulerPolling` itself had no caller, confirmed via grep - likely dead since whenever its own last reader was removed in an earlier, undocumented cleanup). So "move it into a Provider" would have invented React state with nothing to own; the real fix was smaller - extract `loadSchedulerStatus` into its own pure DI-factory module (`helpers/pr-scheduler-status.helpers.js`), matching the "every live consumer builds its own separate instance of the same factory" pattern this entire migration already uses for every other shared vanilla primitive, instead of relocating a closure behind a differently-named bridge.
    - **The fix:** `index.page.js` and `useQuickCheckActions.jsx` (the bridge's only 2 real callers) each now build their own instance of the new factory - `index.page.js`'s own instance calls `renderSchedulerStatus` (its own local function, unchanged - still also called directly by `JobEventsProvider.jsx`'s SSE connection, a separate, real consumer this phase doesn't touch) directly; `useQuickCheckActions.jsx`'s own instance calls `window.renderSchedulerStatus` (the one piece that must stay a bridge, since `renderSchedulerStatus` itself is vanilla and SSE-shared, not something this hook can import). `window.loadSchedulerStatus`/`window.getSupportsSchedulerPolling` and the `supportsSchedulerPolling` module `let` are deleted outright - no remaining assignment or reader anywhere.
    - **A pre-existing hygiene issue found and fixed while here, unrelated to this bridge specifically:** `eslint.config.mjs`'s own `sourceType: "module"` file list for `src/ui/helpers/*.js` had accumulated 5 stale entries pointing at files deleted in earlier phases without ever being cleaned up (`pr-data-tabs.helpers.js`/`pr-management-tabs.helpers.js`, Phase H.22; `pr-author-insights.component.js`, Phase H.17; `pr-auto-render-navigation.helpers.js`/`react-callbacks.helpers.js`, phase unclear - predates this session's own tracking), plus the 18 files this session's own Phase H.23 deleted. Harmless (an unmatched glob is silently ignored, confirmed by `npx eslint .` staying green throughout), but exactly the kind of "stale documentation found during the sweep" item this plan's own history fixes on sight - removed all 23 stale entries, added this phase's own new helper file.
    - **Verified:** full jest suite (260 suites/2508 tests, up from 259/2504 - 1 new helper test file added, `useQuickCheckActions.test.jsx`'s one affected test rewritten to assert the real `fetch("/view-prs/scheduler")` call instead of a now-gone mocked bridge); `npx eslint .` clean project-wide; full Playwright smoke suite (55/55 passing, including "Trigger auto run and Quick check buttons," which exercises this exact forced-refresh path end-to-end in a real browser).
    - **Not closed by this slice:** `markPollSuccess`/`showPollFailureWarning`'s own small poll-success/failure UI state, and the 2 "Apply filters (local)" fallback bridges (`applyFiltersFromCache`/`debouncedApplyFilters`, zero known dependents since H.19) - both untouched, each its own small, independent, unentangled piece.
  - **Phase H.25 - `window.markPollSuccess`/`window.showPollFailureWarning` deleted outright. DONE (2026-10-20).** Unlike `loadSchedulerStatus` (H.24), this pair genuinely fit the "move into the Provider" shape the audit originally predicted for the whole cluster - both functions' one real caller, `pollForDataChanges`, already lives in `PrDataProvider.jsx`, and both own real, poll-adjacent UI state (`lastSuccessfulPollCheckAt`/`lastPollErrorAt`/`hasActivePollWarning`) that belongs alongside the fingerprint/manifest tracking `trackingRef` already holds, not invented state like H.24's flag turned out to be.
    - **The fix:** `lastSuccessfulPollCheckAt`/`lastPollErrorAt`/`hasActivePollWarning` added to `DEFAULT_POLLING_TRACKING_STATE`/`trackingRef` (`PrDataProvider.jsx`); `markPollSuccess`/`showPollFailureWarning` now defined inline in the `pollForDataChanges` DI object, reading/writing `trackingRef.current` directly and calling `window.showWarningNotification`/`window.hideErrorNotification` (Snackbar.jsx's own bridges, genuinely vanilla/React-owned, unaffected) - no `window.markPollSuccess`/`window.showPollFailureWarning` bridge needed at all, since the one real caller is already a sibling in the same file. `describePollFailure` (a small pure helper, `showPollFailureWarning`'s only dependency) and a fresh `createPrFormattingHelpers()` instance (for `formatIsoDatetime`) moved/added alongside. `index.page.js`'s own copies of both functions, the 3 module `let`s, `describePollFailure`, `formatIsoDatetime`'s destructure, and `showWarningNotification`/`hideErrorNotification` (both now-orphaned, single-purpose wrappers) are all deleted outright - confirmed via grep each had no reader beyond the 2 deleted functions.
    - **Verified:** full jest suite (260 suites/2508 tests, unchanged - no test needed rewriting, since `pr-data-polling-orchestration.helpers.test.js` already tested the pure orchestration helper via plain DI mocks, unaffected by where the real implementations live); `npx eslint .` clean project-wide; full Playwright smoke suite (55/55 passing).
    - **Not closed by this slice:** the 2 "Apply filters (local)" fallback bridges (`applyFiltersFromCache`/`debouncedApplyFilters`, zero known dependents since H.19) - the one remaining piece of cluster 1, untouched.
  - **Phase H.26 - `window.applyFiltersFromCache`/`window.debouncedApplyFilters` deleted outright - the last `window.*` bridges in this entire audit. DONE (2026-10-20).** A dedicated research pass, then a fresh empirical "stub both to no-ops, run the full jest + Playwright suites" re-check against the post-H.23 code (the pre-H.23 check from H.18/H.19 was stale once the pipeline underneath these 2 bridges was itself deleted), confirmed zero remaining dependents on the re-filtering/re-render side - `renderPrData(payload, repo)` called with an unchanged payload now only forces a wasteful whole-app re-render via `PrDataProvider`'s non-memoized Context value, nothing else.
    - **A genuine surprise the research surfaced, not covered by "zero known dependents":** `window.debouncedApplyFilters` wasn't just a stray trigger - `FilterStateProvider.jsx` fired it 150ms after 12 listed Context fields changed, `index.page.js`'s own delegated `#run-script-form` listener fired it on the same fields plus several more, and a real, visible, user-clickable "Apply filters (local)" button (`react-app.jsx`, portaled into the "Visibility Filters" card) called it directly too. That button's *other* call, `window.persistViewFilterOptionOverrides?.()`, turned out to be the **only** save trigger for 14 fields/lists - `scope-mode`/`filter-pr-numbers`/`always-show-in-review`/5 `attention-*` checkboxes/`attention-no-activity-mode` (none of these had their own per-field persist call, unlike `attention-author-thread-resolution-mode`/the 2 `change-filter-*` fields, which already auto-persisted independently of the button), the 6 `FilterOptionSelect` "Any (with/without)" fields (a known, documented persistence-gap exception, see sub-phase 7.4's own history - deliberately excluded from auto-*apply* but always relied on this same button for persistence, not realized as a *second* gap until now), and the `label`/`exclude-label`/`author`/`assigned`/`approver` multi-select lists (confirmed by a Playwright test that deliberately persists via a raw `/view-prs/user-defaults` PUT specifically to avoid "checking all four boxes and clicking that button"). Simply deleting the bridges/button would have silently broken saving/restoring all 14 across page reloads - caught and corrected (a direct user decision after being shown this finding) before any deletion landed, not discovered via a test failure afterward.
    - **The fix:** the button, its container (`index.html`'s now-empty `.form-actions` wrapper removed entirely, not just the button), its CSS, and `window.applyFiltersFromCache`/`window.debouncedApplyFilters`/`pr-apply-filters-cache.helpers.js` are deleted outright. All 14 previously-button-only fields/lists get the same auto-persist-on-change call every other field here already had - 9 scalar fields and the 6 `FilterOptionSelect` ids added to `index.page.js`'s delegated listener's `persistOnChangeIds` set (replacing the dead `debouncedApplyOnChangeIds`/`debouncedApplyFilters` pairing), and `label-list`/`exclude-label-list`/`author-list`/`assigned-list`/`approver-list` added to the same multi-select "change" listener the 4 already-persisting lists used. `FilterStateProvider.jsx`'s own `useDebouncedEffect` call (whose only purpose was firing the now-deleted bridge) is deleted in full, not just its inner call.
    - **Real test fallout, both jsdom and real-browser:** `index.html.test.js`'s ~45 `click "Apply filters (local)"` call sites across ~20 tests - most were simply redundant once their own preceding field change already auto-applied/auto-persisted live and got deleted outright; ~10 needed a real fix, not just deletion, split into 2 categories found by actually running the suite rather than assumed: (1) tests relying on test-order bleed-over from a neighboring test's own leftover trigger (made self-contained, given their own explicit trigger instead); (2) a genuine ordering gap unique to this suite's static-markup harness - a raw DOM `.checked =`/`.value =` write or a static element's native "change" event firing *before* this file's own `syncFilterStateFromElement` call updates Context (a real mounted React field updates Context synchronously inside its own onChange, before the native event finishes bubbling to the delegated listener - this suite's simulated fields can't do that), so the auto-persist captured stale data; fixed by persisting explicitly once Context is correctly synced, same fix shape in each case. `e2e/smoke.spec.js`'s own `clickApplyFiltersAndWaitForPersist` helper (used ~28 times) is renamed `waitForFilterPersist` and no longer clicks anything - it calls `window.persistViewFilterOptionOverrides?.()` directly via `page.evaluate` and awaits its own returned Promise, which is simpler and strictly more reliable than clicking-then-racing a `waitForResponse` listener (no risk of the listener starting after the response already landed).
    - **A second, real race found only by actually running the full Playwright suite, not just the file in isolation:** 3 tests that check/uncheck `label-list` or remove a filter via a `TableFilterChips` "x" (a direct Context update, not a native DOM event) without waiting for persistence to land left a stale override in the shared webServer's backing file, corrupting several *later*, unrelated tests' own assumptions about a clean filter state (manifesting as "Fix flaky test" mysteriously missing from the table in tests that never touched labels at all) - each passed individually but failed only in the full run. Fixed by awaiting `waitForFilterPersist` after each such reset, same reasoning the suite's own pre-existing comments already document for the analogous `scope-mode`/`always-show-in-review` cases.
    - **Verified:** full jest suite (259 suites/2500 tests - `pr-apply-filters-cache.helpers.js`'s own test file deleted, `index.html.test.js` updated in place, no new/removed test files elsewhere); `npx eslint .` clean project-wide; full Playwright smoke suite (55/55 passing, re-run from a clean state after resolving the cross-test contamination above, not assumed from a single partial run).
    - **Not closed by this slice:** nothing - this closes the last 2 bridges named anywhere in the "Remaining-effort audit" below. The audit's own "zero `window.*` bridges" goal is met for every cluster it ever tracked.
    - **Follow-up, found during code review of this same phase (2026-10-20): the button removal itself left a real persistence gap, fixed before any user hit it.** `TableFilterChips.jsx`'s "x" button (`useTableFilterChips.jsx`'s `onRemove`) clears a filter via a direct Context update (`setValue`/`toggle`), never a native DOM `"change"` event - the vanilla auto-persist-on-change listeners this phase added to `index.page.js` can only ever see a real native event, so they never fired for a chip removal. Before this phase, the "Apply filters (local)" button was the only way such a Context-only change ever got persisted (a user would click it manually afterward); once the button was deleted, nothing replaced that path, so removing any filter chip stopped persisting the removal at all - reloading the page would silently restore whatever filter the user had just removed via its chip.
      - **The fix:** reinstated a debounced Context-level watcher in `FilterStateProvider.jsx` (the same `useDebouncedEffect`/150ms shape the deleted `debouncedApplyFilters` effect used, but pointed at `persistViewFilterOptions` - the correct, real persist function - instead of the old, always-wrong "apply" target), watching `values` plus all 3 selection Contexts (`rowFilterSelection`/`authorThreadResolutionSelection`/`changeFilterActorSelection`) so it catches every one of this Provider's 26 persist-relevant fields, regardless of whether the change came from a native event or a direct Context call. This is a deliberate "double-hookup" with the vanilla listeners for native-event-driven changes (same harmless redundancy several fields in this migration already have) - the real gap it closes is the Context-only path those listeners structurally cannot see.
      - **Verified with a dedicated new regression test** (`e2e/smoke.spec.js`, "removing a filter chip persists the clear on its own, with no explicit persist call at all") that removes a chip and reloads relying *entirely* on the reinstated debounced watcher, with no test-side persist call at all - proving the fix holds up unaided, the way a real user's click actually would. Full jest (259 suites/2500 tests) and full Playwright (56/56, including the new test) both green.
    - **Second follow-up, found via a direct question about why a "vanilla" delegated listener still existed at all (2026-10-20): it didn't need to.** The first follow-up fixed the real gap (Context-only changes) by adding a Context-level watcher, but left the vanilla `#run-script-form`/multi-select-list delegated listeners (this phase's own first-pass fix) in place alongside it as an assumed "harmless double-hookup." Asked directly why anything vanilla remained when the whole point was full React ownership - on inspection, that listener pair had no purpose left at all: it existed solely to call `persistViewFilterOptionOverrides?.()`, which the new Context-level watcher already does, for the exact same 26 fields, reacting to the exact same state. **Deleted both listener blocks outright** (the `persistOnChangeIds` Set, the `#run-script-form` delegated listener, and the 9-list multi-select "change" listener) - `FilterStateProvider.jsx`'s own watcher is now the sole, fully-React-owned persistence trigger for every view-filter field and multi-select list, with no vanilla DOM wiring left for any of it.
      - **Real test fallout, caught by actually running the suite:** one jsdom test ("given author thread-resolution controls... persisted immediately") assumed a *synchronous* persist tied to its own triggering event (true under the old vanilla listener, false now that persistence is 150ms-debounced) - fixed with an explicit `fetchMock.mockClear()` before its second assertion, same pattern used elsewhere in this phase. Two Playwright tests ("use built-in merge pattern"/"ignore commit patterns") raced a bare "wait for *a* PUT" network listener against the debounced watcher's own redundant restore-triggered write (see the first follow-up's own note) and intermittently matched the wrong one - fixed by switching both to `waitForFilterPersist` (calls the real persist function directly and awaits its own Promise, sidestepping the network-race ambiguity entirely) instead of a `Promise.all`-raced `waitForResponse`.
      - **Verified:** full jest suite (259 suites/2500 tests), `npx eslint .` clean project-wide, full Playwright smoke suite (56/56).
    - **Third follow-up, found via a broader direct request to sweep for any other leftover vanilla code (2026-10-20): 2 more fully dead code paths, unrelated to each other, surfaced by the same question applied more widely.**
      1. **`helpers/pr-ui-option-scroll.helpers.js`'s `registerUiOptionPersistenceHandlers`** - a second, independent copy of exactly the pattern the second follow-up just deleted, missed because it lived in a different helper file: a vanilla delegated `#run-script-form` "change" listener watching specifically `#scope-mode`, debouncing a call to `persistViewFilterOptionOverrides?.()` on a separate 150ms timer. `scope-mode` is one of `FilterStateContext`'s own `TEXT_FIELD_IDS`, already covered by `FilterStateProvider.jsx`'s Context-level watcher (the same one the second follow-up installed) - this listener had been fully redundant since that watcher landed, just never noticed because it's a one-field special case tucked inside an otherwise-unrelated "backfill log auto-scroll" helper file. Deleted outright: the handler, its own debounce timer, the `persistViewFilterOptions` DI param, and `index.page.js`'s one call site; 3 now-pointless unit tests (`pr-ui-option-scroll.helpers.test.js`) removed with it.
      2. **`helpers/pr-filter-panel.helpers.js`'s 11 selection/metadata-filter getters** (`getSelectedAuthorLogins`/`getSelectedAssignedLogins`/`getSelectedApproverLogins`/`getSelectedIncludeLabelNames`/`getSelectedExcludeLabelNames`/`getCustomCommentsFilter`/`getOtherNotesFilter`/`getPrDifficultyFilter`/`getRallyStoriesFilter`/`getRallyLinksFilter`/`getAnalysisOfPrFilter`) **plus a same-named, unrelated dead export in `helpers/form-parsing.helpers.js`** - a different kind of leftover: not a redundant listener, but functions multiple earlier phases' own comments claimed were "still needed," each citing the next one down the chain, none actually checking the end of the chain. H.23's own comment said these 11 getters stayed because `getFormBody()` called 3 of them "as plain function calls" - but H.20 (landing the same day, in the same audit) deleted `getFormBody()` itself, and the stale comment left behind afterward instead claimed they were "still genuinely needed by `createPrFilterSelectionInputsHelpers`' own instance" - a factory that does not exist anywhere in this codebase (confirmed via grep, likely never real or itself deleted without a trace long before this session's own tracking began). `index.page.js`'s real destructuring of this factory already only pulled out `setupMultiSelectDropdownClosing` - every one of these 11 getters had zero actual caller anywhere, exercised only by their own now-deleted unit tests. The unrelated `form-parsing.helpers.js` export of the same name read `option.selected` off a `<select id="author">` that was replaced by the `author-list` checkbox multi-select (`RowFilterSelectionContext`) long ago - equally dead, equally only self-tested. Both deleted outright, along with the DI params/internals (`getFilterStateValue`/`getSelectedMultiSelectValues`/`readFilterStateOrDomValue`) that only fed them; `pr-filter-panel.helpers.js` keeps only `setupMultiSelectDropdownClosing` (a real, still-vanilla dropdown-outside-click-closes behavior with no React owner, now given its own direct unit test since the old test file only ever exercised the deleted getters). 5 stale comments elsewhere (`index.page.js` x2, `react-app.jsx`, `MultiSelectListPortals.jsx`, `FilterOptionSelect.jsx`, `FilterStateProvider.jsx`) citing these dead functions as the reason some other code was shaped the way it was are corrected to point at what's actually true now (`FilterStateContext`'s own `values` object covers all of it).
      - **Verified:** full jest suite (259 suites/2493 tests, down from 2500 - net of 7 obsolete tests removed across the 2 helper test files, 3 new dropdown-closing tests added replacing tests for deleted code); `npx eslint .` clean project-wide; full Playwright smoke suite (56/56).
    - **Fourth follow-up, prompted by a direct challenge to this same phase's own "deliberate exception" framing for `window.refreshAvailableRepoLabels` (2026-10-20): 2 of its 3 real callers didn't actually need a window bridge at all - only 1 was structurally blocked, and that's a different thing from "mistakenly left vanilla."** `useRefreshAvailableRepoLabels.jsx`'s own comment (written during H.26) called this bridge "the 1 deliberate exception" because `refreshAvailableRepoLabels`'s state was "genuinely shared with a second, automatic caller inside index.page.js's renderPrData" - true as far as it went, but it never asked whether the *fetch itself* could move into `RepoLabelsProvider.jsx` (the Context that already held its *result*) rather than staying vanilla with the Context only mirroring it. It could: `ApplyLabelSelect.jsx`/`PrActionsCell.jsx` already read `RepoLabelsContext`'s `labels` reactively, so once the fetch itself became real `RepoLabelsProvider` state, both real descendant callers (the "↻ Labels" button; the Provider's own "repo changed, refetch" effect) could call it directly via `useRepoLabels().refresh()` - no bridge needed for either. The 3rd caller, `FilterStateProvider.jsx`'s restore-on-mount effect, is genuinely different: `FilterStateProvider` wraps `<AppRoot/>` from `mountAppRoot()` (an ancestor), while `RepoLabelsProvider` is a descendant AppRoot renders further down its own JSX - a parent cannot consume a Context its own child provides, full stop, no refactor of this scope changes that. That's not a leftover vanilla shortcut; it's the one case where a `window.*` bridge is the *correct* tool, same category as `window.setStatusMessage`/`window.handleRequestMoreMerged` elsewhere in this migration.
      - **The fix:** `index.page.js`'s `availableRepoLabels`/`isFetchingRepoLabels` module state, its `#repo` DOM read, `resolveRepoForLabelsFetch`, `refreshAvailableRepoLabels`, and `populateApplyLabelSelect` are deleted outright, along with `pushPayloadToReactTable` (confirmed to have zero remaining caller once the label-refresh block - its last one - was gone; the same "wasteful re-render" bug class H.26 already fixed elsewhere, since both real Context consumers already re-render from `labels` state changing on their own). `RepoLabelsProvider.jsx` now owns the real fetch as its own `refresh` callback, reading the current repo from `usePrData()`/`useFilterState()` (no DOM read at all - "repo" is already a real `FilterStateContext` field) and calling `setLabels` via closure (no `window.setAvailableRepoLabels` bridge needed either). `resolveRepoForLabelsFetch` moved to a new pure helper, `helpers/pr-repo-labels.helpers.js`. `useRefreshAvailableRepoLabels.jsx` (the pass-through hook) is deleted; the "↻ Labels" button is now `RefreshLabelsButtonLive` (react-app.jsx, same `TableFilterChipsLive`-style wrapper pattern), calling `useRepoLabels().refresh()` directly from inside `<RepoLabelsProvider>`'s own subtree - not threaded down from `AppRoot`'s outer scope, which structurally can't reach it. `window.refreshAvailableRepoLabels` itself stays, but as a thin forwarder `RepoLabelsProvider` installs onto itself (`window.refreshAvailableRepoLabels = (...args) => refresh(...args)`), exercised by exactly one real caller now (`FilterStateProvider`'s restore effect) instead of 3.
      - **Verified:** full jest suite (258 suites/2496 tests - `index.page.labels.test.js`/`useRefreshAvailableRepoLabels.test.jsx` deleted, `pr-repo-labels.helpers.test.js` added, `RepoLabelsProvider.test.jsx` rewritten around the real fetch instead of an injected `window.*` spy); `npx eslint .` clean project-wide; full Playwright smoke suite (56/56, including "the Run tab's apply-label dropdown and a row's own apply-label dropdown share the same real labels list," which clicks the real `#refresh-labels-btn` in a real browser).
    - **Fifth follow-up, prompted by a direct instruction to reconsider the structure so that NOT EVEN the one remaining caller needs `window.*` (2026-10-20): the "parent can't consume a child's Context" framing from the fourth follow-up was correct, but it only ruled out calling DOWN into the child - reacting to a signal flowing DOWN from the parent was never blocked at all, and that's the actual fix.** `FilterStateProvider.jsx` is a genuine ancestor of `RepoLabelsProvider.jsx` (confirmed again: `FilterStateProvider` wraps `<AppRoot/>` from `mountAppRoot()`; `RepoLabelsProvider` is a descendant AppRoot renders further down its own JSX) - which means Context already flows from the former to the latter for free, the same direction `values.repo` itself already flows for the `refresh()` fallback. The fourth follow-up's bridge called *down* into the child imperatively instead of using that free, already-working direction - an avoidable leftover, not a necessity.
      - **The fix:** `FilterStateProvider.jsx` gains one new piece of its own Context value, `restoredRepoOverride` (a plain `useState(null)`, set once inside the existing restore-on-mount effect when `overrides.repo` is truthy). Deliberately a field of its own, not a repurposing of `values.repo`: watching `values.repo` directly in `RepoLabelsProvider` would mean a label fetch on every keystroke as the user edits the repo field, not just this one restore event, a real behavior change the original vanilla code never had (it only ever fired once, from the restore effect itself). `RepoLabelsProvider.jsx` reads `restoredRepoOverride` via `useFilterState()` and reacts with a second effect, the same shape as its existing `selectedRepo`-watching one, sharing the same `lastTriggeredRepoRef` dedup guard. `window.refreshAvailableRepoLabels` - the fourth follow-up's own "this one stays, and that's correct" bridge - is deleted outright, along with the effect that installed it onto `window`. There is now no `window.*` bridge anywhere in the repo-labels data path at all, for any of its 3 real triggers (the button, the `selectedRepo` effect, the `restoredRepoOverride` effect).
      - **Verified:** full jest suite (258 suites/2500 tests - `FilterStateProvider.test.jsx` gained a new `restoredRepoOverride` describe block testing the signal at its source; `RepoLabelsProvider.test.jsx`'s own former "window.refreshAvailableRepoLabels bridge" describe block was rewritten into "reacts to a restored repo override," exercising the same real restore-fetch-then-labels-fetch path end-to-end with no `window.*` spy at all); `npx eslint .` clean project-wide; full Playwright smoke suite (56/56).
    - **Sixth follow-up, prompted by a direct "is there any window code left anywhere" audit request (2026-10-20): a project-wide grep for every production `window.*` bridge found `TabStateProvider.jsx`'s own `window.setActiveDataTab`/`window.setActiveManagementTab` had zero remaining callers at all.** Its own header comment cited `navigateToPrInTable`/`navigateToAuthorInsights`/the `navigateToPrInTableFrom*` bridges as the reason these existed - but those were already migrated (Phase G.1/H.16) into `useNavigateToPrInTable.jsx`/`useNavigateToAuthorInsights.jsx`, real hooks that call `setActiveDataTab` straight off `useTabState()`, no window bridge involved. A grep confirmed the only remaining references were this Provider's own tests. **Deleted outright** (not migrated - there was nothing left to migrate): the bridge-installing `useEffect`, and the 6 tests in `TabStateProvider.test.jsx` that existed solely to cover it, plus 2 now-pointless `delete window.setActive*Tab` cleanup lines in `DataTabButton.test.jsx`/`ManagementTabButton.test.jsx`. This is the same "stale justification citing already-migrated callers" pattern as the third follow-up's dead getters, not a new architectural question.
      - **Verified:** full jest suite (258 suites/2494 tests, -6 exactly matching the deleted bridge tests); `npx eslint .` clean project-wide; full Playwright smoke suite (56/56).
      - **Scope note:** this was one bridge out of a much larger remaining set (the "Remaining-effort audit" below undercounts what's left project-wide, since it only ever covered `index.page.js`'s own bridges - `PrDataProvider.jsx`, `AuthorInsightsDraftsProvider.jsx`, `AutoRenderBlockingProvider.jsx`, `BackfillStatusProvider.jsx`, `PrActivityQueueProvider.jsx`, `Snackbar.jsx`, `StatusTextProvider.jsx`, `NotesDirtyProvider.jsx`, `ActionLogSection.jsx`, and `ActorNamesTab.jsx` all still publish real bridges too). Several of these (`AutoRenderBlockingProvider.jsx` in particular) have their own detailed header comments documenting a *real, tested* structural reason for the ref-based bridge pattern (a previous `useState`+`flushSync` attempt broke in production, caught only by Playwright, because `flushSync` can't be called from inside another component's `useEffect`) - those need to be re-examined on their own merits, one at a time, not assumed necessary nor assumed stale.
    - **Seventh follow-up, asked to work through the remaining bridge list outright rather than stopping after each one (2026-10-20): `window.showErrorNotification`/`showWarningNotification`/`hideErrorNotification` (Snackbar.jsx) split into a real Provider, closing 9 of its 10 real callers' own bridge usage.** Unlike the dead `setActiveDataTab`/`setActiveManagementTab` bridges, this one had genuine callers - but almost all of them were already React (8 hooks: `useQuickCheckActions`/`useAckLabelActions`/`useRunScript`/`useBackfillActions`/`useTriggerAutoRun`/`useRowCheckboxActions`/`useSinglePrUpdate`/`useActivityDrawerActions`, plus `PrDataProvider.jsx`'s own poll-warning path) reaching the bridge only because Snackbar.jsx's state had nowhere else to live.
      - **The fix:** split `Snackbar.jsx` into `state/SnackbarContext.jsx` (the Context + `useSnackbar()` hook) and `components/SnackbarProvider.jsx` (owns the state, wraps `<AppRoot/>` from `mountAppRoot()` - same "wrap from the root-mount call, not from inside AppRoot's own JSX" shape as `StatusTextProvider`/`BackfillStatusProvider`/`PrActivityQueueProvider`/`TabStateProvider`, for the identical reason: AppRoot's own body and several descendants call these hooks directly). `Snackbar.jsx` itself is now just the markup, reading `useSnackbar()` - still portaled into `#error-snackbar-root` from inside `AppRoot`'s own JSX (a genuine descendant of the new Provider). All 9 React callers now call `showError`/`showWarning`/`hide` from `useSnackbar()` directly instead of the window bridge.
      - **The one caller that keeps the bridge, correctly:** `index.page.js`'s own `notifyFailureSnackbar`/`showErrorNotification` (its `initPage()` startup-failure paths for `loadStoredData`/`loadBackfillStatus`) run as genuinely vanilla module-level code, outside any React component, possibly before React has even mounted - there is no Context for them to read. `SnackbarProvider.jsx` still publishes the 3-function window bridge for exactly this caller, same category as `window.setStatusMessage`/`window.handleRequestMoreMerged` elsewhere in this migration - not a leftover, a structural necessity.
      - **Test fallout:** every one of the 8 hooks' own test files mocked the bridge as `window.showErrorNotification = (...args) => snackbarCalls.push(args)` - each rewritten to pass a spy via a real `<SnackbarContext.Provider value={{ showError: spy }}>` wrapper instead (same "real Provider value, not a window mock" pattern Phase H.8's `useStatusText()` migration already established). The integration suite (`index.html.test.js`) was trickier: its own `installReactSnackbarMountBridge()` mounted `<Snackbar/>` in a *separate*, disconnected RTL tree, relying on the old Snackbar.jsx's own effect to install a *global* window bridge that any other tree's hooks could reach - with the bridge gone, hooks in `FilterStateTestRootHarness`'s tree (quick-check, single-PR-update, the poll-warning path) need a *real* Context ancestor, and the one true `<Snackbar/>` UI this suite's 5 DOM-text assertions (`#error-snackbar-message`) check needs to be the *same* Provider instance. Fixed by deleting `installReactSnackbarMountBridge()` entirely and instead wrapping `FilterStateTestRootHarness` itself with `SnackbarProvider`, portaling `<Snackbar/>` into `#error-snackbar-root` from inside that same tree - confirmed via grep that `BackfillControlsTestHarness` (a separate, disconnected tree using `useBackfillActions()`) has no DOM-text snackbar assertions anywhere in this suite, so its own `useSnackbar()` calls safely resolve to the Context's no-op default there.
      - **Verified:** full jest suite (258 suites/2494 tests, unchanged count - every change this slice made was rewiring an existing test's mock, not adding/removing tests); `npx eslint .` clean project-wide; full Playwright smoke suite (56/56, including the dedicated "Snackbar (error/warning notifications)" test).
    - **Eighth follow-up, same session, continuing through the bridge list: `window.triggerActionLogLoad`/`window.triggerActorNameCacheLoad` (ActionLogSection.jsx/ActorNamesTab.jsx) deleted outright - neither had a caller that genuinely needed a bridge.**
      - **ActorNamesTab.jsx:** a grep confirmed `window.triggerActorNameCacheLoad` had zero callers outside the component itself (its own header comment already said "this tab's own buttons/status/rows are ALL now React-owned" - the bridge was pure self-indirection, a component calling itself through `window` for no reason). `load` is now a local `useCallback`, called directly by the re-activation effect and by `refreshBoth` (itself now just `= load`).
      - **ActionLogSection.jsx:** different shape - this one's bridge had a real caller, but that caller (`index.page.js`'s `#action-log-refresh-btn` click listener, a genuinely vanilla static DOM button) didn't need to stay vanilla at all. Gave the button's static HTML slot a `-root` id (`#action-log-refresh-btn-root`, same "Live button" shape as `RefreshLabelsButtonLive` in react-app.jsx) and had `ActionLogSection.jsx` itself portal a real `<button>` into it, calling its own local `load` (also now a `useCallback`, shared with the re-activation effect) directly - no bridge needed for either of the 2 callers any more. `index.page.js`'s own `loadActionLog`/`#action-log-refresh-btn` listener deleted outright.
      - **Verified:** full jest suite (258 suites/2493 tests, -1 net: ActionLogSection.test.jsx stayed at the same count (1 test rewritten to click the real button instead of calling the bridge, 1 new test added asserting the portal itself) while ActorNamesTab.test.jsx dropped from 11 to 9 (its own "bridge triggered manually" and "unmount removes the bridge" tests collapsed into the existing Refresh-button-click test, which already covered the same reload behavior through the real UI)); `npx eslint .` clean project-wide; full Playwright smoke suite (56/56, including both tab-specific tests, which assert via `getByRole`/text content and needed no changes since the accessible button shape is unchanged).
    - **Ninth follow-up, same session: `window.handleRequestMoreMerged` (MergedRequestMoreAction.jsx's "Request more" button) replaced with `state/useRequestMoreMerged.jsx`, same shape as `useBackfillActions`/`useTriggerAutoRun`.** Its own comment already called out the bridge as the one piece still vanilla ("The actual request logic... still lives in vanilla") despite the button itself being fully React-owned since Phase H.4 - the new hook builds its own instance of the already-pure `pr-merged-request-more-action.helpers.js` factory, reading `useStatusText()`/`useSnackbar()` directly instead of `window.renderRequestActivity`-adjacent indirection, and `MergedRequestMoreAction.jsx` now takes the result as an `onRequestMore` prop instead of calling the bridge itself. `getLatestSelectedRepo`'s DI slot intentionally stays a dead-fallback empty string, not a new `usePrData()` dependency - confirmed via `useMergedRequestMoreActionOptions.jsx`'s own comment that this component's `repo` prop already IS `PrDataContext`'s `selectedRepo`, so the fallback path the old vanilla code needed (for a *second*, independent caller `index.page.js` itself might have had) is unreachable for this hook's one real caller. `index.page.js`'s own vanilla instance (`isRequestMoreMergedPending`, the `prMergedRequestMoreActionHelperFactory` import, the now-dead `DEFAULT_REPO` import) all deleted outright - confirmed zero other callers of any of them first.
      - **Verified:** full jest suite (258 suites/2493 tests, net 0 - every change was rewiring an existing test's mock (window bridge → `onRequestMore` prop/a small hook-calling test harness), not adding/removing tests); `npx eslint .` clean project-wide; full Playwright smoke suite (56/56).
    - **Tenth follow-up, same session: `PrActivityQueueProvider.jsx`'s 8 bridges (`markPrsBusy`/`clearPrsBusy`/`markPrsQueued`/`clearPrsQueued`/`beginBulkActionBatch`/`markBulkActionChunkInFlight`/`markBulkActionChunkDone`/`finishBulkActionBatch`) deleted outright - all 8 were already fully dead, the same "stale comment citing an already-migrated caller" pattern as `TabStateProvider`'s bridges.** The Provider's own comment called `pr-ack-label-actions.helpers.js`/`pr-quick-check-actions.helpers.js` "real external callers... structurally unable to ever become Context consumers" - true when written, stale by the time of this audit: a grep confirmed those 2 files are pure DI factories with zero `window.*` references of their own, and their only actual callers (`useAckLabelActions.jsx`/`useQuickCheckActions.jsx`) already inject these same functions straight from `usePrActivityQueue()`. Every remaining reference was in this Provider's own tests or `PrTableApp.test.jsx`'s, simulating "a vanilla caller" that no longer exists.
      - **Test fallout, the largest of this session's follow-ups:** `PrActivityQueueProvider.test.jsx` rewritten to call the Context's own singular `markPrBusy`/`clearPrBusy`/`markPrQueued`/`clearPrQueued` directly (via a `Probe` component) instead of the plural, array-taking window forms - the array-looping behavior itself was never a real requirement beyond the bridge's own convenience, since every real caller already wraps a single-PR call in its own loop. `PrTableApp.test.jsx` gained a new `ActivityQueueProbe` component (same shape) so its 2 describe blocks could do the same; the "bridge removed on unmount" tests in both files are gone outright (nothing to remove any more).
      - **Playwright fallout:** 2 tests directly called `window.markPrsBusy`/`markPrsQueued` from `page.evaluate` to simulate a bulk action without actually running one. The busy-indicator test was rewritten to drive the real Ack workflow through the UI (fill `#pr-numbers`, click "Apply ack only", with the `/view-prs/ack` POST delayed 500ms via `page.route` to make the in-flight window observable) - this surfaced a second, genuinely unrelated test-setup gap along the way: `#repo` (inside the collapsed "Run Script options" `<details>`) wasn't being explicitly set, so the real request's `repo` field was empty, producing a busy-state key (`::1`) that never matched the real row's key (`octocat/hello-world::1`) - fixed by opening that `<details>` and filling `#repo` explicitly, not relying on an earlier test in this shared-webServer suite having already persisted it. The queued-vs-busy test was **deleted, not rewritten** - reproducing its 4-chunk scenario faithfully through only this fixture's 3 PRs would mean engineering a PR-number list that exploits `pr-ack-label-actions.helpers.js`'s own `CONCURRENCY_LIMIT`/`CHUNK_SIZE` constants, a fragile test tightly coupled to implementation details, for coverage `PrNumberCell.test.jsx` (pure dot-rendering from props) and `PrActivityQueueProvider.test.jsx`/`PrTableApp.test.jsx` (the Context-level exclusion rule, including a real multi-chunk flow) already provide without that coupling.
      - **Verified:** full jest suite (258 suites/2488 tests, -5: -3 from `PrActivityQueueProvider.test.jsx` (the "registers 8 bridges" test, plus one of its two duplicate multi-chunk-flow tests collapsing into one now that there's only one call path), -2 from `PrTableApp.test.jsx`'s now-impossible "bridge removed on unmount" tests); `npx eslint .` clean project-wide; full Playwright smoke suite (55/56 by count, -1 for the deleted queued-state test, all passing).
    - **Eleventh follow-up, same session: `AuthorInsightsDraftsProvider.jsx`'s bridge count cut from 12 to 2 - `getAuthorInsightsComposerDraft`/`updateAuthorInsightsComposerDraft`/`resetAuthorInsightsComposerDraft`/`getAuthorInsightsEditDraft`/`getAuthorInsightsEditDraftMap`/`updateAuthorInsightsEditDraft`/`resetAuthorInsightsEditDraft`/`setAuthorInsightsManualComments`/`getAuthorInsightsManualCommentsLoadState`/`loadAuthorManualComments` deleted outright; `getAuthorManualCommentsForLogin`/`getAuthorInsightsDraftsSnapshot` kept, confirmed genuinely still needed.** This Provider's own header comment already half-admitted the staleness without drawing the conclusion: `index.page.js`'s own inline comment at the proxy site said several of these "were only ever repackaged into prAuthorInsightsDraftsHelpers, which only ever fed pr-author-insights.component.js (deleted outright)" - true, but that file's comment stopped short of asking whether the Provider should still publish them. A grep settled it: `AuthorInsightsCommentsSection.jsx` (their only real consumer anywhere) already calls every one of the 10 via `useAuthorInsightsDrafts()` directly, and `index.page.js` itself has zero live references to any of the 10 (only its own 2 real reads - `getAuthorManualCommentsForLogin`, used by `getBlockingAuthorInsightsLogins`'s own DI, and `getAuthorInsightsDraftsSnapshot`, fed into that same call - both confirmed genuinely vanilla, no render-tree position, kept).
      - **Test fallout:** `AuthorInsightsDraftsProvider.test.jsx`'s own tests for the 10 deleted bridges rewritten to go through the Context `Probe` directly instead (e.g. `window.getAuthorInsightsComposerDraft(...)` → `probeValue.getComposerDraft(...)`); `AuthorInsightsCommentsSection.test.jsx` gained a small `DraftsProbe` component (same shape) for its one test that asserted via `window.getAuthorInsightsComposerDraft` after typing into the composer.
      - **Verified:** full jest suite (258 suites/2488 tests, net 0 - every change was rewiring an existing test's assertion, not adding/removing tests); `npx eslint .` clean project-wide; full Playwright smoke suite (55/55, unaffected - no Playwright test referenced any of these 10 bridges).
    - **Twelfth follow-up, same session, auditing `FilterStateProvider.jsx`'s remaining 4 bridges individually rather than trusting its own header comment wholesale.** `window.getFilterStateValues`/`window.setFilterStateValue` confirmed genuinely necessary: `PrSelectionCell.jsx` (mounted once per table row) reads/writes them deliberately non-reactively, since a real `useFilterState()` subscription there would re-render every row on every keystroke in any of 30+ unrelated fields - the exact kind of perf-justified exception this migration has accepted elsewhere, not a leftover. `window.persistRunScriptOptionOverrides` had exactly one real caller, and it was already React: react-app.jsx's "Run script" button, inside `AppRoot` - a genuine descendant of `FilterStateProvider`. Exposed `persistRunScriptOptions` via this Context's own value instead and deleted the bridge outright. `window.persistViewFilterOptionOverrides` turned out to have **zero** production callers left at all - a grep found only this file's own and the integration suite's tests still calling it; the real "Apply filters (local)" button's own call to it was removed in an earlier phase, and every real save now happens via this Provider's own debounced effect, calling `persistViewFilterOptions()` directly, not through the bridge. Left in place anyway, explicitly as a test-only hook, not closed this round: ~9 call sites across `index.html.test.js` use it as a "force the persist now" determinism shortcut, and converting all of them to drive the real 150ms debounce via fake timers is a separate, dedicated pass, not worth bundling into this one given the blast radius.
      - **Test fallout:** `index.html.test.js`'s own `RunScriptActionButtonsTestHarness` (already a genuine `FilterStateProvider` descendant) now calls `useFilterState().persistRunScriptOptions()` directly instead of the bridge, same change as the real app.
      - **Verified:** full jest suite (258 suites/2488 tests, net 0); `npx eslint .` clean project-wide; full Playwright smoke suite (55/55).

**Scope note, same as every phase before this one:** this reconciliation itself doesn't implement anything - it's the "stop and check the whole plan is still honest" pass the user asked for directly, prompted by noticing that several "Not closed by this slice" notes had never been followed up on. Each new phase above gets the same bounded-slice-plus-full-verification treatment as A-F before it's considered done.

## 🗺️ **Remaining-effort audit: what's left to reach zero `window.*` bridges (2026-10-16)**

Asked directly, after H.11: how much is actually left to fully migrate everything to React. Research only, nothing implemented this entry - a grep-grounded inventory of the 39 `window.*` bridge *assignments* still in `index.page.js` (2,894 lines), grouped by what's actually blocking each one from closing, plus relative sizing against the H.1-H.11 phases already done.

**Correction to how this was first presented (direct user instruction, 2026-10-16): there is no 6th "genuinely permanent" category.** An earlier verbal summary of this audit described `window.setStatusMessage`/`window.handleRequestMoreMerged`/`window.getFormBody`/`window.loadSchedulerStatus` as "deliberately kept" / permanent vanilla boundaries. That's the exact mistake the plan's own standing policy (2026-10-12, above) already named and closed the door on - "every `window.*` bridge... gets an actual landing spot... 'hard to close soon' is not the same as 'permanent'." Restating these 4 correctly, each gated on one of the 5 clusters below, not exempt from any of them:
- `window.setStatusMessage` - closes as a side effect once the vanilla rendering functions that still call it directly (`renderBackfillStatus` in cluster 4, `renderPrData`-adjacent code in cluster 1) move into React; no separate phase of its own.
- `window.handleRequestMoreMerged` - `MergedRequestMoreAction.jsx`'s click handler stays vanilla only because `pr-merged-request-more-action.helpers.js`'s own async orchestration hasn't moved to a hook yet - same shape cluster 1's `renderPrData` pipeline already blocks on, not a separate concern.
- `window.getFormBody` - reads the Run Script form's fields via `FormData`, even though every one of those fields is already a React-rendered input bound to `FilterStateContext`/`RowFilterSelectionContext`. Likely closeable on its own, independent of the other clusters - **landing spot: new cluster 6 below**, not folded into a bigger one, since nothing blocks it architecturally; it just hasn't been tried.
- `window.loadSchedulerStatus` - used by `useQuickCheckActions` (already a real hook) and the vanilla scheduler-status poll/forced-refresh path; closes once that poll path is itself owned by a Provider (cluster 1's own orbit - `PrDataProvider` already owns `pollBackfillStatus`/`pollForDataChanges`, scheduler-status is the one sibling poll left outside it).

**The 5 real clusters, by what blocks each one, loosely ordered by size (grounded in the 39 bridges' line numbers in `index.page.js`, not a guess):**

1. **UPDATED, WITH A DEFINITIVE EMPIRICAL FINDING (2026-10-19 - see cluster 5 above). `renderPrData`/polling pipeline (~12 bridges: `computePrDataManifest`, `getManifestDelta`, `mergeDataDeltaPayload`, `computePrDataFingerprint`, `computePrDataMetaFingerprint`, `getDataPollRenderAction`, `getLatestStoredPayload`, `applyLatestPrData`, `renderPrData`, `markPollSuccess`, `showPollFailureWarning`, plus `window.loadSchedulerStatus`'s own dependency above).** Still the biggest and most central, but the "why it's not closeable" list is now stale: data-meta text/filter chips (H.12) and Author Insights (H.17) have both already moved off this pipeline onto live Context derivations.
   - **The cluster-5 research's "stub and run Playwright" suggestion was carried out directly**: `window.applyFiltersFromCache`/the local `debouncedApplyFilters` were temporarily stubbed to no-ops and the full suite run. Full Playwright smoke suite: **55/55 still passed** - including "Export tab's field catalog and Preview JSON" (resolving that entry's one previously-unverified dependency: confirmed NOT actually dependent on this pipeline either). Full jest: **4 real failures**, all in `index.html.test.js` - `attention toggle changes update row icons and persist non-default overrides`, `attention toggle changes update row icons and persist non-default overrides` (sibling test), `draft attention icon logic follows include-draft toggles`, and `given both allow and deny starter lists include the same actor, when policy mode switches, then only the active mode list is enforced` (itself testing "Needs Attention" smart-group membership, per its own comment).
   - **Definitive conclusion**: `applyFiltersFromCache`/`debouncedApplyFilters`/the `renderPrData`/`prDataTabOrchestrator` pipeline they drive are **not dead** - they are the one remaining real mechanism that recomputes "Needs Attention" rule-based attention icons/smart-group membership (`attention-include-pending-comments`/`attention-no-activity-mode`/`attention-include-draft-changed`/the thread-resolution-policy-driven smart group, etc.) on the real React-rendered table. This tracks exactly with this plan's own long-standing, deliberate scope boundary: the live-filtering Context work (`useVisiblePrNumbers`/`useFilteredPrRows`) was always scoped to *row-level table filters* only, explicitly excluding Needs Attention toggles ("Needs Attention toggles... live on other tabs/settings, not row-level table filters" - see the filter-chips feature's own scope note). Nothing in that scoping ever changed, so this result isn't a surprise in hindsight, just now confirmed rather than assumed.
   - **Revised landing spot**: closing this cluster for real means migrating "Needs Attention" rule-based icon/smart-group computation onto a live Context derivation too (the same shape `useVisiblePrNumbers` already established for row filters) - a genuine, substantial feature port, not a bridge-renaming exercise. The one still-open thread from the prior pass (whether `currentActorLoginAliases`/`currentViewerLogin`'s other vanilla-only consumers - `collectRequestedReviewers`/`collectAssignedUsers`/`collectApproversFromRow` - are independently dead) is now moot for *this* question, since the pipeline as a whole is confirmed still load-bearing regardless.
   - Rough size: **2-4x** a typical H.x sub-phase, unrevised - the empirical result didn't shrink the ceiling, it just replaced a guess with a confirmed scope: porting Needs Attention rule computation to a live derivation, same shape as the original row-filter work.
   - **Correction (Phase H.18, 2026-10-19): the "Needs Attention rule computation" framing above was itself wrong.** A dedicated research pass found that computation was *already* fully live in React (`NeedsAttentionContext`/`NeedsAttentionProvider`, `PrAttentionCell`, `PrTableApp`'s own smart-group `useMemo`) - the vanilla pipeline's only real role was incidentally forcing `NeedsAttentionProvider` to re-render (a `usePrData()` punch-through hack working around a stale-closure bug in how that Provider read filter values), not computing anything. Fixed with a small, surgical rewrite (subscribe to `FilterStateContext` directly) - re-running the SAME stub-and-test empirical check against the fix dropped the jest failures from 4 to 1, confirming Needs Attention now has zero dependency on this pipeline. The 1 remaining failure (`given both allow and deny starter lists...`) is a *different* feature (Review Conversations thread-resolution-warning text) with its own, still-real dependency on this pipeline - narrower than "the whole pipeline is needed for Needs Attention," but not yet zero. Revised size for the remainder: smaller than 2-4x now that 2 of 3 known dependents (row filters, Needs Attention) are closed - see Phase H.18's own plan entry.
   - **Correction (Phase H.19, 2026-10-19): the last known dependent (the Review Conversations thread-resolution-warning text) is now also closed, by the same "stale re-render trigger, not missing computation" root cause as H.18** - `ReviewThreadsSection.jsx`'s own `computeReviewThreadsData` memo excluded the live policy from its dependency array, so it only ever picked up a policy change when the vanilla pipeline forced a `pr`/`actorsMap` reference change. Fixed by depending on the policy's own stable, Context-derived reference directly. `applyFiltersFromCache`/`debouncedApplyFilters` now have **zero known remaining dependents** (row filters, Needs Attention, and this warning text are all confirmed closed) - but `renderPrData` itself (the manifest/fingerprint/delta machinery below) still does real, unported work, so "zero known dependents on the 2 fallback bridges" is not the same claim as "cluster 1 is closed." See Phase H.19's own plan entry.
   - **SUBSTANTIALLY CLOSED (H.23, 2026-10-20).** The `renderPrData` pipeline itself (`prDataTabOrchestrator`, `deriveRunPrDataContext`/`deriveRenderPipelineState`, and 17 helper files feeding them) is deleted outright - a dedicated research pass found 2 of the "3 genuinely live things" this pipeline still did (per `index.page.js`'s own, by-then-stale comment) had already been superseded (Author Insights: H.17; "conditional label refresh": Phase G.5's `RepoLabelsProvider`), and the 3rd (fingerprint/manifest bookkeeping) was redundant on every poll-triggered render, genuinely needed only for the initial page load. `renderPrData` now computes its only 2 surviving outputs (repo resolution, fingerprint/manifest bookkeeping) directly, the same way `applyFreshPrPayload` already did. **Still open:** `window.loadSchedulerStatus` (small, independently closeable - no poll loop left since H.2's SSE switch) and `markPollSuccess`/`showPollFailureWarning`'s own small UI state, plus the 2 "Apply filters (local)" fallback bridges (`applyFiltersFromCache`/`debouncedApplyFilters` themselves, zero known dependents since H.19 but not yet deleted). See Phase H.23's own plan entry for the full writeup, including 2 real, subtle dependencies the research pass missed (`currentActorLoginAliases`'s indirect reader via `normalizeActorLogin`'s default param, and `pr-dom-traversal.helpers.js`'s second real consumer) - both caught during implementation, not before it.
   - **CLOSED (H.24, 2026-10-20). `window.loadSchedulerStatus`/`window.getSupportsSchedulerPolling`.** **Correction to this entry's own "closes once owned by a Provider" framing (below):** there was no automatic poll loop left to own by the time this landed (retired Phase H.2) and the flag it used to gate (`supportsSchedulerPolling`) had zero remaining reader - so the real fix was extracting `loadSchedulerStatus` into its own pure DI-factory helper (`pr-scheduler-status.helpers.js`), with `index.page.js`/`useQuickCheckActions.jsx` (its only 2 real callers) each building a separate instance, same "every live consumer builds its own instance" pattern as everywhere else in this migration - not a Provider move. See Phase H.24's own plan entry for the full writeup, including an unrelated `eslint.config.mjs` hygiene fix found while here (23 stale file-list entries for already-deleted files, accumulated across several earlier phases, removed in the same pass).
   - **CLOSED (H.25, 2026-10-20). `window.markPollSuccess`/`window.showPollFailureWarning`.** Unlike `loadSchedulerStatus` just above, this pair genuinely was the "move into the Provider" case - both own real poll-adjacent UI state, and their one real caller (`pollForDataChanges`) already lives in `PrDataProvider.jsx`. `lastSuccessfulPollCheckAt`/`lastPollErrorAt`/`hasActivePollWarning` joined `trackingRef`; both functions are now defined inline there, reaching `window.showWarningNotification`/`window.hideErrorNotification` (Snackbar.jsx's own bridges) directly - no bridge needed for either function itself. See Phase H.25's own plan entry for the full writeup.
   - **CLOSED (H.26, 2026-10-20). `window.applyFiltersFromCache`/`window.debouncedApplyFilters` - the last 2 bridges anywhere in this audit.** A fresh empirical re-check (post-H.23 code, not the now-stale pre-H.23 one H.18/H.19 ran) confirmed zero remaining re-filtering/re-render dependents. The real-world complication: the "Apply filters (local)" button these 2 bridges drove was a real, visible feature whose *other* call, `persistViewFilterOptionOverrides`, was quietly the only save trigger for 14 fields/lists with no persist call of their own - deleting the button required adding auto-persist-on-change for all 14 first, a direct user decision made after being shown that finding, not a silent regression. See Phase H.26's own plan entry for the full writeup, including a second real cross-test contamination bug (3 Playwright tests leaving a stale persisted filter behind) found only by running the full suite, not the file in isolation.
   - **CLOSED (H.26, 2026-10-20). `window.applyFiltersFromCache`/`window.debouncedApplyFilters` - the last bridges in this entire audit.** A fresh empirical re-check (stub both to no-ops, run the full jest + Playwright suites against the post-H.23 code) confirmed zero remaining dependents, same conclusion H.18/H.19 reached pre-H.23 - but research surfaced something neither bridge's own "dead" framing accounted for: the "Apply filters (local)" button they drove was a real, currently-visible, user-clickable feature whose OTHER call, `window.persistViewFilterOptionOverrides?.()`, was quietly the *only* save trigger for 14 fields/lists (`scope-mode`/`filter-pr-numbers`/`always-show-in-review`/5 `attention-*` checkboxes/`attention-no-activity-mode`, the 6 `FilterOptionSelect` "Any" fields, and the `label`/`exclude-label`/`author`/`assigned`/`approver` multi-select lists) - confirmed by a Playwright test that deliberately persists via a raw API call specifically to avoid "checking boxes and clicking that button". Deleting the button outright (the user's own choice, after being shown this finding) required adding auto-persist-on-change for all 14, matching how every other field here already auto-persisted - not just a bridge deletion.
2. **CLOSED (H.15/H.16/H.17, 2026-10-19). Author Insights cluster (~5 bridges: `selectAuthorInsightsAuthor`, `navigateToPrInTableFromStats`, `navigateToPrInTableFromAuthorInsights`, `saveAuthorManualComment`, `updateAuthorManualComment`).** All used to route through `renderAuthorInsights` (`pr-author-insights.component.js`), a vanilla rendering component untouched since the Track B/C conversions moved its *sections* to JSX but left the orchestrating render function itself vanilla. H.15 closed `saveAuthorManualComment`/`updateAuthorManualComment`; H.16 closed `navigateToPrInTableFromAuthorInsights`/`navigateToPrInTableFromStats` by reusing the existing `useNavigateToPrInTable.jsx` hook; H.17 closed `selectAuthorInsightsAuthor` - `AuthorInsightsSelector.jsx` now owns the author-selection validation/fallback rule itself (`resolveValidAuthorLogin`, the same pure function `useNavigateToAuthorInsights.jsx` already used), and `pr-author-insights.component.js` is deleted entirely. Closing this cluster also closed cluster 5's own last bridge as a side effect, exactly as predicted below.
3. **CLOSED (H.21/H.22, 2026-10-19). Tab panel visibility (2 bridges: `activateDataTab`, `activateManagementTab`).** Small bridge count, large blast radius - deceptively the *least* bounded of the 5, or so it looked before research. **Correction (H.21's own research pass):** the "cross-cutting, touches most of the component tree" framing was accurate for the bridges' blast radius, not for the content layer - every panel's content was already fully React-rendered, and `TabStateProvider`/`TabStateContext` (Phase A) already tracked which tab was active as real state; closing this cluster never needed new Context wiring in the content layer, just moving who *writes* each panel's `hidden` attribute. H.21 closed the safe precursor (`useIsTabPanelVisible`/`useHasTabPanelBeenVisible` reading `TabStateContext` directly), finding and fixing a real Playwright-only regression along the way (the `TabStateProvider`-nested-inside-`AppRoot` bug class H.20 had just fixed for `FilterStateProvider`). H.22 closed the rest: both bridges and their 2 helper files deleted outright, replaced by a new `TabPanelVisibilitySync` component owning all 9 panels' `hidden` attribute reactively, with the 2 real side effects `activateTab` used to also drive (Action Log/Actor Names reloading on re-activation) moved into those components' own effects. The ~60-call-site test-harness risk the research flagged as "genuinely concentrated, hard-to-split" turned out closeable with one local test-only shim (a plain DOM-write helper, no Context) rather than a rewrite of every call site - see Phase H.22's own plan entry for the full writeup.
4. **CLOSED (H.13/H.14, 2026-10-19). Backfill status rendering (~8 bridges: `getBackfillTrackingState`, `setSupportsBackfillLogPolling`, `setIsBackfillActionPending`, `updateBackfillStatusFromPayload`, `setBackfillLogMessage`, `renderBackfillStatus`, `loadBackfillStatus`, `autoScrollBackfillLogToBottom`).** H.11 closed the tab-activation *trigger*; H.13 deleted `autoScrollBackfillLogToBottom` outright (confirmed dead); H.14 moved `renderBackfillStatus`/`updateBackfillStatusFromPayload` and the tracking state (`supportsBackfillLogPolling`/`isBackfillRunning`/`isBackfillActionPending`/`lastBackfillStateKey`) into a new `BackfillStatusProvider`, same shape as `StatusTextProvider` (Phase H.8), as originally sized. `loadBackfillStatus` itself (the fetch) stays vanilla in both index.page.js and useBackfillActions.jsx, each with their own instance - a separate, pre-existing concern this cluster's own bridges never covered.
5. **PARTIALLY CLOSED, AND PARTIALLY MISCATEGORIZED (research pass, 2026-10-19 - see cluster 1 below). The "Apply filters (local)" vanilla fallback pipeline.** The research this entry asked for (post-H.17: do `applyFiltersFromCache`/`debouncedApplyFilters`/`loadStoredData`/`summarizeAckRefreshWarnings`/`refreshAvailableRepoLabels` have any reason to stay vanilla beyond the now-closed Author Insights dependency?) found this cluster's own original 6-bridge grouping was wrong from the start, not just partially stale:
   - **`loadStoredData`, `summarizeAckRefreshWarnings`, `refreshAvailableRepoLabels` were never actually blocked by Author Insights at all** - grep confirms zero relationship to it in any direction. `loadStoredData` is the app's sole initial-page-load fetch *and* the real fallback-reload path for the live bulk ack/label-action flow (`pr-ack-label-actions.helpers.js`). `summarizeAckRefreshWarnings` is a pure ack-refresh-error summarizer, exclusively consumed by that same ack/label-action flow. `refreshAvailableRepoLabels` is independently load-bearing for 3 real consumers (the "↻ Labels" button, `RepoLabelsProvider`'s repo-change effect, `FilterStateProvider`'s restore-on-mount effect). **Corrected: these 3 are genuinely-needed, independent functionality, not pending on anything - removed from this cluster's remaining-effort list entirely.** They were only ever grouped here because they happened to be among `index.page.js`'s remaining raw `window.*` bridges, not because they shared a real code path with Author Insights.
   - **`applyFiltersFromCache`/`debouncedApplyFilters` show strong evidence of now being fully dead** (not just Author-Insights-related) - `applyFiltersFromCache`'s only real effects are a cosmetic status-message string and a React re-render with unchanged payload; the actual filtering it used to drive is fully superseded by `useVisiblePrNumbers`/`useFilteredPrRows` (live, Context-driven, confirmed by the Phase 7 smoke test "the 'Applied filters' chip/summary text updates live too, with no 'Apply filters (local)' click at all"). But confirming this *for real* means tracing `renderPrData`/`prDataTabOrchestrator`'s full pipeline (`deriveRenderPipelineState`/`deriveViewerFilterSetup`/`commitViewerContext`/`deriveScopeSettings`, etc.) - which is **cluster 1's own territory** (the `renderPrData`/polling pipeline, already sized as the biggest remaining cluster, 2-4x), not a quick cluster-5 wrap-up. **Landing spot: moved into cluster 1's own scope below**, with this research as a confirmed head start, not resolved independently here.
   - `getFormBody`'s 3 DOM-scan dependents stay cluster 6's concern, unaffected by this correction.
6. **CLOSED (H.20, 2026-10-19). `window.getFormBody` (1 bridge).** Read the Run Script form fields via `FormData` even though every one of those fields (`repo`/`limit`/`mergedLimit`/`jobs`/`prNumbersInput`/`openMode`/`ackChanged`/`showReason`/`quiet`) was already a Context-backed React input, plus 3 multi-select DOM scans shared with cluster 5. **Correction (direct user instruction, 2026-10-16): the "no architectural blocker found yet, 0.5-1x" sizing below was wrong, found out by actually starting it.** Its 3 real callers (`useRunScript`/`useAckLabelActions`/`useQuickCheckActions`) were called from `AppRoot`'s own body, which was an **ancestor** of `FilterStateProvider`/`RowFilterSelectionProvider` (both rendered *inside* `AppRoot`'s JSX, never wrapped from `mountAppRoot()` the way `StatusTextProvider`/`PrActivityQueueProvider` were) - closing it for real needed the same Provider-tree move Phase G.3 did, just one level further out. H.20 did exactly that: moved `RowFilterSelectionProvider`/`AuthorThreadResolutionSelectionProvider`/`ChangeFilterActorSelectionProvider`/`FilterStateProvider` to wrap `AppRoot` (from `mountAppRoot()`), added `state/useFormBody.jsx` reading both Contexts directly, and deleted `getFormBody()`/its bridge outright - see Phase H.20's own plan entry for the full writeup, including a new "Provider lifted above a Provider it used to be nested inside" risk class (confirmed inert) and a second static-markup/test-harness bug it found along the way, same class as H.18/H.19's own.

**Overall (updated 2026-10-20 - every cluster in this audit is now closed):** Cluster 2 (Author Insights) is done (H.15-H.17). Cluster 6 (`getFormBody`) is done (H.20). Cluster 3 (tab panel visibility) is done (H.21/H.22). Cluster 1 - the biggest, most central cluster, and the only one left - is done across 4 sub-phases: the `renderPrData` pipeline itself is deleted outright (H.23); `window.loadSchedulerStatus` is closed (H.24 - extracted to its own DI-factory helper, not moved into a Provider as first guessed, since no automatic poll loop was left to own); `markPollSuccess`/`showPollFailureWarning` are closed (H.25 - moved into `PrDataProvider.jsx` directly, the one bridge pair in this cluster that actually did fit the original "move into the Provider" prediction); and `applyFiltersFromCache`/`debouncedApplyFilters` - the "Apply filters (local)" button and its 2 bridges, the last ones anywhere in this audit - are closed (H.26, after first adding auto-persist-on-change for 14 fields/lists the button turned out to be the sole save trigger for). **This audit's own goal - zero `window.*` bridges across every cluster it ever tracked - is met.** Phase H itself was scoped as one phase before splitting into 26 sub-phases once its real size became clear.

## ✅ **Window-bridge elimination, Phase 1: three same-tree hook swaps (2026-10-21)**

Plan: [WINDOW_BRIDGE_ELIMINATION_PLAN.md](WINDOW_BRIDGE_ELIMINATION_PLAN.md).

- `JobEventsProvider.jsx` now calls `pollForDataChanges` from `PrDataContext` (held in a ref so the SSE effect never reconnects on identity change) instead of `window.pollForDataChanges`.
- `useBackfillActions.jsx` now takes the tracking flags and setters from `useBackfillStatus()`. `BackfillStatusProvider` gained a ref-backed `getBackfillTrackingState` on its Context, pulled forward from Phase 4: state values captured in the hook's `useMemo` would go stale mid-flow, which the old bridge never did.
- `NotesSection.jsx` now uses `usePrData().beginRequestActivity`. Four test files that render a real `NotesSection` (`NotesSection`, `PrRow`, `PrInsightsRow`, `NotesDirtyProvider`) now supply a `PrDataContext`.
- Tests: 1572 pass, 1 fails (`InsightsHookSection`, pre-existing: `dompurify` isn't installed). No tests added or removed.
- Not run: `eslint` (`eslint-plugin-react-hooks` missing from node_modules) and Playwright.
- Environment note: the default shell is Node 16, but Jest 30 needs Node 18+; use `PATH=/opt/homebrew/bin:$PATH`.

Plan: [WINDOW_BRIDGE_ELIMINATION_PLAN.md](WINDOW_BRIDGE_ELIMINATION_PLAN.md). Mount-tree change only; no caller was touched.

- Both Providers moved out of `AppRoot`'s returned JSX to wrap it from `mountAppRoot()`, inside `SnackbarProvider`, with `AutoRenderBlockingProvider` outside `PrDataProvider`. `<PrDataPolling/>` moved with them as `AppRoot`'s sibling. The inner `AutoRenderBlockingProvider` that wrapped just the two blocked-status portals is gone; those portals resolve to the outer one.
